# Change history

What shipped in `avatar-studio/`, one entry per feature or pass, in the order it landed, with the short commit hash of each change. Each entry ends with what an independent review of that commit found and where the fix landed, so a reader can see which guarantees are tested and why a piece of code looks the way it does.

The route reference is in [API.md](./API.md); the setup and feature overview is in the [README](../README.md).

- First self-review pass (12 findings: provider compatibility, photo-avatar upload, webhook hardening, polling leak, truncation handling, duplicated helpers) fixed in `fb55f82`
- Second self-review pass (12 findings: SSRF on image URLs, open redirect after login, global login rate-limit backstop, batch allowance reservation and concurrency, failed-row reporting, server-reported usage, swallowed delete/rename errors, double rename submissions) fixed in `e269ac9`
- Third self-review pass over the brand kit, folders, bulk, API key, and webhook commits: deliveries moved after the response (`after()`), selection cleared on filter changes, last-used writes throttled; fixed in `489714a`
- Fourth self-review pass over workspaces: keys minted under a workspace now end with the member's membership, and deletion revokes everything; fixed in `9530cbf`
- Services layer split into one module per domain under `src/lib/services/` with a re-exporting index, so `@/lib/services` imports are unchanged; stale `lint` script removed (`next lint` no longer exists in Next 16); `386535b`, `57ce0d5`
- Fifth self-review pass: the Permissions-Policy header denied the microphone to the app itself, so voice-clone recording failed with `NotAllowedError` (reproduced in Chromium, fixed and asserted in the security-headers e2e test); `57ce0d5`
- Sixth self-review pass: the website embed now delegates the microphone to its iframe (`e31e1a9`); email preferences are part of the account export and deletion (`25106fb`); provider language names ("English", "Spanish (Mexico)") resolve to speech-API tags for the recogniser and browser voices (`06b4cd3`)
- Seventh self-review pass at phone width (390px): the editor scrolled sideways (grid columns could not shrink, toolbar and side tabs too wide) and the video page's action row ran off-screen; both fixed and re-measured with no element past the viewport (`1bfd153`, `67b3fa0`, `0d1af8d`); a "Viewers can download" checkbox that only flipped after the save returned now updates optimistically (`af7debf`)
- Security review (four findings, three confirmed at high confidence and fixed in `72239ed`): any `Authorization: Bearer` value skipped the proxy's session check while `/api/script` and `/api/voices/preview` validated nothing (anonymous use of the paid script and TTS providers; in password mode also the workspace routes); `/auth/callback?next=/%09/evil.com` redirected off-site because the URL parser strips tabs; the SSRF guard missed IPv4-mapped IPv6 literals in the hex form the URL parser emits. The fourth finding (editors reaching account wipe and billing) was judged within the documented role model but tightened anyway. Each fix has unit and e2e coverage
- Eighth pass, an independent review of the day's diff (8 findings): retries and batch rows written once so a fast provider webhook is not overwritten, a refused retry opens the recorded failed video instead of piling up silent copies, the share download link opens in a new tab (bare `download` is ignored cross-origin), the chat recogniser cannot send a transcript after "Start over" or unmount, batch quality uses the schema default, API docs corrected on frame sizes and which providers honour quality; fixed in `f2a70db`
- Ninth pass: the SSRF guard checked a hostname's addresses and then let `fetch` resolve the name a second time, so a changing DNS answer could still reach a private host; page import, avatar images, and webhook delivery now connect through an undici agent whose lookup returns only the vetted addresses (proved against a local server with a name that does not resolve: the pinned fetch succeeds, an unpinned one gets `ENOTFOUND`); IPv6 check tightened to 2000::/3 with mapped and NAT64 forms unwrapped; editors see a read-only plan and data card instead of buttons that return 403; `b87a402`, `06fdd69`. An independent review of those two commits then found that the one-off client was closed before the body was read (every response over ~50 KB stalled until the abort timer), that the script route still used the global fetch so page import was not pinned, and that the redirect message could never appear; all three fixed in `a4a965f` with tests that drive the pinned client against a local server (3 MB body intact, 1 MB cap enforced, 270 KB page import verified in the built server)
- Tenth pass: the library polled the list while renders were in flight but the list never asked the provider, so cards stayed "Processing" until the detail page was opened or a webhook arrived (and the polling never stopped); the list now refreshes in-flight renders, with an e2e test that watches a card reach Completed with no detail-route request; `994ff78`
- Eleventh pass: the library returned and rendered every video; `GET /api/videos` now takes `q`, `status`, `folder`, `batch`, `sort`, and `limit` and reports `matched`, `total`, `folders`, `rendering`, and `hasMore`, the page loads 48 cards with a "Show more" button and polls on the server's in-flight count; e2e creates a 50-row batch and pages through it; also restores a `GET /api/videos` docs heading an earlier edit had blanked; `34c5647`
- Twelfth pass, an independent review of the two library commits (5 findings): a slow response for an earlier path could overwrite the newer page and restart polling on a URL the page had left (per-request tickets in the hook), a filter change after "Show more" sent a wasted request (extent keyed to the filter tuple), two concurrent refreshers could announce one completion twice (single-flight per render plus a re-read before the write), the refresh wrote a stale snapshot over edits made meanwhile (writes build on the stored record), and a render whose provider check keeps failing hogged the refresh slots (deferred for a minute); fixed in `c3a3e0b` with unit coverage
- Per-scene presenters: a scene may name its own `avatarId` and `voiceId`; the render context resolves the whole cast (custom portraits prepared for each), scenes store presenter and voice names, the editor offers a "Scene N presenter" row whose choice drives the stage preview and Listen, drafts and copies keep it, and the detail page lists it; unit tests cover the HeyGen inputs and the D-ID refusal, the two-scene e2e test picks a second presenter; `eb9382d`. An independent review found two things, fixed in `029c557`: a scene whose presenter had since been deleted kept the dead id (the select showed the video's choice but Generate got a 404), now dropped once the lists load, and the e2e stage assertion was vacuous
- Voice library filters by language and gender with a "N of M voices" count; `9d5b8bc`; the editor's voice tab has the same language select
- A personalized batch uploaded a custom portrait once per row (rows render five at a time and each prepared its own copy); rows now share the context and preparation is single-flight per avatar, so a batch uploads once; `2c90808`
- Scenes can be moved earlier or later and duplicated (script, background, and presenter included), with an e2e test checking the rendered order; `a657154`
- My templates: save a video as a template and reopen the editor from it; own store kind, part of export and deletion; unit tests cover copying, ownership, defaults, deletion, and the cap, and an e2e test runs the whole flow; `76bdd9d`. An independent review found that brand-kit defaults could overwrite a saved template's voice, captions, and quality depending on which request landed last; fixed in `fb3340c` with the e2e test now opening the template against a competing kit
- Avatar library filters by gender and name with an "N of M" count; `05b74a5`
- Share-page call to action: `PATCH /api/videos/:id` takes `shareCta` ({ label, url } or null), set from a card on the video page; unit and e2e coverage; `38b5428`. An independent review found four things, fixed in `99a3062`: schema failures reached the page as a generic "Invalid request" (the client now shows the first field issue, on every page), the share button always used white text even on a light accent, a long label could widen the share page on a phone, and a status poll started before a save could land after it with the old record (the page refetches after a share change)
- Running time per scene: each scene chip shows its estimated duration and the script header reads "N words · ~Xs this scene · ~Ys total"; chips carry an accessible label and `aria-current`; e2e covered; `f90121c`
- Call-to-action clicks: the share button sends one beacon per click (`POST /api/videos/:id/cta`, public, one per viewer per 10 minutes, counted only while a completed video carries the button) and the video page shows "Button clicks"; unit and e2e coverage (two popup clicks, one counted); `52b1657`. An independent review of these commits found no blocking defect; its two low findings are fixed in `b588610`: a page on another site could count views or clicks for visitors who never opened the share page (browser beacons whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` is not same-origin are now ignored) and a loop forging the client address could inflate a count without bound (600 views or 300 clicks per video per ten minutes, with the viewer's own window checked first so a refresh does not use up the ceiling)
- Share link offline: `PATCH /api/videos/:id` takes `shareDisabled`; the share page, its captions, and both beacons answer the public with 404 while the owner keeps access (the captions route falls back to the request's owner); "Share link is live" checkbox on the video page; unit coverage plus e2e in open mode (from the page) and password mode (anonymous vs owner); `e60e6b4`
- Link previews: the share page generates Open Graph and Twitter metadata (title with brand name, script excerpt cut at a word boundary, thumbnail, and the video with its frame size once rendered; "Video not found" with noindex for an offline or unknown link); excerpt helper unit-tested, tags asserted in the offline e2e test; `886d81e`
- Password-protected share links: `PATCH /api/videos/:id` takes `sharePassword` (4–64 characters or null); the salted scrypt hash lives in its own `sharelock` record (redacted in exports, removed with the video) and the video carries only `shareLocked`; `POST /api/videos/:id/unlock` (public, rate limited per client and per video) sets an httpOnly cookie derived from the hash, so a changed or removed password signs every visitor out; share page, captions, and beacons honour it, the owner never needs it, and a protected link previews with its title only; Protect / Change / Remove form on the video page; unit, open-mode e2e (owner and visitor), and password-mode e2e (anonymous vs owner); `bf6db46`. A security-focused independent review of that commit found a critical defect fixed in `20def6e`: the lock record reused the video's id, and because the Supabase table keys every kind by id alone, saving a password there would have upserted the video's row into a lock and back, leaving no lock and a check that failed open (the in-memory store keys per kind, so tests could not see it); the lock is now keyed `sharelock_<video id>` with the key pinned by a unit test, a video marked locked with no lock record stays closed, the unlock cookie is SameSite=None and Secure in production so embedded share pages can unlock, the visitor's password is trimmed like the owner's, and a correct password clears the attempt counter. The same commit carries an independent review's findings on the offline-link and preview commits: the beacons answered an offline video with 200 and its real counts although the docs said 404 (now 404 like the page), two share saves in flight could let the earlier response undo the later one (merged on the page), and the preview excerpt could split an emoji or CJK character (cut by code point)
- Home insights total call-to-action clicks with the click rate per view and show clicks beside views in the most-viewed list; `369e3d1`
- The in-memory store now refuses the same id under two kinds, mirroring the Supabase table's single primary key, so an id collision like the share-lock one fails in unit tests and e2e instead of in production; `ce37c13`
- Conversations export: `GET /api/agents/:id/conversations?format=csv` downloads up to 1000 conversations (times, visitor email and name, message count, first question, last reply) with formula-safe cells; "Download CSV" in the transcripts panel; serialiser unit-tested, e2e downloads the file; `93b4e72`
- Favourite avatars and voices: `POST /api/prefs/favorites` stars or unstars (newest 50 per kind on the preferences record), starred items list first on the Avatars and Voices pages and in the editor's pickers, with the star beside the control rather than inside it; also caps share-password attempts per client across all videos, lets the Interactive page's presenter actions wrap at phone width (a latent overflow the phone-width test now provokes by creating a presenter), and keeps avatar tiles full width; unit and e2e coverage; `0311666`. An independent review of the store-invariant, CSV, and favourites commits found one medium and three low issues, fixed in `222e709`: two quick stars could lose the first on Supabase (saves now run one after another, clicks before the first load are ignored), the voice row nested two buttons inside a role=button div (the name block is now the select button, keyboard-reachable), and the CSV lacked a byte-order mark for Excel. The same commit adds share badges to library cards and fixes a flip-back the full suite exposed under load: a merged save kept share flags the server had dropped, so the "Share link is live" box bounced off until the refetch landed
- Custom cover image: `PATCH /api/videos/:id` takes `cover` (data:image URL, PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF, 2 MB, or null); bytes in their own `cover` record (removed with the video) and `cover: { updatedAt, mimeType, bytes }` on the video, where `updatedAt` versions `GET /api/videos/:id/cover`, which follows the share page's offline and password rules with the owner always allowed; poster on the video page, cards, share page, and link preview; browser-side downscale to 1280px; unit, open-mode e2e (upload, serve, share, card, offline, remove), and password-mode e2e (unlocked visitor, stranger, owner); `0ec729c`. An independent review found one medium and three low issues, fixed in `514dbfb`: the versioned cover was marked cacheable by shared caches even behind a password or an offline link (now `private` unless the link is freely shared), the account export loaded every cover's bytes to report sizes (the video carries them now and cover rows are left out of the export), a malformed data URL crashed the request instead of answering 400, and the offline rule for covers was only tested where everyone is the owner (now checked against a stranger in password mode); transparent PNGs also land on white instead of black
- Share link expiry: `PATCH /api/videos/:id` takes `shareExpiresAt` (ISO time in the future, or null), stored as a UTC instant; once it passes, the share page, captions, cover, and beacons answer the public with 404 while the owner keeps access; Set / Change / Clear expiry with a local date-and-time field and an "Expires" or "Expired" badge on the video page, "Expired" badge on cards; unit coverage (schema, switch-over, owner exception, beacons, clearing), open-mode e2e drives the form, password-mode e2e watches a stranger lose the page, captions, and cover when a short expiry passes; `d874187`. An independent review found one medium and four low issues, fixed in `9c0fa72`: a shared cover's public cache lifetime could outlive the expiry in a CDN (now capped at the expiry, asserted in e2e), the schema accepted wall-clock times without an offset which the server would read in its own zone (an offset is now required and an offset value is checked to store the right instant), the unit test raced an 80 ms window (it now moves the stored instant behind the clock), the typed expiry was cleared even when the save failed, and the e2e date could land in a daylight-saving transition on non-UTC machines (noon now)
- Send by email: `POST /api/videos/:id/share` emails the share link of a finished video to up to ten de-duplicated recipients with an optional note through the email provider (the demo provider records the message and the response says nothing was delivered); 409 while the link is off or expired, 60 recipients per owner per hour, a password-protected link tells the recipient a password is needed without including it; "Send by email" card on the video page, disabled while the link is off; unit tests cover the schema, preconditions, ownership, message contents and escaping, the allowance, and partial provider failures; e2e drives the card and the API; `3fe655c`. An independent review found one medium and six low issues, fixed in `c39a107`: the allowance was keyed on the active workspace so one account could multiply it by creating workspaces (now charged to the signed-in sender with an instance-wide backstop of 600 per hour, and a refused batch takes nothing from the allowance), the link's host came from the request rather than `APP_URL`, a total provider failure showed a generic 502 instead of the provider's error, a partial failure kept the delivered addresses in the box so a retry would email them twice, the status live region was mounted with its first message so screen readers missed it, replies went to the app's address instead of the sender, and the docs missed two response codes
- Watch-through: the share-page player posts `POST /api/videos/:id/watch` with the quarter reached (public; one per viewer per quarter per 10 minutes, 600 per quarter per video, same cross-site, offline, password, and completed rules as views); the video carries `watch: { q25, q50, q75, q100 }` and its page shows a "Watch-through" row with the share of views played to the end; unit tests cover the schema, the rules, and the per-quarter windows, e2e posts beacons and reads the row; `46d9753`. An independent review found one high and three low issues, fixed in `6adf3ed`: quarter beacons that landed together (a seek past several markers, or a view and a quarter) each overwrote the others' write (the share counters of one video are now written one after another within the instance, a beacon reports the furthest point reached so every marker not yet counted lands in one write, the player chains its beacons, and a unit test fires five beacons at once), the row was ellipsised at laptop widths hiding the 100% figure (it wraps now and shows for every finished video), the tooltip's completion rate was unbounded (clamped), and the docs still named two beacons
- Finish rate in Home insights: the share of viewers who played a shared video to the end across the library, and each most-viewed video's own rate, both capped at 100%; unit and e2e coverage; `8900ec3`. An independent review of the counter-lock and finish-rate commits found one medium and three low issues, fixed in `1ab3fb1`: the per-video lock map never released its entries (the cleanup compared the wrong promise), owner edits and late provider webhooks could still write a stale snapshot over the counters (edits now write under the lock and carry the stored counts, a repeated terminal webhook is ignored, unit-tested with a view landing mid-edit), the layout check measured an inline element, and the home finish rate read 0% for libraries whose views predate watch marks (now "no data")
- Analytics CSV: `GET /api/videos/analytics` downloads one formula-safe row per video (status, created, folder, duration, views, views in the last seven days, last viewed, watch marks, finish rate, clicks, share link) with a "Download CSV" link on the Home insights panel; row builder unit-tested, e2e reads the file; `66530d8`. An independent review found no defect and three test gaps, closed in `7b01dd3`: a 401 assertion for the route in password mode, the e2e now reads the file with the app's own CSV parser, and the unit test round-trips the rows through the serialiser
- Bulk share links: `POST /api/videos/bulk` takes `{ action: "share", ids, shareDisabled }` and the library selection bar gets "Links off" and "Links on"; unit and e2e coverage; `19e2710`. An independent review found one high, one medium, and two low issues, fixed in `f0c3275`: the share buttons opened the delete confirmation before switching links (only Delete confirms now, and the e2e asserts which dialogs appear), every action made the Delete button spin (each button shows its own spinner), videos deleted elsewhere were skipped silently (the library now says how many were skipped), and the "Links on" tooltip now notes that a password or expiry set on a video stays in place. A second review of that fix found three small items, carried in `8ff1ee4`: the skipped notice pluralised on the wrong count and called an already-deleted video a skip, and the selection bar's other controls stayed live during a request so a new selection could be wiped when the earlier request finished
- Views per day: every counted share view lands on its UTC day in `viewsByDay` on the video (last 90 days kept); the video page shows a "Views by day" row with a fourteen-day sparkline (each bar names its day and count on hover, the total is read out) and the analytics CSV gains `views_7d`; the map is a beacon-owned counter like the other counts; unit tests cover the roll-up, the window, the series, the sum, and the column, e2e reads the sparkline and the column; `8ff1ee4`. An independent review found two low issues, fixed in `49901ba`: a key that was not a date survived the ninety-day window, and the view test read the day from the clock rather than the stored write
- Trash: deleting a video moves it to the trash (`deletedAt`), where it leaves the library and its share page, captions, cover, beacons, and email sharing answer the public with 404 while the owner can still open, restore, or delete it for good; trashed videos are removed with their side records 30 days after deletion the next time the owner's videos are listed; `GET /api/videos?trash=true` lists the trash newest deletion first and every page carries the trash count, `DELETE /api/videos/:id?permanent=true` and `POST /api/videos/:id/restore` act on one video, and `POST /api/videos/bulk` gains `restore` and `purge` (purge reaches only trashed videos); a failed render in the trash cannot be retried until restored; the library gets a Trash view with Restore and Delete forever in the selection bar and "N days left" badges, the video page a banner with the purge date and both actions; unit tests cover trashing, the public 404, counters surviving, restore, idempotent trashing, foreign ids, the trash query and count, the retry refusal, the purge after the retention period with its lock, and the bulk rules; the bulk e2e drives the flow through the library and the video page; `49901ba`. An independent review found three medium and six low issues, fixed in `933aa15`: the lazy purge was unbounded, sequential, duplicated across concurrent listings, and one failing removal turned every library, home, and settings request into a 500 (now 20 per listing, one pass per owner at a time, failures logged and left for the next listing, the due video gone from the owner's point of view either way), a write that read a video before a purge could recreate it without its lock and cover (writes now refuse a record that is no longer stored, and a trashed video past retention reads as not found and cannot be restored even before a listing purges it), one library page loaded the owner's videos twice, the trash view polled for a trashed in-flight render, `?permanent=true` purged a live video although the bulk rule and the docs said only from the trash (409 now), `?trash=1` was a 400 (accepted), emptying the trash with an empty library left a view with no way back (returns to "No videos yet", and restoring from the trash view stays there), a trashed video's page still offered Share and Embed links that 404 (hidden, and the email form says to restore first), and a confirm said "them" for one video
- Folder management: "Rename folder" and "Remove folder" on the chosen folder in the library; `POST /api/videos/folders { from, to }` moves every video in the folder (trashed ones included, so a restore lands in the renamed folder) to the new name, merges onto an existing name, or unfiles them when the new name is empty; unit tests cover the move, the merge, the removal, another owner's folder, and the schema; the folder e2e renames and removes through the library; `933aa15`. An independent review found two medium and three low issues, fixed in `4baeb05`: a video deleted for good while a rename ran aborted it half-applied (skipped and not counted now), a trashed video past its retention in a bulk selection failed the whole request instead of being reported (reported now, for restore, move, and share), a purge removed the video row before its lock and cover so a failure part-way orphaned them (side records first, row last, so the next listing retries), such a video could still be written through PATCH and answered 409 on retry (not found, like reads), and the bounded-purge test depended on the store's list order
- Presenter layout per scene: a scene may carry `layout: { scale, x, y }` (0.5–1.5 and -1..1); the editor's "Presenter layout" sliders size and move the active scene's presenter with the stage preview following, the default is stored as absent, drafts, copies, and templates keep it, the video page lists it per scene, and HeyGen receives it as the character's `scale` and `offset` (other renderers use their default); unit test on the HeyGen inputs, e2e drives the sliders and reads the stored scene; `4baeb05`. An independent review found three low issues, fixed in `c97a2b1`: the preview offset moved the portrait by a share of its own box rather than the frame (a frame-sized layer moves now, the portrait only scales), an explicit default layout sent through the API was stored and shown as a customisation (dropped on create), and the folder rename race test proved only that a thrown error was swallowed (the record now vanishes between the listing and its turn)
- Caption position and size: a video may carry `captionStyle: { position: "bottom" | "top", size: "small" | "medium" | "large" }`; the WebVTT file puts a `line:5%` cue setting on every cue for a top position, the share player carries the size as a class the stylesheet turns into a cue font size, the editor's Settings tab offers both once captions are on with the stage preview following, drafts, copies, templates, batches, and retries keep it, and the video page shows it; the default is sent and stored as absent; unit tests cover the cue setting and the schema, e2e creates a styled video, reads the VTT and SRT, checks the player class, the video page, and the editor's copy; `c97a2b1`. An independent review found one medium and two low issues, fixed in `62f1bd6`: a HeyGen render burns captions in at its own position and size, so a styled video would have shown two caption sets (HeyGen is told not to burn them in when a style is set, so the subtitle track is the only source, with the help text and docs saying so), the stage badge sat under a top caption bubble, and the batch docs missed the field
- Rename photo avatars: `PATCH /api/avatars/:id { name?, gender? }` renames a photo avatar or changes its gender (`null` clears it); library presenters answer 404; videos keep the name they were rendered with; a pencil on each photo avatar on the Avatars page; unit tests cover the rename, untouched fields, the cleared gender, ownership, library presenters, and the schema, the avatar e2e renames through the page; `62f1bd6`. An independent review found one medium-to-high, one medium, and two low issues, fixed in `6ced145`: the share page's subtitle track sat behind the CC menu, so a styled video (which no longer burns captions in) showed no captions until the viewer switched the track on (the track is on by default now, and the help text and docs say the downloaded file of a styled video carries no captions), shared presenters kept the avatar's old name because they copy it at creation (a rename updates them), a portrait upload that finished after a rename wrote the old row back (only the asset ids are written now, and an avatar deleted during the upload stays deleted), and a default caption style spelled out through the API was stored and rendered without burned-in captions while the editor's identical video got them (stored as absent whichever client sent it)
- Rename cloned voices: `PATCH /api/voices/:id { name }` renames a cloned voice; provider voices answer 404; videos keep the name they were rendered with; a pencil next to the delete button on the Voices page; unit tests cover the rename, untouched fields, the library order, the rendered video, ownership, provider voices, and the schema, the voice-cloning e2e renames through the page; `6ced145`. An independent review found one high, two medium, and two low issues, fixed in `9522c02`: switching the share page's track on by default would have drawn a second caption set over a HeyGen file that already carries captions (one rule, shared by the HeyGen adapter and the share page, says when captions are burned in, and only for such a file does the track wait in the CC menu), the presenter rename sync could leave stragglers that a repeated rename never reached and could answer 500 for a rename that had landed (synced by divergence, best effort, logged), a visitor's turn wrote its counters from a presenter read before a multi-second chat so a rename or edit meanwhile was reverted (counts on the stored row), the portrait upload replaced the asset-id map instead of merging it, and the docs did not say the provider's copy of a renamed voice keeps its name
- Default voice per photo avatar: `POST`/`PATCH /api/avatars` take `defaultVoiceId` (any voice from `GET /api/voices`, cloned ones included; an unavailable voice is a 400, `null` clears it, and deleting a cloned voice clears it from every avatar that named it); a select under each photo avatar on the Avatars page and in the create panel; the editor switches to the voice when the avatar is picked and opens with it when the avatar is the first in the list (drafts, templates, copies, and the brand kit still win); unit tests cover creation, the unavailable and foreign voice, update, clearing, the deleted clone, and the schema, the avatar e2e sets the voice on the page and watches the editor follow it; `9522c02`. An independent review found one medium and two low issues, fixed in `c7b9b9c`: the editor's first voice depended on which list request landed first (when the voices arrived before the avatars the first avatar's default voice was never applied; the pick now waits for the avatar list), a restored draft, template, copy, or brand kit whose saved voice was gone opened with another presenter's default voice (the restored presenter's own default now), and a visitor's turn with a presenter deleted during the chat wrote the presenter back (nothing is written, the reply is still returned); the same commit lets a scene's own presenter bring its default voice along unless the scene already chose a voice
- Video backgrounds: a scene's `background` may be `{ type: "video", value: <http(s) URL> }`, which HeyGen loops behind the presenter and fills the frame (other renderers use their default); the editor's Background tab takes the URL, the stage previews it, the video page names it, and the schema refuses a value that is not a URL; unit tests cover the HeyGen mapping and the schema, the multi-scene e2e sets one and reads it back from the stored scene; `c7b9b9c`. An independent review found no defect above low severity; three notes are carried in `f0d67d8`: the mid-chat deletion test now checks the store for transcript rows rather than an API call that would refuse any deleted presenter, the background schema's length limit names itself, and the stage previews a video background only once its URL is complete rather than requesting every half-typed value
- Share-page description: `PATCH /api/videos/:id` takes `description` (up to 500 characters; an empty string removes it, a retried render keeps it); a Description card on the video page, the share page shows it under the player, and link previews use it in place of the script excerpt; unit tests cover trimming, keeping, removal, the limit, and the retry, the share e2e saves one from the page and reads it on the share page and in the Open Graph tags; `f0d67d8`. An independent review found four low issues, fixed in `851a641`: the page's saves folded the server's answer into its record four different ways, so a response landing out of order could bring back a removed description or wipe a saved one (one merge helper now, which drops the optional keys the server no longer carries), the link preview carried the raw description rather than a trimmed excerpt, the library search skipped descriptions, and the retry docs missed the field. A review of that fix found two low notes, carried in `9acf24a`: the title and folder saves now refetch like the other saves and the cover joins the keys the merge may drop
- Knowledge page for shared presenters: `POST`/`PATCH /api/agents` take `knowledgeUrl`, a public web page read once with the script writer's page import guard (public host, HTML only, 1 MB; a page that cannot be read is a 400) and kept on the agent as reference material handed to the model after the owner's instructions; visitors never see it, `PATCH` with the URL reads it again and `null` forgets it; a field on the Interactive page and a "knows <host>" note in the presenter list; unit tests cover the read against a fake fetch, the visitor view, the demo provider answering from the page, the private host and a missing page, the re-read, and forgetting; the presenter e2e sees the form refuse a private host; `9acf24a`. An independent review found one high and four low issues, fixed in `59bb180`: zod's `partial()` keeps a field's default, so any `PATCH` on a shared presenter (turning the link off, switching email capture on, forgetting the page) reset its instructions, turned the link back on, and reset the language and email capture, a defect present since the agents shipped (the patch schema is now built from fields without defaults, with unit and e2e assertions that a one-field patch leaves the rest alone), the knowledge marker was duplicated as a hand-written pattern in the demo provider (one definition next to the chat persona type), the page text was presented to the model as the owner's own words (labelled untrusted reference material), the agent routes lacked the timeout the other fetching routes carry, and the guard's messages and docs did not name the knowledge field or its limits
- Brand kit default background: `PUT /api/brand` takes `defaultBackground` (an image or video background as on a video; a colour is the primary colour's job); a select and URL field on the Brand page; the editor's first scene starts with it; unit tests cover keeping it, dropping a colour, and the URL rule, the brand e2e sets an image and reads it off the editor's stage; `59bb180`. An independent review found two low form issues, fixed in `23b19f8`: removing the kit left the background and quality fields filled so a later save brought them back, and choosing an image or video with no URL saved silently as no background (Save stays disabled until the URL is typed)
- Opening line for shared presenters: `POST`/`PATCH /api/agents` take `greeting` (up to 200 characters; an empty string removes it), shown on the talk page as the presenter's first message before the visitor types, kept in the transcript, and restored by "Start over"; a field on the Interactive page and the public view carries it; unit tests cover trimming, the visitor view, removal, and the limit, the presenter e2e greets a visitor, counts the turn, and starts over; `c99dd28`. An independent review found one high issue, fixed in `e9b91d5`: with a greeting set the transcript sent to the live model began with the presenter's own message, which the Messages API refuses, so every visitor turn would have failed under the Anthropic provider (the provider now puts a short visitor turn in front of a leading presenter message and joins neighbouring turns from one side, unit-tested)
- Embed options: `/share/:id?autoplay=1` starts the player muted and `?loop=1` repeats it (the last half second counts as the end for watch-through); the video page's Embed button offers both as checkboxes in the iframe snippet (with `allow="autoplay"` when chosen); the share e2e checks the player's attributes with and without the options; `e9b91d5`. An independent review found two low notes, carried in `df6ec13`: the chat window sent to the model starts at a visitor turn once the transcript outgrows twenty turns, so a mid-conversation reply is never presented as the presenter's opening, and the share e2e visits the autoplaying page after its beacon checks
- Suggested questions for shared presenters: `POST`/`PATCH /api/agents` take `starters` (up to three questions of 80 characters; an empty list removes them), offered as chips on the talk page until the visitor's first message, where a chip sends its question; a field on the Interactive page and the public view carries them; unit tests cover trimming, the visitor view, removal, and the limits, the presenter e2e clicks a chip and sees the reply; `df6ec13`. An independent review found five low notes, carried in `32f4dd3`: the Interactive form refuses a question over 80 characters with a clear message instead of a generic 400, chips are disabled while push-to-talk is listening and end it when clicked, chip keys are stable for repeated text, a chip click keeps whatever the visitor had typed, and the chip row is a labelled group
- Preview clips on library presenters: a presenter that comes with `previewVideoUrl` plays it, muted, while its tile is hovered or focused, with a play badge saying so; the first two demo presenters carry a sample clip; the avatar e2e hovers one tile and sees the clip, another and sees none; `32f4dd3`. An independent review found one medium and two low issues, fixed in `6d72058`: the hover clip painted over the favourite star and the selected check, so the star on a preview tile could not be clicked with a mouse (the clip now follows the portrait with no stacking order of its own, and the e2e clicks the star while the clip plays), chips carried calls that could never run, and a failed chip send overwrote a typed draft; D-ID presenters now bring their preview clip as well
- Pronunciation list: `PUT /api/brand` takes `pronunciations` (up to 50 `{ word, sayAs }` rules); every render hands the renderer a copy of the script with each word replaced, whole and case-insensitively, while the stored script and its captions keep the spelling; a textarea on the Brand page (one rule per line); the field schemas shared by videos, templates, batches, and the brand kit move to a leaf module so the video service can read the kit without an import cycle; unit tests cover the replacement rules, parsing and formatting, the kit, the limit, the renderer's copy versus the stored script, and another account's kit not applying; the brand e2e saves rules and reads them back; `808a37c`. A self-check found that a spelling containing `$&` or `$1` was expanded by the replacement call, fixed in `2e19236` with a replacer function and a pinned test; the Brand page also refuses a rule over the length limits with a clear message. An independent review then found that rules were applied one after another over the rewritten text, so a spelling fed the later rules ("Nair → Nye-er" plus "ER → E.R." produced "Nye-E.R."); fixed in `fa52adc`: every word matches in one pass, longest first, with the spelling returned verbatim, pinned by unit tests; the page also refuses more than 50 rules with a message instead of dropping the rest
- Voice pitch: a video may carry `pitch` (a whole number from -50 to 50) that HeyGen applies as the voice's pitch shift while other renderers keep the natural pitch; `0` is stored as absent; a slider in the editor's Settings tab next to the speaking speed, kept by drafts, copies, templates, batches, and retries, shown on the video page; unit tests cover the HeyGen voice settings, the schema bounds, the absent zero, and the template copy; the multi-scene e2e sets a pitch and reads it back; `5639550`. An independent review found no defect; its docs note (the templates and batches sections omitted the field) is carried in `a8416f4`
- Comments on videos: `GET`/`POST /api/videos/:id/comments` and `DELETE /api/videos/:id/comments/:commentId`; notes for the owner and workspace members, credited to the signed-in person (a script with an API key to the key's owner), up to 1000 characters and 200 per video, the author removes their own (403 for someone else's), never on the share page, part of the account export, and removed with the video when it is deleted for good; a Comments card on the video page; unit tests cover posting, ordering, another owner, deletion rules, the schema, the export, the purge, and the cap; the share e2e posts and removes a comment and checks the share page never carries it; `a8416f4`. An independent review found three low issues; the one that mattered is fixed in `e8f489e`: the routes credited a comment to the workspace whenever resolving the signed-in person failed for any reason, so a transient auth failure left a member's comment undeletable from the page (the fallback now covers only a key call with no session, and any other failure is an error), and a leftover import is gone
- Translated subtitles: `POST /api/videos/:id/subtitles { language }` translates the script through the script provider and keeps it as a track (up to five languages; the same language again refreshes it, matched without regard to case), `DELETE ...?language=` removes one, `GET /api/videos/:id/captions?lang=` serves it as SRT or WebVTT, and the share player offers every track in its subtitle menu with the right language code; a "Subtitle languages" card on the video page; unit tests cover the translation, the cap, the refresh, another owner, removal, an edit landing during the translation, and the cleared field, the share e2e adds Spanish from the page, downloads it, sees the track on the share page, and removes it; `03b9c3c`. An independent review found two medium and four low issues, fixed in `4ea5430`: an add decided the language key and the cap from the record read before the slow translation, so two overlapping requests could store one language twice or pass the cap together, and a re-read outside the counter lock could drop a concurrent track (adds and removals now build on the record as stored under the lock, deciding both there, with a race test driven by a gated translator), a translation into a language written without spaces was one cue for the whole video (cues split at East Asian sentence and clause marks and long runs are cut by character), a trashed video accepted new translations (409 now, and the card is hidden in the trash), a language named `__proto__` answered 201 and stored nothing (the name must be letters), the race test did not exercise the race it described, and the library listing carried every track's text on each card. A review of that fix found the race closed in one direction only and the cue cutting still blind to Thai, fixed in `e1b6bb5`: an owner edit, a trash, or a restore that read the record before its write (a password edit hashes in between) wrote its pre-translation copy over a track added meanwhile (those writers now take the tracks from the stored record, as they did the counters, with a test that runs the translation inside the password edit's hashing window), a Thai script, which marks nothing with punctuation and spaces its phrases, or a Japanese sentence with a Latin name in it was still one long cue (cues in an unspaced script are kept to 40 characters whether or not they contain a space: cut at clause marks, then at spaces, then by character; clause pieces are no longer joined back up to 90 characters; a closing bracket or quote stays with its sentence), a track stored under a key the tightened language rule no longer accepts could not be removed (the remove route takes the stored key as it is), and the race test picked its own result (it asserts on both). A review of `e1b6bb5` found minor items, carried in `c9957b2`: a mixed sentence was judged by its majority script, so a Latin run with a long Japanese tail still produced a cue of more than 40 dense characters (a cue's room is now measured per character, an unspaced one counting two and a quarter Latin ones), an owner edit that overlapped a trash could pull the video back out (the trash mark joins the fields an edit takes from the stored record, and trash and restore write under the lock), Korean is named in the docs as read at the dense rate, and the overlapping-edit test's title claimed a trash and a restore it did not overlap. A review of `c9957b2` found no defect; three low notes are carried in `cdad0f9`: restoring a live video or trashing a trashed one wrote the unchanged record (on Supabase, an upsert that bumped the row's timestamp; nothing is written now), a sentence of exactly 40 dense characters and a full stop left the stop as a cue of its own (it stays with its cue), and a comment called Korean unspaced
- Activity feed: `GET /api/activity?limit=` lists what the people in an account or workspace did, newest first (videos created, trashed, restored, and deleted for good, singly or in bulk, batches started, members joined, left, removed, or given a role, API keys created and revoked, the brand kit saved or removed, shared presenters created and deleted), each event crediting the signed-in person (or the API key's owner) with the subject's name; routes record events after the work succeeds and a failed record never fails the request; the last 200 events per owner are kept, the feed is part of the account export and goes with the account's data; the Workspace page shows the feed under the members list; unit tests cover recording, ordering, scoping, sentences, the label cut, the cap, the query limit, the export, and the swallowed failure, the workspace e2e reads the feed after creating a video, joining, and trashing. An independent review found one low-to-medium and several low issues, fixed in `d153f15`: the single-video `DELETE` read the video first and so answered 404 for a trashed video past its retention that it used to purge (the delete now reports its own title and whether anything changed), a member opening an invite link again was logged as a join and the e2e pinned it, the person lookup sat outside the best-effort boundary so a failure there could fail a finished request (one helper now resolves and records inside it), a render or batch the provider refused outright was logged as created, a role set to its current value was logged, the event clock was per route bundle rather than per instance (held on globalThis like the store), the test's broken store was restored without try/finally, and the docs now say to re-run `schema.sql` on Supabase. A review of `d153f15` found one low-to-medium item, fixed in `a031dde`: whether a trash changed anything was decided from the record read before the lock, so two overlapping trashes of one video both logged an event (the builder now sets the flag under the lock, with a test that runs two trashes at once)
- Viewer role: workspace members may be `owner`, `editor`, or `viewer`; a viewer sees everything the team made, watches, downloads, and comments, but every route that creates, changes, or deletes content answers 403 while a viewer's active owner is a workspace (comments and favourites excepted), viewers cannot mint API keys, and a member made a viewer loses the keys they minted under the workspace; the invite form and the members list offer the role, the sidebar marks a view-only workspace, and the invite email, the join page, and the activity feed name it; unit tests cover the viewer invite and join, the edit and account checks, the schema, the default, the key revocation on demotion, and the last-owner rule; the workspace e2e checks the invite option and creates a viewer invite. A self-review in place of the independent one (which stopped on a usage limit) found the edit check resolved the active workspace with its own cookie parse rather than the one reads use; both now share one lookup, so an edit always lands on the owner a read would show
- View-only state for viewers: the Create and Personalize pages show a notice and disable Generate while the active workspace gives the person the viewer role, and the sidebar hides its Create video button, so a viewer is told why instead of meeting a 403; the role is read once from the workspace list, editors never see the notice (it stays hidden while the list loads and if it fails to load, since the server still enforces the rule); the workspace e2e checks that an owner keeps every create control A self-review found Translate, Avatars, Voices, Brand kit, and Interactive still let a viewer fill in a form before the 403; each now shows the same notice and disables its create or save button (the voice cloner is hidden), and an e2e stands in a viewer's workspace list to check every page
- Audio scenes: a scene can speak recorded or uploaded audio instead of a voice, and the presenter lip-syncs to it (HeyGen `voice.type: "audio"` with an uploaded asset, D-ID `script.type: "audio"` in a single-scene video, timed by the clip in demo mode). The editor records from the microphone or takes any audio file the browser can decode, converts it to 16 kHz mono WAV, and saves it as a clip (`/api/clips`: PCM WAV whose length the server computes from its format, or MP3 with a stated length; 3 MB, 90 seconds, 20 per account), reusable from a "Saved clips" menu; the clip's metadata and bytes are separate records so lists never load audio, each renderer uploads a clip once and caches the asset id, a clip from another account reads as not found, the export lists clips without provider ids or bytes, the scene keeps the typed transcript for captions and search, its duration chip shows the clip's exact length, and the video page names the clip; unit tests cover the WAV reader, validation, limits, ownership, deletion, retries of a deleted clip, the export, the HeyGen and D-ID request bodies, and the demo timing; an e2e uploads a WAV in the editor, generates, checks the stored scene and the page, reuses the clip, and deletes it. An independent review found one medium and four low issues, fixed in the follow-up commit: clips up to five minutes could not be uploaded on Vercel, whose functions take at most 4.5 MB per request (clips are capped at 90 seconds and 3 MB, 3.8 MB as base64, and longer narration goes across scenes), a recording that ran to the limit came out a fraction over it and was refused (the converter trims a recording to the limit and refuses a file clearly longer), a recording kept running with no Stop button after switching to a scene with a clip and could land on the wrong scene (the panel is keyed per scene, so leaving it stops and drops the recording, and an upload that finishes later is saved but not applied), the editor summed per-scene rounded estimates so short typed scenes read too long (typed scenes are estimated together again), and deleting an account listed every clip's audio bytes (audio rows are removed by id beside their clip); WAV is now checked for PCM with its rate computed from its parts, and MP3 for an ID3 tag or frame sync. HeyGen's acceptance of WAV assets is still unverified against the live API
- Video review and approval: in a workspace, anyone who can edit asks for a review of a video (with an optional note), a workspace owner approves it or asks for changes (with a note saying what), and the person who asked or an owner can withdraw a pending request (`POST /api/videos/:id/review`); a new request after a decision starts over, a decided review cannot be decided again, viewers and API keys cannot decide, and personal-space and trashed videos are refused. The video page has a Review panel that offers only the actions the person's role allows, library cards carry an "In review", "Approved", or "Changes requested" badge, the library filters by review state (`?review=`), and the activity feed names each request and decision; unit tests cover the whole flow, the permissions, the refusals, an owner edit in flight while a review lands (the review is one of the fields an edit takes from the stored record), the filter, and the feed sentences, and an e2e takes a video through request, changes, a second request, and approval, then checks the badge, the filter, the feed, and the refused re-decision. An independent review found nothing blocking and five low issues, fixed in the follow-up commit: the panel offered Withdraw to a member made a viewer, whom the server refuses (it needs edit rights now), a queued, rendering, or failed video could go for review and be approved (only finished renders can), an approval outlived changes to what the share page shows (changing the title, description, cover, or call-to-action clears it; filing, sharing settings, and an unchanged title keep it), every API key in a workspace shared one requester id and so could withdraw another key's request (keys can ask but not withdraw), and an older race let a render finishing (status refresh or provider webhook) write back a record read before a review, edit, or beacon landed (both now read and write under the video's lock)
- Several languages at once: the Translate page picks up to five target languages (checkboxes, Spanish to start with) and starts one dubbing job per language, each titled with its language; `POST /api/translate` takes `targetLanguages` (repeats ignored without regard to case) beside the single `targetLanguage`, whose response shape is unchanged, and a job the provider refuses is kept as failed while the rest start; unit tests cover the schema, the cap, the repeats, the titles, and ownership, and an e2e dubs one video into three languages from the page and checks the list, the API's list and single forms, and the empty list
- Mentions in comments: in a workspace, `@email` naming a member records them on the comment (up to 10, matched without regard to case, the author and non-members ignored) and each is emailed the comment after the response, with a link that opens the video and switches the reader into its workspace (`/videos/:id?ws=`); the comment box offers the active workspace's teammates as "Mention" chips and each comment shows who was notified; unit tests cover the matching, the email (subject, escaped title, reply-to, link), a failed send that does not stop the rest, and personal spaces, and an e2e follows a mention link from the personal space into the workspace and picks a teammate chip An independent review found nothing blocking and one medium and five low issues; fixed in the follow-up commit: an author could post and delete a comment in a loop to mail teammates without limit (mention emails are now capped per author and per instance, and over the cap the comment posts without mail), a crafted `?ws=` link could switch a reader out of their workspace (only a workspace they belong to and are not already in is switched to, and the rest of the URL is kept), `alice@bob@example.com` mentioned bob (a mention must start the text or follow a space or opening punctuation, and addresses with an apostrophe or non-Latin letters now match), the comment said "Notified" even when no mail went out (it now says "Mentioned"), and a chip clicked after typing `@` left a stray `@` and could pass the 1000-character limit. Members' stored addresses are still the ones they joined with; a changed sign-in address is not picked up until they rejoin
- Caption editing: the video page has a Captions card where an editor fixes each line's wording and when it shows, adds or removes lines, and saves (`PUT /api/videos/:id/captions`), or goes back to lines timed from the script (`DELETE`); the corrections serve the share page's subtitle track and the SRT and WebVTT downloads, `?format=json` returns the lines currently served, lines must be in order without overlaps (the error names the line), saving clears an approval, and edits are one of the fields an owner edit in flight takes from the stored record; unit tests cover the schema, serving and reset, line breaks inside a line, the approval, the trash, ownership, and an edit landing mid-save (checked to fail without the protection), the open-mode e2e corrects a line, adds one, checks the SRT, VTT, and JSON forms and the overlap error, and resets, and the password-mode e2e checks an anonymous save and reset are refused An independent review found nothing blocking (the public captions path refuses anonymous, viewer, and bad-key edits in every mode) and one medium and seven low issues, fixed in the follow-up commit: a line holding `-->` (or `<`, `&`) could add cues of its own to the file the public share player loads, skipping the order and count checks (WebVTT text is now escaped and SRT arrows rewritten), the library list carried every video's corrected captions (now left off cards like subtitle tracks), a decimal comma was refused although comma-locale phone keypads offer nothing else, adding a line after one shorter than 0.2 s made an empty span, the editor's controls stayed live during a save or reset so "Captions saved" could describe unsaved changes and a load could land after a reset, an empty line's error did not name the line, rows were keyed by position, and the card claimed the share page used the lines when the video's captions are off and did not say translated tracks keep following the script
- Pauses in scripts: a Pause button in the editor puts `[pause 1s]` at the cursor (any length up to ten seconds, `[pause]` meaning one); the stored script keeps the marker, HeyGen and ElevenLabs voices get a `<break>` tag, D-ID's Microsoft voices get SSML with the text escaped, the demo render lasts the extra time, captions leave a gap of the pause between lines (pauses squeezed to half the running time at most, so every line shows), word counts skip markers while running-time estimates add them, and markers stay out of captions, the share page's transcript and link preview, translated subtitles, library search, browser speech previews, and pronunciation rules (a rule for "pause" cannot rewrite one); unit tests cover parsing, the cap, every renderer's request, caption gaps, estimates, the translator's input, search, and pronunciations, and an e2e inserts a pause from the editor, checks the cursor, the count, the caption gap, and the share page An independent review found nothing that crashes or loses data and five medium and two low issues, fixed in the follow-up commit: D-ID's Microsoft voices were sent a `<speak>` document where D-ID wants a fragment it wraps itself, pauses past an engine's longest break (three seconds for ElevenLabs, five for Microsoft) would have been cut short while captions and estimates counted them in full (long pauses are now several breaks, and D-ID's ElevenLabs input carries `ssml: true`), a trailing pause stretched every caption over the silence, translated subtitles lost their gaps because the translator got the script without markers, the AI writer's Improve actions could translate or drop markers (markers now travel as `[[P1]]` tokens the model is asked to keep and are restored after), the editor's caption preview showed markers and Listen could cut one in half and read it out, and a scene of nothing but pauses passed validation. Whether HeyGen accepts break tags next to a stray `<` or `&` in the same text is not known without a live call
- Dub options: the Translate page can turn lip-sync off (the voice is dubbed and the mouth left as filmed) and name how many people speak (1 to 10, or detect automatically); `POST /api/translate` takes `audioOnly` and `speakers` in either form, HeyGen gets them as `translate_audio_only` and `speaker_num` (left to its defaults otherwise), each job stores them and the list shows "voice only" and the speaker count; unit tests cover the schema bounds, the batch passing both to every job, the stored defaults, and HeyGen's request body, and an e2e sets both from the page and checks the list, the API, and an out-of-range count An independent review found nothing blocking; its low findings are fixed in the follow-up commit: the speaker count carried over to the next submission (it now resets, while the lip-sync choice stays), the new controls stayed live for viewers, and `null` was refused where an API client means "not set"; the docs now say the ten-speaker cap is this app's own
- Email gate on share pages: "Ask for an email before playing" on the video page makes the share page show a short form (email, optional name) in place of the player until the viewer fills it in (after the password, on a protected link); `POST /api/videos/:id/lead` records the viewer once per address per video (lower-cased, return visits counted), sets a 30-day cookie that lets them through, fires `lead.captured` with `source: "share"` on the first visit only, and is rate limited per viewer and per video; the video page's Viewer emails card lists them with a CSV download (`GET /api/videos/:id/leads`, formula cells defused); views are only counted once a viewer is past the gate; leads are their own store kind (`videolead`, re-run `supabase/schema.sql`), part of the export and the account deletion, kept when the gate is turned off, and deleted with the video; unit tests cover the toggle, one row per address, the single webhook, the refusals, owner scoping, the CSV, library cards, the export, the trash, and the purge, the open-mode e2e passes the gate as a fresh visitor and checks the remembered pass, the owner's card, the CSV, and the refusals, and the password-mode e2e checks that an anonymous viewer cannot read the list An independent review found no high-severity hole and three medium and six low issues; fixed in the follow-up commit: an embedded gated video never played in browsers that block third-party cookies (the page waited for a reload that came back without the cookie, with the button stuck), so the gate now shows the player as soon as the email is accepted and both share cookies are `Partitioned` in production (the password form's button no longer sticks either); one sender could use up the per-video cap and lock every viewer out, so past it viewers are let through unrecorded; link previews still carried the video and the script, now left out for a gated link; workspace viewers could read the emails (now editors and owners only); `lead.captured` gained a `source` field (`presenter` or `share`) and the webhook card says so; and the card no longer polls while the gate is off. One low finding remains: two first submissions of one address at the same instant can both fire the webhook (the workspace finding is fixed in the next entry)
- Deleting a workspace deletes what it owns: the review of the email gate pointed out that a deleted workspace's videos, transcripts, and now viewer emails stayed in the store, unreachable but kept; `DELETE /api/workspaces/:id` now runs the same purge as the account deletion (provider voice clones and clip audio included) before it removes the keys, webhook, members, and invites, so a failure part-way leaves the workspace in place to try again; the confirmation says so; a unit test checks that a refused attempt removes nothing, that the workspace's videos and leads go, and that the owner's personal space stays An independent review found the purge safe from running on the wrong owner and one high, one medium, and four low issues, fixed in the follow-up commit: a Stripe Pro plan kept billing after its plan row was deleted, leaving no way to reach the billing portal (the workspace and account deletions now refuse with a 400 until the plan is cancelled), Supabase returns at most 1,000 rows per listing so a busy presenter's conversations or a video's leads could be left behind (each kind is listed again until it comes back empty, with a stop if a row will not go), API keys stayed valid while the purge ran (they are revoked first), a failed provider voice delete was swallowed with its row removed so a retry could not find the clone (it now stops the run, the row kept), the confirmation left out presenters, clips, templates, and the plan and took one click (it lists them, mentions the plan, and asks for the workspace name), and the purge now checks it is given the workspace's own id; a unit test covers the refused paid plan and a store that lists two rows at a time A review of that fix found one high and three medium issues, fixed in the next commit: a delete refused for a renewing plan had already revoked every workspace key, breaking the team's integrations (the plan is checked before anything is touched, pinned by the test), a voice provider that refused for good (a revoked key, a clone under another account) failed every retry after most data was already gone (keys and voices now go first, a passing failure stops the run before anything else is lost, and a permanent 400/401/403/422 is counted as `voiceLeftAtProvider` and the run moves on), cancelling in the portal did not unblock the delete on Stripe's current API, which moved the period end onto the subscription items (the end date is read from `cancel_at`, the subscription, or its first item), and a subscription that ended at Stripe without a delivered webhook blocked deletion for good (the guard asks Stripe before refusing; a lookup that fails still refuses). Two low items are fixed with them: a mistyped workspace name now says nothing was deleted, and a late Stripe event no longer recreates a plan row for a deleted workspace A third review found that treating a 400, 401, or 403 from the voice provider as permanent deleted the voice row while the clone (a person's voice) stayed at ElevenLabs, though a rotated key is fixable, and the count never reached the page; that rule is gone: any failure other than the provider no longer having the voice stops the run with the row kept, and the error names the voice. It also found the Stripe check could be wrong both ways, fixed together: it now lists all of the customer's subscriptions, so an unpaid or incomplete subscription the app shows as Free still blocks, a plan with no stored subscription id is checked, and a not-found answer for a key of the other mode ("a similar object exists in live mode") refuses instead of reading as gone; a checkout that completes after its workspace was deleted is recorded again, so the ids Stripe bills are kept (only later subscription events for a deleted workspace are ignored); the real Stripe lookup is unit-tested against a stubbed fetch A fourth review found nothing blocking and two medium issues, fixed in the next commit: a former customer on Free whose subscription went unpaid was told to use "Manage subscription", which the plan card showed only on Pro (it now shows for every Stripe customer, and the message no longer says Pro), and a test-mode customer looked up with a live key after going live refused every delete (a test-mode object cannot bill, so it reads as nothing to cancel; a live object behind a test key still refuses). The low items are fixed with them: a workspace delete asked Stripe twice, the second time after revoking the keys (once now), the voice error now says the keys were already removed, answers 409, and leaves out the provider's response body, a voice ElevenLabs answers with 400 `voice_not_found` counts as deleted, a full page of subscriptions with more behind it refuses instead of passing unchecked, and tests now cover incomplete, past-due, and scheduled cancellations, keys going first, and the single lookup. Known gap: a checkout completed for a workspace already deleted is recorded but its subscription is not cancelled at Stripe A fifth review found nothing blocking; its low items are fixed in the next commit: the Free plan's "Manage subscription" shows only while Stripe is the billing in use (under demo billing its portal would have rewritten a Stripe customer's plan and hidden it from the deletion check), the voice error counts only clones removed at the provider, a missing ElevenLabs voice is recognised from the parsed `detail.status` of a 400 rather than any mention in the body, the single-lookup test checks the keys still work while Stripe is asked, and the confirmations no longer say "Pro" A review of that commit found no defect; its one related note is fixed in the next commit: under demo billing the Pro plan's "Manage subscription" could still rewrite a Stripe customer's plan, so demo billing now refuses to change any plan Stripe has billed. A review of that found the guard narrower than the deletion check: a Stripe Pro plan recorded from a subscription event, with no customer id yet, could still be rewritten; fixed in the next commit (demo billing leaves every Stripe plan alone, and subscription events record the customer id they carry)
- No billing for deleted workspaces: the gap left open above (a checkout completed after its workspace was deleted kept charging, with no portal to cancel it from) is closed. The completed checkout cancels that subscription at Stripe (`DELETE /v1/subscriptions/:id`, an already-gone one counting as done) and records nothing; if Stripe cannot be reached the ids are kept and the webhook answers 500 so Stripe retries; a renewing subscription event for a deleted workspace (its checkout event missed) cancels it the same way, while final ones are ignored; the webhook route now turns any failure into a 500 for a retry instead of an unhandled error; a unit test drives all of it against a stubbed Stripe API An independent review found one medium and three low issues, fixed in the next commit: a retry that cancelled after a failed first attempt left the ids recorded by that attempt as a lasting "Pro, active" row for the deleted workspace, and the test removed the row itself before checking (the row now goes with the cancellation, and the test no longer cleans up for it), a not-found answer for a live subscription behind a test key counted as cancelled (it now fails for a retry, as the renewal check does), an in-memory store could take a workspace another instance holds for deleted and cancel a paying subscription (cancelling now needs a store every instance shares, Supabase), and the test now also covers the renewing-event retry, an already-gone subscription, the live-mode answer, and that the DELETE carries no body. A review of that fix found a medium test gap and one low issue, fixed in the next commit: nothing pinned the Supabase store's shared-by-every-instance flag, so dropping it would have silently turned the cancellation off (a store test checks it now), and a cancellation removed the deleted workspace's plan row even when it held another checkout's still-owed ids (only this subscription's row goes, with a two-checkout test)
- Caption looks: beside position and size, a caption style takes a `look` (classic white on a dark box, bold on a solid box, yellow on a dark box, or minimal white with a shadow and no box; classic is stored as absent), chosen from a Caption look row in the editor's Settings tab, previewed on the stage, applied to the share player's subtitle track and translated tracks as a cue style, kept by drafts, copies, templates, batches, and retries, and shown on the video page; a look on its own counts as a style, so HeyGen leaves the captions to the track; unit tests cover the schema, the classic default, and the stored video, and the caption e2e checks the share player's class, the video page, the editor's selection, and the stage preview An independent review found one medium and three low issues, fixed in the next commit: the bold look did not show bold on the editor's stage (a fixed medium weight won over it in the stylesheet; each look now sets its own weight), the e2e only checked the class (it now checks the rendered weights), the minimal look failed AA contrast at the small size on a white stage and the axe scan never reached that state (it gets a faint box on a light stage, and the caption e2e runs axe on the stage caption and the look buttons on white), and the help text and API docs said the look went into the subtitle file (only the position does) A review of that fix found the new axe step could not fail on contrast: the presenter layered under the caption and the look buttons scrolled out of view left every contrast check undecided, which the step did not look at; it now scrolls the buttons into view, hides the presenter, and asserts that contrast was decided and passed for the stage caption and the chosen look button. The help text and API docs are corrected again: position, size, and look all apply in the share player, the WebVTT file carries the position only, and the SRT none of the style
- Post the link: a finished video's page has a Post the link card with LinkedIn, X, Facebook, WhatsApp, and email-app links, each opening that network's own compose screen (in a new tab; the email link hands off to the mail app) with the share page (and, where the network takes one, the title) filled in, so nothing is posted until the person confirms there; the links are built in one place (`src/lib/social.ts`) with every part encoded, are offered only while the share link is live, and the card says when viewers will need a password; unit tests pin each network's link and the encoding, and the share-by-email e2e checks the links, their new-tab attributes, and that they go when the link is turned off An independent review found one medium and five low issues, fixed in the next commit: the copied, embedded, and posted links were built on the host in the owner's address bar while the share email used `APP_URL`, so an owner on a preview deployment or a `www.` alias could post a link behind deployment protection (`GET /api/videos/:id` now returns `shareOrigin`, built by one `publicOrigin` helper that the share email and the analytics CSV use too, which also drops a trailing slash from `APP_URL`, and the page builds every share link on it); the post links rendered before the origin was known would have been relative had the video ever arrived first (they now wait for it); the email-app link opened an empty tab in some browsers (it no longer takes a new tab); nothing told screen-reader users the links open a new tab (each says so, and the card text does too); a title cut mid-emoji made the link builder throw (half a surrogate pair is dropped first); and the tests did not cover `#`, `+`, non-ASCII titles, or three of the five links in the browser (the unit tests now do, with a test of `publicOrigin`, and the e2e checks every link's address and tab behaviour by exact name)
- Talk links on the public origin: the interactive page's copied `/talk/:id` links and website embed snippets were built on the host in the owner's address bar, the gap the review of `9080c90` left open; `GET /api/agents` now returns `shareOrigin` from the same `publicOrigin` helper, and the page builds both on it
- Timestamped comments: a comment on a finished video can be pinned to the moment the player is at (a "Pin to 0:12 in the video" box under the comment field); the note shows its time as a button that seeks the player there, `atSec` is stored to tenths and checked against the video's length, and a mention email names the moment and links to `/videos/:id?ws=<workspace>&t=<seconds>`, which opens the player at it; unit tests cover the stored time, the length check, the email text and link, and the clock format, and the comments e2e pins a note, seeks with it, and opens a `?t=` link An independent review found one medium and five low issues, fixed in the next commit: the length check used the stored length, which for demo renders is estimated from the script while the player shows a 12-second sample, so a pin past the estimate was refused (demo renders are no longer checked, and pinning needs a finished render); a note typed while the video played was pinned to wherever playback had reached at submit (the moment is now taken when the box is ticked and stays put); the player time lived on the whole video page, re-rendering it every second of playback (the comments card follows the player itself); the time button said "Play from" but only seeks ("Go to 0:08 in the video"); the e2e pinned inside the estimate and never played on after ticking (it now pins past the estimate, plays on, and checks focus after seeking); and the docs gave the link without `ws` and said the length check applied only to finished videos
- Brand glossary: the brand kit takes up to 50 terms to keep untranslated (a Never translate field, one per line); translating a script and adding a subtitle language swap each whole, case-insensitive occurrence for a `[[T1]]`-style token before the model sees the text and put back the source's spelling after, so the spelling of a product name does not depend on the model (pause markers are stepped over, and the Claude prompt is told to keep the tokens); dubbing jobs are not covered; unit tests use a translator that rewrites every word to show the terms and pauses come back and that other actions and other owners are untouched, and the brand-kit e2e saves and reloads the list (its translate call checks the route only, since the demo writer copies its input) An independent review found three medium and five low issues, fixed in the next commit: a term next to Chinese, Japanese, or Thai text was never matched, since those letters counted as part of the word (scripts written without spaces now count as a boundary); a combining mark, a decomposed accent, or an underscore did not, so "कमी" lost its vowel sign and "@acme_cloud" was split (they continue the word now, and text and terms are compared in composed form); the e2e translate check could not fail (it is labelled as the route check it is); a token the model wrote as `[[t1]]`, `[[ T 1 ]]`, or `[T1]` leaked into the result (any case, spacing, or single brackets are taken back); text in the script that already looked like a token was overwritten on restore (it is kept as written); the model did not know what a token stood for, so the words around it could not agree in inflected languages (the prompt lists each token's term); a one-letter term held back every article (terms are at least two characters); and the tests did not cover any of this
