- Logo watermark: the brand kit takes a corner (Logo on the player: off, or one of four corners) and the share page lays the logo over the player there, the embed included; the overlay is decorative (`aria-hidden`, clicks pass through to the controls, and the bottom corners sit above them) and the video file is not changed; the header logo and the watermark draw on one CSS variable so an uploaded logo is sent once; the brand-kit e2e uploads a logo, picks a corner, and checks the overlay An independent review found two medium and four low issues, fixed in the next commit: the CSS-background header logo was always 140px wide, so a square logo left a gap before the brand name, and it vanished from printouts and image-blocking modes (the header is an `<img>` again, and an uploaded logo is served by `GET /api/videos/:id/logo`, a cacheable, sandboxed route on the share page's rules, so the page carries a short URL instead of the image itself); the watermark sat a fixed 56px up, over two-line or burned-in captions and a third of the way up a small embed (it is sized and placed in shares of the frame, at least clear of the controls, and rises above the caption band on a captioned video); it is not shown in fullscreen or picture-in-picture, which the docs now say; a corner stayed saved after the logo was cleared and came back with the next logo (clearing the logo clears it, and the setting is disabled without one); and the e2e only checked `pointer-events` and counted copies in Next's hydration payload (it now checks the element under the mark is the video, the route's bytes and headers, the 404 once the link is offline, and that the page no longer carries the file)
- Talk-page logo by link: the public presenter view (`GET /api/talk/:id`) sent an uploaded brand logo inline as a data URL of up to a few megabytes on every page load; it now links `GET /api/talk/:id/logo`, which shares its serving code (types allowed, cached briefly when current, sandboxed for SVG) with the share page's logo route and follows the talk page's rules; unit tests cover the link, the response headers, and the refused types, and the brand-kit e2e fetches the talk logo and sees it go with the presenter An independent review of `807458a` and this commit found two medium and four low issues, fixed in the next commit: the watermark took two object-position classes that do not combine, so in every corner one of the two was dropped and the logo sat centred along one edge of its box (which edge depended on the logo's shape) (one class per corner now, and the e2e checks the computed position); an uploaded AVIF, BMP, or ICO logo, which showed when inlined, became a broken image because the route served only five types (the route serves every type an `<img>` shows, and the kit, the upload picker, and the file check accept only those); a versioned logo was cached for a year whatever the version, so a replaced logo or an offline link lived on in caches (only the current version is cached, for an hour at most and never past the link's expiry; anything else is `no-store`); emptying the logo URL by hand kept the corner (it clears it now, as the Clear button does); the talk link's paused state and a new save's new address were untested (both are now); and the API docs did not say the talk logo is a relative path or that caches may keep it briefly An independent review of `0dd9295` found one medium and three low issues, fixed two commits later: a kit saved earlier with a logo in a type no longer accepted (TIFF, HEIC) could not be saved at all, every save answering 400 with nothing on the page naming the logo (the page now leaves such a logo out, says so, and saving removes it; a review of that fix found the page's check read only the type, so a stored logo with no comma or a payload that does not decode still blocked every save, and both now run one shared check, `logoDataUrlValid`); the kit's check read the type from the data URL while the route decoded it, so `data:image/PNG;…` or a data URL without a comma passed the kit and showed broken (both now use the route's own decode, in any case); only one watermark corner was tested (a unit test covers all four placements, moved to `src/lib/watermark.ts`); and a browser that reports no type for an AVIF or ICO file had it refused (the type is read from the name then)
- Cover from a frame: the video page's cover card has a Use current frame button that makes the frame the player is paused on the cover, downscaled like an upload and saved through the same `cover` field; the frame is read from a separate video element loaded with CORS, so a host that does not allow reading frames fails with a message and the page's own player is never affected; the cover e2e pauses on a frame, uses it, and checks the saved JPEG has the video's width An independent review found two medium and several low issues, fixed in the next commit: the brand page's check of a stored logo (above); the capture's one message blamed the host for every failure, including an expired link or a format the browser cannot play (it now names both causes; capture still needs the video's host to allow cross-origin reads, which provider CDNs may not, and serving frames through the app is left as a follow-up); the hidden element preloaded the whole file (metadata only now, then the seek); a seek to the position the element was already at, or a duration the browser could not tell, could leave it waiting the full 15 seconds (it always seeks, a frame on when it is already there, and an unknown length is not used); the button showed no progress (it does now, and the others wait for it); a tall portrait frame was only capped in width (it fits a 1280px box); and the e2e checked only the cover's width (it now captures the start and a later moment, sees two different covers, and the same moment again gives the same one). A second review of those fixes found no high-severity issues and four small ones, fixed in the commit after: Safari can report a seek before the frame there is decoded, which could save a blank cover (the capture now waits until the frame is ready); the shared logo check refused base64 in the URL-safe alphabet and percent-escaped base64, which the route used to serve (both are accepted, and served as the same bytes); typing a logo URL and deleting it again hid the notice that saving removes the stored logo (it stays until a new logo replaces it); and uploaded covers were still capped only in width (they fit the same 1280px box as frames, so "downscaled like an upload" holds). A later full run caught a blank cover once (a frame captured at 3s came back one flat colour: Chromium can report a seek before the frame there can be drawn, as Safari does); the capture now redraws a flat frame up to five times, 120 ms apart, and keeps a scene that really is one colour after the last try, and the cover e2e passed ten runs in a row with it
- Voice emotion: videos, templates, and batches take an `emotion` (Excited, Friendly, Serious, Soothing, or Broadcaster, HeyGen's own spelling) chosen in the editor's Settings next to pitch; HeyGen receives it as `voice.emotion` on every scene whose voice supports one (the voice list now carries HeyGen's `emotion_support` as `emotionSupport`, and a voice that reports `false` is sent none), other renderers keep the voice's natural delivery; a retry, a template, and Edit a copy keep it, a draft drops a value no longer offered, and the video page lists it. An independent review found one medium and two low issues, fixed in the next commit: discarding a restored draft kept its emotion, so a fresh video could render Excited without Settings ever being opened (discarding now resets it, and the caption style, which had the same gap); the editor's hint looked only at the video's voice, not at a scene's own voice that takes no emotion (it now names every voice in use that HeyGen says speaks naturally); and the video page listed an emotion for renders that never apply one (it says so for renderers other than HeyGen). A second review of those fixes found one medium and three low issues, fixed in the commit after: Discard draft reset the editor to built-in defaults rather than the brand kit's, and kept the draft's presenter and voice (it now clears both and applies the kit again, as a fresh editor does); a video whose every scene plays recorded audio still listed its emotion as applied (the video page says it was not); the hint said the emotion applies to supporting scenes even when no voice in use supports one (it then says the emotion does not apply to this video); and the caption-style reset had no test (the discard e2e now sets a top position, checks the draft saved it, and finds the default after discarding)
- Share-page transcript: once a video has rendered, the script under the share player becomes a transcript of its caption lines (an editor's corrected captions when there are any, pause markers left out), each with its start time; a line seeks the player there and plays (or leaves it paused where autoplay is refused), and the line being spoken is marked (`aria-current`) and kept in view inside the list, which scrolls on its own so the page never jumps; a video still rendering, or one that failed, keeps the plain script; the e2e checks the lines match the caption JSON, that a line moves the player to its time, and that the first line is marked at the start. An independent review of it and of the second emotion fix found one medium and three low issues, fixed in the next commit: the e2e's check that the clicked line is marked could never run with the demo clip's timing (it now asserts the line spoken where the player stopped, and that exactly one is marked); a line stayed marked through a pause or after the last line ended (lines now end at their end time); the transcript found the player through an id written in two places (one shared constant now); and Discard draft could still apply the draft's presenter when the presenter lists loaded after the click (the pending choice is dropped). A viewer who switches to a translated subtitle track still reads the script-language transcript, left as a follow-up. A review of those fixes found one low issue, fixed in the commit after: a seek to a line's start can read back a fraction of a millisecond early, so a line clicked while the player stayed paused could leave the previous line marked (times are compared in whole milliseconds now)
- Transcript follows the subtitle track: the share-page transcript shows a translated track's lines (timed as that track is in the player, and marked with the language's `lang` code) while the viewer has it switched on in the player's subtitle menu, and the script's lines otherwise, with the heading naming the language; switching mid-video marks the new language's current line at once; `transcriptsFor(video)` builds the sets on the server from the same cues the caption routes serve, and the subtitles e2e turns the Spanish track on and off and checks the transcript's lines, heading, and `lang`; closes the follow-up noted on the transcript entry. An independent review of it found one medium and two low issues, fixed in the next commit: tracks were matched to transcripts by label, so a subtitle language an owner named "Captions" would be taken for the script's track (each translated track now carries an id made from its language, and the transcript matches on that); a language with no known code left the lines in the page's language for screen readers (they are marked `und`, as the player's track is); and switching language kept the list's scroll even when the new lines' heights moved the marked line out of view (it scrolls again). A review of those fixes found one medium issue, fixed in the commit after: the track id was a slug of the language, so names in other scripts ("日本語", "Русский") or differing only in punctuation shared one id, and the transcript could show another language's lines (the id now escapes the exact name, and a unit test covers non-Latin and punctuation-only differences). Its review found three low issues, fixed in the commit after: the order check in the new test compared the stored keys with themselves (it now names the expected languages); the test could reach a real translator if one was configured (it passes a stand-in); and a language stored before names were checked could hold half an emoji, which made encodeURIComponent throw and the share page fail to render (the track id and its captions URL now replace a lone surrogate first, through one `wellFormed` helper that the social links share)
- Caption typeface: a caption style takes a `font` (sans, the player's own and the default, serif, rounded, or mono), chosen in the editor next to the look, previewed on the stage in the same font stack, kept by drafts, copies, templates, and retries with the rest of the style, listed on the video page, and applied by the share and embed player (`cue-font-*` classes on `::cue`, system font stacks only so the player never waits on a download); like a look, a chosen typeface makes the share page's subtitle track carry the captions instead of HeyGen's burned-in ones, and the default is stored as absent. An independent review of it, the blank-cover fix, and the caption typeface found one medium and two low issues, fixed in the commit after: the half-emoji language no longer broke the share page, but its track still could not be read, downloaded, or removed, and the video page's download link still threw (language names now match with any lone surrogate read as U+FFFD, and the video page sends the same URL-safe form, so every route reaches the stored track); the flat-frame check stepped through pixels in a line that, for frames whose height is a multiple of 400, sampled a single column (it now samples a 20 × 20 grid across the frame); and a restored draft kept a caption look or typeface this build does not offer, which the server then refused (unknown ones are dropped on restore). Its review found two low issues, fixed in the commit after: two legacy names that differ only in their lone surrogate read alike once repaired, so the service now tries an exact name before the repaired match (a later review noted that pages and routes can only send the repaired form, since a URL cannot carry a lone surrogate, so over HTTP such a pair still resolves to its first track; names have been checked as letters since before this, so only a video holding two such pre-check names is affected, and it was left at that rather than adding a second, lossless key parameter to both routes); and a restored draft's caption position and size are checked too, falling back to the default when a build no longer offers them
- Batch voice settings and template start: the batch page takes speaking speed, pitch, and emotion like the editor (the batch API already accepted them), and a "Start from a saved template" select (shown when templates exist) fills the script (scene scripts joined by blank lines), presenter and voice, aspect ratio, quality, captions and caption style, voice settings, and the first scene's background; the personalized-batch e2e test checks each row carries the emotion and pitch and that a template fills the form; `5b1869c`. An independent review found four things in it, fixed in the commit after: a template picked before the avatar and voice lists loaded lost its presenter and voice to the first in each list (they are now held until both lists are in, and a voice that is gone gives way to the presenter's default, as in the editor); a template whose scenes add up to more than the 5000 characters a batch script takes left Generate disabled with no reason (the page now says so); the first scene's own presenter and voice were ignored; and the e2e test left its template behind and never checked that the caption style and background, which the page has no controls for, reach the rows (it now generates from the template, asserts both, and deletes it)
- Presenter framing: a scene's layout takes a `framing` (full, the default and stored as absent, close-up, or circle), chosen in the editor's Presenter layout panel next to size and position, previewed on the stage (a zoom toward the head and shoulders, or a disc over the background), kept by drafts, copies, templates, and retries with the rest of the layout, listed on the video page, and sent to HeyGen as the avatar's `avatar_style` (a photo avatar has a square or a circle only, so a close-up keeps its square); unit tests cover the HeyGen styles, the photo-avatar mapping, the stored default, and the schema, and the multi-scene e2e test frames a scene in a circle; `c761db9`. An independent review of it and of `6b27ff0` found nothing blocking and six low issues, fixed in the commit after: a photo avatar's close-up was previewed and listed as a close-up although HeyGen renders its full frame (the editor now previews and labels the framing the presenter gets, with Close-up disabled for a photo avatar, and the video stores it that way); a restored draft's layout was not checked (an unknown framing is dropped and the size and offset kept in bounds, like the caption style); the batch page's too-long message stayed after the script was shortened (it now follows the length); a template whose first scene names a deleted presenter fell back to the first in the list instead of the template's own; the batch hint now says scene layouts are not used; and the video page notes when the renderer does not apply the layout
- Script import: the editor's Import script button reads a plain-text or Markdown file (up to 1 MB) into scenes. A heading or a `---` line starts a new scene and the paragraphs between are its script; a file with neither gets one scene per paragraph, packed together when there are more than a video's 20 scenes. Markdown list markers, emphasis, code ticks, blockquote markers, tags, and link targets are left out of what is spoken, the first top-level heading becomes the title while the video is still untitled, and written scenes are replaced only once confirmed; unit tests cover the splitting, packing, and Markdown cleanup, and an e2e test imports a Markdown file (refusing first, then confirming) and an empty one; `943e71c`. An independent review found that the import kept to 5000 characters per scene while a video takes 5000 across all scenes, so any document past about 800 words imported but could not be generated; fixed in the commit after, along with the rest of its findings: the import keeps what a video holds (cut at a sentence end, never inside a pause marker) and says when it left the rest out, and the editor shows the script's length and holds Generate once the scenes together pass 5000 characters; a paragraph with only a pause joins its neighbour instead of becoming a scene with no words; only real tags are removed, so "from <1% to >5%" and `<help@example.com>` read as written; a wrapped line starting with a number is not taken for a list item, code spans keep their text, and a heading may end in `#`; a title heading no longer turns a document into one scene; underlined headings and YAML front matter are understood; Markdown cleanup applies to `.md` files only, plain text being read as written; importing over scenes that hold a clip, a presenter, or a layout asks first like over typed ones; and the presenter layout summary and Reset follow the framing as it renders. The tests now pass every import through the video schema; `6c1c48d`. A second review of that commit found that a cut could still leave half a pause marker or a word fragment as a scene and that a near-full document with a huge last paragraph froze the editor for half a minute (the cut is now a single scan of the room left, at a sentence or paragraph end, else between words outside a marker, never a piece under 40 characters), and that a bullet list right under a paragraph line, `<b and c>` in prose, a list item or a plain line above `---` (taken for an underlined heading), prose between `---` lines in a `.txt` file (taken for front matter), and a pause-only section were lost or mangled; also fixed: sentence ends before a quote or bracket count, HTML comments, fence lines, reference-link definitions, and table rules are not spoken, table cells are read in turn, `* * *` is a break, and backslash escapes read as their character. A seeded test of 300 random documents checks every import against the video schema; `f09af4b`. A third review found that a speaker-labelled script (`[NARRATOR]: Welcome…`) was dropped whole as link definitions, that code inside a fence was still read as Markdown (a shell comment became the title, a `---` a break), and that unclosed `**`, `__`, or links on a long line took minutes; fixed in the commit after: a link definition needs a URL-like destination and nothing but a quoted title after it, fenced code is read as written and never as headings or breaks, emphasis and link patterns stop at the line or the closing mark, the document is read only well past what a video keeps (40,000 characters, lines to 10,000), and an early short sentence no longer stands in for a cut between words that keeps most of the room. Also: link destinations may hold one level of parentheses, a quoted list item is a list item, emphasis needs a matching close, `&amp;`-style entities, `~~strikethrough~~`, footnote marks, and task boxes read as text, any escaped punctuation reads as itself, a table row ends as a sentence, and a line of only `=` or `#` is a break. The reviewer's 8,000-document fuzz runs clean; `17e2442`. A fourth review, of scripts as people write them, found one-word replies in a speaker-labelled script (`[GUEST]: Thanks.`) still taken for link definitions, a script handed over in a ```` ```markdown ```` fence (as chat assistants do) read as code, footnote definitions spoken, bold holding an asterisk left marked, long Chinese or Japanese paragraphs importing nothing, Google Docs image placeholders spoken, a long embedded image ending the reading early, and a Word plain-text export (UTF-16 or Windows-1252) decoded as UTF-8; fixed in the commit after: a definition must point at something link-like (<…>, a scheme, a path, an anchor, `www.`, or a domain), a fence around the whole document marked markdown, md, text, or nothing is taken off, a backtick fence cannot open on a line holding another backtick and one that never closes fences nothing, footnote definitions and their continuation lines are skipped, bold may hold a single `*` and emphasis may wrap lines (a paragraph is read as one), `。！？؟।` end sentences and text without spaces is cut between characters as a last resort, `![][image1]` and data-URL images leave only their alt text and definitions no longer count toward the reading limit, a short sentence keeps its place unless it leaves most of a large room unused, unclosed comments are found with a single scan, and files are decoded by their byte-order mark, as UTF-8 when valid, else as Windows-1252; `c534d92`. A fifth review found nothing blocking; fixed in the commit after: a UTF-8 file with a stray byte was read whole as Windows-1252 (now it stays UTF-8 unless most of its non-ASCII bytes are not UTF-8, and a legacy file is read in the code page Windows uses for the browser's language), a chat answer with a lead-in had its ```` ```markdown ```` script read as code (such a fence now only drops its own lines, and fences may use four or more marks), pause markers next to brackets were taken for links, front matter inside a wrapped document was spoken, sections past 20 were dropped though the text fitted (they are packed like paragraphs), Notion `<aside>` and Obsidian `[!NOTE]` callouts and `&#x…;`, `&eacute;`-style entities were spoken as written, a reply of only a domain or `Mr.Smith` was taken for a link definition, and repeated `![` made the embedded-image pass slow (now one scan); `82cbf48`. A sixth review found four regressions and a few gaps, none blocking; fixed in the commit after: a UTF-8 file already holding � characters was read as Windows-1252 (those bytes now count as text, and UTF-8 is kept whenever its valid multi-byte characters outnumber its bad bytes, which also covers short English scripts with a stray byte), a code block inside a ```` ```markdown ```` script closed the script's fence (the fence now runs to its last unlabelled close, and with only a short lead-in and sign-off around it the script alone is read and its heading is the title), definitions of bare file names (`[logo]: logo.png`), anchors, and any label the document refers to are not spoken, an image embedded in angle brackets left its base64 behind and an unclosed one swallowed the prose after it, a Western file read on a Japanese, Chinese, or Korean system is decoded strictly and falls back to Windows-1252 (and Serbian in Latin letters uses the Central European page), and the fence index and label scan no longer grow with the square of the document; `1f4d2e0`. A seventh review found two blocking regressions and three smaller ones; fixed in the commit after: a ```` ```markdown ```` script closed at the last bare fence in the document, so a second version in its own fence, or a ```` ```bash ```` block in the sign-off, was spoken with its fence marks (fences are now paired in order, a labelled one inside opening a code block that the next bare fence closes, and several script fences are all read without their fence lines); a fence holding only a heading, an example in a tutorial, imported nothing (the prose around it is read); an embedded image with a title left its base64 behind and pushed the rest of the script out; a Japanese or Chinese file cut short or holding one pasted character was decoded whole as Windows-1252 (a few bad bytes among many characters now keep the code page); and a speaker's reply such as `[HOST]: Absolutely!` was dropped when the label also appeared as a reference; `3d1ed4a`. An eighth review found two regressions in that commit, fixed in the commit after: a Western file read on a Japanese, Chinese, or Korean system was decoded in that code page again whenever its curly quotes happened to pair with the next letter (a few bad bytes are now forgiven only when the high bytes come in runs, as double-byte text has them, and a C1 control character, which EUC-KR and Big5 produce for a byte they cannot place, counts as a bad byte), and a bare code block inside a ```` ```markdown ```` script ended the script, dropping what followed; a script fence left open now also runs to the end of the document; `fbe810a`. A ninth review found two regressions in that commit, fixed in the commit after: a Polish, Turkish, or Lithuanian file with a pasted emoji was read as Windows-1252 (the run test and the C1 rule now apply to the double-byte pages only, and a single-byte page is read strictly as before), and a code block in a chat answer's lead-in made the whole answer be read (only a bare fence after the script's close counts)
- Video Agent: like HeyGen's Video Agent, a prompt (3–10,000 characters, optionally with a public web page as the only source of facts) becomes a whole video. The script writer returns a plan (title, orientation, and one scene per idea with a full or close-up framing) through structured output with the Claude writer, or a deterministic one in demo mode; the plan is kept to ten scenes and 5000 characters, and the brand kit's presenter, voice, background, quality, and captions setting are applied, with a chosen presenter bringing its own default voice. `POST /api/video-agent` renders it straight away (`mode: "generate"`) or returns the draft (`mode: "plan"`), which the new Video Agent page opens in the editor once, like a template, through session storage, so a refresh comes back to the autosaved copy rather than the plan; the page is in the sidebar, on Home, and in the phone-width, accessibility, and viewer-role e2e passes; unit tests cover planning, the brand kit, refusals, fitting, and a page-sourced render, and an e2e test reviews a plan in the editor and generates a square video; `f3eff67`. An independent review found nothing blocking and five smaller issues, fixed in the commit after: a refused or cut-off plan from the Claude planner surfaced as a raw parse error (the request now checks the stop reason before reading the plan, and a plan it cannot read says so), Generate ran the planner before checking the render allowance (an owner at the limit now gets the 429 first, at no cost), cutting an over-long first scene could leave half a pause marker or only pauses (the cut drops a partial marker and a scene with no words), a chosen presenter whose default voice had gone skipped the brand kit's voice for the first one listed (voices are now tried in order: asked for, the presenter's, the kit's), and the editor trusted the hand-off's aspect, quality, captions, and backgrounds (unknown values fall back to the editor's defaults); `f606baa`. A review of that commit found that the pause-marker strip took everything after any unclosed bracket ("Press the [ key, …" kept two words); it now removes only what is left of a split marker, and a first scene that cuts down to pauses gives way to the next scene instead of ending the plan
- Dub quality and audio: video translations take a quality (Speed, the default, or Precision for a slower, more natural dub), "Remove background music", and "Enhance speech", chosen on the translate page next to lip-sync and speakers and kept as preferences between videos; the HeyGen adapter moves to the v3 Video Translate API (`POST` and `GET /v3/video-translations`, `mode`, `disable_music_track`, `enable_speech_enhancement`), a finished dub links its SRT and VTT subtitle files, a completion callback reads the job's status once to pick those up, and the job list shows the options; unit tests cover the schema, the stored defaults, the exact v3 request and status mapping, and the callback, and an e2e test dubs at precision without music; `4032662`. An independent review found nothing blocking; fixed in the commit after: the translate page polled a list that never asked the provider, so without webhooks a dub never left "processing" (the list now moves up to five running jobs on per poll), a job a poll read could overwrite a callback that landed meanwhile and lose its subtitle links (the job is read again before each write), a late or repeated callback could undo a finished dub, a job started through v2 before the move kept failing its v3 status read (a 404 there now asks v2), HeyGen's presigned links were stored once and expired (a finished HeyGen job is read again after an hour, with a one-hour back-off when that fails), and the status reads now time out after ten seconds; `688a04e`. A review of that commit found that a store error during a refresh failed the whole list (and so stopped the page's polling), that a failed renewal could write an expired link back over one a parallel request had just renewed, that every open tab asked the provider about every running job on every poll, and that five jobs the provider could not answer for kept older jobs from ever being asked about; fixed in the commit after: a refresh that fails leaves its job as stored, a failed renewal re-reads the job first, each job is asked about at most every 15 seconds from listings with the least recently asked first, and a job HeyGen knows under neither API is marked failed. The race test now checks the callback's video as well, so it fails without the guard; `8db3e6d`. A review of that commit found that a brand-new job HeyGen briefly answered 404 for was marked failed for good (nothing polls a failed job); fixed in the commit after: a job the provider has no record of is only marked failed once it is over 30 minutes old
- Translation proofreading: like HeyGen's proofread flow, "Proofread the lines first" on the translate page stops a dub once its lines are translated; the job shows them, one box per line with its start time, corrections can be saved, and "Approve and dub" makes the dub from the approved lines. The HeyGen adapter creates a v3 proofread with the dub's options, polls it, reads its SRT, and on approval points HeyGen at the corrected SRT (served by this app at `/api/translate/:id/srt` behind a per-job secret that never reaches the browser) before calling generate, after which the job is polled like any dub; the demo provider has Spanish, French, and German lines. New `PATCH /api/translate/:id` and `POST /api/translate/:id/dub`, and `parseSrt` for SRT files; unit tests drive the demo and the exact HeyGen requests, and an e2e test corrects, saves, reloads, and dubs; `6f0548f`. An independent review found six issues, fixed in the commit after: a dub HeyGen did not know yet was failed for good when the lines had taken over 30 minutes to read (the grace period now counts from `dubStartedAt`); the one-dub guard held within one server instance only (the start and saves are now conditional writes, a new `putIf` on both stores, a single filtered `UPDATE` in Supabase); a start that hung or died left the job "Starting the dub…" forever (the HeyGen calls time out after 20 seconds, the lines come back after two minutes with a warning, and a failure to record an accepted dub no longer reopens the lines for a second one); the account export carried the secret; the SRT link fell back to Vercel's usually protected deployment URL (a HeyGen proofread now needs a public https `APP_URL`, and is refused without one); and two editors could overwrite each other's corrections (edits carry the version they were opened at). A proofread whose subtitle link HeyGen refuses now fails instead of being asked about forever; `ccbb0af`. A second independent review confirmed those fixes (the Supabase `putIf` is one conditional `UPDATE … RETURNING`, never retried) and found five smaller issues, fixed in the commit after: a generate that HeyGen accepted but answered after the 20-second timeout reopened the lines at once, inviting a second billed dub (a failure that does not say whether HeyGen acted, a timeout, dropped connection, or 5xx, now keeps the lines claimed and says the dub may have started, and they reopen with the warning after two minutes); recording an accepted dub is retried and its HeyGen job id logged when it still fails; the proofread poll wrote with a plain `put` after its check, so a slow poll could undo corrections or a claim (now `putIf` too, and lines still not ready after a day are given up); a 403, 404, 408, or 429 on the subtitle link right after completion failed the job although a fresh link may work; and the localhost check also refused hosts such as `localhost.example.com`. Tests now assert that `putIf` asks PostgREST for the row back, that two saves of one version let only one through, the unanswered dub, and the one-day give-up
- Dubs keep the brand glossary: the translate page offers "Keep the brand glossary's N terms as written" whenever the brand kit has terms (on by default, listing the first few), and each dub made with it stores the kit's terms at that moment and shows "keeps N brand terms". The HeyGen adapter turns them into a v3 brand glossary's `do_not_translate_terms` and names it in the translation or proofread (`brand_glossary_id`): one glossary per set of terms, named after a digest of them and created once with an `Idempotency-Key`, then reused (in this instance from memory, elsewhere by looking the name up after a `409`), so editing the kit never changes what an earlier dub kept; a glossary HeyGen cannot set up fails the dub instead of dubbing without the terms. Unit tests cover the stored terms, the exact glossary request, reuse in any order, the lookup by name, and the failure, and an e2e test dubs with and without the terms; `95bed78`. An independent review found nothing blocking and six smaller issues, fixed in the commit after: a remembered glossary deleted in HeyGen since failed every dub with those terms until a restart (a dub refused while naming one now finds or makes the glossary again and is sent once more); the lookup by name read only the first 100 glossaries (it now follows the paging cursor, through a client call that keeps the envelope); a create still in flight in another instance could leave the lookup empty (a miss is tried again twice, a second apart); a dub submitted before the brand kit loaded on the page went without the terms (the page now always asks and the server decides); the e2e test did not check that it restored the kit; and the docs listed the request flag as stored
- Forced translations: the brand kit takes "Always translate as" rules, one per line as `term = translation` with an optional `[Language]` (up to 50), which the brand page reads back, refuses line by line when it cannot read them, and saves as `forcedTranslations`. A script translation or a subtitle language renders each term as its translation word for word (it reaches the model as a token whose meaning is the translation, so the sentence agrees with it), a language's own rule beats one for every language and a rule for a base language covers its regions, and a dub made with "Follow the brand glossary" stores the rules for its language and sends them to HeyGen as the glossary's `forced_translations` (a different set of rules is a different glossary, and a glossary of kept terms only keeps its old name). The brand page's glossary hint no longer says dubs ignore it. Unit tests cover the parser, the per-language choice, script and subtitle translation, what each dub stores, and the exact HeyGen glossary, and the glossary e2e test writes the rules on the brand page and dubs with them; `6b89d41`. An independent review found nothing blocking and fixed in the commit after: a rule for a region (`Spanish (Mexico)`) also reached its base language and other regions, depending on the order of the rules (only a rule without a region covers regions now); a translation ending in `[…]` was read back as a language (a bracket must name a language, and the kit refuses a translation ending in `]`); a term both kept and forced went to HeyGen in both lists while script translation translated it (a dub now leaves it out of the kept terms for that language); a translation naming no language skipped English rules; a term matched in another case form (`ſ`) came back untranslated (spellings are now compared folded); the glossary digest sorted rules by the server's locale; and the translate page counted rules rather than terms. Tests now cover the rule order, the regional rules, the English default, the overlap per language, and the exact digest of a kept-only glossary
- Exploratory QA pass: an independent session drove every page of the demo build in a browser (create, render, share, translate with proofreading and the glossary, the Video Agent, batch, interactive, templates, workspaces, settings, phone width, console and network) and found six issues, fixed together: Ctrl/Cmd+Enter in the editor rendered with settings from before the last change of quality (the shortcut now calls the current render function); the batch page ignored the brand kit (it now starts from the kit's presenter and voice, or the presenter's own voice, format, quality, and captions, and the server puts the kit's background behind rows that name none); the interactive page offered only the first six presenters and ignored the kit and a presenter's own voice; a Windows-1252 CSV upload garbled accented names (the batch page decodes uploads like the script importer); a template's `{placeholders}` could be rendered as words (the editor now names the unfilled ones above the scenes); and the Video Agent's "let the agent choose" format ignored the kit's default format. Unit tests cover the batch background and the agent format, and an e2e test covers the batch and interactive defaults, the CSV encoding, and the shortcut, with the placeholder notice in the template test; `8a6dab8`. An independent review found nothing blocking and three small issues, fixed in the commit after: a prompt asking for a wide format ("a widescreen YouTube explainer") took the kit's format instead (a prompt that names landscape, widescreen, horizontal, YouTube, or 16:9 now keeps 16:9); a format, quality, or captions choice made on the batch page before the kit arrived was overwritten (settings changed by hand are kept); and a batch from a saved template whose presenter is gone fell back to the first presenter rather than the kit's
- Second exploratory QA pass (seven findings, fixed together): deleting videos, emptying the trash, or deleting all data gave the month's render allowance back, since it was counted from the videos still stored; each owner's month now has a usage record that counts a render when it starts (conditional writes, and an insert-only first write, so concurrent renders each count), gives it back once when the provider reports it failed, and survives deletion. Viewers could read the webhook's signing secret (which would let them forge deliveries) and change the workspace's render email and stars; the secret is left out for them, both writes answer `403`, and the settings and pickers show read-only controls and no stars. Below 1024px the closed drawer's links stayed in the tab order, Escape did not close it, and opening it left the focus behind; it is now hidden when closed, takes the focus, closes on Escape, and returns the focus to the menu button. The editor's Improve menu and AI writer close on Escape with the focus back on their buttons, the Improve button reports `aria-expanded`, and their unlabelled selects and inputs have labels. Renaming a video from the keyboard returned the focus to the page body; it returns to the title. Adding a subtitle language now clears an approval, as edited captions do; `b4135d1`. An independent review of that commit found five things, fixed in the next commit: two server instances recording the same provider failure (the transition lock is per instance) would both refund it, so a refund now needs a per-video marker only one of them can insert; a usage write that failed after the provider had accepted the render answered an error (inviting a second render, and in a batch stopping the whole request), so the count is now taken before the video is stored and a failure is logged instead; a render started just before midnight on the 1st could be counted in one month and refunded in another, and a render from before the counter existed could cancel one that was counted, so each counted render carries the month that counted it (`countedIn`) and only those are refunded; a rename that failed after Enter could later pull the focus back to the title; and the viewer e2e test could pass its no-stars check while the role was still loading
- Uploaded background images: like HeyGen's own uploads, the editor's Background tab takes a picture from the computer (PNG or JPEG as it is; WebP, GIF, and photos over 3 MB redrawn as a JPEG that fits), lists "Your images" to pick from, and deletes one from every scene that used it. Images are stored in their own kind with the bytes apart (like clip audio), named by scenes as `/api/backdrops/<id>`, checked before a render or a batch starts, uploaded to HeyGen once as an image asset (`image_asset_id`) and remembered, and included in account export and deletion. Unit tests cover storage, typing by content, the caps, ownership, a deleted image, the brand kit's image in a batch, export, deletion, the single upload, and the HeyGen request; an e2e test uploads a PNG and a GIF, renders with one, and deletes it; `8f3d23f`. An independent review found seven smaller things, fixed in the next commit: the editor now owns the image list and gives any scene naming an image that is gone (deleted in another tab, or restored from a draft, template, copy, or plan) a plain background with a note, instead of a blank stage and a refused render; templates and the brand kit check an uploaded image when saved; an image background must be an http(s) URL or exactly an uploaded image's path, so a near miss never reaches HeyGen as a URL; conversion paints transparency white, closes the decoded bitmap, and reports a canvas too large to encode; an upload whose listed row fails takes its bytes back out and a delete removes the bytes first, so none are orphaned; recording an asset id cannot bring back an image deleted meanwhile; and the image is served `no-cache`
- PDF slides to video: like HeyGen's PDF import, "Import slides" in the editor takes a deck saved as PDF and makes one scene per page (up to the 20-scene limit): pdf.js (the legacy build, since the modern one calls `Map.prototype.getOrInsertComputed`, which current browsers do not all have) draws each page in the browser at 1920 pixels on the long edge, the page is uploaded as a background image, its text becomes the draft script (titles and bullets turned into sentences, the whole kept within the 5000-character script budget with short pages' leftover room going to long ones), and the format follows the first page's shape. It confirms before replacing scenes, checks the 50-image allowance first, and says which pages were left out, shortened, or have no text. Unit tests cover the text handling; an e2e test builds a three-page PDF, imports it, checks the scenes, scripts, title, and that the page was really drawn (a 1920x1080 image with the title's dark pixels), and refuses a file that is not a PDF; `ee5c21b`. An independent review found eight things, fixed in the next commit: a worker that failed to load left the import on "Reading…" forever (pdf.js now starts the worker from `workerSrc` and reports a failure, and opening a deck times out after a minute); the script budget ignored the blank line between scenes, so a full deck could come out at 5038 of 5000 characters; the 50-image check read a list that might not have loaded, and a partial upload then replaced it (the allowance is now read fresh and the list refetched); scenes edited during a long import were replaced without asking again (it now asks, and a title typed meanwhile is kept); 4:3 slides were cropped by the stage (each page is now centred in a frame of the video's format, white bars around it); the JPEG fallback was not re-measured (the quality steps down until it fits or the page is refused); every page's picture was held until the end (pages are now drawn and uploaded one at a time on one canvas, released afterwards); and a viewer whose role was still loading could start an import; `ebabfe1`
- Third exploratory QA pass (nine findings over the new images, slide import, and workspaces, fixed together): drafts were kept under one key for every space, so opening Create in a workspace showed the personal draft and, finding its images missing there, rewrote them as plain backgrounds for good; each space now keeps its own draft (Personal under the original key). Deleting an image left templates and the brand kit naming it, so batches, saving a template, and saving the kit failed with a raw id; a delete now gives them a plain background, a video saved as a template swaps out an image that is gone, and the brand page shows a refused save next to its button too. The portrait stage was sized width-first and capped in height, so it came out near square and cropped portrait slides; it is now sized by its height. The brand kit can pick one of the uploaded images as its default background, and its preview says so. The image allowance is 100 (two full decks), and a slide import checks it before asking to replace the scenes. A format picked while slides import is kept, Import script waits for a slides import to finish, the deleted-image note is the editor's ordinary dismissable note, GIF is offered in the file picker, a file that is not a picture says so, and the delete buttons are larger; `ccc792b`. Its CI run failed: the brand page's second error banner repeated every save message, so a test that reads one by its text found two (now save messages show only next to Save, other errors at the top). An independent review then found five things, fixed in the same commit: when the workspace list failed to load the editor fell back to the Personal draft, bringing the spoiled-draft bug back (no draft is restored or saved then); words typed before the space was known were overwritten by a restored draft (it no longer restores over them); deleting an image removed it before tidying the kit and templates, so a failure part-way could not be finished, and a kit saved meanwhile could be overwritten (the tidy-up now runs first with conditional writes that re-read on conflict); the brand page kept an uploaded image's path after it was deleted or after switching to a video background (it is cleared); and the portrait stage lost its shape below a 292-pixel column (its height is also capped by the screen's width)
- PowerPoint speaker notes as scripts: like HeyGen's PowerPoint import, "Import slides" also takes a `.pptx`. Its slides are read in presentation order from the deck's XML (fflate unpacks only the presentation, slide, and notes parts, never the pictures), each scene's script is its slide's speaker notes (the body placeholder of the notes page) or, without notes, the slide's own text less footer, date, and number, and hidden slides are left out as in the slide show. On its own a `.pptx` gives one scene per shown slide on the current background (browsers cannot draw PowerPoint slides); chosen together with the deck saved as PDF, each page is drawn behind the presenter and scripted from its slide's notes, with hidden slides dropped when the PDF left them out and a warning when the counts still differ. Unit tests cover notes and text with XML entities, presentation order, split runs, hidden slides, and non-PowerPoint files; an e2e test imports a deck alone and with its PDF and checks the refusals (a mutation that pairs hidden slides fails it) (`758edd8`). An independent review of that commit found that PowerPoint's own line breaks (`<a:br>` holding run properties) glued words together, an empty run with attributes (`<a:t xml:space="preserve"/>`) leaked raw XML into the script, slide parts not named `slideN.xml` were never unpacked and the slide was dropped (shifting every later page's notes), a `.pptx` imported over a PDF's scenes put page 1 behind every slide, equations were read twice and tables not at all, a password-protected deck got advice to save it as .pptx, and several counts read "1 slides" or "its text" for many; all fixed in `fa9b337`, with unit tests for each parser case and an e2e step that imports the PDF and then the .pptx (each mutation of a fix fails a test)
- Fourth exploratory QA pass (nine findings over the Video Agent, templates, batches, the video page, the share page, translation, and the interactive presenter, fixed together): a gated share page sent the player source, poster, download link, button, description, and every transcript line in its page data and only hid them, so the email form could be skipped by reading the source; the page now carries only the form and the lead answer returns what lies past it (`shareBodyFor`, rendered by one `ShareWatch` component for open and gated links alike), with the e2e test checking the source for the file URL, script, button, and download. A personalized batch rendered a row whose cells left the script empty (and one longer than a video holds), spending a render; every row is now checked before any starts, the preview names the rows, and Generate waits. Enter on an unchanged title reopened the field (the same key pressed the title button that took the focus); the CSV upload on the batch page was out of the tab order and could not take the same file twice; removing a caption line kept "Captions saved"; the focus fell to the page after a folder change, saving a template, posting or deleting a comment, restoring from the trash, deleting a template, starting a dub, and passing the email gate; the language chips showed no keyboard focus; a deleted presenter's transcripts stayed open with a dead download; and four messages said the wrong thing (a talk page repeating its heading, the approval note leaving out captions and subtitles, "messages" counting only questions, and the placeholder note reading as a requirement). The batch checks have unit tests (a mutation that drops the guard fails one); the share leak, batch row, title key, CSV upload, and caption status have e2e assertions (`251fbb4`). An independent review of that commit found the gate still let two things through, fixed in `0bbcd59`: the page's link-preview tags carried the description, and the captions route served every line of a gated video's transcript to anyone; a gated preview now has its title and cover only, the captions answer `401` until the visitor has the gate's cookie, and the cookie's value is derived from the video (a constant `1` could be typed in to skip the form and the lead record). It also found that a blank or pauses-only batch script blamed every row's cells, that Enter confirming a Japanese or Chinese input method's candidate saved the title or folder mid-word, that the new focus returns could pull a viewer back after they had moved on during the request (they now act only when the focus was lost), and two small accessibility points (the focused player region had no name; the upload's ring showed on mouse clicks). A password-mode e2e test checks the gated page, the captions route, and a forged cookie (removing the captions check fails it); unit tests cover the gate cookie and the blank template
- Word scripts: "Import script" also takes a `.docx`, as HeyGen's script upload does. Only `word/document.xml` is unpacked (fflate, in the browser); paragraphs are read in order with split runs joined, tabs and line breaks as spaces, tracked deletions, field codes, and text boxes left out, and a table read one row per line with its cells joined by commas. A Title (or first top-level heading) before any text names the video, and every other heading or outline-level paragraph starts a new scene, as a Markdown heading does; the words are taken as written (a `*` stays a `*`). A Word 97–2003 `.doc` or a password-protected file says how to save it. The XML unescaping is shared with the PowerPoint reader (`xml-text.ts`). Unit tests cover the reader (including empty runs with attributes, whose tag would otherwise swallow the next run); the script-import e2e test imports a document. Also rides along: a batch cell holding only invisible characters (a zero-width space) counts as empty (`f803166`). An independent review against real Word output found, all fixed in `5f32624` with a unit test each (and each fix's removal failing one): a deleted paragraph mark or table row (a self-closing `<w:del/>`) opened a match that ran to the next deletion and dropped everything between, sometimes the whole document; moved text was read at both its old and new place; a heading turned into body text with changes tracked kept its old style from the change record; headings in a German, French, or custom style were not recognised (styles are now read from `word/styles.xml` by their built-in English names and outline levels, following `basedOn`); an empty content control's placeholder ("Click or tap here to enter text.") and a table of contents were read as script, the latter also keeping the title from being taken; hidden text and the small readings above Japanese characters were read aloud; a table inside a cell split its rows into separate scenes (nesting is now counted); symbols joined the words around them; and a `.doc` or other Word file picked under another name was imported as binary noise (it is now recognised by its first bytes). The batch check also treats direction marks and soft hyphens as invisible, and a template of invisible characters as empty
- Video Agent documents: as HeyGen's agent reads attached files, the Video Agent page takes a PDF, Word, PowerPoint, or text file to write from. It is read in the browser (`document-text.ts`: pdf.js text only, nothing drawn, for a PDF; the Word and PowerPoint readers above; the script decoder for text) and only its words travel, up to 12,000 characters cut at a word, as `document: { name, text }`; the planner gets it as its source of facts, named alongside a reference page when there is one. A file with no words (a scanned PDF) says so; the PDF opening is shared with the slide import (`openPdf`). Unit tests cover the reader and the planner's source (the demo planner speaks the source's sentences, so a test can see what it was given); an e2e test attaches a PDF, checks that only its words were sent, and finds them in the planned scenes (`a8eb909`). An independent review found, fixed in `feaa580`: the attached file's chip sat inside the field's `<label>`, so a click on its name pressed Remove; a binary file not named or typed as a PDF (a PDF called `report`, a picture) was decoded as text and sent as the source of facts (formats are now told by their first bytes, pictures and recordings refused, and decoded text with NULs or many control characters refused); a large text file was decoded whole before the 12,000-character cap (now only the first ~49 KB); a two-byte check sent Windows-1251 text starting "РП" to the Word reader; an OLE `.ppt` or `.xls` got Word's message and a zip was read by its name rather than its contents; the cut dropped a whole word at an exact word end and could split an emoji; the demo planner spoke the lines naming each source; and focus and announcements were lost on attach and remove (focus now moves to Remove and back, one status line is always present, and the error is tied to the field). Unit tests cover each reader case (each guard's removal fails one); the e2e test attaches an unnamed PDF, clicks its name, and checks focus
- Per-scene speaking speed: as HeyGen's studio lets each scene set its own pace, a scene may carry `speed` (0.5–1.5). The editor's scene row offers it next to the scene's presenter and voice (the video's speed is the default), the running-time estimate and Listen follow it, HeyGen receives it as that scene's `voice.speed`, the demo render and the caption timing estimate take each scene at its pace, and the video page lists it. A speed equal to the video's, or on an audio scene, is stored as absent; templates and copies keep it. While adding it, the editor's scene builder turned out to copy only presenter fields, so a copy, a template, a restored draft, or a scene whose presenter had been deleted would have lost its speed; it now carries it. A unit test checks what is stored, sent to HeyGen, timed by the demo render and captions, and kept by a template (each path's removal fails it); the multi-scene e2e test sets a scene's speed, finds it on the video page, and sees it again in an edited copy; `32a0081`. An independent review found these, fixed in the commit after: the captions still shared the running time evenly across scenes, so a slow scene's lines left the screen early (each scene now gets a share by its length at its pace, an audio scene by its clip's, and a translated track with a paragraph per scene keeps that timing); a lone scene left after a removal, or loaded from a copy or template, kept a speed the slider could not change (it now becomes the video's speed); the scene list offered the video's own speed again; the caption estimate ignored an audio scene's clip length; and the batch page's template hint, the video page, and the docs did not say that scene speeds are not used by a batch or that D-ID keeps its natural pace. A unit test checks the per-scene caption timing (removing the split or the clip length fails it); the multi-scene e2e test removes a scene and sees the slider take the one left's speed. A second review of that fix found four more, fixed in the commit after: scenes written without spaces (Chinese, Japanese, Thai) counted as one word each, so every such scene got an equal share (scene lengths, and the demo render's, now weigh such text by its characters as caption lines do); D-ID videos were timed by scene speeds D-ID ignores; a scene holding a blank line made its translation fall back to even timing (a translation's paragraphs are now grouped by how many each scene holds); and a short line before a long pause stayed up through half the pause, since the cap on pauses (half the running time, for a short render) applied per scene, whose share already counts its pauses. A unit test covers each (reverting any one fails it)
- Background fit: as HeyGen lets a background image or video fill the frame or be shown whole, a background may carry `fit` (`cover`, the default and stored as absent, or `contain`; dropped on a colour). The editor's Background tab offers "Fill the frame" and "Show all of it" for a picture or video, a scene keeps its fit when its picture is changed, the stage previews it, HeyGen receives it as the background's `fit` (an image by URL or by uploaded asset, and a looping video, which was always sent as `cover`), templates, copies, and drafts keep it, and the video page says when a background is shown whole (and that renderers other than HeyGen ignore it). Unit tests check what is stored, kept by a template, and sent to HeyGen (removing the HeyGen field or the stored-as-absent rule fails them); the multi-scene e2e test shows a looping video whole, sees the stage preview it, finds it on the stored scene, and sees it again in an edited copy; `568de5f`. An independent review found these, fixed in the commit after: the video page showed the fit only for the first scene (each scene's line now says "background shown whole"); the brand page dropped a default background's fit on every save (it now has a "Show all of it" choice and keeps what was saved); the docs said other renderers fill the frame, when they show their default background; and an image filling the frame was sent with no `fit`, leaving HeyGen's own default to decide (it is now sent as `cover`, as a video's always was). The brand e2e test saves and reloads the choice, and the multi-scene one reads it on the scene's line
- Brand kit from a website: as HeyGen can import a brand kit from a public website, the brand page's "Fill from a website" reads the site's name, logo, and colours and fills the form for review (nothing is saved until Save). `POST /api/brand/import` fetches the page on the server through the same public-address guard as the script writer, now able to follow up to three redirects with each hop checked (a bare domain is taken as https), and `brand-import.ts` reads the declared name (`og:site_name`, `application-name`, or the title's first part), logo (JSON-LD, touch icon, or the largest favicon), and colours (theme colour, tile and pinned-tab colours, then CSS variables named primary, brand, or accent, preferring a coloured one to white or grey). Unit tests cover the parsing, colour forms, redirects (including one to a private address, and endless ones), and a page that names nothing; an e2e test sees a private address refused and a site's details fill the form without saving; `62d1c48`. An independent review found these, fixed in the commit after: the parser's regular expressions backtracked on crafted pages (a 40 KB page took 19 seconds, and one megabyte would hold the server indefinitely), so elements are now found with indexOf and every pattern runs on a capped piece with bounded repeats, and a test reads nine hostile one-megabyte pages in under a second and a half each (the old parser does not finish in a minute); a numeric entity naming no character, or a JSON-LD array of a few hundred thousand items, crashed the request with a 500 (both are now handled, and any parse failure is a 400); an address with a port was taken for a scheme; a redirect to a broken address gave a misleading message; the name was often the page's own title ("Home | Acme" gave "Home"; the part naming the host, else the shortest, is now taken, with markup dropped); the import replaced a name, logo, or colours already filled in without asking (it now asks, and declining fills only what is empty); a logo that failed to load left an error at the top of the page (it now shows by the logo); a plain-http logo would have been blocked on share pages (only https logos are offered); and the docs overstated the time limit and named the rate limit per editor rather than per workspace
- Silent scenes: as HeyGen lets a scene hold the presenter without a word (a title card, a slide to read), a scene whose script holds only pause markers is now a silent one instead of being refused, up to 60 seconds, as long as some scene has words or an audio clip. HeyGen receives it as a `silence` voice of that duration; D-ID and the demo renderer pause for it as they do for any pause; its length counts in the running time, the demo render, and the caption timing, and it leaves no caption line. The editor says "Silent scene: the presenter holds for 4s", refuses a video that is all silence or a silent scene over a minute, and leaves Listen off for it; the video page lists "silent for 4s". Unit tests cover what is accepted and refused, the HeyGen request (removing the silence mapping fails it), the caption timing, and the demo render's length; an e2e test refuses an all-silent video, makes a silent second scene with the Pause button, and finds it stored and listed; `191e1e0`. An independent review found these, fixed in the commit after: the voice picker previewed the joined script, which could open with a minute of breaks when the first scene was silent (it now previews the first scene with words); a translated track timed its scenes only if the translation kept the silent scene's pause as a paragraph (paragraphs with words are now matched, so a silent scene keeps its share either way); Improve could rewrite a silent scene into speech; pause lengths were summed without rounding ("silent for 0.30000000000000004s") and a silence under a second was held a full second by HeyGen but counted shorter elsewhere (silences are now 1 to 60 seconds, to a tenth); the stage showed an empty caption box, the share page blank lines, and the scene picker a speed for a silent scene (a speed on one is now dropped); the 60-second bound refused an audio scene whose transcript was only pauses though the editor allowed it; and templates had no bound at all. Unit tests cover the bounds and rounding, the audio scene, the dropped speed, the template bound, and the translated track with and without the pause
- Fifth exploratory QA pass (over PowerPoint, Word, and Video Agent document imports, per-scene speed, background fit, the website brand import, and silent scenes, driven in a browser on a demo build; most of it held up, and these were fixed together): opening Edit a copy or a template overwrote the editor's unsent draft, and a reload of the copy threw its edits away (a copy and a template now keep their own draft, restored on reload with the usual banner, and Discard goes back to the original); PowerPoint and PDF text added a full stop after a pause marker or a closing quote, which turned a slide of only a pause into a spoken one (a line ending in either is left as it is); the brand page's import compared the website's details with the form as it was when Fill in was pressed, so a name typed while it ran was replaced without asking (it now compares with the form as it stands); a document the Video Agent could not read left the keyboard focus on the page body (it goes back to the file field); a Word or text section of only pauses was folded into the scene before it (a section set apart by a heading or `---` is now a silent scene when it lasts one to sixty seconds, while a pause paragraph inside running text still joins its neighbour); a scene speed equal to the video's was dropped on save, so a copy whose video speed changed moved the scene too (a chosen speed is kept, the scene picker offers the video's own speed again, and a lone scene's speed becomes the video's, which the video page shows); the templates API accepted a template of nothing but silence; a template card showed a silent scene's raw marker; and a response that was not JSON (an error page from a proxy) showed the parser's message. Unit tests cover the slide punctuation, the import sections, the kept and lone scene speeds, and the silent template; an e2e test keeps an editor draft and a copy's edits apart across a reload
- Find and replace: as a studio script editor offers, the editor's Find button opens a panel that searches every scene's script at once (optionally matching case or whole words), shows how many matches it finds in how many scenes, and replaces them all, the replacement taken literally (a `$&` stays as typed); pause markers are never matched inside, so a search for "pause" or "2s" cannot break one. Enter in the replacement field replaces, Escape closes the panel and returns the focus to the Find button. Unit tests cover the counts, the options, the literal replacement, and the markers; an e2e test replaces a street name across two scenes and finds nothing inside a marker; `2211dc3`. An independent review found these, fixed in the commit after: Replace all could not be undone, the draft autosave keeping the result (an Undo now stands beside the note while the scripts are as it left them, and typing retires both); it rewrote what a recorded clip says, the captions then disagreeing with the speech, and could turn a silent scene into speech (both are now left alone); Ctrl/Cmd+Enter in the replacement field replaced and also generated the video from the old script (that shortcut is now the editor's alone); a replacement made while Improve was rewriting a scene was overwritten by its answer (Replace all waits for it); a search for "name" broke a template's `{name}` placeholder (placeholders are stepped over like pause markers); a typed accented word did not find the same word written with a combining accent, as imports often are, and whole words never matched Chinese, Japanese, or Thai text (both now do); and the note lingered after the scripts changed. Unit tests cover the placeholders, the accents, the unspaced scripts, and an untouched script; the e2e test undoes a replacement and sees the Undo retire after typing
- On-screen text: as HeyGen lets a scene carry a text element, each scene can show one line of text for its whole length (a title, a name and role, a price), at the top, middle, or bottom, small, medium, or large, in any colour. The editor has an "On-screen text" field under each scene's script, with position, size, and colour once there is text, and the stage previews it placed and sized as a share of the frame; the request schema keeps it to 120 characters on one line, drops blank text, and stores the defaults as absent; templates and copies keep it; HeyGen receives it as the scene's `text` element, placed and sized in pixels of the output frame (the same shares the stage uses); the video page lists it per scene, noted as not applied on other renderers. Unit tests cover the schema, storage, templates, and the HeyGen element; an e2e test drives the editor, the render, and a copy. HeyGen's text element is sent in the shape its developer forum shows (`type`, `text`, `font_family`, `font_size`, `font_weight`, `color`, `position`, `text_align`, `line_height`); the units of its position and size, and the element itself, have not been checked against a live render. An independent review of `e6097dd` found one blocking defect and ten smaller ones, fixed together: a scene whose own presenter, voice, or clip no longer existed was rebuilt without its text, so a copy, template, or draft lost it silently (the scene now keeps everything but the missing piece, with an e2e test that fails without the fix); a scene holding only text now counts as content for the import confirmation and the draft autosave; the stage drew the text wrapped while the render draws one line, so it now draws one line with spaces collapsed and warns when the text will not fit across the frame at its size, and notes when captions share its edge; the preview badge moves off a top edge the text uses; a one-scene video shows its text on the video page; the 120-character limit is measured after spaces are collapsed; the batch page says on-screen text from a template is not used; a `{placeholder}` left in on-screen text is flagged like one in a script, and the Find box says it searches scripts; the label is tied to its field by a generated id and to its notes.
- Scene chapters: a rendered video of two scenes or more lists one chapter per scene on its share page, each starting where its scene does in the caption timing (the scene timing moved from the video service into `src/lib/chapters.ts`, shared by the captions and the chapters, so the two cannot drift apart) and titled from the scene's on-screen text, else its first words cut at a word, else "Pause" for a silent scene; a chapter plays the video from its start, and the one playing is marked as the video plays. The video page lists the same chapters (estimated until the video renders) with a "Copy for YouTube" button that copies them in the `0:00 Title` form a YouTube description reads, and says whether YouTube would show them as chapters (at least three, the first at 0:00, each ten seconds or longer). They reach an email-gated share page only with the rest of its body, after the gate. Unit tests cover the timing, the titles, the description form, YouTube's rules, and the share body; an e2e test drives the video page copy and the share page jump and marking. An independent review of `e00a1cc` found two blocking defects and six smaller ones, fixed together: chapters ignored captions an editor had corrected, so a jump could land before the corrected line and a title could repeat words removed from the captions (each chapter after the first now starts at the corrected line nearest its scene and, without on-screen text, takes that line's words); and a rendered video whose renderer reported no length timed its chapters from the estimate, so later ones could point past the end of the clip (the share page now leaves out chapters past the clip's own length once the player knows it, and the video page says the times are estimates because the length was not reported rather than "until the video renders"). The YouTube check now judges the whole seconds the copied text gives; titles are cut at whole characters (an emoji is never split) and keep a last word that fits; scenes starting within the same second as the chapter before are left out so no two chapters share a time; a chapter title cut short on the share page shows in full as a tooltip. Chapters keep the script's titles and times on a translated subtitle track.
- Sixth exploratory QA pass (over find and replace, on-screen text, and scene chapters, driven in a browser on a demo build; no console errors and no sideways scroll at 390px, and these were fixed together): correcting the captions of a video with a silent scene moved that scene's chapter onto the next scene's line and dropped the next chapter, and a scene whose caption lines were deleted took a neighbour's line as its title (each corrected line now belongs to exactly one scene, and a chapter starts at its scene's first line and takes all of them as its title, otherwise keeping its estimate and its script; a silent scene keeps its estimate); a silent scene without on-screen text is no longer a chapter of its own titled "Pause" (one that opens the video is "Opening"); titles drawn from caption lines lose a trailing comma; Replace all refuses a replacement that would take the video past its 5000 characters, saying how long it would be, instead of leaving Generate blocked; the Find panel says that what recorded clips say and on-screen text are left as typed when the video has them, and Enter in the search moves to the replacement; the on-screen text field, its counter, and the server count the same whole characters after collapsing spaces, and pasted text past the limit is cut there. In demo mode the sample clip is shorter than the length the mock reports, so the share page can list fewer chapters than the video page; a real renderer reports the clip's own length. An independent review of `7bd7993` found three blocking defects and several smaller ones, fixed together: a chapter could still take the previous scene's last line (even with captions saved unchanged), and times shifted by a couple of seconds gave scenes their neighbours' lines, because lines were matched to scenes by a time window; each corrected line now belongs to exactly one scene (in order, when the editor kept as many lines as the script's captions have, however far their times moved; otherwise by where the line's middle falls), and an audio scene whose lines were all removed is titled "Scene N". An Undo button showed beside a refused Replace all and could restore scripts from before later typing; Undo now shows only while the last replacement can be taken back, the refusal is said first and only while the scripts are as it found them (cleared when the panel opens or closes), and a replacement that shortens a script already over the limit is made. Typing in the middle of a full on-screen text field dropped the end of it and moved the caret; only what is typed or pasted is now cut, the caret stays after it, an input method's composition is left alone until it ends, and the field, its counter, and the server count the characters a reader sees (a flag or an emoji with a skin tone is one, and none is ever split), with room for spaces typed before they are collapsed. The Find note now reads "Searches the typed scripts; … is not changed" and is tied to the search field.
- Make other sizes: as HeyGen resizes a video for other platforms, the video page has an "Other sizes" card that renders the video again in the shapes it is not (a 9:16 portrait for stories and reels, a 1:1 square for feeds), each a new video with the same scenes, presenter, voice, look, quality, captions, and voice settings, titled with its shape, in the same folder, and linked back to the original ("Made from another size of this video"); before anything renders it names any scene whose on-screen text would not fit one line in a shape. `POST /api/videos/:id/resize` checks the month's allowance for every size before starting any, as a batch does; the request a stored video renders from is now built in one place for retries and resizes. Unit tests cover the copies, titles, folder, link back, refusals, and the allowance; an e2e test makes a portrait size from the video page and follows the links both ways. An independent review of `0936e1e` found one blocking defect and eight smaller ones, fixed together: each size re-checked the allowance as it started, so a render made elsewhere in between could start one size and then answer a 429 that lost it (and two requests at once could pass the allowance anyway); the sizes now share one render context and the up-front check, as a batch's rows do, and a size that cannot be created is reported beside those that started instead of discarding them (the docs now say that requests at the same moment can between them pass the allowance, as batches can). Copies now keep corrected captions and subtitle languages; titles are cut at whole characters; a size made from a size is titled and linked from the original; a retried size keeps its link; the card does not offer a size it already started, so a second click cannot make it twice; "Made from" names the original, says when it has since been deleted, and shows on a trashed copy too; the fit warning sits outside the checkbox label, tied to it; and each size started is recorded in the workspace activity feed.
