- Seventh exploratory QA pass (over Make other sizes and a walk through the editor, library, video and share pages, batch, templates, translate, the brand kit, and settings, driven in a browser on a demo build; no console errors or failed requests across 19 routes, and these were fixed together): after a reload the card offered and made the same sizes again, and a size offered the original's own shape, each duplicate spending a render (the server now leaves out every shape the video has, its own, the original's, and its live sizes, refusing a request of only those, and `GET /api/videos/:id/sizes` lets the card list the sizes with links and offer only the missing shapes, from the original or any size); the embed snippet loaded the whole share page, so the player was cut off inside the iframe (`/share/:id?embed=1` is now the player alone, filling the frame, and the snippet points at it); share-page chapters ran off a phone screen with a long title; a title of flag emoji could make a size's title longer than the 120 the request schema counts (titles are now cut by its count at whole characters); the card showed the Make button while a viewer's role was still loading; focus now moves to the size just made, which is announced, the original in the trash says so, and an empty choice reads "Choose a size"; the AI writer and Improve replaced a script with no way back, and now offer Undo while the scene still holds what they wrote, returning focus to the script; and the editor's title field, the library's status filters, the video page's folder button, the brand kit's aspect choices, and the plan's allowance line now say what they are; `30e14e6`. An independent review of that commit found two blocking defects, fixed in the commit after: an AI rewrite (Improve or the writer) that came back after its scene was moved, removed, or typed in was written by position into whatever scene now stood there, and its Undo then wrote the first scene's old script over that one, losing it (a rewrite is now applied only while its scene still holds the script that was sent, and otherwise says the scene changed; Undo checks the same way); and a size whose render could not be started still counted as a shape the video had, so the card offered it again and Make answered "already has" (a failed size is listed as failed and its shape can be made again). Also fixed: a trashed original's shape is no longer offered from its sizes (it can be restored, so it would be a duplicate), the focus goes to the size just made rather than the first link (on a copy, the original) and stays put when none started, the embedded player is the largest box of the video's shape that fits the iframe, after the email form too, so the logo and captions sit on the picture and not in black bars, the embedded password form scrolls in a short frame, a title that is a single long character (a letter with a run of combining marks) is cut rather than emptied, the card says when the sizes cannot be loaded, and the e2e test checks the card has loaded before it asserts a shape is not offered. Two requests at the same moment can between them make the same shape twice, as with the allowance.
- Voice design (HeyGen's voice designer): the Voices page's Design a voice panel takes a description (with three examples to start from) and an optional sample, asks the voice provider for previews of it (ElevenLabs `POST /v1/text-to-voice/design`, three MP3 previews; in demo mode three previews the browser reads at different pitches), and keeps the one chosen as a voice of the account (`POST /v1/text-to-voice`), tagged `designed voice`, listed first with the clones and usable in every video. Designed and cloned voices share the five voices an account keeps, checked before previews are asked for so none are paid for that could not be saved; designs are limited to ten an hour per account; viewers do not see the panel; `/api/status` reports `voiceDesign`. Unit tests cover the checks, the demo flow and the shared limit, and the ElevenLabs requests against a stubbed fetch; an e2e test designs, chooses, saves, and finds the voice. The ElevenLabs endpoints follow its API reference and are checked only against stubbed requests. `2f25a89`. An independent review found three blocking defects, fixed in the commit after: with ElevenLabs, every account's designed voices (and clones, since they shipped) were in the one ElevenLabs account the app uses, so they appeared in every other account's voice list and could be rendered with there (the shared list now leaves out ElevenLabs' cloned and generated voices, so each account sees only its own, from its records, listed once); the hourly limit on designs was per workspace and per instance with no backstop, so one person with many workspaces could run up the provider bill, and a full account used up a design to be told so (designs now also stop at sixty an hour across accounts, a full account is refused before anything is counted, and saves are limited too); and saves or clones sent together could all pass the five-voice count (they now run one at a time per account within an instance, the count checked again under the lock). Also fixed: the panel shows only once the role is known to be an editor's (a viewer no longer sees it while the workspace loads), a description ElevenLabs refuses is a 400 with its reason instead of a server error, design and save errors are announced, the focus returns to the description after a save, a stopped demo preview no longer resets the one playing, the demo note says the saved voice also uses the browser's voice, the voice row's buttons name a voice of your own rather than a clone, the tests check that a full account never asks the provider for previews, that clones count toward the same five, and that eight saves at once keep to five, and the e2e test removes its voice even when it fails. D-ID renders with ElevenLabs voices through its own integration, which may need D-ID to hold the ElevenLabs key for private voices; clones have the same limit, and it is not yet checked against a live account.
- Presenter from a description (HeyGen's AI avatar from a prompt): the New photo avatar panel can draw a presenter who does not exist from a written description (with an example to start from). HeyGen's v3 prompt to avatar draws a square portrait (`POST /v3/avatars`, `type: "prompt"`), followed until it is ready (`GET /v3/avatars/looks/:id`); the finished portrait is read once through the public-address guard and handed back as a data URL, so it fills the panel's preview and is named and saved like an upload, keeping its picture after HeyGen's link expires, and it renders through the same talking-photo path as any photo avatar (HeyGen's v2 video API). In demo mode an illustrated stand-in follows the description's colours. Editors only, ten portraits an hour per account and forty across accounts per instance, a description HeyGen will not draw explained as a 400, and `/api/status` reports `portraitGeneration` (D-ID has no such API, so the panel leaves it out). Unit tests cover the checks, the demo flow, and the HeyGen requests, processing, failed, and finished looks (wrapped or not) against stubbed responses; an e2e test draws, saves, and finds the presenter. The HeyGen calls follow its API reference and are not checked against a live account. `72bb873`. An independent review found four blocking defects, fixed in the commit after: the job id was HeyGen's look id, so any editor of any account could follow another's portrait, or any look in the shared HeyGen account, by its id (a job is now a record under the account that asked, with the studio's own id, and anyone else's answers not found); following a finished job re-downloaded and re-encoded the picture on every check with no limit (the finished portrait is kept on the job, so a check after that asks HeyGen for nothing, and checks are limited to 300 an hour per account); a demo name starting with an emoji crashed the stand-in (initials and the name are now cut by whole characters, and the initials are escaped for the SVG); and a portrait of 3.3 to 6 MB passed the check but could not be answered or saved within one request (the cap is now 3 MB, and a picture too large or not downloadable ends the job as failed with its reason). Also fixed: portraits are drawn 4:5 like an uploaded portrait, a refusal for the plan or credits is explained as a 400, a finished look with no picture fails instead of waiting out the panel, and a portrait that finishes after another picture was chosen leaves that picture in the preview and offers "Use the drawn portrait". The drawn look is used as a talking photo, the path the rest of the app renders with HeyGen's v2 video API, rather than as a v3 avatar look, which renders through v3 video creation; and the look stays in the HeyGen account, where whether HeyGen's avatar list shows it to every account is not yet checked against a live key. Re-run `supabase/schema.sql` so the store accepts the new `portrait` kind.
- Eighth exploratory QA pass (over voice design, a presenter from a description, other sizes, and the editor's rewrites, then a walk through the other pages, driven in a browser on a demo build; no console errors or failed requests, and these were fixed together): the AI writer's script went into the wrong scene when two scenes were both empty and one was moved while it wrote, and Improve threw away a rewrite when its scene had only moved (a rewrite now goes to the very scene it was asked for, which keeps its identity until it is edited, wherever it has moved, and is refused only when that scene was typed in or removed, with the reason shown beside the script); a size on the video page read "rendering" until each was opened (the sizes list asks the renderer about sizes still rendering, as the library does, and the card checks again every five seconds while one is); a 60-character voice name pushed the voices page sideways at phone width (the list's columns shrink and the name truncates); Escape anywhere on the page discarded the new photo avatar panel, a drawn portrait included, without asking (Escape now belongs to the panel, asks before a picture or name is lost, and the focus returns to "New photo avatar", also after creating one); a portrait could not be uploaded from the keyboard (an "Upload a portrait" button joins the tile); page errors were not announced (the error banner is an alert, and rewrite errors show beside the script); the focus was lost after drawing a presenter, using the drawn portrait, a failed design or save, deleting a voice, Replace all, its Undo, and moving a scene to either end (each now moves it to the outcome or the next control); the voice designer said only on a click that the account's five voices were used (it shows how many of five are used and is disabled when full, and its refusal clears once one is deleted); and the voice, avatar, and video searches, the sample text, and the brand logo address had no label, and each voice's play button now names its voice. `56bad58`. An independent review found two blocking defects, fixed in the commit after: a rewrite that came back after another scene was chosen switched the editor back to its own scene, so the next keystrokes meant for the chosen scene went into the rewritten one (a rewrite now keeps the selection, and the focus, only when its scene is still the one being worked on); and a check of the sizes that left before Make could answer after it and drop the size just made from the card, offering its shape again (Make now asks again, which retires the earlier check). Also: the card does not check while the tab is hidden, the voice designer takes the account's count and limit from the voices list rather than a copy of the limit, an Escape that closes an open list inside the photo avatar panel no longer closes the panel, and the phone-width check covers the whole page.
- HeyGen v3 (HeyGen retires v1 and v2 on October 31, 2026). Rendering, the avatar and voice lists, uploads, and custom portraits now use v3:
  - Lists are `GET /v3/avatars/looks` and `GET /v3/voices`, every page followed. A photo avatar still being prepared, or one without a picture, is left out of the avatar list.
  - Uploads are multipart `POST /v3/assets`.
  - A custom portrait becomes a v3 photo avatar (`POST /v3/avatars`, `type: "photo"`). It is waited for for up to about 20 seconds. One still being prepared is kept and asked about again, and a render meanwhile fails with a `400` saying to generate again in a minute. Portraits uploaded as v1 talking photos are made again once, under a new key, rather than trusting the old id as a look.
  - Videos are one `type: "studio"` `POST /v3/videos` with an `avatar_video` scene per scene: its presenter, its script and voice (with speed and pitch) or its clip, and its background. Status is read from `GET /v3/videos/:id`.
  - A silent scene lip-syncs to an uploaded WAV of silence, since v3 has no silence input.
  - The prompt-avatar answer is read from `avatar_item.id`.
  - A translation unknown to v3 is asked about on v2 until v2 answers `404` or `410`.
  - The v3 studio has no presenter layout or framing, on-screen text element, background fit, or voice emotion. The editor, the batch page, and the brand kit now say so where those settings are made. They are still previewed and kept with videos, copies, and templates, but not sent. Voices no longer carry `emotionSupport`.
  - Interactive avatars still use HeyGen's v1 streaming API, documented as needing a move to LiveAvatar.
  - All of this follows HeyGen's API reference. It is checked against stubbed requests (new `heygen-v3.test.ts`, the request-shape tests rewritten for the studio body), not against the live API.
  - An independent review of `b7c6edf` and `b048dca` found the focus hook sound. It found three blocking defects in the migration, each fixed with a test that fails without it:
    - v3 lists only public voices unless asked, so voices cloned in the HeyGen account vanished and videos naming them failed. The account's own voices and looks are now listed first.
    - Clips and background images uploaded through v1 kept their ids under `heygen` and would never be uploaded again. v3 asset ids now live under `heygen:asset`, so old ones are uploaded once more.
    - A photo avatar still being prepared was waited for on every render, one presenter after another, which could take a batch or a multi-presenter video past the 60-second function limit. A known pending look is now asked about once without waiting, and presenters are prepared side by side.
  - Fixes for its optional findings in this change's own code:
    - Errors while asking about a look are no longer read as "still preparing", and a lost or failed look is made again.
    - The docs now give the true status for a pending render (a stored failed video, `502`).
    - The account's own looks are listed without the page cap.
    - `#rgb` colours are spelled out, and other colours are refused plainly.
    - No silence is uploaded for a render refused anyway.
    - A portrait's look is not listed beside it.
    - The brand page names HeyGen as the renderer that fills the frame.
- LiveAvatar for the interactive avatar. HeyGen retires its v1 streaming API on October 31, 2026, together with v1 and v2; its replacement is LiveAvatar.
  - The server makes a session token with the account's `LIVEAVATAR_API_KEY` (`POST /v1/sessions/token`, FULL mode, with the configured `LIVEAVATAR_AVATAR_ID` or a library presenter's id, and `LIVEAVATAR_VOICE_ID` as the persona's voice when set). It then starts the session with that token and keeps the token server-side, for keep-alive and stop.
  - LiveAvatar has no server call that makes the avatar speak. The browser joins the LiveKit room and sends each reply as an `avatar.speak_text` command on the `agent-control` data channel. It then tells the server, which keeps the session alive. A failed keep-alive is logged rather than thrown, so a reply is never said twice.
  - A custom portrait without a configured LiveAvatar avatar keeps the animated portrait. A session that cannot start is stopped.
  - HeyGen's v1 streaming adapter and its client calls are removed.
  - The shapes follow LiveAvatar's published web SDK (`@heygen/liveavatar-web-sdk`) and two open-source clients (LiveKit's and Pipecat's LiveAvatar plugins). The docs site and API are not reachable from this build environment, so the adapter has not been run against the live API. New `liveavatar.test.ts` covers it.
  - An independent review of `f03a4e7` found the token kept server-side and the scoping unchanged. Its findings were fixed, each with a test:
    - Replies could vanish: the page marked a session live as soon as it joined the room, and LiveKit drops data sent before LiveAvatar's avatar and agent arrive, so a quick first reply went nowhere and was not said by the browser either. The page now waits for both, as LiveAvatar's SDK does (the e2e test fails without the wait), and the browser speaks until then.
    - The keep-alive came only after a reply, so a quiet visitor's session could end unnoticed. The page now sends one every minute (`{ keepAlive: true }`), and treats `session.stopped`, a departing avatar or agent, or a dropped room as the end, closing the session and speaking with the browser again.
    - Sessions the registry forgot were never stopped. They are now stopped when they expire and when the tab closes, and LiveAvatar is asked to end any session after 15 minutes.
    - Only the avatar's tracks are shown.
    - A studio presenter's id is no longer sent as a LiveAvatar avatar (it never was one): without `LIVEAVATAR_AVATAR_ID`, presenters keep their portrait.
    - LiveAvatar's refusals are logged instead of being shown to anonymous visitors.
    - Command ids fall back like the SDK's on pages without `crypto.randomUUID`.
    - The speak call reports `spoken: false` when the browser said the reply.
    - The demo-lock test now proves streaming is locked.
    - The README states what a visitor's room token allows.
- Scenes without the presenter, as HeyGen's scenes without an avatar: a scene with an image or video background can leave the presenter out (`hidePresenter: true`), and its picture or video then fills the frame.
  - It is narrated by the scene's voice, plays its clip, or holds in silence. HeyGen renders it as a v3 whole-frame `image` or `video` scene: a silent picture is held for its `duration`, and a video loops muted under the narration. D-ID refuses it.
  - The editor has a "Show the presenter" checkbox per scene. It is disabled on a colour background, and choosing a colour brings the presenter back. A hidden scene hides the presenter from the stage and drops the presenter and layout controls.
  - Copies, templates, and drafts keep it, and the video page lists it.
  - The video page also stops claiming that HeyGen applies layout, background fit, or on-screen text: since v3 they are labelled preview only.
  - New `hide-presenter.test.ts` and an e2e test, which fails when a copy drops the setting.
  - An independent review of `8ff5f52` found the HeyGen request bodies valid against the v3 schema. Its findings are fixed, the first with an e2e test:
    - Deleting a scene's uploaded image gave it a plain colour but kept "no presenter", leaving a bare colour on the stage and a render the server refused (`400`). The same happened to a copy, template, or draft whose image was gone, and when the brand kit's colour arrived late. Every way a scene's background becomes a colour now goes through one rule that brings the presenter back, and so do the server's template and backdrop clean-ups.
    - A hidden scene's presenter is no longer sent or resolved. Otherwise a custom portrait would have been prepared on HeyGen, and possibly failed the render, for a scene that shows nobody.
    - The hint says D-ID refuses such a video instead of claiming it shows the presenter.
    - The video page marks a lone hidden scene and the Avatar row when no scene, or not every scene, shows the presenter.
    - The checkbox's hint is no longer part of its accessible name.
    - The batch page says templates' scenes without the presenter are not used.
    - The docs cover the template field and the README sentence reads in order again.
- Photo avatar motion (HeyGen's Avatar IV expressiveness and motion prompt). A video can set how much a photo avatar's face moves (`expressiveness`: low, medium, or high) and its gestures in words (`motionPrompt`, up to 300 characters, one line).
  - HeyGen gets both on every scene whose presenter is a photo avatar, meaning an uploaded portrait or a HeyGen photo avatar look. It gets neither for video avatars, which refuse a motion prompt on HeyGen's default engine, or for scenes without the presenter.
  - The editor's Settings tab has a "Photo avatar motion" panel that says when no scene shows a photo avatar. Drafts, copies, templates, retries, and other sizes keep it.
  - The video page lists it. The page's emotion row now says that no renderer takes an emotion since HeyGen's v3 API.
  - New `photo-motion.test.ts` and an e2e test, which fails when a copy drops the setting.

  - An independent review of `c5a90d9` found the request bodies valid against HeyGen's v3 schema and the photo-avatar check right for every presenter that reaches HeyGen. Its findings are fixed, each with a test that fails without the fix:
    - A batch started from a template dropped the motion setting, and the batch API had no field for it. The batch API now takes `expressiveness` and `motionPrompt`, and the batch page carries them from the template like caption style and background.
    - A gestures text over 1,200 characters got zod's generic length message instead of the documented "at most 300 characters".
    - The video page no longer says "(photo avatars only)" for a HeyGen video whose scenes all leave the presenter out.
    - The editor panel no longer says "no scene shows one" while the presenter list is still loading.
    - The tests now check that retries, other sizes, batch rows, and spelled-out templates keep the setting, and the hidden-scene check is no longer vacuous. The API docs list the template fields and say other sizes keep photo avatar motion.
- Voice accent (HeyGen's `voice_settings.locale` for voices that report `support_locale`). A video can ask its voice for one of 34 accents, such as `en-GB`, `en-AU`, `es-MX`, or `fr-CA`.
  - Voices that speak several accents are marked `accents: true` in the voice list. The editor's Settings tab offers the accents of that voice's language, and is disabled with a hint when no voice in the video offers any.
  - HeyGen gets the accent on every spoken scene whose voice offers accents and speaks the accent's language, narrated pictures included. Scenes with recorded audio or silence, voices of one accent, and voices of another language keep their own.
  - Drafts, copies, templates, retries, other sizes, and batches keep it. The batch page carries a template's accent. The video page lists it.
  - New `accent.test.ts`, a voice-list check in `heygen-v3.test.ts`, and an e2e test.
  - An independent review of `48732df` found the request bodies valid against HeyGen's v3 schema and the accent kept through every path. Its findings are fixed:
    - A voice of a known language with no listed accents (Swedish, Polish, Cantonese) was treated as a voice of no single language. It was offered every accent and, as a scene voice, sent the video's English one. Such a voice is now offered none and sent none, with a unit test that fails without the fix.
    - The editor picks the accent's voice from the voices that actually speak a scene, and sends no accent that no speaking voice takes. A copy's accent kept for a voice with one accent says so, and the e2e test covers it.
    - While the voice list loads, the control no longer says that no voice offers an accent. Its hint is announced with it (`aria-describedby`), so a disabled control explains itself.
    - The hint and the video page say that HeyGen applies the accent where the voice offers it. The unit test covers recorded-audio and silent scenes, and the e2e test picks Emma itself.
    - The API docs list the voice list's `accents` field and say that the accent follows the voice's language. They also note that HeyGen documents `locale` as a free tag, so the list is unconfirmed against a live account.
- Avatar engine (HeyGen v3's `engine: { "type": "avatar_v" }`). A video can ask for Avatar V, HeyGen's higher-quality engine, in place of its default, Avatar IV.
  - HeyGen looks that list `avatar_v` in `supported_api_engines` are marked `avatarV: true`. HeyGen gets the engine on every scene whose presenter's look supports it. Other presenters, uploaded portraits, and scenes without the presenter stay on Avatar IV.
  - So do photo avatars given gestures: Avatar V refuses a motion prompt for a photo avatar it renders without a reference look, and the request cannot tell whether one exists.
  - The editor's Settings tab has an "Avatar engine" choice that says when no presenter in the video supports Avatar V, and sends nothing then. Drafts, copies, templates, retries, other sizes, and batches keep it. The video page lists it.
  - Two of the demo presenters are marked as supporting it, so the choice can be tried without a key.
  - New `avatar-engine.test.ts` and an e2e test.
  - An independent review of `4b20e03` found the engine body valid against HeyGen's v3 schema and the setting kept through every path. Its findings are fixed:
    - A photo avatar given expressiveness without gestures was sent both `expressiveness` and Avatar V, which HeyGen rejects, failing the whole render. One rule, `rendersOnAvatarV`, now keeps a photo avatar with either motion setting on Avatar IV, in the renderer and in the editor alike. The editor no longer sends Avatar V when no scene would use it. A unit test fails without the fix.
    - Avatar V can be refused where Avatar IV is not, for instance a reference look whose consent is missing, and a retry asks again. The stored error now says the video asked for Avatar V and that a copy set to Avatar IV renders.
    - The video page says Avatar V was asked for, and the API docs say that two demo presenters carry `avatarV`.
- QA pass 9, an independent check of how the last five features combine: the v3 move, scenes without the presenter, photo avatar motion, voice accent, and Avatar V.
  - It generated 864 HeyGen request bodies across every combination of those settings and scene types, and validated them against the v3 schema with no violations. All five settings survived every save path together. Its findings are fixed:
    - The editor offered and sent accents from one voice only: the first scene voice in order, even a silent one. A copy with an English and a Spanish voice offering accents showed its `es-MX` accent as "not sent" and dropped it. The editor now offers the accents of every voice that speaks a scene, ignoring clips and silent scenes, and sends an accent when any of them takes it. The hint names those voices, and the e2e test covers the two-voice copy.
    - A presenter that no scene shows (every scene hides it, or brings its own) is no longer uploaded to HeyGen and waited for before the render, with a unit test.
    - The video page's Pitch row says when the renderer does not apply it. Its Avatar engine row covers videos with no presenter and photo avatars given motion settings. The editor's Avatar V option gives the right reason when nothing renders on it.
    - The API docs say which HeyGen scene type each scene becomes, and that a narrated scene without the presenter takes the accent.
- Gestures for video avatars on Avatar V. HeyGen's v3 API takes a motion prompt for a video avatar when it renders on Avatar V, though not on Avatar IV, so `motionPrompt` now reaches those scenes too.
  - Photo avatars get it as before, on Avatar IV. Expressiveness stays photo-only.
  - The editor's motion panel says when gestures will reach a video avatar, and the video page notes it. A unit test checks that a video avatar on Avatar V gets the prompt and one on Avatar IV never does. The e2e test checks the panel's wording.
  - An independent review of `668ea50` found every body valid against HeyGen's v3 schema, and the editor following the provider's rule. Its findings are fixed:
    - The video page claimed gestures for a video with expressiveness alone. It now names them only when there are some, and only where the look supports Avatar V.
    - The panel and the row are now titled "Avatar motion". On Avatar IV, the panel says to choose Avatar V when a presenter's look would take gestures there.
    - The README and code comments no longer say gestures are for photo avatars only.
    - The unit tests now cover a video avatar on Avatar V given both settings (gestures, never expressiveness). They also cover a look that supports Avatar V left on Avatar IV (no gestures), which fails if the rule tests the look alone, and a scene's own presenter on Avatar V.
- Transparent background (HeyGen v3 `output_format: "webm"`). A one-scene video that shows the presenter can render the presenter alone over a transparent background, as a WebM with an alpha channel for compositing.
  - HeyGen's studio type has no WebM output, so such a video is sent as v3's single-avatar video (`type: "avatar"`) with the scene's voice or clip, speed, accent, motion, and engine. It is sent without the scene background, which HeyGen refuses with WebM, and without burned-in captions; the share page subtitles it.
  - The avatar must be trained with matting. HeyGen checks this and refuses other avatars with a clear error, and the look listing gives no way to know beforehand, so the editor's hint says so. D-ID refuses the setting.
  - The editor's Settings tab has a "Transparent background" checkbox for one-scene videos. The stage previews a checkerboard in place of the background. A video with more scenes keeps the choice on screen but does not send it.
  - Templates, retries, other sizes, and batches keep it. The video page shows it in the Background row.
  - New `transparent.test.ts` and an e2e test.
  - An independent review of `1a60204` found every body valid against HeyGen's v3 schema. Its findings are fixed:
    - A transparent video with default captions was treated as having them burned in, though none are sent. The share page's subtitle track stayed off by default, and the caption editor said the picture kept the original wording. Transparent videos now count as not burned in, with a unit test on the share page body.
    - The API docs said D-ID answers `400`. It answers `502` with the failed video, because the renderer refuses the request after the video is stored, as it does for its other refusals. The docs now say so.
    - The background was still looked up and uploaded though it is never sent. An uploaded image deleted since could fail a retry or a resize, and a colour HeyGen could not take failed the render. Neither happens now, with unit tests.
    - The link preview typed a HeyGen WebM as MP4, because a signed link's query hid the extension. It now reads the path, and a transparent HeyGen video is always WebM.
    - The stage's label used the hidden background's contrast on the checkerboard. The hint no longer claims which avatars are trained with matting, says photo avatars and uploaded portraits are likely refused, and names which rule stops a video.
    - A batch started from a transparent template says so and offers to use the background instead, with an e2e test.
- Short clips (HeyGen v3 AI Clipping). A finished video can be cut into short highlight clips: HeyGen picks the best moments and cuts a clip of each chosen length (30 seconds, 1 minute, or 3 minutes), framed 9:16, 16:9, or 1:1, with captions burned in or not, guided by an optional note on what to look for.
  - `POST /api/videos/:id/highlights` starts a set with a fresh link to the HeyGen render, as `POST /v3/ai-clipping`. `GET` lists the video's sets and follows any still being cut (`GET /v3/ai-clipping/:id`), renewing finished clips' signed links once they are half an hour old. `DELETE` forgets one set.
  - A length as long as the video or longer, a video not finished or in the trash, and a sixth set are refused. Starting is limited per account and per instance. D-ID has no such API. The demo renderer stands its sample video in for each clip.
  - The video page has a "Short clips" card: lengths the video is long enough for, framing, captions, and what to look for, then each set with its clips to watch and download, and its virality score. Viewers see the sets; editors make and delete them.
  - Sets are a new `highlight` store kind, deleted with the video when it is deleted for good and with the account, and part of the export. Re-run `supabase/schema.sql` so the store accepts it.
  - New `highlights.test.ts` (schema, the demo flow, refusals, deletion, the HeyGen requests and status mapping against stubbed responses, link renewal) and an e2e test. The HeyGen calls follow its API reference and are not checked against a live account.
  - An independent review of `82a5420` found the HeyGen request and status mapping as documented, the roles right, and no XSS or SSRF path through the clip links. Its findings are fixed:
    - Following sets had no limit: every listing asked HeyGen once per set still being cut, concurrent pages did not share a check, and a finished set whose check kept failing (a job HeyGen no longer has, say) asked again on every page view. Now each set is asked about at most every few seconds with one check at a time, a failed check of a finished set waits five minutes, listing is limited to 300 an hour per account, and a job HeyGen answers `404` for fails with a reason. Unit tests cover the waits and the expired job.
    - Starting a set while a check was under way could drop the new set from the card and stop following it, and a delete could briefly come back. The card now asks again after both, as the other sizes card does.
    - HeyGen's job id reached the page; the server now leaves out the renderer, its job id, and the owner.
    - Deleting a set now asks first and moves focus to the next set or the card. Download links name their clip. A finished or failed set is announced. A page left open asks again for fresh links once they are old. A failed first load is shown instead of hiding the card. A video at five sets says so instead of offering a button the server refuses.
    - A refused start no longer uses up the hour's starts. A video deleted for good while a set was being started no longer leaves the set behind. Another video's set is a `404`. Only `https` clip links are kept. A HeyGen `403` is no longer blamed on the video, and the fresh-link check has a time limit.
- Clean up a recording (HeyGen v3 filler word removal). A recording someone spoke off the cuff (a webinar, a screen recording, an interview) or a video of the library has its filler words ("um", "uh") and overlong silences cut out, and one cleaned video comes back.
  - `POST /api/cleanups` takes a library video (sent with a fresh link to the HeyGen render) or a public https link (a private or local address is refused), as `POST /v3/filler-word-removals` with the cleanup's id as the idempotency key. `GET` lists the account's cleanups and follows any still running (`GET /v3/filler-word-removals/:id`); `DELETE` forgets one.
  - A finished cleanup shows the cleaned video, how many filler words were cut, and the length before and after. A failure, an expired job, and a finished job without a video each give a reason.
  - Following jobs now goes through one shared throttle (`job-checks.ts`) for short clips and cleanups: a running job is asked about at most every few seconds with one check at a time, and a finished job's links are renewed once old, waiting five minutes between tries.
  - The new "Clean up" page has the form (a link or one of my videos) and the list. Viewers see the list; editors clean and delete. Starts and listings are rate limited; starts the studio refuses do not count. D-ID has no such API; the demo renderer stands its sample video in.
  - Cleanups are a new `cleanup` store kind, removed with the account and part of the export. Re-run `supabase/schema.sql` so the store accepts it.
  - New `cleanups.test.ts` (sources, the demo flow, refusals, the cap, the HeyGen requests and answers against stubbed responses) and an e2e test. The page is added to the accessibility, phone width, and viewer checks. The HeyGen calls follow its API reference and are not checked against a live account.
  - An independent review of `e9e5b8f` found the HeyGen request and answers as documented, the roles and the public-address guard right, and the refactor a faithful move. Its findings are fixed:
    - The page asked for the list every five seconds while a cleanup ran, so a long recording reached the listing limit after about 25 minutes and the page stopped following it for good. Now the page asks every ten seconds and only while the tab is visible, a failed check does not stop the next, and listing is limited to 1,500 an hour. The short clips card gets the same interval and limit.
    - The idempotency key was new on every request, so it protected nothing. Now a start may carry an `Idempotency-Key` header (the page sends one per start, kept until it succeeds or the source changes): the same key returns the cleanup already stored without counting against the limit, and a first try that reached HeyGen but was never stored sends HeyGen the same key. Unit and e2e tests cover the repeat.
    - A cleanup of a library video outlived the video when it was deleted for good; it now goes with it, and one started while the video was being deleted is not kept, with tests.
    - One listing renewed every old link at once; now at most five per listing, the least recently checked first, for short clips too. A link longer than 2,000 characters, or one that is not a URL, is refused. The empty list no longer shows while loading. After a start from the library the choice is cleared and focus moves to the new cleanup. The video picker says when it lists only the 200 newest. The page says "filler cuts", as HeyGen counts them, and the share removed is worked out from the two lengths. HeyGen refusals without a reason no longer read as portrait advice. The start is checked once instead of twice.
- QA pass 10, an exploratory pass in the demo build over the transparent background, short clips, and cleaning up a recording, and how they combine with trash and restore, copies, templates, other sizes, the share page, the viewer role, the account export and deletion, the keyboard, and phone width. The server rules held under every probe (lengths, the caps, private links in every form tried, idempotent repeats, roles). Its findings are fixed in `a8e9e45`:
  - At phone width the Clean up page scrolled sideways once a cleaned recording had a title of about 30 characters, and every transparent video's page did too (its Background row, or a clip set's long note, set the width of the page's single column). Both grids now have a column that can shrink; the phone width test now covers a transparent video and a long title.
  - Deleting a video for good also removes its short clips and the recordings cleaned from it, and the confirmation now says so (one video or several).
  - A button left focus on the page when a request it started was refused: a loading button was disabled outright. It now stays focusable (marked busy, ignoring clicks), on every page; the cleanup e2e test checks the focus.
  - On the Clean up page an error about a link stayed after switching to a library video, and a failed list load hid a refused start; both are shown, and a change of source clears the old one. Viewers can no longer type in the form. Sets and cleanups that finish in the same check are announced together.
  - `transparent: false` was refused; it is now a normal video, as absent is. The same idempotency key with another recording is a `409`. The account export leaves out the renderer and its job id for clip sets and cleanups. With a transparent background the editor's captions hint says the subtitles stay on the share page. The two hourly limits say when to try again.
- HeyGen Studio templates (HeyGen v3 templates API). The Templates page lists the renderer's API-ready templates (layouts made in HeyGen Studio with named slots), and each opens a form with one field per slot, starting from the template's own values: text, a link to an image, video, or audio, a presenter, a voice. Rendering makes a library video.
  - `GET /api/studio-templates` (HeyGen `GET /v3/templates`, paged, reused for two minutes), `GET /api/studio-templates/:id` (its variables and scenes), and `POST /api/studio-templates/:id/videos` (HeyGen `POST /v3/templates/:id`).
  - Each value given must be one of the template's variables, of the same type; one left out keeps the template's. Every text is sent, as HeyGen fills no text a request leaves out, and a text with no default must be given. Media links must be public https addresses.
  - The video carries the template and the values sent, a script of the scenes filled in for captions and search, and the template's aspect. It is counted and followed like any render; a retry sends the template again. "Make other sizes", saving it as a template, and the editor's copy are not offered, as the template owns the layout. Its page names the template and links back to its form.
  - Two stand-in templates in demo mode; D-ID has none. New `studio-templates.test.ts` (listing, the variables checks, the filled script, render, retry, refusals, the HeyGen requests against stubbed responses including an older field name) and an e2e test. The HeyGen calls follow its API reference and are not checked against a live account.
  - An independent review of `410a390` found the HeyGen requests, the roles, the guards, and the render path's counting as documented. Its findings are fixed:
    - Every variable came back to HeyGen as the template's GET answered it, cleaned of nothing: a presenter with no id was sent as an empty id, and a missing asset, a base64 default, or a read-only field went into every render and every retry (overriding later edits in HeyGen Studio). Now only the texts and the values given are sent, the GET is kept to the fields this studio uses, and a text with no content reads as empty (a `400` to fill in, not a crash).
    - A new picture or video dropped the template's fit and playback; it now keeps them unless given.
    - A presenter or voice given is checked against the template's own and what the account may use (`400` instead of a failed render).
    - Saving another account's template video as a template answered `400` instead of `404`.
    - Closing the form now returns focus to its card, clears the `?studio=` link, and an error can be closed; a viewer sees why the form is read only; link fields are `url` inputs; a template's detail is reused for a minute.
  - The review also found an older leak, fixed here: the renderer lists the looks made in its account for any account (photo avatars made from uploaded portraits, presenters drawn from a description) to every account sharing its key, so they showed as library presenters to everyone. They are now left out of every listing and refused by id for any other account. The unit test that expected another account to see such a look now expects the opposite.
- Lip sync (HeyGen v3 lip sync). A new Lip sync page puts new audio on a video and has the speaker's mouth redrawn to match: a line re-recorded after the shoot, a voice-over replaced, a dub of one's own. `2664eb1`
  - The footage is a finished library video (sent as a freshly signed link) or a public `https` link; the audio is an audio clip (uploaded on the page, converted to WAV in the browser, or one saved for an audio scene, uploaded to HeyGen once as an asset and reused) or a public `https` link. The quality is fast (the default) or precise.
  - `POST /api/lipsyncs`, `GET /api/lipsyncs`, `DELETE /api/lipsyncs/:id`, built like cleanups: the studio's own id, an `Idempotency-Key` that makes a retried start return the same job (and is passed to HeyGen), checks before the hourly limit counts, at most five renderer checks per listing, links renewed when they get old, at most 50 per account, gone with their library video when it is deleted for good, and in the account export without the renderer's job id. The delete-for-good confirmation now names lip-synced copies too.
  - The demo renderer finishes a few seconds later with its sample video; D-ID has none. New `lipsyncs.test.ts` (what is accepted, demo flow, library and clip sources, retries by key, deletion with the video, the cap and export, the HeyGen requests and answers against stubbed responses, and a renderer without it); an e2e test uploads a clip, syncs a library video at the precise quality, checks the key, the listing, a refused private link, and deletion; the page is in the phone-width, accessibility, and viewer checks.
  - An independent review of `2664eb1` found the HeyGen requests, owner isolation, roles, link checks, and clean-up with the video as documented, and nothing blocking. Its findings are fixed:
    - An uploaded file was always converted to 16 kHz mono WAV, which is fine for a scene HeyGen voices but here becomes the video's whole soundtrack; an MP3 (up to 3 MB and 90 seconds) is now kept as it is, its length read in the browser, and the page says what happens to other files. An e2e test uploads a real MP3 and checks it is stored and served as `audio/mpeg`.
    - A clip over 90 seconds was refused with advice to split it across scenes; it now suggests a link to the full track.
    - A clip list still loading when an upload finished could land without the new clip, leaving it chosen but not shown; the list is asked again after an upload.
    - A start sent again with the same key while HeyGen was still working on the first came back as a raw `500` naming HeyGen's URL; it is now a `409` to try again (for cleanups too).
    - A refused start kept its key, so trying again after fixing the cause (topping up, say) could get the same refusal back from HeyGen; a refusal now starts a new key, and only a lost answer keeps it (for cleanups too).
    - The test that a failure drops the old link held no link to drop; it now does, and fails if the link is kept. The docs now say a failed clip upload or a start HeyGen refuses counts against the hourly limit; link fields are `url` inputs.
    - Not changed: a clip whose HeyGen asset HeyGen no longer knows (after the API key is changed) is refused rather than uploaded again. HeyGen asset ids do not expire, and a key change strands every cached upload (portraits, backgrounds, audio scenes) alike, so it is not handled for lip syncs alone.
- New looks (HeyGen v3 Look Packs). A photo avatar's tile on the Avatars page has a "New looks" button: choose one of eight packs (four style packs that change the wardrobe and palette, four role packs that dress and set the presenter for a job), the pack's women's or men's looks, and a landscape or portrait frame, and HeyGen makes about five looks around the avatar's face.
  - `POST /api/look-packs`, `GET /api/look-packs`, `DELETE /api/look-packs/:id`. The avatar is made into a HeyGen photo avatar first if no render did so (one HeyGen is still preparing is a `409` to retry), then HeyGen gets `POST /v3/avatars/looks` with the avatar's look as the identity reference and the pack's template for the chosen gender. Built like lip syncs and cleanups: the studio's own id, a client `Idempotency-Key` passed to HeyGen, checks before the hourly limit counts, at most 20 packs per account, and in the account export without the renderer's look ids.
  - Each look is followed like a drawn presenter (`GET /v3/avatars/looks/:id`); once ready it is kept as a photo avatar of the account (its picture downloaded once through the public-address guard, named after the avatar and the pack, with the avatar's gender and default voice), rendering as the look with nothing to upload. Its id is derived from the pack and the look, so a check repeated elsewhere never makes a second one. Forgetting a pack keeps its presenters.
  - Fixed on the way, a gap in the privacy fix of `5cdfc77`: looks the studio made were left out of the shared listing only while their avatar or portrait was stored, so deleting a photo avatar, or the account that drew a presenter, handed its look to every account. Every look the studio makes (photo avatars, drawn presenters, the looks of packs) is now also kept as a `madelook` record with only its id and no owner, never deleted or exported.
  - Demo mode makes five drawn stand-ins at once; D-ID has none. New `lookpacks.test.ts` (what is accepted, the demo flow and the avatars it makes once, retries by key, the cap and the export, looks hidden after an avatar, a portrait, or an account is deleted, the HeyGen requests against stubbed responses, and a renderer without it); an e2e test dresses a photo avatar of no stated gender in the men's Healthcare pack in portrait and checks the request, the key, the five presenters, and forgetting the pack.
  - An independent review of `83e237b` found the HeyGen request and all sixteen template ids as documented, and no way for one account to restyle, list, or render with another's looks through the normal paths, but three blocking gaps in the privacy record, all fixed here:
    - A drawn presenter's look lived only on its portrait job, which is cleared a day later without a record, so the look was handed to everyone a day after it was drawn. A look is now recorded the moment it is drawn, and again when its job is cleared.
    - The store's `list` read one answer of PostgREST's, at most 1,000 rows, so past 1,000 records (or 1,000 avatars across accounts) the oldest looks were listed to everyone again; this was true of every listing in the app. `list` now reads page by page, ordered by time then id so no row is skipped or read twice, with a test across three pages.
    - A photo avatar deleted while HeyGen was making its look (an upload and a wait of some seconds, on its first render or first look pack) left that look unrecorded. The look is now recorded as soon as it is made.
  - And these, also fixed:
    - One look whose check failed (HeyGen busy, or a look HeyGen lost) stopped the whole pack's check, so the looks already finished were downloaded and written again on every check, undoing a rename or bringing back a deleted presenter, and a lost look kept the pack polling forever. Each look is now checked on its own, a lost one ends as failed, and a presenter is written only once.
    - The record kept every value of a deleted avatar's provider ids, which for D-ID is the hosted address of the user's portrait; only renderer looks are kept now.
    - A start HeyGen answers `409` for (the same key still being worked on) made the page drop its key, so a retry could pay for a second pack; such an answer keeps the key, on the Lip sync and Clean up pages too.
    - Forgetting the last pack lost the focus; it moves to the New photo avatar button. Looks that became ready were announced by a count, which a pack forgotten in the same check could hide; they are now announced by name, once each. The start route has 60 seconds, as a first start may make the photo avatar first. Forgetting a pack records its looks again. The docs now say which starts count against the hourly limit and that each made presenter carries its look as any photo avatar does.
    - Not changed: `GET /api/avatars` returns every photo avatar's picture as a data URL, so many large ones can make the answer large; uploads already did, and packs add up to five pictures of at most 3 MB each. Looks whose avatars were deleted before this commit had no record and are not backfilled.
- QA pass 11, an exploratory browser pass over HeyGen templates, Clean up, Lip sync, and New looks in demo mode, with workspaces, a viewer, phone width, the keyboard, retries, and sources deleted while jobs run. Fixed, each with a test:
  - The five looks of a photo avatar with a long name were all named exactly like the avatar, as the pack and number were cut off at 80 characters; a long name is now shortened instead, so `· Stylish business-casual 3` always survives (a flag or an emoji is never split). The "(cleaned)" and "(lip-synced)" marks on a long video title were cut the same way and are kept now too.
  - A HeyGen template render retried after a lost answer rendered (and counted) a second video. The form now sends an `Idempotency-Key` that fixes the new video's id, so the retry returns the video already made; a new key is made when the form changes or the render fails. The editor's ordinary Generate has no such key yet: a lost answer there is still a second render if retried.
  - A viewer was offered Rename, Delete, and default-voice controls on photo avatars (the server refused them), and Delete on a video's page; they are hidden now.
  - The New looks panel stayed open for a photo avatar deleted from its tile, and starting then said "Only your own photo avatars can be given new looks"; the panel closes when its avatar is deleted on the page, and the message says the avatar no longer exists (as it does for one deleted in another tab). Deleting a photo avatar moves the focus to the next one's controls, or to the New photo avatar button.
  - Uploading audio on the Lip sync page disabled the button, dropping a keyboard user's focus; it is busy instead. A start refused for a deleted audio clip now clears the choice and asks for the clips again.
  - A cleanup or lip sync that left the list with its video (deleted for good) vanished silently, the status line still saying it was on its way; its leaving is announced now.
  - Smaller: Escape closes the template form; template messages name a slot as the form labels it ("Cover image", not `cover_image`); a request that gets no answer at all says the server could not be reached instead of the browser's "Failed to fetch"; a look pack says how many of its presenters were deleted since; a long unbroken title breaks to fit the video page at phone width.
  - Not changed: each photo avatar's provider ids, its renderer look among them, still reach its own account (the API and the export), as documented; another account cannot use them.
  - An independent review of `990f0e4` found two blocking faults in the retry keys, both fixed here: the new plain message for a request with no answer at all made it look like a refusal, so all four keyed pages (template renders, Clean up, Lip sync, New looks) dropped their key after a lost answer, the case the key exists for (now only a 4xx answer is a refusal, with a client test); and a template render retried while the first was still being sent, or after its function died, rendered twice, as the key was checked only against stored videos (the row is now reserved before HeyGen is asked, a retry meanwhile is told the render is still being sent, and HeyGen gets the video's id as its own key). Also fixed: the same key with another title or captions, or for a trashed video, is a `409`; a replay records no second activity; the key tests now count renders, so they fail if a second one happens; a check landing during the page's own delete no longer announces it as deleted elsewhere; the title on the video page breaks anywhere so a long unbroken word fits a phone; "since deleted" counts only presenters that were listed and are gone; Escape on the template form does nothing while an input method composes or from a text box.
- Digital twins (HeyGen v3 digital twin). A "Digital twins" section on the Avatars page trains a presenter from footage of a real person: a public link to one person speaking to camera, a name, an optional gender, and the account's word that the person agreed. HeyGen trains a look from the footage, and the person records their consent on HeyGen's page, from a link the account copies and sends them (it lasts a day; a new one can be made after it expires or a refusal). Once trained and consented to, the twin is kept as a photo avatar of the account.
  - `POST /api/twins`, `GET /api/twins`, `POST /api/twins/:id/consent`, `DELETE /api/twins/:id`; built like the other renderer jobs: the studio's own id, a client `Idempotency-Key` passed to HeyGen, checks before the hourly limit counts, training and consent followed at most every few seconds and five twins per listing, at most ten per account, and in the account export without the renderer's ids or the picture. The twin's look is recorded as one the studio made from the moment it starts, so it is never listed to another account, also after the twin or the account is deleted. The presenter is written once (its id derived from the twin), so a rename or delete is never undone.
  - Demo mode trains a drawn stand-in at once, and consent counts as given a few seconds after the link is made (the link is the studio's own public `/demo-consent` page, which says so). D-ID has none. New `twins.test.ts` (what is accepted, the demo flow with consent required before the presenter exists, link expiry and renewal, retries by key, the cap and the export, privacy of the look, the HeyGen requests and refusals against stubbed responses, and a renderer without it); an e2e test trains a twin, checks the request and the key, gets the consent link, waits for consent, finds the presenter, and forgets the twin; the viewer test checks there is no New digital twin button.
  - Not included yet: uploading the footage from the computer (HeyGen takes links up to 32 MB, and a request to the studio is limited to 4.5 MB, so an upload would have to go straight from the browser to HeyGen's storage).
  - An independent review of `d9bc2d1` found three blocking faults, fixed in the following commit: the voice HeyGen clones from the footage (`default_voice_id`) was dropped, so it sat in HeyGen's account voices, which are listed to every account, and was never deleted; a start whose answer was lost after HeyGen made the twin left its look and voice unknown, so the look could be listed to every account; and the Forget dialog said "Its training is not kept", which was not true.
    - The voice is kept with the twin and recorded as one the studio made (like its looks), so no other account's list offers it; once the twin is ready it becomes the account's own voice ("Name (twin)") and the presenter's default voice. Forgetting a twin that never became a presenter deletes the clone at HeyGen (`DELETE /v3/voices/:id`, a `404` counts as deleted), as does deleting the account; deleting the twin's voice from the Voices page deletes it at HeyGen too.
    - The twin is stored before HeyGen is asked, with its id as the key, and an answer lost on the way back is asked for again with the same key (by a retry after two minutes, or the next listing), so HeyGen answers with the twin it made; one unanswered for a day is marked failed, and a refusal keeps nothing. No digital twin of the key's own HeyGen account is listed in the shared library any more, recorded or not (HeyGen's preset presenters, some of them digital twins, still are).
    - The dialog says what stays (the presenter and voice it became), that an unused cloned voice is deleted, and that HeyGen keeps the trained twin, never offered to anyone else.
    - Also fixed: consent is asked about once the twin is trained, so a workspace HeyGen lets skip consent is ready without a link; a character HeyGen no longer has ends the twin as failed; a twin's failure says "train a twin from that footage", not "draw that presenter"; the consent write cannot bring back a twin forgotten meanwhile; viewers and the export no longer get the consent link; only new links count against the hourly limit; the page stops asking once a link has expired, and announces a refused consent; the New digital twin button is hidden when the renderer has none; `/demo-consent` is a `404` with a real renderer, and the demo's consent follows the stored link time rather than one server instance's memory.
    - Tests: the voice kept out of other accounts' lists and kept, deleted, or deleted with the account as described, the lost answer asked again with the same key (with and without a client key, and given up after a day), a refusal keeping nothing, an unrecorded HeyGen twin never listed, the link hidden from viewers, and a lost character; the e2e test checks the twin's voice and the dialog, the public `/demo-consent` check moved to the signed-out password-mode test, and the viewer test waits for the twins section before checking the button is absent.
  - Running the full suite on that fix found that it also hid HeyGen's preset presenters that are digital twins (the Avatar V listing test); `7198998` hides only the key's own account's twins. An independent review of both then found nothing blocking, but five gaps, fixed in the next commit with tests: a start whose answer was lost and that was then forgotten (or its account deleted, or left unlisted for a day) never learned its voice, which HeyGen then listed to every account; HeyGen's voice listing now leaves out the default voice of every digital twin look of the key's own account, and forgetting such a twin or deleting its account asks HeyGen once more first so the voice is deleted. A lost start was asked for again on every listing, every ten seconds per open tab, for a day; now at most every five minutes. Every voice listing read all twins and all made looks of every account; made voices have their own small table now (`madevoice`). A twin's voice took one of the five places for cloned voices; it no longer does. The kept voice had no language, gender, or preview; it takes them from HeyGen. The Forget dialog no longer promises what a renderer without voices of its own cannot do, and a consent still under review after its link expired is asked about once a minute instead of never.
- Voice cloning with HeyGen voices (HeyGen v3 voice clone). With HeyGen as the renderer, and so the voice provider, the Voices page's Clone your voice now works, as it did only with ElevenLabs: HeyGen clones from one WAV or MP3 recording, so the page joins the recorded or uploaded samples in order into one 16 kHz mono WAV (at most 90 seconds, which fits one request) and says so; the server uploads it as an asset, clones it with background noise removed, and waits about ten seconds for it.
  - A clone HeyGen could not make is refused with its reason and deleted there; one still being made is kept; a complete one keeps HeyGen's preview. A plan without cloning or HeyGen's clone limit is a `400` with HeyGen's reason. Deleting the voice, or the account, deletes it at HeyGen.
  - HeyGen lists the key's clones with its own voices to every account, so every clone the studio makes (with any provider) is recorded as made and left out of other accounts' lists, as a twin's voice is; the operator's own HeyGen voices are listed as before.
  - Not included: HeyGen's voice design returns voices of its catalog rather than new ones, so Design a voice stays with ElevenLabs. A clone kept while HeyGen was still making it is not asked about again (it has no preview until it is cloned again, and a render with one HeyGen later failed fails with HeyGen's reason).
  - An independent review of `8c37740` found one blocking gap and four more, fixed in the following commit with tests: the clone was recorded only after it had been waited for, so for up to ten seconds HeyGen listed it to every account, who could render with it; it is now recorded the moment HeyGen names it. A clone whose answer was lost was never recorded at all and was listed to everyone for good (ElevenLabs keeps clones out of its own listing; HeyGen does not); every clone is now named at HeyGen with the studio's mark, marked voices are never listed, and a lost one is found by its mark and deleted. HeyGen's limit on clones for the whole shared key, or a plan without cloning, was shown to an account as its own to fix; it now says to ask the studio's operator. HeyGen's rate limit was a `500` with HeyGen's answer in it; it is a `429`. The page decoded every sample in full before cutting at 90 seconds; samples past the cut are no longer decoded. The language given is now passed to HeyGen. The e2e test now joins two different samples and checks their order and the 90-second cut.
  - New `voice-clone-heygen.test.ts` (offered from one recording, the requests, waiting until complete, privacy from other accounts, deletion with the voice and the account, a clone still being made, one HeyGen failed, and HeyGen's refusals, against stubbed responses); an e2e test checks that a provider cloning from one recording is sent the samples joined into one WAV.
- QA pass 12, an exploratory browser pass over Digital twins and voice cloning in demo mode, with a viewer, phone width, the keyboard, retries, refused inputs, the export, and axe. Nothing blocking; fixed, each with a test:
  - A workspace viewer was offered Rename and Delete on the space's own voices (the server refused them); they show only to editors, and only once the role is known, as do the cloner, New digital twin, Forget, and the consent row, which flashed before.
  - A twin that became ready while the keyboard was in its consent row dropped the focus to the page; it moves to the twin.
  - Cloning a voice said nothing and dropped the focus; it is announced and the focus returns to the name. Removing a sample moves the focus to the next one, or to Upload, and the same file can be picked again.
  - The cloner took more than five samples across picks, then failed with a raw "Too big" message; it keeps five and says why. One 8 MB file failed with "Request body must be JSON", as the request was cut at the platform's size: samples are now 3 MB in total (10 MB a file when they are joined into one recording, which is cut at 90 seconds first), the limits have messages written for people, and a body too large to read says so.
  - The voice cloner's and designer's headings are `h2`, so the page's headings go in order (axe).
  - Smaller: Get the consent link is busy while it is asked for, so a double click asks once; copying the link again is announced again; the reason Train the twin is disabled is shown under it; a twin's voice name keeps within what a rename allows when the name has emoji; a footage link with a name and password in it is refused rather than kept and shown back; the voice rename and delete buttons sit after the name, so a long name truncates before them at phone width.
  - Not changed: the presenter a twin becomes carries its renderer look id to its own account, as every photo avatar does (documented); demo mode's copy names HeyGen, as it stands in for it.
  - An independent review of `b223c27` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit: the focus move for a twin that became ready also took the focus of someone who never had it (scrolling the library with the mouse, the page jumped back to the twin); it now moves only when the keyboard was in that twin's removed row. A recording skipped the 3 MB total and could be dropped silently past five samples; it is checked and refused with a reason like a file. Repeated messages (a second removal) are announced again, and recordings are numbered so two never share a name. A click on one twin's consent button no longer blocks another's. The cloner waits for the provider to be known, so a HeyGen account never sees the 3 MB rule for a file it would join. A twin voice name is cut by code points as a last resort. The docs say a request over the platform's limit is a `413` on Vercel.
- Background music (HeyGen's music catalog and HyperFrames). A Music page finds a track by describing it in plain words (or one of three suggestions), lets each match be heard, and mixes the chosen one under a finished library video at one of four volumes; the result is listed to watch and download.
  - `GET /api/music/tracks`, `POST /api/music`, `GET /api/music`, `DELETE /api/music/:id`; built like the other renderer jobs: the studio's own id, a client `Idempotency-Key` passed to HeyGen, checks before the hourly limit counts, the job followed at most every few seconds and five per listing with its link renewed, at most fifty per account, gone with its video and the account, and in the export without the renderer's ids. The track is looked up again when the mix starts, as the catalog's links are signed for a short time.
  - HeyGen's studio videos take no music track, so the mix is a HyperFrames render: the studio sends a one-page project (`src/lib/music-mix.ts`) with the video full frame and its own sound, and the track under it at the chosen gain, laid end to end to the video's length and faded in and out. Videos with a transparent background, no recorded length, or over 30 minutes are refused.
  - Demo mode has three short tracks of its own (`public/demo/music-*.mp3`) and stands the sample video in for the mix a few seconds later. D-ID has none.
  - New `music.test.ts` (the page sent: segments, fades, escaping, dimensions; the demo flow; what is refused; retries by key; the cap, the export, and deletion with the video and the account; the HeyGen catalog and HyperFrames requests against stubbed responses, with the zip unpacked and read; a renderer without it); an e2e test searches, hears, chooses, mixes, waits, and deletes; the viewer test and the WCAG scan include the page.
  - An independent review of `b1e0e8b` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit: asking HeyGen about a render had no time limit, so one stuck answer could hold every listing on the instance; it has ten seconds, as the other checks do. A start looked its track up again in HeyGen's catalog before any limit counted, so made-up track ids could search without end on the shared key; each start now counts against the account's searches. The start's three calls could run past the route's 30 seconds; the route has 60 and the search eight. A deleted mix left its render, a full copy of the footage, in HeyGen's account; deleting a mix, its video for good, or the account now deletes the render too. Smaller: a track under nine seconds under a long video stopped partway (a cap of 200 copies; now as many as the video needs), the render is asked for at 25 frames a second and high quality, the docs say what the volumes mean, the failed and lost render paths have tests, and the delete e2e checks where the focus goes.
- Cinematic shots (HeyGen's Cinematic Avatar). A Cinematic page takes a description of a shot (the setting, the action, the camera; three examples to start from), one to three of the account's presenters, a shape, a quality, and a length of 4 to 15 seconds or HeyGen's choice, with an option for HeyGen to enrich a short description, and starts the shot as a library video; its page shows the description and the presenters, and "Make the shot again" fills the form with it.
  - The shot is a video like any (shared, downloaded, translated, counted against the allowance, followed until ready), carrying `cinematic` with what it was made from; it has no script, voice, or captions, so the caption download and editor, subtitles, other sizes, the editor's copy, and saving it as a template are not offered, and the server refuses all but the download (an empty file), and the share page shows no transcript. A retry sends the same shot again.
  - The presenters are the account's own (another account's reads as not found), photo avatars prepared at HeyGen first as for any render. A client key fixes the video's id with its row reserved before HeyGen is asked, shared with template renders (`startReserved`), and HeyGen gets the video's id as its key. HeyGen refuses a `callback_url` for these, so none is sent.
  - New `cinematic.test.ts` (the request's limits; the demo flow; only the account's presenters; retry and every refusal; the key; the HeyGen request with a photo avatar's look and a library look, automatic length, and a refused start, against stubbed responses) and an e2e test (the presenter limit, the request sent, the video page, and making the shot again); `/cinematic` joins the viewer, phone-width, and axe page lists, as does `/music` the phone-width one.
  - An independent review of `d5310bd` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit: "Make the shot again" for a shot whose presenter was since deleted kept that presenter chosen but unlisted, so the form could not be sent; it is now left out, with a notice naming it. A chosen presenter past the sixty listed could not be unticked, and is now always listed. A shared shot showed an empty transcript box. A folder name could be longer than the library allows anywhere else. The same key with another length, quality, title, or enrichment returned the first shot, and now is a `409`; a retry names the presenters as they are now; a shot's description is found by the library search; the filled-in notice is shown on the page, an example asks before replacing a written description, the reason the button is disabled is shown, and the character count is no longer part of the description's name. A reserved row of another account (an id collision) is refused rather than returned. New tests cover another account's HeyGen look and a look the studio made, passed straight as presenters; every compared setting of a key and a trashed key; the search; the share page; renamed presenters on retry; and a renderer without shots.
- QA pass 13, a browser pass over background music and cinematic shots (keyboard, names, phone width, viewers, bad requests, and how they meet the rest of the library). Nothing blocking; fixed:
  - The Music page listed only the videos finished when it opened; it now asks again while renders are in flight, and when the tab comes back. Mixes of one video had controls of the same name, and videos of one title identical choices; mixes are named with their track and day, and videos with their shape and day. The download link downloads.
  - Starting a shot or a template render twice at once with one key logged two "created" activities (a replay was told apart by time); only the request that starts the render records it now. The same key with another description or title (music) or folder (shots) returned the first start instead of a `409`.
  - The Cinematic page put the presenters after the button that needs one; they now come first in reading and Tab order. "Make the shot again" keeps the shot's folder, and says when the video to fill in is not a shot or cannot be opened. A shot's page no longer offers Translate (there is nothing to dub), and its share page no longer renders an empty paragraph.
  - Messages: a deleted presenter is named rather than shown as an id when a shot is retried; a missing video, a long title, and a long folder name read as the forms say them.
  - An independent review of `489e5fb` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit: a title changed on the Music page after a lost answer was sent with the old key, now a `409`, so the title now renews the key like the other choices; the page's check for newly finished videos stops after ten minutes (a start that never came back stays queued); and the start callback is kept out of the stored video on every render path, not only shots and template renders.
- Voice changer (HeyGen's instant voices and speech to speech). A Voice changer page makes an instant voice from one recording of a person, with a box confirming they agreed to it, and speaks a recording again in one of the account's voices, keeping its words and timing, optionally cleaning up background noise first; the results are listed to play and download.
  - Voices and recordings are the account's: the voice's name at HeyGen carries the studio's mark and its id is noted as the studio's, so HeyGen's voice listings never offer it to another account; it is deleted at HeyGen with its record and with the account (before anything else goes, so a voice HeyGen would not delete stops the deletion). Recordings are the account's audio clips, uploaded once as HeyGen assets; the upload helpers the Lip sync page used moved to `src/lib/clip-upload.ts` for both pages.
  - HeyGen answers a conversion in up to two and a half minutes, longer than a start waits: one not done in 45 seconds is followed by asking again with the same idempotency key (HeyGen returns the result without charging twice), given up after half an hour so a key HeyGen no longer keeps is never sent again, and never asked about once finished.
  - New `voice-changer.test.ts` (the requests; the demo flow; what is not the account's; a voice not ready; the cap; retries by key; the account's deletion and export; the HeyGen requests against stubbed responses, with the mark, the asset, the key kept across checks, a slow and a busy conversion, a refusal, a failed and a gone voice; giving up) and an e2e test of the page; `/voice-changer` joins the viewer, phone-width, and axe page lists.
  - An independent review of `a0dac42` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit: the studio's mark on a voice's name was random, so a start retried with the same key after a lost answer sent another request, which HeyGen refuses, leaving the first voice unrecorded; the mark is now drawn from the key, and HeyGen's `conflict` is told apart from a start still being sent. A conversion unanswered for half an hour was given up without asking, though HeyGen keeps a key's finished answer for a day; it is now asked once more and given up only if still running, and never asked once a day old. A server error or dropped connection at the start deleted a conversion that might still be running, and the page then sent a new key; both now keep following it with the same key. The start's wait is counted from the request's arrival, so a slow first upload (now bounded to 30 seconds) cannot run it past the platform's limit, and the page asks for the list again after an error. HeyGen's 401, 403, and 429 read as messages, not raw errors; the two upload buttons have names of their own, and the disabled buttons say what is missing.
- QA pass 14, the voice changer driven in a browser against the demo renderer (seven should-fix findings, no blockers):
  - An error from either card showed only at the top of the page, out of sight of the button pressed; each card now shows its own, and the top banner is kept for the lists.
  - A clip deleted elsewhere, or a voice deleted in another tab, stayed chosen and was sent; the choice is now cleared when the clip or voice leaves the list, and a "no longer available" answer clears the voice and reloads.
  - The chosen clip can be deleted from the page itself (it asks first); a clip or voice already gone counts as deleted rather than an error.
  - A good upload left an earlier upload's error showing; it is now cleared.
  - While a voice is still being made, the Voice field said "Make a voice below first"; it now says the voice is being made.
  - Two instant voices of one name (case and spaces aside) could not be told apart in the picker; a second is refused with a 409.
  - Account deletion's confirmation now names instant voices and recordings among what is deleted.
  - An independent review of `ef7d32a` found one blocking problem; fixed in the following commit. The new e2e assertions on `role="alert"` also matched Next's route announcer (an alert in a shadow root), so they could not pass; they are now scoped to the card.
