Should-fix items: the "good upload clears the message" step was already cleared by an earlier choice (it now uploads with the message still showing); the delete-clip confirmation said scenes keep the clip, though a scene or draft still using it needs another; and a failed voice, never offered, blocked its own name. Nits: clearing from one card cleared the other card's message too; the focus was lost when the delete button went with its clip (it now moves to the choice), and the button was live during an upload.
- Text spoken in an instant voice: the Voice changer page has a "Speak text in a voice" card (a ready voice, up to 2,000 characters of text, an optional title), and the result is kept with the recordings in other voices, marked "from text", to play and download.
  - `POST /api/voice-changer/speech` (thirty an hour per account, ninety per instance) stores the record before asking HeyGen Voice's text to speech (`POST /v3/models/audio/tts`, `model: "heygen-voice-1"`) with the record's id as the `Idempotency-Key` and waits for most of a minute; a text HeyGen refuses is a `400` with its reason, and nothing is kept. A client key makes a retried start return the same text, and a key used for a recording is refused for text and the other way round.
  - Recording and text share the voice checks, the cap of 50, the export, and deletion; the HeyGen request and its refusals share one mapping. The demo renderer stands its sample in, its length as the text would take to read.
  - Unit tests for the demo flow, keys, refusals, titles, export, and the HeyGen request against stubbed responses (a slow answer dropped, a retry with the same key, a refusal, a cut-off start given up unasked); the voice changer e2e test speaks a text in the voice it made.
  - An independent review of `e67738a` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. A text not done in time was followed by asking HeyGen's text to speech again every few seconds for half an hour, though HeyGen documents a key's replay only for speech to speech, so each ask could be paid for; a text not spoken within the minute is now dropped with a `502` to try again, and one left marked as being spoken (its request cut off) is given up after 90 seconds without asking. Also: a voice gone from one card no longer clears the other card's choice; editing the text or title clears its card's message; titles are cut by characters as a reader counts them, and a text of punctuation alone keeps its first characters; the card shows only when the renderer can speak text (`speech` in the listing); the e2e test finds the recording's card by its title and checks the text's row for the renderer's fields; and the docs say which keys conflict.
- QA pass 15, text spoken in an instant voice driven in a browser against the demo renderer (four should-fix findings, no blockers):
  - A voice gone while chosen cleared its choice and disabled the button under the keyboard's focus, which fell to the page; the focus now moves to the voice choice (in either card).
  - A message in one card was cleared by a choice or start in another (one message slot, cleared unconditionally); each card now clears only its own.
  - The character count failed contrast (2.6:1) and was hidden from screen readers, so a paste cut at 2,000 characters went unannounced; it is now readable text tied to the field, and says so, politely announced, at the limit.
  - Nits: a whole text's default title keeps no closing punctuation ("Hi there in Narrator"); the empty list no longer says "on the left", which is above on a phone; the card's fields wait while the text is being spoken. Left as they are: the shared `Field` puts its hint in the label's accessible name (app-wide, a change of its own), and two starts with one key at the same instant can both reach the renderer, as for every keyed start in the studio.
  - An independent review of `adc7249` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. A region made live in the same update as its text is seldom announced, so the limit is now announced by a status region always on the page; a start in one card had stopped clearing the lists' message, which could then stay up after everything else succeeded; the count no longer says a paste was cut when exactly 2,000 characters were typed; a default title also drops a closing ellipsis, quote or bracket, so it is not taken for a cut; and the e2e test checks that the focus goes to the voice choice when the voice is gone. Still as before: the cards share one message, so a failure in one card while another is busy shows in its own card and replaces the other's.
- Sound effects in a video with music: the Music page searches HeyGen's sound-effect catalog (`GET /v3/audio/sounds?type=sound_effects`, `?type=effects` on `/api/music/tracks`) and places up to five effects, each heard first and set to play from a moment of the video (to a tenth of a second), with the background track.
  - `POST /api/music` takes `effects: [{ trackId, query, atSec }]`; an effect at or past the video's end is a `400` before anything is asked, each description is searched again once at the start (counted against the account's searches) for its fresh link, and one no longer offered is a `400`. The mix keeps the effects with their names, earliest first; a start retried with the same key and the same effects in any order is the same mix, and other effects are a `409`.
  - The HyperFrames page plays each effect once from its moment at full volume, cut where the video ends (one of unknown length plays to the end), and leaves out one past the end; the music stays faded in and out under them.
  - The demo renderer has three short effects of its own (whoosh, chime, pop). Unit tests for the page, the demo flow (order, keys, refusals, the search count) and the HeyGen requests against stubbed responses; the music e2e test adds two effects, catches one past the end, removes one, and checks the request and the listing.
  - An independent review of `d299754` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. The track's and each effect's catalog searches ran one after another, each allowed 8 seconds, so a slow catalog could take a start past the function's minute; they are now counted first and asked together. The "plays at" field rewrote what was being typed (an emptied field became "0", "1.25" became "1.3" mid-entry, and a number field may read "1." as empty); it is now a decimal text field kept as typed and read to a tenth, an empty one asks for a moment and holds "Add music" back, and the value is tidied when the field is left. Also: moments read to a tenth on both page and server (59.96 seconds is 1:00.0, not 0:60.0, and 40.06 on a 40.04-second video is no longer "0:40 past 0:40"); one effect twice at one moment under two descriptions is the same start in either order; the past-the-end warning is announced; `MusicTrack` has its comment back; and the API reference names the demo effects and the millisecond the server keeps.
- QA pass 16, sound effects on the Music page driven in a browser against the demo renderer (two blocking findings, four should-fix):
  - The chosen effects' names were cut to "W.." at 1280 pixels (the "plays at" field kept the form's full width, as `w-full` outranked `w-20`), and results cut theirs beside the player; the field is now its own narrow width, and names have a line of their own in results and wrap in the list. On a phone, a long name widened the effects fieldset (a fieldset is as wide as its content unless told otherwise) and the page scrolled sideways; both fieldsets now shrink with the card, checked at 390 pixels in the e2e test.
  - An effect "no longer offered" cleared the music's track list instead (the two messages share their words); each now drops its own search results and sends the focus to its own search field.
  - A very long number read as "1.85e+22:20.0" and "Infinity"; the field now takes up to half an hour of seconds, reads a decimal comma as a point, and says when an entry is not a number of seconds. An invalid field points at the warning (`aria-describedby`), and one left empty or unreadable is announced when it is left.
  - A search that found no effects said so only to screen readers, and a search error showed at the top of the page, off-screen on a phone; both now show under the effects' search field, the error tied to it.
  - Nits: the warning names every effect at or after the end with its moment, and says "at or after" (a moment at exactly the end read "0:20.0 past 0:20.0"); two of one effect are numbered in their names; the warning is announced when the late effects change rather than at every key; the music's search button is "Search music"; and the API says a moment must be within the first 30 minutes. Left as before: the shared `Field` puts its hint in the field's accessible name (app-wide).
  - An independent review of `a802dab` found one blocking problem, in the e2e test (an "Add music" enabled check followed at once by a disabled one, which could never pass); fixed in the following commit. Also: the effect search's error was pulled up over its hint; the warning now gives every kind of problem, each in its own sentence, since every field it concerns points at it; the note is emptied once nothing is late, so the same warning is announced again if it comes back; an entry of leading zeros ("00001") is read like any other number; an effect "no longer offered" is said beside the effects, where the focus goes, and the message says to remove it; the half-hour limit is one constant shared by the page and the server; "Removed …" names a numbered effect by its number; and the API reference gives the half-hour limit.
- A recording or text spoken in one of the account's voices can be lip-synced onto a video: each finished result on the Voice changer page links to the Lip sync page with it chosen ("In one of my voices"), and `POST /api/lipsyncs` takes `voiceChangeId` as the new audio (exactly one of `clipId`, `voiceChangeId`, `audioUrl`).
  - The recording must be the account's and finished; one still being spoken, failed, another account's or deleted is a `400` before HeyGen is asked. HeyGen is sent the recording's own link (its output, so not checked as a public link), and the lip sync keeps the recording's id and title (`voiceChangeId`, `audioName`), shown in its card; a retried start with the same key and the same recording is the same lip sync.
  - A unit test of the start, the link sent, the key, the refusals and the schema; the voice changer e2e test follows the link and checks the choice.
  - An independent review of `d7e5c3f` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. Every finished recording was offered and sent however old, though its link is the renderer's and signed for a limited time, so a start could pass, count, and fail at HeyGen; recordings are now offered and accepted for an hour after they are ready (`VOICE_RESULT_LINK_MS`, as the studio's other HeyGen links are treated), and the Voice changer's link to lip sync shows only meanwhile. A recording chosen by the link but not on offer (deleted, another account's, too old) was still sent with nothing shown; it is now cleared with a note. Also: a recording made by another renderer is refused; the hint says so when recordings cannot be loaded; the id is trimmed; the link's choice is read once and taken out of the address; and the API reference lists `voiceChangeId` and `audioName` in the lip sync's fields.
- QA pass 17, lip sync from a voice recording driven in a browser against the demo renderer (three should-fix findings, no blockers):
  - A recording chosen by a link but not on offer (deleted, made up, too old) was cleared with a note only screen readers heard; the reason now shows under the choice, tied to it. And while the recordings could not be loaded the hidden choice was still sent; "Lip-sync video" now waits for a recording that is on offer.
  - The Voice changer's new "Lip-sync a video" link squeezed the result's player to a sliver on a phone (no scrubber at 390 pixels, no play button at 320); the player now keeps a width of its own and the links wrap below it.
  - Nits: a start refused as over an hour old, or as another renderer's, clears the choice and asks for the list again; both pages draw the choices again each minute, so a recording leaves the choices and its link once it is over an hour old; a long recording name wraps in the lip sync's card; an empty `clipId`, `audioUrl` or `voiceChangeId` is one left out; and taking the link's choice out of the address keeps the rest of it. Left as before: lip syncs of one video share a delete button's name, and the shared `Field` puts its hint in the field's name.
  - An independent review of `617416f` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. The e2e check of the note matched the screen-reader copy rather than the note under the choice, and its disabled-button check held only because no video was chosen; it now reads the note and the choice's description, chooses footage first, and checks that choosing a recording again takes the note away and lets the start through. The note also goes on a start and on choosing "In one of my voices" again, only a voice recording is cleared, and the refusals that clear the choice are matched from their start.
- Animated email previews: the video page's Email button copies a shared video as a moving picture linked to its share page, the way HeyGen's GIF thumbnails go into outreach emails. As HTML a mail client pastes the linked picture itself; a plain-text field gets the link.
  - The picture is `/share/:id/preview.gif`, a fixed public address that redirects to the renderer's animated GIF of the video: HeyGen's `gif_url` (read from a finished render's status and its webhook, https only), or a 3-second GIF of the demo render. The renderer's link is signed for a limited time, so it is asked for again once 30 minutes old, at most once per video every 5 minutes on each server instance however many inboxes open the email, and a video finished before previews were kept gets one on its first open.
  - A link that asks for an email first shows the cover instead (the footage would stand in for the form, as in its link preview); a password-protected, offline, or unfinished video has no picture (`404`), and the button says so for a password. A renderer without GIFs (D-ID) gets the still.
  - Unit tests of the renewal, the single call per open burst, the wait after a failure, the gate and password rules, HeyGen's status and webhook fields, and the escaped snippet; an e2e test copies the button's HTML and text, follows the redirect to a loaded image, and checks the gate and password; the password-mode e2e test reads the preview without a session.
  - An independent review of `211de49` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. The redirect was cached for 5 minutes even when the share link expired sooner; it is now cached no longer than the link lives. A GIF link the renderer no longer had, or one that could not be renewed, was served after it expired; the renderer's answer without a GIF now drops it, and a link not renewed for a day gives way to the still. The still (HeyGen's thumbnail) was never renewed; it is now renewed with the GIF, for a link that asks for an email first too. A failed save of the renewed link answered 502 rather than the link. The rate limit is per server instance, as the docs now say. And a GIF that comes with the finished render is stamped, so the first open does not ask again; a browser without rich clipboard writes copies the drawn picture rather than showing raw HTML; the Email button's hint says when the share link is off; and the unit tests now cover the dropped GIF, the renewed still, the give-up, the expiry, the trash, and the stamp at completion.
- QA pass 18, email previews driven in a browser against the demo renderer (four should-fix findings, no blockers):
  - With the share link off or expired the Email button copied a picture that answers 404 and said "Email copied", warning only in a hover title; it is now disabled while the link is off, expired, or password-protected, with the reason shown beside it and tied to it, since a disabled button's title reaches neither keyboard nor touch users. The share-off note names the email preview too.
  - Nothing told screen-reader users a copy worked (a changed button label is seldom read out); Share, Embed, and Email now announce it in a status region.
  - Without rich clipboard writes, the fallback copied the page's styles into the email and the picture's alt text as the plain text; the copy command's event is now given the snippet and the share link themselves.
  - Nits: Email sits before Embed, so Embed's options stay beside it; the button keeps its width when it says "Email copied", so it does not jump to the next row on a phone; the snippet carries a height for mail clients that ignore its style; and the demo GIF is 480 wide, the width the snippet shows it at. Left as before: the buttons in that row are 38 pixels tall, and the demo's poster (an SVG) does not show in Gmail or Outlook. Covers made on the video page are JPEG; one set as WebP through the API does not show in desktop Outlook, and Outlook 2007–2019 shows only a GIF's first frame.
  - An independent review of `650aace` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. The snippet's new height would squash a cover of another shape in Outlook (the picture is the cover for a link that asks for an email first), so a video with a cover is sized by width alone. The fallback's e2e check could pass on the clipboard the first copy left; it now checks that the rich clipboard is gone and starts from a sentinel. The reason under a disabled button now says what to do (turn the link on, set a later expiry, or that a password rules it out), the button follows an expiry passing while the page is open and the expiry being saved, and copying the same thing twice is announced twice. The changelog's line on cover formats in Outlook is corrected. Tests: the Share announcement and an expiring link.
- HeyGen-directed videos: the Video Agent page offers HeyGen's own Video Agent beside this studio's planner, the way HeyGen's app makes a whole video from one request. HeyGen writes the script, chooses the presenter, voice, and visuals (the ones named here, when named), and renders.
  - The request, its length and language, and any attached document and page are folded into one brief of up to HeyGen's 10,000 characters and sent to `POST /v3/video-agents` in its one-shot mode. The video is followed as the agent's session until the session names its render, then as that render: the library and video page move it on as they look (HeyGen's webhook for the render matches only once it has moved), and the email preview follows the render like any other. A session that finishes without a video, one HeyGen no longer has, or one that starts no render in three hours, fails with the reason.
  - HeyGen makes landscape or portrait videos: square is not offered (and refused by the API), and "let the agent choose" is portrait for a request naming a vertical format. HeyGen keeps the script it wrote, so the video has no captions file, subtitles, caption edits, other sizes, template, or copy to edit; its page shows the brief, who directed it, and the presenter and voice it was told to use, and "Ask the agent again" opens the Video Agent page with the request, length, language, format, page, presenter, and voice filled in (an attached document is named but has to be attached again, as only its words were sent). A retry sends the brief again, and it is found in the library by its request.
  - The demo renderer stands in with the sample video, moving from its session to its render halfway through, so the whole path runs without keys.
  - Unit tests of the brief, the presenter and voice, the shapes, the session to render move, the refusals, the retry, the search, the share page, and the requests HeyGen's agent gets (body, the session followed to its video, a failed session, a refused brief); an e2e test drives the page from the choice to the finished video and back.
  - An independent review of `fa72738` found one blocking finding, in the new e2e test (the request was also the video's title, so the text matched twice); fixed in the following commit. A session that finished without a video, or that HeyGen no longer had, was followed forever; it now fails with the reason, as does one that starts no render in three hours. "Ask the agent again" lost the length, language, and page, so one click remade the video differently; they are kept with the video and filled in, and an attached document is named, to attach again. "Let the agent choose" read "customer success stories" or "a highlight reel" as a vertical format; only a platform's vertical format or the shape counts now. The length and language lead the brief, so a long request is cut before them; the renderer is checked before the page is read; an account out of HeyGen credits is told so; the prefill keeps what was typed meanwhile; the changelog no longer says HeyGen's webhook follows the session; and the unit test of the job's move now checks what is stored.
- QA pass 19, HeyGen-directed videos driven in a browser against the demo renderer (six should-fix findings, no blockers):
  - Typing while "Ask the agent again" was still loading kept the request but overwrote the length, language, format, and presenter; now a brief that lands after the form was changed fills in nothing and says so.
  - A presenter or voice deleted since showed as "Let HeyGen choose" but was still sent, and refused; one no longer listed is now not chosen, and the page says so.
  - A request near 10,000 characters silently lost an attached document (while the video named it) and its own last words; such a request is now refused, saying by how much to shorten it or which source to remove.
  - The pressed button was disabled while starting, so the focus fell to the page and stayed there after an error; it now stays on the button. A start HeyGen refused left a failed video in the library with no way to it; the error now links to it. And the two choices' names took in their whole description lines; each is now named by its title and described by its line.
  - Nits: "let the agent choose" is restored as such when asked again, and a wide format named outright (a landscape explainer, a YouTube video) wins over a platform mentioned in passing; switching between the agents clears an error; attaching the document again clears the note asking for it; the page's subtitle describes HeyGen's agent when it is chosen; a video that could not be opened is not named by its raw id; the Brief card lists the length, language, page, and document, scrolls a long request in place, and says the agent writes the script while it is still rendering. Left as before: "a shorts-style recap" is landscape, and the 9:16 player at 320 pixels and the Details heading order predate this.
  - An independent review of `4cc8d81` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. Letting a wide word win made "a vertical video for YouTube" landscape; now a shape named outright decides (vertical first), then the platform named first. The notes about the prefill replaced one another (a document to attach again, then a presenter gone, then a voice gone); they are now listed together. The scrolling request carried a name its paragraph may not have; it now scrolls in a named region. The focus went back to the button after an error even for someone typing elsewhere meanwhile; only a lost focus goes back now. And choosing HeyGen's agent before the prefill lands no longer stops it, attaching a document counts as an edit, a failed video's brief is not described as being written, and a request too long for its sources is refused before the page is read.
- HeyGen's webhooks reach directed videos: HeyGen's Video Agent is now given the studio's webhook and the video's id (`callback_id`), so its webhooks find the video while the studio still follows the agent's session. A `video_agent.success` moves the video to the render it names, the render's `avatar_video.success` or `.fail` then finishes or fails it (found by the render, or by the id if HeyGen passes it on to the render's webhooks), and a `video_agent.fail` fails it, without waiting for the library or video page to look. Only a directed video of that renderer still following its session is found by its id this way; one already following its render is matched by the render as before. A unit test drives the webhooks: the move, the finish, a render's webhook arriving first, a failed session, and ids that must match nothing.
  - An independent review of `1016073` found nothing blocking; fixed in the following commit. A `video_agent.success` carrying a link finished the video with it, though the agent's payload is not documented and the link may not be the video's; it now only moves the job, and the render's webhook or a check finishes the video with its length and picture. A webhook found by the id could overwrite a newer move made by a check, and a check could apply a stale answer about the session after a webhook moved the job; each now leaves a job the other moved alone. A failed session's webhook no longer moves the job; a repeated webhook that changes nothing is not written; the docs no longer say HeyGen passes the id on to the render's webhooks, only that it would be used if so; and the unit test covers another renderer's video and a stale check. (`callback_url` and `callback_id` are fields HeyGen documents for `POST /v3/video-agents`.)
- A directed video's render is asked about as soon as HeyGen's agent names it: if the render's own webhook came first without the studio's id (HeyGen does not document passing it on) it matched nothing, and the video waited for the next look; the `video_agent.success` that moves the video to its render now checks the render at once, so one already finished is finished then. A unit test sends the render's webhook first, then the session's. (`ec7665d`; its review found HeyGen's video status call had no time limit, now bounded to 10 seconds in `96d5593`.)
- A new look of a photo avatar from a description (`b50884f`): the New looks panel offers a look described in words besides the packs (the clothes, the setting, the light; up to 1,000 characters), with up to three pictures of the clothes or the place to go by, in a 4:5, 16:9, or 9:16 frame. HeyGen keeps the face and draws that outfit and setting (`POST /v3/avatars` with `type: "prompt"` and the avatar's look as `avatar_id`); the look is followed like a pack's and kept as a photo avatar (`Dana · Described look`). A retried start with the same key compares the description and a digest of the pictures, kept on the server only. The picture shrinking of background uploads moved to a shared helper. Unit tests cover the request checks, the demo flow, the picture checks, retries, the HeyGen request and its refusals; an e2e test draws a look with pictures in the browser.
  - An independent review found a retry could never get back a look HeyGen had drawn when its answer was lost: each attempt uploaded the pictures again, so the same key went out with a different body. The pictures now go inline as base64, a retry sends the same body, and no assets are left in HeyGen's account; HeyGen's `conflict` (a key taken with other details) is told apart from a start still in progress. The add-pictures control lost the focus when it was disabled while reading or removed at three pictures, and showed no focus ring; it now keeps the focus, moves it to the last picture when full, and rings. The description box's name no longer carries the live character count, and the docs say what goes to HeyGen. Fixed in `464e9ee`.
- QA pass 20 (`0cf60e1`): a browser pass over looks from a description. A field of the other kind (a pack's gender on a described look, a description on a pack) was dropped without a word; Escape threw away a typed description and its pictures; the picture count was cut off with the description; two described looks of one avatar had the same name; a file only claiming to be a PNG was refused only on Draw; the remove buttons were 18 px; a picture picked while others were read was dropped silently; and a library presenter's id was called a deleted photo avatar. All fixed, with unit and e2e tests.
  - The review found the numbering that told described looks apart could still repeat a number (after a forget, or two starts at once), reloaded every avatar on each check, and leaked into the API; looks are now named after the start of their description. A picture is passed through only when its first bytes say PNG or JPEG, so a WebP named .png is converted, and Escape asks in either mode and leaves an input method's Escape alone. Fixed in `5ff2bdc`.
- B-roll clips generated by HeyGen Video (`88e2387`): describe a shot, or bring a picture to life as its first frame, for 5 to 15 seconds in 16:9, 9:16, or 1:1 (a picture keeps its shape), at standard 768p or HD 1080p (`POST /v3/models/videos`, model `heygen-video-1`). Clips are followed until ready and their signed links renewed when old, to watch and download, and a finished one loops as a video background by a fixed address (`/api/footage/:id/clip`), chosen under Generated clip in the editor's Background tab; that address is what is stored, and a render looks the clip up with a fresh link. Starts take an Idempotency-Key, with the picture inline so a retry sends the same body. Unit tests cover the request checks, the demo flow, pictures, retries, the HeyGen requests and answers, and the render lookup; an e2e test makes a clip, picks it in the editor, renders with it, and deletes it.
  - An independent review found the `footage` kind missing from the Supabase check constraint (no clip could be stored there once HeyGen was paid; re-run `supabase/schema.sql` after upgrading, and a unit test now compares the store's kinds with the constraint), a clip still marked as being made refused as not ready by every render until a listing asked about it, a deleted clip left in the brand kit and templates (breaking every batch and Video Agent render), a kit or template allowed to name a clip that is not the account's, renewals not throttled while HeyGen fails, and D-ID renders refused over a clip D-ID never loops. Fixed in `3367489`.
- QA pass 21 (`9815d24`): a browser pass over b-roll clips. Choosing Square then Portrait left neither quality checked; the start error sat above the form, off-screen on a phone; the editor's Generated clip list never picked up a clip made after it opened; a retry with a new title kept the old one; a deleted clip was refused without naming the scene and with no warning in the editor; a picture over 3 MB was told to send "shorter audio"; a malformed clip id answered 400 with the id pattern instead of 404; Download had no download attribute; the brand kit could not pick a generated clip; the clip select's name carried its hint; and the editor's tabs did not say which was selected. All fixed, with unit and e2e tests.
  - The review found that moving the error next to the start button hid delete failures (shown above the list now), that HD chosen for a picture came back after the picture was removed on a square frame, that the brand kit form kept a clip deleted in another tab and failed the save, and that the e2e test left a brand kit behind for later tests. Fixed in `5a189ca`. The download attribute only applies to same-origin links, so a HeyGen clip still opens in a new tab to be saved from there.
- Inline pictures in HeyGen's base64 asset shape (`54c6159`): a b-roll clip from a picture and a look described with pictures sent `{ type: "base64", content }`, which HeyGen's strict `AssetBase64` (`{ type, media_type, data }`) refuses, so neither could start against HeyGen; both now go through one helper that names the picture's media type, and the request-body tests pin the shape.
- B-roll clips that feature your pictures (`e771776`): HeyGen Video's `reference_to_video` builds a shot around up to three pictures of a product, a place, or a person, which the description names as `<Picture 1>`, `<Picture 2>` (a button puts each label in), in a chosen frame or the first picture's shape (standard only). The clip keeps how many pictures it features, not the pictures; their digest, in order, is part of a retried start's comparison. Unit tests cover the checks, the stored clip, retries, and HeyGen's body; an e2e test drives the page.
  - The review found that removing a picture left the description's labels pointing at the wrong pictures (HeyGen does not rewrite them, so the clip would be made wrong and billed), that a label always went at the end because pressing its button took the focus, that a stale "Like Picture 1" frame could drop HD on its own later, that "Open on a picture" with no picture still made a clip, that the size message said "1 MB" for a 900 KB limit, that a picture could be removed while another was read, and that each picture-use choice was described by the selected one's hint. Removing a picture now takes its label out and renumbers the rest, a label past the pictures is refused on the page and by the server (`400`), labels go where the cursor was, and the rest are fixed, with unit and e2e tests. Fixed in `1aec17e`.
- QA pass 22 (`012da67`): a browser pass over featured pictures on the B-roll page. A `<Picture N>` label typed or left in "No pictures" or "Open on a picture" mode still started a clip HeyGen could not match (the page now holds the start with the reason, and the server refuses a label with no pictures featured); putting a label in at the 2,000-character limit cut text off the end or left half a label (it now changes nothing and says there is no room), and could split or overwrite an existing label or glue itself to the next word (one tested helper now places it: a selection is replaced unless it touches a label, a cursor inside one moves past it, and it is spaced from the words around); removing a picture was not always announced, or was announced with the wrong details; each picture-use choice was named by its whole hint; an oversized picture did not say which, and length and frame refusals read as validation codes; a file the browser could not read was not named; and the option rows and Download link were under 24 px tall. All fixed, with unit and e2e tests. A JPEG cut short is still accepted when its first bytes are right; HeyGen refuses it with its own reason.
  - The review found nothing blocking. Its optional findings are fixed in `ab4f4e3`: the size check on each picture was skipped when another field was wrong, the remembered cursor was not forgotten when a removal renumbered the description (so the next label could split a word), and a space was put between an opening bracket and a label; the tests now cover selections over labels, a cursor at a label's edge, and the removal announcement.
- Longer b-roll clips, exact descriptions, and repeatable takes (`89b352b`): HeyGen Video's iteration controls. Clips from a description or a first frame run up to 30 seconds (15 when they feature pictures or follow the description exactly, HeyGen's limit), with the length slider following the settings; "Follow my description exactly" sends `prompt_enhancement: "disabled"`; each finished clip keeps the seed HeyGen used, and "Make again with changes" fills the form with its description, settings, and seed, so the next clip is the same take with only the changes ("Use a new take" lets HeyGen pick again). `exact` and `seed` are part of a retried start's comparison. Unit tests cover the limits, the seed's range, the HeyGen body, the reported seed, and retries; an e2e test makes a take again.
  - The review found nothing on the server. On the page, "Make again" kept pictures added for another clip (sent with the take's seed), brought a clip shaped like its first picture back with a fixed frame, could drop a first-frame clip's HD choice, and could be pressed while a clip was starting; "Use a new take" lost the focus; and the e2e test sent no clip over 15 seconds. Fixed in `199c63a`. The longer e2e test then caught the length slider falling out of step: when its limit dropped to 15 seconds the browser clamped the value itself, unseen by React, so moving back to 30 later changed nothing; the slider was keyed by its limit (`c77b52b`), which QA pass 23 replaced with writing the clamped value through the input's property, so the slider also keeps its focus (`9b76c70`).
- QA pass 23 (`9b76c70`): a browser pass over longer clips, exact descriptions, and takes on the B-roll page. Choosing HD while a take made again from a clip shaped like its picture waited for that picture was undone when the picture was added (the frame went back to "Like Picture 1" and the clip to standard, unannounced); the length slider lost its keyboard focus when its limit changed under it; "Put in the description" and "Make again with changes" had accessible names that did not start with their visible text; and the API's length messages offered 30 seconds to clips held to 15, with zod's own wording for a wrong type. All fixed, with unit and e2e tests.
  - The review found the HD fix incomplete: the take's restore was read from the render in which the picture was picked, so a frame or HD chosen while a large picture was still being read was still undone; it is now held in a ref. It also found that a number too large to be exact was reported as not whole; the range is now checked first. Fixed in `83c48dd`, with a unit test for the order (the read-time window is not covered by e2e: a test picture reads too fast to choose in between).
- Photo shoots (`2f6c098`): HeyGen Image (`heygen-image-1`, `POST /v3/models/images`) on a new Photo shoot page. One to five photos of a person's face and one scene picture (a person whose pose, clothing, light, and setting to take), with the account's word that it may use the face, give one to five photos in the scene's shape or one of nine others, followed until ready and their signed links renewed, to view and download. Pictures go inline as HeyGen's base64 assets, six of at most 500 KB fitting one request; the shoot's id is HeyGen's `Idempotency-Key`, and a client key makes a retried start return the same shoot (compared on the pictures in order, shape, count, seed, and title). Capped at 50 per account and rate limited; the pictures are never kept; shoots go with the account's export (without the renderer, its job, or the pictures' digest) and its deletion. Unit tests cover the request, the demo, retries, the cap, HeyGen's body and answers; an e2e test makes a shoot.
  - The review found nothing blocking. Fixed in `6ea3540`: a failed shoot named the bad picture by HeyGen's 0-based field (`identity_images[1]` for the page's Photo 2), now as the page numbers it; a photo without an `https` link left a completed shoot short, now it fails; HeyGen's 503 reached the page as its raw answer, now a plain "try again" that keeps the key; an oversized photo's named message came after an unnamed one, so the page showed the unnamed one (B-roll's featured pictures had the same gap, fixed with it); a second ready shoot of the same default name was not announced, and default names did not tell shoots apart (an untitled shoot is now named after its scene picture); the shape, count, title, and consent could be changed while a start was in flight, giving its retry a new key; the list loaded HeyGen's full 4K files as thumbnails, now sized for display; the demo's photos were links a browser will not open; and the page was missing from the phone-width, WCAG, and viewer e2e checks. HeyGen's photos are on another origin, so Download opens them in a new tab rather than saving them, as B-roll clips do.
- QA pass 24 (`1afbb67`): a browser pass over photo shoots. A long title widened the list column until the form was squeezed to 144 px and the page scrolled sideways at desktop width (B-roll's list had the same grid), and a long file name widened the form past a phone's width; the demo drew every photo as a portrait whatever shape was asked for; adding a photo of the person or the scene was not announced; two untitled shoots from one scene picture had the same name, button names, and announcement; and the API answered a long title in zod's words, put a title's mistake before a picture's, and gave a list that is not one two messages. All fixed, with unit and e2e tests: an untitled shoot is now named after its scene and the time it started, kept for the start's key so a retry sends the same body, and "Like the scene" fits its select.
  - The review found the new e2e overflow checks could never fail (studio pages scroll inside `<main>`, not the document); they now check `<main>` and fail with the layout fix removed. It also found error banners did not wrap a long unbroken file name, the demo shape check could measure before the picture loaded, the title's time was matched for one locale only, and the docs overstated which picture checks come first. Fixed in `fac6c54`.
- Bring a photo shoot's photo to life (`ce56243`): each finished photo of a photo shoot has a "Bring to life" link that opens the B-roll page on it as the clip's first frame, the description started with HeyGen's advice for a person in the first frame ("Keep the person exactly as they appear in the first frame."), ready for one camera move. `POST /api/footage` takes `shootPhoto` (`{ shootId, photo }`): the shoot is looked up for the account, finished and with that photo, its link renewed when old, before the hour's starts are counted, and HeyGen gets `image_to_video` with the photo by its link (`{ type: "url", url }`); the clip keeps `fromShoot`, so "Make again with changes" opens on the same photo. Unit tests cover the request, the lookups, retries, and HeyGen's body; an e2e test brings a photo to life and makes it again.
  - The review found a photo the demo drew, or one made with another renderer, could be sent to HeyGen as a link it cannot fetch (after the hour's start was counted), and a link that could not be renewed could be sent expired; both are now refused first. On the page, a photo shoot looked up slowly could replace a picture chosen meanwhile or another clip's form, arriving from "Bring to life" could take the focus from another field and announce a sentence it had not added, the reasons a photo could not be opened were run together, and the link was offered where clips cannot be made. A retry whose first try reached HeyGen unstored, with the link renewed in between, sends HeyGen another body for its key; it is answered with a `400` to start again, as documented. Fixed in `3f1798a`.
- QA pass 25 (`5e657fc`): a browser pass over bringing a photo shoot's photo to life. Every untitled clip from a photo was named after HeyGen's first-frame advice it starts with ("Keep the person exactly as they appear in the first frame. A…"), in the list, the announcements, and the delete buttons (now named after what follows it); choosing "No pictures" or "Feature my pictures" while a photo was looked up was overridden when it came back; adding a picture of one's own lost the focus; the first-frame preview cropped a portrait photo to a landscape strip; "Bring to life" showed before it was known clips could be made; a link with a photo number that was not one was ignored silently and left in the address; and a viewer arriving by a link got an editor's hand-off. All fixed, with unit and e2e tests. Going back and forward after the hand-off starts the form afresh, as the cleaned address means.
  - The review found choosing "Open on a picture" during the lookup dropped the photo silently, the lookup started only once the person's role was known and could replace a picture chosen meanwhile, the advice followed by only punctuation gave an empty title, "Bring to life" showed to a viewer while their role loaded, and a retry spanning the change of titles would conflict. Fixed in `201a250`.
- Presenter from a photo shoot's photo (`70444c5`): `POST /api/avatars` takes `shootPhoto` (`{ shootId, photo }`) in place of `imageUrl`; the server looks the photo up for the account (another account's is a `404`), reads it once through the public-address guard, checks the bytes are a PNG or JPEG of at most 3 MB, and keeps it with the avatar as a data URL, so the presenter keeps its picture after HeyGen's link expires and works with any renderer. Each finished photo on the Photo shoot page has a "Make a presenter" link (editors only) that opens the new photo avatar panel with it as the portrait and the focus on the name. The shoot photo schema is now shared with b-roll clips. Unit coverage of the schema, ownership, the guard, the byte check, the size cap, and stale links; an e2e test runs the hand-off from the Photo shoot page to a saved avatar.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Its findings, fixed before the commit: "New photo avatar" with the panel open threw away its contents; a slow shoot lookup could open the panel after the person had opened or closed one themselves; the note naming the photo was not read with the name field; one keystroke in the address field lost the photo for good; a photo that will never do (redirected, over 10 MB, not an image) said to try again; and Escape asked before discarding a photo that stays in its shoot anyway. Not measured: whether HeyGen Image photos fit the 3 MB a photo avatar keeps (a larger one is refused, saying to upload a copy under 4 MB).
- QA pass 26 (`a2223ff`): a browser pass over making a presenter from a photo shoot's photo, at desktop and phone width (no sideways scroll on either page), with an axe scan of the open panel (clean), the keyboard order from the name field, a workspace viewer arriving by the link (no panel), and a video rendered with the new presenter. The panel cropped a photo shoot's photo to its 4:5 portrait tile, so a 16:9 photo showed as a narrow strip of what the server keeps; a shoot's photo is now shown whole.
  - The review found the viewer test's arrival check could never fail (the address is cleaned for editors too, and an unknown shoot opens nothing either way; it now answers the lookup with a finished photo and requires that a viewer never looks it up, shown to fail with the guard removed), that the tile kept its dashed drop-zone look around a photo shown whole, and that a lint suppression had drifted off its line. Fixed in `9764d97`. Uploaded portraits keep their cropped preview; the library tile crops every photo avatar to 4:5, as before.
- HeyGen Video Agent styles (`74e7245`): a video HeyGen's Video Agent directs can be composed in one of its curated styles (`GET /v3/video-agents/styles`, sent as `style_id`). The Video Agent page offers them when HeyGen's agent makes the video, as cards with each style's picture, shape, and a sample to watch, filtered by group (cinematic, retro tech, iconic artists, pop culture, handmade, print), or no style; `GET /api/video-agent/styles` lists them. With the shape left to the agent, a 9:16 style makes a portrait video unless the request names a shape or a platform. The video keeps the style's id and name, shows it on its page, and asks the agent again in it; a style HeyGen no longer lists, or one sent to this studio's own planner, is a `400`. The demo renderer offers five drawn stand-ins. Unit coverage of the listing (both pages, duplicates, links that are not https, unknown groups and shapes), the request HeyGen gets, the shape rule, and the refusals; an e2e test picks, filters, sends, shows, and asks again. Also corrects the README's "not included" line, which still said avatars could not be trained from video.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: a portrait style overrode a wide shape or platform named in the request; a style asked again was still sent, unseen, when the styles could not be listed; a start refused while a style was named kept the listing for minutes; the picker's ids could clash and a choice hidden by the filter was not announced; third-party thumbnails now load without a referrer. Not changed: the listing reads at most five pages of 100 styles.
- QA pass 27 (no commit): a browser pass over Video Agent styles: the arrow keys move between styles, a style chosen before switching to this studio's planner is not sent, asking again while the styles cannot be listed clears the old style and says so, and the video page of a styled video fits a phone. No defects; two similar notes appear in that failure case, left as they are.
- Scene edits of a HeyGen-directed video (`cbb5c4f`): a finished directed video's scenes are read from HeyGen (`GET /v3/videos/{render}/scenes`) and the ones described are remade by its Video Agent (`POST /v3/video-agents/{session_id}` with an `edit_plan` against that render's `edit_version`), the rest keeping their script and visuals. The edited cut is a new library video titled "(edited)", counted like any render, linking to the video it edits and listing each changed scene's instruction; the video edited is kept as it was. The studio now keeps each directed video's agent session (`agentSessionId`) from the start, so videos directed before this change cannot be edited (they say to ask the agent again). `GET`/`POST /api/videos/:id/scenes`, an "Edit scenes" panel on the video page for editors, three sample scenes in demo mode. Unit coverage of the request rules, ownership, trash, versions, scene numbering, retries, and the HeyGen requests and refusals; an e2e test reads the scenes, edits one, follows the cut, and sends it again and stale.
  - An independent review found one blocking defect: HeyGen refusing the changes (a version that moved on, a scene not the video's) became a `502` and a dead failed cut in the library, as the start was stored before it was sent; the changes are now sent first, a refusal is answered as a `409` or `400` with nothing stored, and only trouble at HeyGen's end leaves a failed cut. Also fixed: the same edit sent twice made two cuts (its id is now derived from the video, version, and changes), edits read the scenes outside the read limit, the title could be cut inside a character, the focus was lost when reading the scenes again failed, and two test checks could not fail. Not changed: an edited cut is followed by its render alone, without the agent session's three-hour limit.
- QA pass 28 (no commit): a browser pass over scene edits at phone width (no sideways scroll, axe clean), the stale-version path (the reason announced, "Read the scenes again" offered), and reading again (what was typed kept, the focus on scene 1). No defects.
- Storyboard review for directed videos (`ee52c77`): "Let me review the storyboard first" runs HeyGen's Video Agent in its chat mode (`mode: "chat"`), so it stops at a storyboard and waits. The video stays processing with `agentWaiting` (its library card says "Waiting for your review"); its page shows the conversation, the agent's storyboard frames included (`GET /v3/video-agents/{session_id}` and its resources, `GET /api/videos/:id/agent`), and takes answers (`POST`): feedback the agent works on and answers again, or the go-ahead, after which the render is followed like any directed video, the conversation staying as the record. A waiting session is never given up on; a working one has three hours from the conversation's latest turn. The demo agent answers each turn after a few seconds with a drawn storyboard. Unit coverage of the turns, the go-ahead, refusals, ownership, the request and messages HeyGen exchanges, and the give-up clock; an e2e test reviews, gives feedback, and goes ahead. Writing the tests found the status refresh never stored the waiting flag (it wrote only when the status, video, or job changed); fixed in the same commit.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: the page stopped asking for good after one failed look (a rate limit, trouble at HeyGen), now backing off and pausing in hidden tabs, with looks shared for a few seconds and resources HeyGen no longer has remembered as missing; a stale "waiting" could outlive the go-ahead, now cleared whenever the render starts or the video ends; the focus was lost when the answer form went; a new agent message was not announced once HeyGen's 40 were full; a finished video whose conversation HeyGen dropped showed an alert; a demo storyboard's link could not open and a video attachment showed a broken picture; demo sessions could share an id and were never pruned. Not changed: an unanswered review stays processing (and counted) until HeyGen ends the session.
- QA pass 29 (`a14e7cc`): a browser pass over storyboard review. Three fixes. The demo agent took "Make the intro shorter" as the go-ahead because it began "make the"; it now approves only on an explicit go-ahead. After an answer, the focus landed on the page body because the status line it was moved to was still empty, and so hidden; it now moves once the line has its words. The "You" label failed contrast and was darkened. The unit and e2e tests now answer with "Make the intro shorter, and add a scene about parking" and check where the focus goes.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed in the commit that adds this entry:
    - The demo agent still took an approval beside a requested change ("Approved, but add a scene", "Don't go ahead yet") as the go-ahead. It now counts as feedback when it asks for anything first.
    - A few plain approvals ("Render the video", "Yes, render", "Go-ahead", "LGTM") had become feedback; they approve again. A table of both cases is unit tested.
    - The focus moved to the status line even if the person had moved on to another field while the answer was on its way. It now moves only from the form or the page body.
    - The focus is now moved in the same frame the words are set, so an identical note can no longer leave a pending focus move for a later announcement.
- Pictures and clips for HeyGen's Video Agent (`99b86c4`): a directed video can carry up to 10 of the account's own pictures (uploaded images), finished b-roll clips, and finished photo shoot photos. They are sent as the agent's `files` on `POST /v3/video-agents`: a picture as a HeyGen image asset, uploaded once as the video starts and remembered as for a background, and a clip or photo by a fresh link. The brief asks the agent to use them where they fit. They are checked and named at every start, a retry included; one deleted, another account's, not finished, or made with another renderer is refused before anything starts. The video keeps `directed.media`, shows it on its page, and chooses it again when the agent is asked again, leaving out any gone since and saying so. The agent page has a picker: choose from the list then press Add, or upload a picture there. The demo agent says how many files it was given. Unit coverage checks the exact files sent (asset or link), the upload reused on a retry, and the refusals; an e2e test chooses, uploads, removes, generates, and asks again after a picture was deleted.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - The picture upload ran before the start, so trouble at HeyGen was a raw 500 with nothing stored; it now leaves a failed video like any start.
    - An upload finishing late could undo a choice made meanwhile.
    - The focus was lost after an upload.
    - Moving through the list with the arrow keys added each item; adding now takes an Add button.
    - An upload during the first list load could be dropped as deleted; uploading now waits for the list, and Generate waits for an upload.
    - Refusals ended with "choose another photo" in the brief's context.
  - Not changed: clips and photos from a renderer used before are offered and then refused by name, as the lists do not say which renderer made them.
- QA pass 30 (`349d4d2`): a browser pass over the pictures-and-clips picker at phone width, with ten items chosen and a long name. Arrow keys in the list add nothing, Add and the focus moves work, the choice stops at 10 with the list and upload disabled, a text file is refused with a message, nothing scrolls sideways, axe finds no violations, and a viewer cannot change it. One finding: the video page ran every chosen name together in one semicolon-joined line; it is now a list, one per line.
- Dub glossaries under HeyGen's one glossary per workspace (`92550d3`). Since October 2026 a HeyGen workspace has one brand glossary, and `POST /v3/brand-glossaries` answers `409` once it has one. The studio made a glossary per set of terms, so in any workspace that already had one, dubs keeping the brand kit's terms or following its forced translations failed. Every dub's rules now go into the workspace's one glossary:
  - Which glossary: the studio's own, else one made in HeyGen's app, else one of the studio's old per-set glossaries (adopted: renamed, and its language-less rules cleared), else a new one.
  - Each rule is a forced translation scoped to the dub's language; a kept term is translated to itself, since HeyGen's don't-translate list applies in every language.
  - How it is written: the glossary is read, merged, written back, and read again to see the write held, with concurrent edits tried again.
  - Shared across accounts: the latest rule for a term and language wins, and regional dubs share their base language's rules; both are documented in API.md.
  - Unit coverage includes a HeyGen mock that keeps one glossary, replays creates by key, and simulates edits it does not notice.
  - An independent review, in two rounds, found blocking defects in earlier drafts. All are fixed before the commit:
    - a constant idempotency key replayed a deleted glossary;
    - an unresolved language wrote a rule for every language;
    - the ten-minute memo stayed stale after another account's rule replaced one;
    - adopting an old glossary kept its language-less rules.
    - Its should-fix findings are fixed too: the glossary is read back after writing, glossary calls have time limits, only refusals about the glossary are retried, and the workspace's own glossary is preferred over the studio's old ones.
- Brand kit pronunciations in HeyGen-directed videos (`b369244`): HeyGen writes a directed video's script, so the brand kit's pronunciations never reached it. With the HeyGen renderer they now go to its voice as the workspace brand glossary's `terms`, scoped to the video's language, with `brand_glossary_id` on `POST /v3/video-agents`. The shared glossary module writes `terms` beside the dubs' forced translations, sending only the list it changes. Each start sends the kit's pronunciations as they are then, a word's first rule only. The video records them and shows them as "Brand kit pronunciations sent", and a language HeyGen's rules cannot name is refused while the kit has any. The demo renderer takes none. Unit coverage checks merging beside existing rules, duplicates, a refusal naming the glossary, and the error wording.
  - An independent review found one blocking defect, fixed before the commit: a kit with a word twice in different case made the merge replace one with the other at every write, so the glossary never settled and every directed video failed; rules are now one per term and language. Also fixed from it: glossary errors read as a brief HeyGen could not take, the demo renderer refused languages and showed pronunciations it never applied, the shared-glossary effects were undisclosed, and the test covered only a fresh glossary.
  - Not changed: an edited cut runs in the original session, so it follows the glossary that session started with.
- QA pass 31, in a browser: the directed-video brief's "Pictures and clips" and "Brand kit pronunciations sent" lists at 1280px and 390px, with a long unbroken word, a non-ASCII word, and a long picture name: no sideways scroll, no clipped text, no axe violations, and the pronunciations list hidden when HeyGen never took the job. Nothing to fix.
- More natural blinking on Avatar V (`d82c99a`): HeyGen's October 2026 API takes `"more_blinking": true` in `engine` on `POST /v3/videos`, for Avatar V presenters that blink too little. A video can ask for it with `moreBlinking`, accepted only with `avatarEngine: "avatar_v"` (refused otherwise) and sent only inside the engine of scenes that render on Avatar V, the transparent single-avatar body included. Copies, retries, other sizes, templates, and batch rows keep it. The editor offers a "More blinking" checkbox once Avatar V is chosen, and the video page shows "Avatar V, more blinking". Unit coverage checks validation, carrying, the HeyGen body, and the refusal wording; the Avatar V e2e test ticks it and checks the request, the stored video, and the copy.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: the template API docs left the field out, a tick left on Avatar IV read as active (the hint now says it is not sent), and a refused render suggested only dropping Avatar V (it now suggests turning blinking off first).
  - Not changed: the batch page applies a template's Avatar V and blinking without showing them, a gap that predates this change for the engine itself; and a template request naming a video is refused if it also sends `moreBlinking` without Avatar V, though those fields are ignored there.
- Turbo and an expressiveness boost for text spoken in an instant voice (`32f8fde`): HeyGen's October 2026 text to speech takes `model: "heygen-voice-1-turbo"` (faster, cheaper speech for instant voices) and `expressiveness_boost` (0 to 1, instant voices only). `POST /api/voice-changer/speech` takes `turbo` and `expressivenessBoost` (in steps of 0.1), stores both with the recording, and counts them in what a repeated `Idempotency-Key` must match. The Voice changer's "Speak text in a voice" card has a Delivery section with a Turbo checkbox and a boost slider, and each recording shows what it was spoken with. Unit coverage checks validation, storage, replay and conflict, a recording from before the options, and the HeyGen body; the voice changer e2e test sets both from the keyboard and checks the request and the recording.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: a finer boost such as 0.04 was rounded to none without a word (now refused), the slider's value was announced twice to screen readers, and the replay of a recording made before the options had no test.
  - Not changed: the review asked to drop "cheaper" from Turbo's description; HeyGen's changelog describes Turbo as "faster, cheaper speech for instant voices", so it stays.
- The batch page shows a template's Avatar V (`59112d7`): a batch started from a template on Avatar V, with more blinking or not, sent both without a word, a gap the review of `d82c99a` noted. The page now says so, naming whether the chosen presenter renders on Avatar V, and "Use Avatar IV" drops the engine and its blinking together. Dropping a carried setting (this one or a transparent background) puts the focus on the template list and announces the change. The Avatar V e2e test starts a batch from such a template, drops it, and checks the request, the focus, and the announcement.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: the notice claimed Avatar V for a presenter that would render on Avatar IV, the focus fell to the page when the button went, and the e2e test did not wait for the batch to be stored or check the template's deletion.
- The expressiveness boost follows HeyGen's default (`3cbaa7b`): HeyGen's instant voice documentation gives `expressiveness_boost` a default of 1.0, its full boost, so `32f8fde`'s "None", sent as nothing, got the full boost. An absent boost now means HeyGen's default; 1 is stored as absent and anything lower, 0 included, is stored and sent. The slider starts at "100% (default)", a recording names its boost only when it is below the default, and a recording stored with 1 earlier replays for a same-key retry. Unit coverage checks 0 reaching HeyGen, 1 left out, and the replay; the voice changer e2e test lowers the boost from the default.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: a record stored with 1 met a conflict on a same-key retry and showed "100%", the hint spoke of a recording in the text card, and the e2e test did not check that a default recording names no boost.
  - The first full run had one failure, a dropped connection ("socket hang up") on `GET /api/voices` in the voice design test, which this change does not touch; it passed three times alone and the full run passed again.
- Instant voices set in a picture of the speaker's setting (`b9c2102`): HeyGen's October 2026 instant voice clone takes an `image` of the speaker in their setting, whose ambience the voice takes on. "Make a voice" offers one of the account's uploaded pictures as the setting; HeyGen gets it inline (never with gender or age), the voice keeps the picture's id and name, and a picture not the account's or deleted since is refused by name before HeyGen is asked. Unit coverage checks the request, the stored name, replay and conflict, both refusals, and HeyGen's refusal wording; the voice changer e2e test picks a setting, checks the request and the voice's "set in" line, and deletes the picture afterwards.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: a failed load of the pictures left the list on "Loading" for good, the hint did not say where pictures come from, the list could be changed while a voice was being made (the choice then cleared on success), and the API docs missed the `400` and the new response fields.
  - Not changed: a refusal from HeyGen names the picture as something to change even when the recording was at fault (HeyGen's own reason follows), and the picture's record is read twice when a voice is made.
- QA pass 32, in a browser (`de85aeb`): the voice changer's Delivery section and setting picture, More blinking in the editor, and the batch page's Avatar V notice, at 1280px and 390px. The voice changer and batch page were clean. The editor at phone width failed WCAG AA contrast on grey text: the notes under the chosen fit, aspect ratio, and quality (4.26:1 on the tinted fill) and the scene chips' running time and the on-screen text hint (2.56:1). All now pass, the aspect ratio buttons say which ratio is chosen (`aria-pressed`, not colour alone), and a phone-width e2e test runs axe over each note.
  - An independent review found the on-screen text character count still at 2.56:1, three weaker states (the Remove scene hover, the import note's Dismiss icon, the empty-scene dot), and a test that could pass without judging the notes themselves; all fixed before the commit, with the input placeholder darkened app-wide. Tightening the test showed that axe skips text outside the viewport without reporting it, so the test brings each note into view first.
- B-roll clips can leave their length to HeyGen (`9bae3a8`): HeyGen's October 2026 HeyGen Video takes `duration: "auto"` and picks 8 to 30 seconds (15 for a clip that features pictures or follows the description exactly), fitted to any lines the description quotes, reporting and billing the length it picked. `POST /api/footage` takes `autoLength: true` (refused with `durationSec`; the 5-second default moved from the schema to the service), the clip keeps `autoLength` and gets `durationSec` from HeyGen's report, and a retried start compares `autoLength` rather than the length picked. The B-roll page has a "Let HeyGen pick the length" checkbox, the lists in the B-roll page, the editor, and the brand kit read "length picked by HeyGen" and then "10 s, picked by HeyGen", and making a take again asks for the picked length. Unit coverage checks validation, the default, storage, retry and conflict, and the HeyGen body and report; an e2e test ticks the box and follows the clip to its remake.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: a remake of a clip whose length HeyGen never reported would have asked for 5 seconds (it is now left to HeyGen again), a remake fixed the picked length without a word (the form now says so), a reported length that is not a number would have been stored as nothing useful (now ignored; a real one is kept in whole seconds within HeyGen's range), and no test showed that a fixed length survives a different report (one does now, and fails without the guard).
  - Not changed: while the box is ticked, the hidden slider follows a lower limit silently, so a length set before ticking it may come back shorter.
- Listen to an instant voice before using it (`db47e9f`): HeyGen's October 2026 HeyGen Voice returns `preview_audio_url` for every active voice, a WAV of it reading a short preview script in its language whose link can expire. `GET /api/voice-changer/voices/:id/preview` reads it on every request, never storing or caching it, and the Voice changer's ready voices get a Listen button with a player under the voice. A voice not ready, failed, or without a preview yet is refused, another account's is not found, and one HeyGen no longer has or reports failed is marked failed with the reason. Unit coverage checks readiness, ownership, a fresh read each time, plain-http and missing links, HeyGen down, and dropped or failed voices; the voice changer e2e test listens by click and by keyboard.
  - An independent review found no authorization gap. Fixed before the commit: a HeyGen failure other than a 404 reached the page as a 500 carrying HeyGen's message, which names the voice there (now a 502 to try again, logged on the server), the button dropped the keyboard focus while loading, listening again to the same link did not play, a voice HeyGen dropped stayed Ready, the message showed at the top of the page rather than by the list, an older listen could overwrite a newer one, and the row's buttons floated once the player opened.
  - Not changed: HeyGen's preview link goes to the browser as recordings' links already do; if HeyGen's link carries the voice's id there, the account that owns the voice can see it. Previews are limited per account and per server instance, like the listing, with no limit across accounts.
- Text spoken in one of HeyGen's public voices (`cbc088c`): HeyGen's October 2026 HeyGen Voice lists the voices it provides to every workspace, each with a sample, and any workspace can speak them. `GET /api/voice-changer/public-voices` lists them by name (kept ten minutes per instance, requests at once sharing one read), and `POST /api/voice-changer/speech` takes a `pv_…` voice as well as an instant one, refusing Turbo and a boost below 1 for a voice that is not instant, as HeyGen applies them to instant voices only. The Voice changer's Speaking voice list gains a "HeyGen voices" group with each voice's sample, and the Delivery controls are disabled with a note for a professional voice. Unit coverage checks the list, speech with and without delivery, retry and conflict, paging, filtering, HeyGen down and recovered, shared reads, and a voice withdrawn; an e2e test speaks in a public voice with delivery set beforehand and checks none is sent.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit: a public voice HeyGen withdrew was answered with advice to make it again and stayed chosen (it is now "no longer offered; choose another", cleared, and the list read afresh), the e2e check that delivery is not sent would have passed with it sent, HeyGen's list had no timeout and concurrent first reads each asked HeyGen, the route had no limit across accounts, the explanation sat inside the disabled controls where keyboard users never reach it, a ticked Turbo stayed shown ticked, the hint was wrong with no voices of your own, a failed list was silent, and wording across the speech route, the service, and the lip sync page still spoke only of instant voices.
- Brand kit pronunciations for one language (`4640659`): HeyGen's brand glossary scopes each pronunciation to a language (`pronunciation_language`), and a brand kit rule can now do the same. On the brand page, `Nair = Naïr [French]` applies only where the voice speaks French, whatever the region, and there it beats the rule for every language. Each scene of a render takes the rules for its own voice's language, or the video's voice's. A voice with no known language takes only the rules for every language. HeyGen-directed videos send the glossary only the rules for the video's language, so a kit whose rules are all for other languages no longer refuses a language HeyGen's rules cannot name. Unit tests cover selection, per-scene voices through `createVideo`, the schema, and directed videos; the brand-kit e2e test saves, refuses, and reloads a rule for one language.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - A language check that accepted any two- or three-letter word ("sic", "USA"). A language must now be one the runtime knows.
    - Voice language codes that never matched the rule's language (`nb-NO`, `iw`, `cmn`, `pt_BR`). These are now mapped to the code the language's name resolves to.
    - Two rules for the same word in regional variants of one language, the second of which could never apply. Only the first is kept.
    - A spelling-ends-in-"]" check that would have blocked saving older kits. It is gone; the brand page now shows such a rule with an empty `[]` after it.
    - A bracket-only line that was silently dropped. It is now pointed out by line.
    - A whitespace-only language that was a `400`. It now means every language.
  - Not changed: a multilingual voice with no language of its own does not fall back to the video's accent; it gets the rules for every language, as before.
- Captions for text spoken in a voice, timed by HeyGen (`b8f8efa`): HeyGen Voice's text to speech can say when each word was said (`with_timestamps`, `original_words` and `original_characters`, at no extra cost). The studio now asks for that on every spoken text and keeps the timings in a `voicewords` row of their own, so the polled listing never loads them. A text with timings is listed as `timed: true`, and the Voice changer page offers its captions as SRT or WebVTT (`GET /api/voice-changer/changes/:id/captions`, viewers too; a `404` for anything without timings or another account's). Caption lines follow the words as said: a new line after a sentence, after a silence, when the line is full, or after 6 seconds, each shown from its first word to its last and never overlapping the next. Word timings are deleted with the text and the account and left out of the export. Unit tests cover parsing, line building, storage, deletion, and an older database that will not take the new row; the voice-changer e2e test downloads both formats.
  - An independent review found these, all fixed before the commit:
    - Characters of a script written without spaces were grouped only at blanks. A Chinese text became one 200-character line and the rest was lost. Each such character is now a word of its own.
    - Cues could overlap when HeyGen's word times overlap. They no longer do.
    - A space was added after East Asian punctuation and between characters outside the basic plane. The existing caption join is now used, and it reads whole characters.
    - A single word wider than a line, such as a link, was never cut. It is now cut into pieces that share its time.
    - Every listing loaded all word timings. They now live in their own row.
    - An e2e check passed without the card it meant to check. It now names that card.
  - Not changed: HeyGen's docs could not be reached from inside the review to confirm `with_timestamps` for every voice type. The API reference and changelog read beforehand document it for `/v3/models/audio/tts` and exclude no model or voice type.
- Free speech for instant voices (`c2a11c0`): HeyGen Voice's `heygen-voice-1-turbo-free` model is Turbo at no charge, within HeyGen's per-user limits and shared free capacity. It has no availability guarantee and is never switched to a paid model. The Voice changer page now offers it as Free under Turbo, and the speech endpoint takes `free`, which implies Turbo. A free start is part of what a repeated `Idempotency-Key` must match. HeyGen's limit or capacity refusal is a `429` that says free speech is at its limit or busy, and nothing is kept. A professional public voice refuses it as it refuses Turbo. Unit tests cover the schema, the model sent to HeyGen, the 429 and 500 messages, idempotency against older Turbo recordings, and a public instant voice; the voice-changer e2e tests send it, show it, and keep it off for a professional voice.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - A free request HeyGen failed (a `500` from its shared capacity) or left unanswered was said to have taken too long for a long text. It now says free speech did not answer.
    - Free stayed chosen when Turbo was turned off and on again. It is now cleared with Turbo.
    - The disabled Free box did not say why. It now says to turn on Turbo.
    - The route's comment did not list `free`.
    - Test gaps for a public instant voice, an older Turbo recording replayed, and a professional voice in the browser were filled.
- MP3 files for text spoken in a voice (`391f2c4`): HeyGen Voice's text to speech takes `output_format`, and with `{ encoding: "mp3" }` it hands back an MP3 at its defaults for MP3 (44.1 kHz mono, 128 kbps) in place of its WAV. The Voice changer page has a File choice (WAV or MP3) for every voice, including HeyGen's professional ones. The speech endpoint takes `format`, stores it with the recording, and includes it in what a repeated `Idempotency-Key` must match. Unit tests cover the schema, the body sent to HeyGen (with Turbo Free too), idempotency, a professional voice, and a HeyGen refusal of the format; the voice-changer e2e test sends it and shows it on the recording.
  - An independent review found one defect, fixed before the commit: the card showed "MP3" before the expressiveness boost, where the e2e test looked for it after. It now comes after.
  - The review also flagged that `output_format` together with `with_timestamps` on `/tts` is checked only against stubs. HeyGen documents a restriction only for its stream endpoint. A refusal naming either is now said as the MP3's, with WAV to choose instead, and the docs no longer promise captions for an MP3 that HeyGen does not time.
  - Also fixed: comments that still said text to speech gives one WAV, and the route's comment, which did not list `format`.
- The language of text spoken in a voice (`75c776f`): HeyGen Voice's text to speech takes the text's `language` and otherwise detects it, and its `Content-Language` header names the language it spoke in. The Voice changer page now has a Language choice ("Detect from the text" by default). The speech endpoint takes `language` by name or code and sends HeyGen its base code. Each recording keeps the language HeyGen reported and shows it ("spoken in Spanish"). Unit tests cover the schema, the code sent, the header kept or ignored, idempotency by language, and HeyGen refusing the language; the voice-changer e2e test chooses one and checks the detected one is shown.
  - An independent review found nothing significant. Fixed before the commit:
    - The codes for undetermined, several, no or private-use languages (`und`, `mul`, `zxx`, `mis`, `qaa`–`qtz`) passed the check. They are now refused, and ignored when HeyGen reports one, so a card cannot read "spoken in root".
