    - HeyGen refusing the language got the generic message. It now says HeyGen Voice does not speak that language.
- Keep a voice recording as an audio clip (`d1d0ddd`): a result of the voice changer lives at the renderer's link, signed for about an hour. "Keep as a clip" (`POST /api/voice-changer/changes/:id/clip`) reads the file once through the public-address guard and stores it as one of the account's audio clips, so a scene can lip-sync to it and a lip sync can use it after the link expires. The file's kind comes from its bytes, a WAV's length from its header and an MP3's from the renderer; the recording remembers the clip, and keeping it again returns that clip while it exists.
  - An independent review found nothing serious. Fixed before the commit:
    - A WAV whose header gives no length (float, 32-bit, extensible) fell back to the renderer's length and was kept, although an upload of the same file is refused. It is now refused too.
    - A link the address guard refuses was a `502` to try again. It is now a `400` that says to download and upload the file instead.
    - The button's name did not contain its visible text ("Keep as a clip"), its waiting state was hidden from screen readers, and the focus was lost when it gave way to the note. The name now starts with the visible text, the button stays focusable while it waits, and the focus moves to the note naming the clip.
    - The route had no `maxDuration`, unlike its neighbours. It now has 30 seconds.
    - A recording changed while being kept lost the link to its clip without a word. That is now logged.
    - A lip sync asked for a recording over an hour old said only to make it again. It now names the clip kept of it, if there is one.
    - The docs claimed "two requests at once make one" without "on one server instance", left out the `409` for a full clip list, and the README left out the hour and size limits.
- Make a video from a kept voice recording (`941622c`): a clip kept from text spoken in the voice changer carries that text as its `transcript`, and "Make a video" next to it opens the editor at `/create?clip=<id>` with one scene lip-syncing to the clip, its name as the title and its words as the script, in a draft of its own. Choosing such a clip in a scene's Audio tab fills an empty script.
  - An independent review found nothing serious. Fixed before the commit:
    - The clip start checked the draft store when the clips arrived rather than when the editor opened, so words typed while the clips loaded were either replaced or, once autosaved, made the start silently skip. It now decides by whether edits were kept when the editor opened, and never replaces typed words or a typed title.
    - Whether the brand kit's default background applied depended on whether the brand kit or the clips loaded first. The clip start's scene now counts as fresh for it.
    - Picking another clip in the Audio tab kept the first clip's words, and removing the clip left them for a voice to read. Words that are still exactly the last clip's are now replaced, or cleared with it.
    - A clip kept before this change had no words to start with. It gets them when it is kept again.
    - The docs did not list `transcript` on a clip, and the README promised a script for recordings spoken again, which have no text.
    - Tests for a reload of the clip start, the Audio tab's fill, filling in words on older clips, and blank words were added.
- Keep the words an audio clip says (`ad48066`): `PATCH /api/clips/:id` renames a clip or sets the words it says, and the editor's Audio tab offers "Keep these words with the clip" when a clip scene's script differs from them, so the next scene or video using the clip starts with them, for uploaded and recorded clips as well as kept recordings.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - An edit could bring back a clip deleted at the same moment, without its audio, and could race a render's cached upload id. Edits, filled-in words, and cached ids are now written only over the row read; an edit of a deleted clip is a `404`.
    - The focus fell to the page when the button gave way to its note, and a note added with its text is often not announced. The note now stays mounted and takes the focus.
    - The note could name a clip whose words were never kept here. It now shows only for the clip it applies to.
    - After one scene kept new words, another scene still holding the clip's earlier words no longer had them cleared by "Use a voice instead". Every set of words a clip has had while the editor is open now counts.
    - An edited clip jumped to the top of the saved-clips list. It now keeps its place.
    - Words padded with spaces past 5000 characters were refused. They are trimmed first.
    - Tests for the delete race, the trimming, a reused clip starting with its words, going back to a voice, and an empty edit were added.
- Assets page (`da4470d`): `/assets` lists the account's audio clips (length and kind, a player, the words they say, "Make a video", rename, delete, upload) and pictures (preview, rename, delete, upload) together, read-only for viewers, with a sidebar entry. `PATCH /api/backdrops/:id` renames a picture.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - The words button went away once the words were kept, dropping the focus to the page. It now stays and says the words are kept.
    - The status message sat in a hidden region until it had text, so its first message was often not announced. The region is now always present.
    - The e2e test matched names a run left part-way could leave behind on a reused server. Its names are now unique per run and matched exactly.
    - The sidebar could not scroll, so its last entries could be cut off on short screens, more so with one more entry. Its links now scroll.
    - The clip limit was copied into the page. It now lives in `lib/types` beside the picture limit.
    - The focus moved to a section's heading even when a delete failed, and that heading showed no focus ring. It now moves only after a delete, with a visible ring.
    - Renaming something deleted elsewhere showed the server's message with its id. It now says it was already deleted.
    - The upload buttons were disabled at the limit without saying why, and the picture buttons were under the 24px target size. Both are fixed.
    - While the role loaded, editors briefly saw "No words kept with this clip". Nothing is said until the role is known.
  - Also fixed: the full suite caught the editor bringing back a draft after "Generate video". An autosave still pending, or re-armed by data arriving before the page moved on, wrote the cleared draft back. Nothing autosaves once a render starts.
- Start a video set against one of your pictures (`04206a9`): "Use in a video" on a picture of the Assets page opens the editor at `/create?picture=<id>` with the first scene set against it and the video named after it, in a draft of its own; the brand kit's default background does not replace it.
  - An independent review found the editor logic sound. Fixed before the commit:
    - The e2e step checked that a draft existed rather than that it held the words typed, so a reload could race the next save. It now waits for the words.
    - A start opened and left untouched saved a draft, because the title it gave the video counted as an edit, so later visits restored it ("Restored your unsent draft"). Such a title is no longer an edit, for clip starts too.
    - The picture's link had no visible text and a title that did not match its name. It now reads "Use", its title matches, and its icon is hidden from screen readers.
- Search the Assets page (`888b9fa`): a search box narrows the clips (by name or the words they say) and the pictures (by name) and says how many of each match.
  - An independent review found no blockers. Fixed before the commit:
    - An edit that stopped matching made its card vanish with the focus inside it, and a new upload that did not match disappeared silently. What was uploaded or changed since the search was typed now stays in view.
    - A search unmounted hidden clip cards and lost words typed into them. Cards now stay mounted and hidden, also when nothing matches.
    - The match count was never announced. It is now a status line.
    - Names were compared without Unicode normalization, so a decomposed "Café" did not match. Both sides are now folded as glossary terms are.
    - The box disappeared when both lists emptied while a search stayed active. It now shows once either list has loaded.
    - The e2e test did not search by a clip's words. It now does, and counts only visible cards.
- Download clips and pictures from the Assets page (`8335f21`): `GET /api/clips/:id?download` and `GET /api/backdrops/:id?download` save the file under the name it was given (any script in `filename*`, an ASCII stand-in in `filename`, the extension from the stored type), and each card has a Download link, viewers included.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking in the header, security, or authorization code. Fixed before the commit:
    - The new link made the picture cards' actions overflow at 320px, hiding Delete. Actions now sit on their own row, which wraps, in the same order as on clip cards.
    - A clip's audio is cached for an hour, so a download after a rename could save under the old name. A download is now revalidated every time.
    - A name holding half of a UTF-16 pair made the download a 500. Such halves, control and invisible formatting characters (a right-to-left override could disguise a name), and characters Windows refuses are now left out.
    - A trailing dot doubled the extension, the ASCII stand-in could end in a dot, and the length cap could split a character. All three are fixed, and the cap counts characters as a person sees them.
    - The icon buttons were under 3:1 contrast. They are darker now.
    - The e2e test downloads before and after a rename, compares the picture's bytes, checks every picture action fits a 320px card, and checks a viewer can download but not change anything.
  - Also found: Chromium in a container with no locale saves any non-ASCII download name as "download". The e2e browser gets a UTF-8 locale, as desktops have.
- Sort the Assets page (`39314ca`): a Sort menu orders the clips and pictures newest first, oldest first, or by name A–Z in the browser's language (digits by value), and holds that order so nothing moves from under the focus; "Sort again" appears once it no longer matches.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - The menu took a row of its own: the shared input style's full width beat the narrower one. It now sits beside the search box, which the e2e test checks.
    - Adding or deleting anything re-sorted both lists, which could move a card from under the focus mid-typing. The order is now set only when a sort is chosen or a list first loads; an upload appears first and a delete leaves the rest in place.
    - After a rename the menu still said "Name A–Z" over a list out of order, and choosing the same option again does nothing in a native menu. A "Sort again" button now appears when the list is out of order, and each sort is announced.
    - The e2e focus check would have passed without the held order. It now renames the first card to a name that sorts last, so the card would move without it.
    - The docs promised accents were always ignored; name order follows the browser's language (in Swedish, Ä sorts after Z). They now say so, and the unit tests pin the language and cover ties and the held-order helpers.
- Rename a saved template (`f2b3b26`): `PATCH /api/templates/:id` changes a template's name or description and keeps everything else, and each saved template on the Templates page has an Edit button opening a form in the card (editors only, as Delete now is too; viewers see the view-only notice).
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - The form always sent both fields, so a description edit could undo another editor's rename made while it was open. It now sends only what was changed in it, and follows a save from elsewhere in fields not touched.
    - Escape closed the form while saving, while an input method was composing, and from the description box, losing what was typed. It is now ignored there, as in the page's other forms, and Cancel waits for a save.
    - A name of spaces was refused in a banner far from the form. It is now explained at the field, which is marked invalid and takes the focus.
    - The viewer check in the e2e test could pass before the role had loaded. It now waits for the view-only notice.
    - The accessibility scan ran with the form closed; it now also runs with it open.
    - A second save under the same name was not announced, and sighted users got no confirmation. Saves now show a note and are announced every time.
    - Smaller points: the card keeps its title heading while edited, the focus falls back to the list's heading when the Edit button is gone, a list read before a save is read again after it, and a template out of reach is "no longer available" rather than "already deleted".
    - Unit tests now cover two edits to different fields racing, a delete between read and write, and giving up after five tries; the docs give the `403`, the `409`, and the trimming.
- Duplicate a saved template (`6cec3fe`): `POST /api/templates { fromTemplateId }` copies a template under "<name> (copy)" (then "(copy 2)" and on), and each saved template on the Templates page has a Duplicate button whose copy appears first with its Edit button focused, ready for a new name.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - A click at the limit still sent a request and was answered by an error after the round trip. It now explains on the spot, with a visible note under the heading that the button points to.
    - The focus moved to the copy even if the person had gone on to edit another card meanwhile. It now moves only if it is still where it was, or was lost.
    - When the source had been deleted elsewhere, the focus fell to the page. It now goes to the list's heading.
    - Names were capped by code point, which the edit form (counting UTF-16 units) could then refuse, and could split an emoji. They are now within 120 UTF-16 units, cut between whole characters.
    - At the limit a missing source answered `429`; it is now looked up first and answers `404`. A copy no longer names the original's video as its source.
    - Unit tests now cover a removed clip, a given description, the source rule, and emoji names; the e2e test checks the announcement and scans the list with the copy in it; the docs give these rules and read in order.
- Rename B-roll clips and photo shoots (`60320c8`): `PATCH /api/footage/:id` and `PATCH /api/shoots/:id` take a new title, and both pages offer Rename beside Delete; a retried start with the same key is still recognised after a rename.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - A rename and a link renewal landing together could lose the renewal: the refresh kept `updatedAt` when only the link changed, so a rename could write the old link back, or a rename landing first made the refresh drop its new link. For a few minutes a shoot's photo could then not be used for a presenter. The refresh now always moves `updatedAt` and, on a miss, applies the renewal to the renamed row; tests cover both orders.
    - The name a clip or shoot was started with, kept for retried starts, appeared in the account export. It is now left out, and the export tests check it.
    - The clip rename answered `{ clip }` where the rest of the API says `footage`; it now answers `{ footage }`.
    - Rename and Delete showed while the role was loading, so a viewer could meet a prompt and then a refusal. Both now wait for an editor role; the Delete icon meets 3:1 contrast and has a tooltip.
    - A doc comment had moved off the delete it described, and the docs said nothing else changes on a rename (the update time does) and split the delete paragraph; all three are fixed.
- Rename cleanups, lip syncs, and videos with music (`d8c0f4f`): `PATCH /api/cleanups/:id`, `/api/lipsyncs/:id`, and `/api/music/:id` take a new title, and the three pages offer Rename beside Delete (editors only); a status check landing during a rename keeps both, and a retried music start with the same key and title is still recognised after a rename.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - When two status checks landed together (two servers, say), the one whose write missed applied its own answer again over the newer row, so a finished job could go back to "processing" and be announced as ready twice. A check now leaves a later answer it finds in place; this also covers B-roll clips and photo shoots, and a unit test lands the checks in both orders.
    - A list check already on its way when a rename was saved could bring the old name back until the next check. Every rename (B-roll and photo shoots too) now asks for the list again, which retires the earlier check.
    - A rename that found the item deleted said so, and then the next check replaced that with "deleted elsewhere". The item is now forgotten first (also on the B-roll and photo shoot pages).
    - Cleanups and lip syncs kept the name they were started with, but their retries never compare titles, so it was never read. Only clips, shoots, and music, whose retries do compare it, keep it now.
    - The unit test for renaming to the same name checked only the title; it now checks that nothing changed, the update time included.
- Rename instant voices and voice changer recordings (`433f8ea`): `PATCH /api/voice-changer/voices/:id` takes a new name (here only; HeyGen keeps the one it was made with, and another voice's name is refused) and `PATCH /api/voice-changer/changes/:id` a new title, with Rename beside Delete on the voice changer page (editors only); a retried start with the same key is still compared on the name or title it was started with. Status writes for voices and recordings now go through the shared conditional write, which also fixes text being lost when its recording was renamed while HeyGen spoke it (the finished speech was never stored, and was later given up as failed).
  - An independent review found one blocking race, fixed before the commit:
    - Keeping a recording as a clip remembered the clip without moving the row's time, so a rename that had read the row just before could write over it and drop the clip: the page then offered Keep again (a second clip), and after the link's hour a lip sync could not fall back to the kept clip. The write now moves the time on (the link's age is read from `checkedAt`, which it leaves alone, and the test now checks that), and a unit test shows a rename that read the row first misses.
  - Optional findings fixed: marking a voice HeyGen no longer has as failed from its preview now goes through the same write, so a rename meanwhile neither stops nor undoes it; a recording check that found the recording finished by its start meanwhile keeps that answer; a rename that finds a voice gone also closes its preview player; and the note for a recording gone is quoted like the others.
  - The full suite also caught a race in the brand glossary e2e test (it typed before the stored kit loaded into the form, which could replace what it typed); it now waits for the kit to load (`b5da2b0`).
  - Left as it is: two renames, or a rename and a new voice, to the same name at the same moment can both pass the name check, a read before a write, as making a voice already documents.
- Search the generated-media lists (`c73f832`): the B-roll (by name or description), photo shoot, cleanup, lip sync (by name or audio), music (by name or track), and voice changer recordings (by name, the words of a text, or the voice) lists each have a search box, as the Assets page does, now through one shared hook: matched in one case and Unicode form, with how many match read out; one started or renamed during a search stays in view until the search changes, and focus after a delete moves to the next one shown.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed before the commit:
    - The box showed only for a list of two or more, so with a search typed and one of two deleted, clearing the box took it (and the focus in it) off the page. It now shows whenever the list has anything in it, which also makes the B-roll e2e step independent of clips other tests made.
  - Optional findings fixed: an empty list no longer shows "Nothing here matches" (and "0 of 0") beside its own empty state; the item focus moves to after a delete, and a recording being kept as a clip, are kept in view while the request is sent, so a search typed meanwhile cannot hide where the focus goes; the B-roll e2e step now checks that focus stays on Rename after renaming during a search.
  - Left as it is: a card a search does not match is taken off the page rather than hidden (unlike the Assets page, whose cards hold typed words), so a player in it stops; and an item announced as ready may be one the search is hiding.
- Quick find (`7704011`): Ctrl+K (⌘K on a Mac), or a Search button in the sidebar, opens one box over the studio's pages, the account's videos (searched on the server by title, description, script, presenter, or voice), and its own and the stock templates, from any studio page; the arrow keys move through the results, Enter opens one, and Escape (or a click outside) closes it and gives the focus back. It follows the combobox and listbox pattern, with the count read out, and the e2e test runs axe on it open and with nothing found.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems in the e2e test, fixed before the commit: a video an earlier test made from the "Property listing walkthrough" template also matched the template search, and the arrow keys could be pressed before the video search answered. The results now say they are busy (`aria-busy`) until the videos are in, and the test waits for that and checks the template among the results rather than as the only one.
  - Optional findings fixed: the last search's videos went only when the next answer came (and stayed after a refused one), so Enter could open a video the new search did not find; they now go as the search changes. A click outside gave the focus back while the dialog was still modal (it now does so once it has closed); Ctrl+K while open no longer starts over; on a Mac only ⌘K opens it (Ctrl+K deletes to the end of a line there), the key is matched by its place on any layout, Enter while composing text (IME) is left alone, and the sidebar's hint reads ⌘K; Escape in quick find no longer also closes the phone drawer beneath it; and with nothing found there is no empty list of results.
  - The axe check also caught a contrast failure of its own: the result kind ("Page", "Video") in the highlighted row was 4.26:1 on its tint, and is now darker.
- Delete several pictures at once (`ca6d9a8`): on the Assets page an editor can tick pictures (or "Select all" / "Select all shown" during a search, which adds to what is picked) and delete them together, one at a time through the existing delete (each also clears the picture from templates and the brand kit), with progress read out. Only pictures the search shows count, so one picked and then hidden by the search is neither deleted nor unpicked; a picture already gone counts as deleted, and one that could not be deleted stays selected, with the reason shown. Viewers see no checkboxes.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - The e2e step did not prove what it claimed: "Select all shown" replaced the selection, dropping the hidden pick before the delete. It now adds to the selection; the test keeps a hidden pick through the delete and checks it survives, checks both deleted pictures are gone at the API, and covers a partial failure (one delete answered 503) and the retry.
    - The focus went to the Pictures heading at the end even if the person had moved on (typing a search, say); it now goes there only from the selection bar or nowhere.
    - The focused Delete button changed its label (and could disappear) as pictures went; it reads "Deleting…" and stays until the run ends. Progress reads "Deleting 1 of 3…" rather than starting at 0, and the message when every selected picture failed no longer says "Deleted 0 of 1".
    - The checkbox and the picture's name share a label, a larger target on a phone; a second click before React re-renders cannot start a second run; and the README puts the new action among the editors' rather than after the viewers' sentence.
- Conversations as scenes (`d538f38`): a Conversation button beside Import script opens a box for a written conversation ("Alex: Welcome back." / "Sam: Thanks for having me."), and each turn becomes a scene, spoken by its speaker. Each speaker (up to six) is cast with a presenter and a voice: the first is the video's own, and each other is offered a presenter, and a voice in the video voice's language, that neither the video nor an earlier speaker is offered, which a choice for one speaker never changes for another. Lines without a name carry the turn on; a speaker's lines in a row are one scene; a stage direction in parentheses on a line of its own, or opening a turn, is not spoken; names are read in bold, in brackets, numbered, after a transcript's time, with a note in parentheses ("ALEX (V.O.):"), and with a full-width colon, and a link, a time, or a line of prose before a colon is not a name. Written scenes are replaced only once confirmed; a conversation longer than a video holds keeps what fits and says so, cut where an imported script would be; a single turn's presenter and voice become the video's, since one scene shows no presenter of its own.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, fixed before the commit:
    - A speaker whose offered presenter had no voice of its own was given the first voice no one had, whatever its language, so in demo mode the fourth to sixth speakers of an English conversation were offered Spanish, French, and Japanese voices. Only voices in the video voice's language are offered now, the presenter's gender first, and with none left a speaker keeps the video's voice.
    - The README promised more than the code does: that no two speakers could share a presenter (a choice can repeat one) and that every stage direction is left out. It now says what is offered, and a direction that opens a turn is now left out as well as a line of only one.
  - Optional findings fixed: a turn cut at the length limit could end in half an emoji, half a pause marker, or a fragment of a word; it is now cut by the script import's own rule (after a sentence, else between words, never a fragment). Numbered lines ("1. Alex:"), a transcript's times ("[00:01:15] Alex:"), and single emphasis ("*Alex:*") are read as names; too many speakers are now named, so a misread line can be found; Escape closes the panel and the button says what it controls; "No speakers found" waits for a pause in the typing instead of appearing at the first letter; a pasted conversation longer than the box takes is reported as cut rather than silently shortened; and the tests now check the offers against the video's own presenter and voice and each other, their language, and each speaker's voice at the server.
  - Left as it is: a line in title case before a colon ("Tip: …", "Spring Market Update: April") reads as a speaker, as a skimming reader would take it; the speakers are listed before anything is made, and the unit tests record the limit.
- Split and merge scenes (`3efcb09`): a Split button beside Pause cuts the selected scene at the cursor, the words after it becoming the next scene with the same presenter, voice, background, layout, and pace (the on-screen text stays with the first), and the cursor goes to its start; a Merge button beside Duplicate joins a scene with the next, the first keeping its settings (and taking the next one's on-screen text when it has none), after asking when the next renders with a presenter, voice, background, or other setting the first does not. A scene playing a recorded clip is neither split nor merged. A merge that leaves one scene hands its presenter and voice to the video, as its pace already was, since a lone scene shows none of its own.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed before the commit:
    - The merge question compared stored fields, not what renders: it asked about a presenter neither scene shows, or the voice of a scene of only pauses, and said "its own presenter" of a scene using the video's. It now names only what the next scene renders with, and reads "keeps its presenter; scene 2's is dropped".
    - A split could leave a side of only a short pause (a silent scene under a second) and a merge could make one over a minute, either blocking Generate; both are refused now, with a note saying why.
    - A split turns the cut into a blank line between scenes, which could take a script at the limit over it; it is refused then. A cut inside a word ("Wel|come") is refused too, while text written without spaces (Chinese, Japanese) can still be cut between any two characters.
    - With a clip scene next after a merge, the focused Merge button became disabled and the focus fell to the page; it now goes to the merged words whenever the button can no longer merge. The README says what the code does, and the tests cover hidden presenters, silent scenes, mid-word cuts (in the browser too), and the exact question.
  - Left as it is: a selected range splits at its start (the selection goes to the new scene), and a note repeated word for word is not read out again, as with the import notes.
- Undo and redo scene changes (`eb68780`): a scene added, removed, moved, duplicated, split, or merged, and scenes imported from a script, slides, or a conversation, can be undone and redone with two buttons that name the change ("Undo remove scene 2") or with Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y (⌘Z and ⌘⇧Z on a Mac) outside a text field, whose own undo takes back typing. The video's presenter, voice, pace, and format come back with the scenes when the change set them; the first scene a step changes is selected; undoing over edits made since asks first. What the editor starts from (a draft, a template, a copy, a plan, a clip, a picture) is not a change to undo, and starting over clears the history.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, fixed before the commit:
    - Undo restored the scenes but not the video's settings the change had set: undoing a merge down to one scene (or a one-speaker conversation) left every other scene on the merged scene's presenter and voice, a removal that left one scene left its pace on the video, and an undone PDF import kept the deck's format. Each change now records the presenter, voice, pace, and format with the scenes, and a lone scene's pace becomes the video's as part of the change, so the record is what the editor shows.
    - Confirming an undo over later edits lost them for good, as Redo brought back the change without them. The edits are now kept with the undone change, so redoing brings them back (and an undo of a redo does the same the other way); the question says so.
  - Optional findings fixed: loading a copy or a template after a quick change could let Undo wipe it, so every load clears the history; on a German keyboard Ctrl+Y (the key where Z is elsewhere) undid rather than redid, so the letter is read from the layout; the buttons were disabled for viewers while the keys worked, and now both work as the other scene controls do; a choice that changes nothing (the colour already picked) no longer counts as an edit made since; a control an undo takes away (a scene's Merge or Remove) no longer drops the focus to the page; sliders and checkboxes no longer block the keys; and the tests check that Ctrl+Z in the script leaves its words, Ctrl+Y, the round trip, and the presenter coming back after a merge.
  - The full run also caught two existing e2e steps whose button names ("Add scene", "Conversation") the Undo button's name now contains ("Undo add scene 2"); those selectors, and "Duplicate scene", match exactly now.
  - Left as it is: a PDF import's uploaded pages stay among your pictures when the import is undone, and the video's title is not part of the history.
- Brand kit closing scene (`f344160`): the brand kit can hold a closing scene, what the presenter says at the end (a call to action, up to 1000 characters, pauses allowed) and text on screen such as a website, and the editor's Closing scene button adds it at the end of a video on the kit's background with the text at the bottom of the frame, in one click that Undo takes back. A new video's one untouched scene becomes the closing scene rather than staying blank before it; the on-screen text is read as a scene's (one line, counted as a reader sees it) and set in the small size when the medium one would not fit the video's format; text without words is refused, as a scene needs something to say.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed before the commit: Remove brand kit cleared every field on the brand page but the closing scene's, so the next save brought back the closing scene just removed. Both fields clear now, and the e2e test checks it.
  - Optional findings fixed: the closing text was added in the medium size, which overflows a portrait or square frame for most real text (the brand page's own example among it), so it drops to the small size when needed and the brand page says about 20 characters fit there; a lone scene with a picture or video of its own, or with the presenter hidden, was taken over as "blank" (it is now followed instead); a second click added the closing scene again (it now says it is already last); the kit's text was checked by code units with its spaces kept, unlike a scene's, so it is now read the same way; the new fields' accessible names no longer include their hints, which describe them instead; the button is called Closing scene everywhere; the README no longer lists it among what applies to every new video; and the tests cover the background, the second click, the removal, long silences, and the text rules.
  - Left as it is: as with the kit's other optional fields, a save that leaves the closing scene out removes it (the API reference says so); and no renderer draws on-screen text yet, so a rendered closing scene says its words over the brand background without the text.
- Drag scenes to reorder them (`3fe946a`): a scene's tab dragged onto another moves the scene there, the others keeping their order (forward or back), as a change Undo takes back ("Undo move scene 3 to 1"), with the moved scene selected and the focus on its tab; the tab under the pointer is ringed, and the Move left and right buttons still move a scene a step at a time by keyboard or touch. One scene, a viewer's editor, and an import under way have nothing to drag.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed before the commit: each tab carried its drag as plain text ("scene 3"), so a tab let go a little low, over the script just below the row, typed those words into the script. The drag now carries a type of its own that only another tab takes.
  - Optional findings fixed: the drag tracked a position rather than a scene, so scenes changed mid-drag (an import finishing) could move another scene or leave the drag stuck; it now carries the scene, is forgotten when the scenes change, and tabs cannot be dragged during an import. The ring is a darker colour set off from the tab (it was under 3:1), and no longer flickers over the tab's own dot and duration; the dragged tab's picture is taken before it fades; the hint names the arrow buttons too, as it is read out to people who cannot drag; and the e2e test now drags forward as well as back, checks the focus, a single scene, and a real drop of a tab on itself (the earlier one never left the spot, so it proved nothing).
  - The full run caught the e2e step checking the focus only after clicking two other tabs (which takes it); the check now follows the drop, and the tab also takes the focus once the drag has ended.
  - Left as it is: the arrow buttons still move a scene without a note, and a note repeated word for word is not read out again, as with the other notes.
- Use a scene's background on every scene (`9b21567`): with more than one scene, the Background tab offers "Use on every scene", which gives the other scenes the selected scene's background (a colour, a picture, an uploaded picture, or a clip, with its fit) as one change Undo takes back and Redo gives again. A scene without the presenter keeps it out under a picture or clip and gets it back under a colour, which cannot fill the frame in its place; the note says which scenes did. Once every scene has the background, the button gives way to a line saying so. Colours match in any case and a picture's absent fit counts as "cover", so nothing that already looks the same is changed or offered. The button is off for a viewer, during a slide import, and while the scene's generated clip is gone or not ready.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed before the commit: the button disappears once every scene has the background, so a keyboard user's focus fell to the page. The focus now goes to Undo, which names the change.
  - Optional findings fixed: the tooltip read "every scene scene 1's" and is now "Give the other scenes this background (and its fit)", with none for a viewer and the clip hint described when the clip is missing; the note says "color", as the tab does, and "the presenter's place"; the line shown once all match no longer contradicts "Each scene keeps its own background"; the action reads the scene shown rather than a position that could be past the end; colours compare in any case and ignore a stray fit; the comments listing undoable changes name this one. The e2e test now checks the focus, that one Undo restores both pictures, the fit, and the hidden presenters while scene 1 keeps its colour (and the history step before it is still there), Redo, the note naming two scenes, and that the scripts reach the render unchanged.
  - Left as it is: while the clip list is still loading (or failed to load), a missing clip is not yet known and can be copied, as the clip hint behaves; the server refuses such a render with a message. The closing-scene check keeps its own comparison, which deliberately ignores fit.
- Add scene puts the new scene after the selected one (`0606bf4`): as in HeyGen, a scene added while scene 2 of 4 is selected becomes scene 3, on scene 2's background, and is selected, rather than going to the end on the last scene's background; with the last scene selected it goes at the end as before. A note says what moved ("Added scene 2 after scene 1; scenes 2–3 are now 3–4.", or "scene 3 is now scene 4" for one), the button's title says where the next scene will go, and Undo takes the inserted scene out in one step, as with Duplicate and Split, which already inserted after the selected scene.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. It confirmed that all of the other e2e tests that click Add scene do so with the last scene selected, so none of them now tests something else.
  - Optional findings fixed: an add at the end clears a note left by an earlier change instead of leaving it to describe the wrong thing; the note names the scenes that were renumbered rather than "the scenes after it"; at the 20-scene limit the disabled button's title says why, and adding the 20th scene moves the focus to its tab rather than dropping it on the page; the README says where a new scene goes. The e2e test no longer assumes the default colour (a brand kit left from another run would have failed it), and also checks that scene 1 keeps its colour after Undo, the note for a single renumbered scene, and the cleared note after an add at the end.
  - Left as it is: Undo of an insert selects the scene after the one that was selected (the first scene the step changes), as Undo of Duplicate and Split do; a note repeated word for word is not read out again, as with the other notes; where the next scene goes is shown in the button's title and the note, not as visible text beside the button.
- Copy a scene and paste it into another video (`7226d37`): Copy scene, beside Duplicate, keeps the selected scene (its words, background and fit, presenter and framing, voice, pace, clip, and on-screen text) in the browser, and Paste scene, beside Add scene, puts it after the selected scene in this video or in another editor tab, which hears of the copy without a reload. A new video's one blank scene (no words, nothing chosen, a plain colour behind it) is replaced rather than left in front of it. A paste is one change Undo takes back. A copy names the space it was made in, and only that space offers it, as its pictures, clips, and recordings belong there; the server checks them again at Generate.
  - The copy is read back through `lib/scene-clipboard.ts`, which holds it to the server's limits (a pace of 0.5 to 1.5, ids of up to 64 characters, a background of up to 2000 that is a web address or the account's own picture or clip, a script within the video's 5000 characters), drops what a scene does not hold, and keeps a hidden presenter only over a picture or clip; the editor restores its layout and on-screen text as it does a draft's. Unit tests cover it.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: a lone scene's pace (which the editor keeps as the video's) is copied with it; a lone scene with a picture or clip behind it is no longer treated as blank and replaced; a paste that fills the video moves the focus to the new scene's tab; the copy keeps a hidden presenter's own presenter and framing, as Duplicate does, for when it is shown again; the parser matches the server's limits instead of passing a pace of 3 or a non-web picture through to a refused Generate; the Paste title leaves out pause markers, cuts the words without splitting a character, and says why it is off for a viewer or during an import; the note says "in this space"; an unused copy time was dropped; and the copy is read again when a tab is shown, focused, or brought back from the page cache, which can miss the storage event. The e2e test also checks Redo, and that a copy made again in the first editor reaches the second without a reload after a copy from another space was refused there.
  - Left as it is: a picture deleted between copy and paste is swapped for a plain background by the editor's existing check, outside the undo history, as in a draft; a tab that has not reloaded since its workspace was switched in another tab still treats the old one as current, as drafts do.
- Keyboard shortcuts (`55a4b4f`): Ctrl+/ (⌘/ on a Mac), or Keyboard shortcuts at the foot of the sidebar, opens a list of the studio's shortcuts with this computer's keys: quick find, Esc for the open menu, panel, or form, Enter to save a rename, the editor's Generate, undo, and redo (Ctrl+Y only off a Mac), and quick find's own keys. Escape, the same keys, Close, or a click outside shut it, the focus going back where it was. The list comes from `lib/shortcuts.ts`, which unit tests check against each computer's keys.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, fixed before the commit. The / key was also matched by its place on a US keyboard, where German, Spanish, and Nordic keyboards have "-", so Ctrl+- opened the list instead of zooming out; the place now counts only on a layout whose key types no Latin character. And the list showed ⌘Home and ⌘End for quick find's first and last result on a Mac, where quick find takes Ctrl+Home and Ctrl+End; it now shows those.
  - Optional findings fixed: the Esc row covers every menu, panel, and form that closes on it (the Improve menu, look packs, renames, and so on), and Enter saving a rename is listed; Generate's Ctrl/⌘+Enter, like undo, is left alone while a dialog is open, so Ctrl+Enter in quick find over the editor opens the result without also starting a render; quick find's Ctrl+K is left alone over the shortcuts list, so a result is never opened behind it; the list's own Ctrl+/ is not handled twice. The e2e test now records any render request while the list is open (the earlier URL check could not fail), and also checks Ctrl+K and Ctrl+- there, the Close button, and that quick find keeps the focus when Ctrl+/ is pressed over it.
  - Left as it is: on a very short screen the foot of the list can sit a little below the window, as quick find's can; the keys are read as their symbols ("Ctrl", "⌘", arrows); `aria-keyshortcuts` names both Control and Meta, as the Search button's does.
- Use a scene's presenter, voice, and pace on every scene (`76a7373`): with more than one scene, the scene presenter row offers to give the others the selected scene's presenter, voice, and pace as one change Undo takes back. The button says what it gives and whose ("Use this scene's presenter, voice, and pace on every scene", or "Use the video's …" when the scene uses the video's, which clears the others' own choices), names only what would change, and gives way once every scene matches, the focus going to Undo. The presenter goes to every scene (one without the presenter keeps it for when it is shown again); the voice and pace to every scene not playing a recorded clip, which keeps its audio. A scene gives its voice and pace only when they are heard (not from a clip or a silent hold). The logic is in `lib/scene-cast.ts`, with unit tests.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed before the commit: the button was named "Use on every scene", as the Background tab's is, and both can be on screen at once, so a screen reader listed two identical buttons doing different things. It is now named for what it gives, and the e2e test checks both with the Background tab open.
  - Optional findings fixed: giving "the video's" is said as such ("Scene 1 now uses the video's presenter, voice, and pace again") instead of as the scene's own; the note and the Undo label name only what changed; a silent hold no longer gives a voice it never speaks; a scene without the presenter takes the presenter too, so it holds when the presenter is shown again; the source is the selected scene by position rather than by object; the title says why the button is off during an import and is left off for a viewer. The unit tests also cover a hidden or silent source, unchanged scenes kept as the same objects, clearing one choice while setting another, and an out-of-range scene; the e2e test also checks giving the video's back and its note.
  - Left as it is: a photo avatar's default voice does not come along (each scene keeps the voice it is given); a close-up framing a photo avatar cannot do, and settings such as Avatar V or an accent that the new presenter or voice does not take, fall back as they do when a presenter is picked in each scene's own list, without a word in the note.
- Listen to all scenes (`dc2a095`): with more than one scene, a button beside Listen plays every scene in turn, from the first, before the video is made: each scene's words through the voice preview in its own voice and pace (cut at sentence ends into pieces of up to 600 characters, never inside a pause marker), a recorded clip as recorded, a silent hold for as long as it lasts. The scene being heard is marked on its tab and read out ("Playing scene 2 of 4"); Stop listening ends it at once, cancelling the request under way. The playlist and the cutting are in `lib/script-preview.ts`, with unit tests; the player is `hooks/useScriptPlayer.ts`.
  - An independent review found three blocking problems, fixed before the commit. The playlist was read from the scenes as they were, so a scene removed or added while it played left it playing words that were gone and selecting a scene that no longer existed (where typing then went nowhere): any change to the scenes now stops it. It moved the selection to each scene as it played, remounting the script box under the user's cursor so keystrokes were lost and Ctrl+Z undid a scene change: the selection now stays where it is and the playing scene is marked instead. And the voice picker's own preview could play over it, while a single-scene Listen still loading when stopped could start afterwards: each preview now stops the other, and the single preview has a run number so a stopped one never plays.
  - Optional findings fixed: Stop cancels the preview request in flight (so stopping and starting again does not pay twice for a scene still coming); every audio link made is let go, including one that failed to play or arrived after Stop; a stopped run never reports an error; the browser's utterance is held while it is said, as a dropped one may never say it ended; a recording that cannot be played names its scene; viewers, whom the preview refuses, cannot start it. The e2e test now times the silent hold, checks the marker and that the selection stays put, waits for the first scene to be said before stopping, and checks that an edit stops it.
  - The full run caught an older test checking that a clip scene shows no Listen button by a name that now also matched Listen to all scenes; it now looks for the scene's own Listen exactly.
  - Left as it is: with a provider that has no preview audio (HeyGen, and demo mode) the browser's own voice reads every scene, so scenes do not sound different; pauses inside a scene are read through on that path; nothing is fetched ahead, so there is a short gap between pieces and scenes; there is no cache, and the preview route has no rate limit of its own.
- ← and →, Home, and End on the scene numbers (`8e846dc`): on a focused scene number in the Scenes row, ← and → select the scene before or after and Home and End the first and last, the focus moving with the selection once the scene is selected; with a modifier the keys are left to the browser. The numbers carry `aria-keyshortcuts` and say so in their hint, and the keyboard shortcuts list names both pairs.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: Home and End went nowhere from a number that had the focus but was not the selected scene (the check compared with the focused number, not the selected one); the focus now moves after the selection, so the number it lands on is already the current one; the keys are announced (`aria-keyshortcuts`, and the hint names them); the list says "on a scene's number in the Scenes row" rather than "tab", which the page never shows; the README says ← and → rather than "the arrow keys", since ↑ and ↓ still scroll. The e2e test now uses four scenes so End is not just one step, checks ← moving back, the ends holding, Home from an unselected number, and both list entries; the unit test matches quick find's entry exactly and checks the scene row's.
  - Left as it is: the row is buttons rather than a tab list, so a screen reader in browse mode keeps the arrows for itself, and Home and End on a clicked number no longer scroll the page; the ends stop rather than wrap, unlike quick find's list.
- Scenes without words named, and blank ones removed in one step (`bd73ac3`): below the script, a notice names the other scenes that still have no words (a pause counts as words), which is why Generate is off, with Go to scene N, which selects the first and puts the cursor in its script, and Remove blank scene N, or Remove K blank scenes, for those with nothing of their own: no words, clip, on-screen text, presenter, voice, layout, or pace, and no picture but a colour or the one next to it. The removal is one change Undo takes back, the selected scene staying selected, the note naming the scenes that went, and the focus going to Undo. Viewers do not see it.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed. A scene counted as blank with only its script, clip, and on-screen text checked, so a PDF page without text (its picture as the background) or a scene with its own presenter, voice, layout, or pace would have gone; those now stay. The notice sat at the top of the page and named the scene just added, so Add scene moved the whole editor down, and typing the first letter in the last empty scene moved it back up; it now leaves out the selected scene and sits below the script, so it never moves the scene numbers or the script box.
  - Optional findings fixed: since the selected scene always stays, the code that kept one when every scene was blank is gone and the selection no longer jumps to scene 1; only the sentence is a live region, not the buttons with it; viewers no longer see a call to generate they cannot act on; the wording says what a pause does; error messages are no longer pushed below it. The e2e test now checks that a scene just added is not named, that a pause counts, the plural notice, button, and note, that a scene with its own pace or on-screen text stays, that the selection stays, the confirm Undo shows when scenes changed since, and Redo.
  - Left as it is: a clip scene with no words is told to add some or a pause rather than to type what the clip says; leaving one scene with its own presenter or voice no longer shows them in the editor (as with Remove scene); the button is off while slides import, though Add and Remove scene are not; Generate does not point at the sentence with `aria-describedby`.
- Every scene's script in one column (`30b3729`): with more than one scene, All scripts shows each scene's script in its own box, one after another, with the scene's number, length, and "no words yet" where it has none, to read and write the video through; the heading names the selected scene. A box clicked or written in selects its scene, so the stage, the scene's settings, and Pause, Split, and Write with AI follow it; the button again shows the selected scene's box alone, its words kept, and the view closes when the video is down to one scene.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed. A box selected its scene whenever it took the focus, so tabbing back from a box to Pause or Split passed through the boxes above and acted on scene 1; a box now selects its scene when it is clicked or written in, and the e2e test checks Shift+Tab leaves the scene being written selected and Pause writes into it. Selecting a scene changes the height of the scene's panels above the list (a clip scene's recorder, the pace and voice lists, the presenter layout), so a click could move the box out from under the pointer; the box is now held where it was on screen.
  - Optional findings fixed: the box and the highlight follow the selected scene even when the selection is past the end; the heading names the selected scene, and the list takes its name from it; the selected box is marked as current and brought into view when the scene is chosen from the Scenes row or a key; the view no longer opens again by itself after the video was down to one scene; the README says when a box selects its scene.
  - Left as it is: the boxes are keyed by position, so after a reorder a box keeps the height it was given; the on-screen text fields stay below the list, for the selected scene; clicking into another scene's box while recording still drops the recording, as leaving the scene always has; the wordless-scenes notice changes as the selection does.
- One scene per paragraph (`675066d`): the script toolbar's One scene per paragraph cuts the selected scene at its blank lines into a scene for each paragraph, each keeping the presenter, voice, background, layout, and pace, the on-screen text staying with the first; a paragraph of only pauses is a silent scene of its own when its length fits one, and otherwise joins the paragraph before it, as a script import does. It is one change Undo takes back, the first of the new scenes selected with the cursor in it, and the note names how the scenes after it are numbered now. The button is there for every scene that speaks, off with a hint when there is one paragraph, so it never appears mid-typing and moves the script box; a clip scene is not cut, as with Split and Merge, and the 20 scenes a video holds are kept to.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: a paragraph of only pauses too short or long for a silent scene hid the button with no reason given; it now joins its neighbour, as the file import does; a blank line inside a pause marker is no longer taken for a paragraph break; the note when the cut would pass 20 scenes says how many it would make; the button no longer appears and changes width while typing; its hint and the README say where the on-screen text goes and that a clip scene is not cut. The unit tests now cover Windows line endings, a pause marker holding a blank line, pause paragraphs joined either way, a script of only pauses, and that the scene cut is left as it was.
  - Left as it is: lines within a paragraph keep their line break rather than being joined with a space, as the import does; the button is not disabled ahead of time at the 20-scene limit, as Split is not; the e2e test does not cover the limit's note, the All scripts view, Redo, or a viewer.
- Download script (`737e0b9`): next to Import script, Download script saves every scene's words as a text file named after the video, the scenes apart on `---` lines, to read, share, or edit elsewhere; Import script reads it back a scene for each. Pause markers are kept, scenes without words are left out and named in the note, and a lone scene's paragraphs stay one scene. The note says it holds the words only (presenters, voices, backgrounds, clips, and on-screen text stay in the editor), and when Import script would not give the same scenes back: a script past 5000 characters is cut, and a silent scene too short or long joins the one before. The file name goes through the same cleaning as downloaded clips and pictures, now shared as `safeFileStem`.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: a line of a scene's own that the import reads as a scene break (`***`, `---`, `* * *`) came back as extra scenes, or a scene of only `***` vanished; such a line is now indented past what the import takes for a break, and the import trims it back. Unit tests cover these.
  - Optional findings fixed: the file name could split an emoji at the length cut (and then fail to encode), and kept invisible formatting characters such as a right-to-left override; it now uses the clips' and pictures' cleaner, and does not double `.txt`; the file opens with a byte order mark, so older Windows editors read it as UTF-8; the link is added to the page while clicked and its object URL kept for a minute, as some browsers read it after the click returns or once a save dialog is answered; the note says "Downloading" rather than claiming the file was saved; the button is off while slides import, and says why when no scene has words; the note, title, and README say only the words go and that line breaks within a paragraph become spaces. The unit tests now go through the bytes as the editor reads them (decodeScript), with non-ASCII text, and the e2e test imports the downloaded file itself.
  - Left as it is: line breaks within a paragraph are read back as spaces, as the plain-text import joins wrapped lines; a pause-only paragraph inside a scene joins the paragraph before it; a clip scene's words come back as a voiced scene; a video of only silent scenes downloads a file the import finds no script in.
- Move scene to (`3d25cbf`): with three or more scenes, a list beside the scene's arrow buttons moves the selected scene straight to any place in the order ("Place 1 (first)" … "Place N (last)", the scene's own place marked "here"), as one change Undo takes back, with a note, the focus staying on the list. The moves made from the list with its arrow keys while it has the focus are one change, so Undo takes the scene back to where it started, and a scene moved back to its place leaves no change at all; Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Shift+Z work from the list. Viewers, and the editor while slides import, cannot use it, as with dragging a scene.
  - An independent review found three blocking problems, all fixed. The arrow keys on a closed list choose each place they pass, so pressing ↓ three times made three moves and three changes to undo; the moves from the list now join into one, through a new `amendLast` in the scene history (unit tested). Ctrl+Z did nothing with the focus on the list, which the shortcut left to selects; this list is now let through. On a phone the list made the scene's row of buttons wider than the card, so the page scrolled sideways; the row now wraps, and the e2e test checks it at 390 px.
  - Optional findings fixed: the list follows the drag's rule for viewers and slide imports; the options read as places ("Place 3", "Place 4 (last)", "Place 1 of 4 (here)") rather than repeating "Move to". The e2e test now covers the arrow keys, Ctrl+Z from the list, the note and Redo after it, and moving back to the start.
  - Left as it is: the move arrow buttons, Duplicate, and Remove stay open to viewers, as before; the scene tab's hint does not mention the list.
- Brand kit caption style (`6c83d9f`): the brand page has the editor's caption style controls (position, size, look, and typeface), saved with the kit as `captionStyle` (the default style as no field). New videos in the editor start from it (with or without captions on by default, so turning them on uses it), batch rows are captioned in it unless a template brings its own, and Video Agent plans carry it into the editor, a video the agent renders straight away keeping it when it has captions. Restored drafts, copies, and saved templates keep their own. The brand page previews it and lists it under "Applied to new videos", and says that a style other than the default shows in the share player only, as HeyGen then leaves captions out of the file. The editor's caption controls and their helpers now live in a shared `CaptionStyleFields` component and `src/lib/caption-style.ts`.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed. On the batch page, setting the template back to None dropped the kit's style, so the rows rendered in the default one; None now goes back to the kit's. Remove brand kit reset every field but the caption style, which then rode into the next kit saved; it now goes back to the default, which the e2e test checks.
  - Optional findings fixed: a video the agent renders without captions no longer stores a style; the batch page reads the kit's style as this build knows it (a look from a newer build falls back) and leaves out a default one; the brand page's preview follows the style only with captions on; `docs/API.md` describes the field on `PUT /api/brand`, the batch page's use of it, and the Video Agent's; the unit tests cover the kit's style on a generated video with captions and without.
  - Left as it is: the batch page shows no sign of which caption style its rows will use; the preview shows the position and typeface, not the size or look; there is no unit test of the plan hand-off read from session storage.
- Brand kit opening scene (`17b17d4`): beside the closing scene, the brand page has an opening scene (what the presenter says first, and a title on screen), saved with the kit as `openingScene` under the same rules (a title without words refused, a silent scene from 1 to 60 seconds, 1000 characters of words and 120 of title). The editor's Opening scene button puts it at the start of a video on the kit's background, its title across the middle of the frame in the size that fits the format, taking over a new video's one blank scene; it is one change Undo takes back, scene 1 is selected, and the note says how the other scenes moved along. A second click says the first scene already is it, and a full video or a script past 5000 characters is refused with a note. The two scenes now share one schema in `brand.ts`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: an over-long title's message named the closing scene's field ("On-screen text"); both scenes' messages now name their scene; the opening scene's issues come first, as it sits first on the page; the title's hint says about 20 characters fit across a portrait or square frame, as the closing one does; the shared type's description covers both scenes; the button no longer shares the AI rewrite's sparkle icon.
  - Left as it is: the opening and closing scene functions in the editor are near copies rather than one function; the e2e test covers the plural renumbering note, not the singular one, nor the scene and character limits, a long title in a portrait frame, or Redo.
- Brand kit colours for on-screen text (`186c45e`): beside the colour picker for a scene's on-screen text, the editor offers white and the brand kit's primary and accent colours as one-click swatches, the one in use marked pressed. A brand colour that repeats one before it (a white primary, an accent the same as the primary) is left out, and with no brand kit there is only the picker. When the text's colour hardly shows on the scene's plain colour background (under 1.5:1 contrast, as the kit's primary does on a new video's primary background), a note says so. The swatches come from `textSwatches` and the check from `textHidden` in `scene-text.ts`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: text the colour of its background now gets a note; the group is named "Colour swatches" rather than "Brand colours" (white is not a brand colour), and the swatches are named without their hex, which stays in their tooltip; the swatches' outline is darker, so the white one shows on the white panel; the e2e test waits for the brand kit before checking that no swatches show, checks that the kit was saved, and counts the swatches directly; the module's header covers its new helpers.
  - Left as it is: the swatches' unused default in the component; no e2e test checks the swatches for a viewer or from the keyboard, both plain disabled buttons as the picker is.
- One scene's on-screen text style on every scene (`d1ffe0a`): beside a scene's on-screen text controls, Use this text style on every scene gives the other scenes' on-screen text its position, size, and colour, each keeping its own words, as one change Undo takes back; scenes with no words on screen are left alone. The note names the scenes changed and, where the style no longer works, those whose text is now too long for one line across the frame or hardly shows on their plain colour background. The button goes once every scene's text matches, and the focus moves to Undo; it is held for a viewer and during a slide import. The helpers are `sameTextStyle`, `withTextStyle`, and `textStyleTargets` in `scene-text.ts`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: the note uses the editor's `scenesPhrase` (with its serial comma) and reads "Scene 2 now shows its on-screen text as scene 1 does" rather than a possessive list; the note also names scenes where the new colour hardly shows on their background; the button is a secondary button like the other "on every scene" ones, disabled with "Wait for the import to finish" during a slide import rather than gone; the e2e test covers Redo; the README line and the comment listing undoable changes are updated; the selected scene's index is shared with the presenter row's.
  - Left as it is: `withTextStyle` normalises the style itself instead of going through the editor's `restoredOverlay` (both store defaults as absent and colours in lower case); the e2e test does not cover the hidden-colour note, which the unit tests of `textHidden` do, nor text moved to where the captions sit.
- Scene names (`7fd130d`): with two scenes or more, the editor has a Scene N name field (up to 40 characters as a reader counts them, so a flag or an emoji with a skin tone is one). The name shows on the scene's tab and in its accessible name ("Scene 2, Kitchen, about 3s"), is kept with the draft, and is sent with the video as `scenes[].name`, which the API stores with its spaces collapsed (a blank one as none) and refuses past 40 characters. On the share and video pages a named scene's chapter takes the name as its title before its on-screen text or first words, and a named silent scene is a chapter of its own. Templates (made from a video or spelled out), copied and pasted scenes, retries, and other sizes keep the names; a split keeps the name with the first part, and a merge keeps the first scene's (or the second's when the first has none), saying when the second's goes. The video page's scene list shows each name; no renderer receives it.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: a draft or a lone scene with only a name now counts as having something to keep, so autosave keeps it and an import asks before replacing it; a merge compares the names as they are kept, so one of only spaces is none and two differing only in spaces are the same; a video brought down to one scene keeps its name field while the scene has a name, so it can be cleared; names are counted as a reader counts characters in the API, the editor, and restored drafts, the field cutting a long paste at whole characters and leaving an input method's composition alone; the raw name is capped before it is cleaned up; the e2e check of the second chapter's title matches it whole; two comments are back above the code they describe.
  - Left as it is: a lone scene's tab still shows its name until it is cleared.
- Markdown headings name the imported scenes (`0393598`): a Markdown script's heading that starts a scene now names it (the name on its tab and its chapter's title), so "## The kitchen" gives a scene called "The kitchen". A heading that only numbers its part ("Scene 2", "Part #3.", "2") names nothing, a numbering prefix is dropped ("Scene 2: Kitchen" and "1. Kitchen" give "Kitchen") while a decimal or range stays part of the name ("Section 2.1 Overview"), a heading of only markup names nothing, and pause markers are left out. The names hold only while each scene is one section of the document: when a pause-only section joins its neighbour or more sections than a video has scenes are packed together, the scenes are left unnamed. The import note says how many were named ("Imported 3 scenes from tour.md, 1 named from its heading."). `scenesFromText` returns them as `names`, and plain-text files and Word documents give none.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: a decimal or range number is no longer cut into the name ("Section 2.1 Overview" had given "1 Overview"); a heading with no letter or digit (`## **`, `## ---`) names nothing; a bare number and "Chapter", "#" numbering count as only numbering a part, and "1. Welcome" gives "Welcome"; the note reads "each named from its heading" when every scene is named; a document without headings is no longer read twice; pause markers are left out of names; the editor's comment on Word imports says their headings do not name scenes.
  - Left as it is: one pause-only section that joins its neighbour leaves every scene unnamed rather than carrying the names through the join; only English words are taken as numbering a part; Word headings and setext second-level headings still name nothing.
- Scene names in Move scene to and All scripts (`e5daa65`): the Move scene N to list names the scene now at each place ("Place 3 (last): The garden"), whose place the moved scene takes, and All scripts heads each named scene's box with its name ("Scene 2 · The garden"), the box described by that header so a screen reader reads the name with it.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed: the list is capped in width, so a long name neither widens the toolbar nor makes it jump while the name is typed; a long name in a box header wraps and the scene's length stays in view; the box is described by its header; the options use a colon, which screen readers speak plainly, where a middle dot may be read out; the new e2e test picks a place by its value, not its text; the code comment says the name is the scene there now.
  - Left as it is: the existing Move scene to test still picks "Place 3" by its text, which holds while its scenes have no names.
- Retitle a made video's chapters (`9777a92`): the video page's Chapters card has Edit titles for editors, one field per scene whose placeholder is what the chapter is called without a name, saving the scenes' names without rendering again; the share page, the chapter list, and the YouTube copy take the new titles at once. `PATCH /api/videos/:id` takes `sceneNames`, new names keyed by scene number (`{ "2": "The kitchen", "3": null }`), so only the scenes named change; a video of one scene or a scene number it does not have is a `400` and nothing else in that request is written; a changed name drops an approval, as a changed title does. A two-scene video with no chapters yet (its second scene silent) shows the card too, and naming that scene gives it one. `sceneChapters` in `chapters.ts` gives each scene's chapter, with or without its name, and `videoChapters` is built on it. The scene-name unit tests now set the store global the app reads (`__avatarStudioStore`).
  - Independent reviews found five blocking problems over three rounds, all fixed before the commit: names refused after the cover was already written or removed (validation now comes first); a status poll landing after the save and putting the old names back (the page refetches, as its other savers do); the focus lost when the form opened and closed (it goes to the first field and back to Edit titles, or the heading); any other edit (a password, a cover, a title) writing back the scene names it had read before a rename landed (every write now takes the scenes as stored at that moment, which a unit test that fails without the fix checks); and the form sending back, as its own, a name another editor saved while it was open (it compares with the names it opened with). Optional findings fixed: only changed names are sent; whether an approval goes is judged against the stored names; the placeholders come from the same code as the chapters, saying when a scene is no chapter (silent, or starting within a second of the one before); the fields are disabled while saving, with the focus returned after a failure; an input method's composition is left whole; the form closes if the video stops being editable; Escape closes it; the labels read "Scene N title"; a scene number over 20 is refused by the schema; the saved message is shown, not only announced.
  - Left as it is: a slow edit (a password or a cover) that read a video while it rendered can write back its "processing" status after the render completed, which predates this change; the per-instance write lock narrows such races rather than closing them across instances.
- Fix: a key pressed straight after another on a scene's tab (End, then →) starts from the tab the first one went to (`7318ded`). The tab keys moved the focus a frame later, so a second key landing before that frame was handled on the tab being left: End then → selected scene 2 instead of staying on the last. The selection is now committed and the focus moved before the handler returns. CI's run of the tab keys test failed on this while the push run passed on the same commit.
- Chapter reach (`771ba2a`): the video page's Chapters card shows, beside each chapter, how many share-page viewers played it ("3 viewers", which a screen reader hears as "3 viewers played this chapter"), once any viewer has played one. The share-page player (embeds too) reports each chapter the viewer plays, once per chapter per page load, through the queue its quarter beacons use: a chapter counts once playback has run on within it, or on from it into a later one, with no seek between (so a chapter too short for two time updates of its own still counts), and one only scrubbed through or landed on while paused does not (`chaptersPlayed` in `chapter-play.ts`, unit-tested); a muted autoplay (an embed's background loop) counts none until the sound is turned on; and a chapter timed past the clip's real end, which the page does not list, is none. `POST /api/videos/:id/watch` takes `{ "scene": n }` beside `{ "quarter" }`, the scene the chapter opens counted from 0, counts it once per viewer per chapter per 10 minutes for a completed video whose link is online and unlocked, and only for a scene that is a chapter (a silent scene of its own, or one starting within the same second as the chapter before, is none), and answers only `{ "counted" }`. The counts are kept on the video as `chapterViews`, keyed by scene so a retitled chapter keeps its count, and are among the counters no owner edit or provider update rolls back. Chapters carry the scene they open (`scene` in the share page's `chapters`).
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed before the commit: the end-to-end test could see the player run on into the next chapter before it paused (it now plays the last chapter); and a video shared before this change would have shown "0 viewers" on every chapter beside a share of all its views, most of which predate chapter counting (counts now show only once a chapter has been played, with no share of views). Optional findings fixed: the player counts only the chapters the page lists and only real playback (not a scrub, a paused seek, or a muted autoplay); the beacon no longer answers with a chapter's count, which a password-protected link's visitor could read; the count's meaning is in visible and screen-reader text rather than a tooltip; a test that a chapter within the same second as the one before is not counted; and the README and API docs (the `400` for a scene of 20 or more, what "per viewer" means, the answer) corrected.
  - A second review found nothing blocking; from it, a muted autoplay's not counting is said under the list, short chapters are counted as above, and Edit titles (from the chapter titles change) now focuses its first field before its handler returns, so letters typed straight after Enter are no longer lost.
  - Left as it is: the server counts any chapter `videoChapters` gives, including one timed past the clip's end, which only the player can see and leaves out; counts can still be lost when two server instances write the same video at once, since the write lock covers one instance, as for the other share counters (a store-side increment would close it); and the per-video ceiling (600 per chapter per 10 minutes) is the one the quarter beacons have.
