- Chapters as a WebVTT file (`8eb015f`): `GET /api/videos/:id/captions?format=chapters` gives a video's chapters as a WebVTT chapters file, the form video players and hosts read, under the captions route's rules (public on an open share link, the owner only while it is offline, the password or email gate's cookie on a protected one): one cue per chapter from its start to the next one's and the last to the video's end, titled on one line and escaped as cue text, with whole-millisecond times; a chapter starting in the last half second or past the end is left out, as the share page's list leaves it out (`listedChapters` in `chapters.ts`, which that list and the chapter-reach counting now use too); a video left with fewer than two chapters is a `404`, and `lang` with it a `400`. The share page's player carries the file as its default chapters track, and the video page's Chapters card has a Chapters file link for a made video.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed before the commit: a renderer's fractional length (HeyGen and D-ID report seconds with decimals) wrote a last timestamp such as `00:00:09.876.5400000000009`, which a WebVTT parser drops, losing the last chapter (times are now rounded, with a unit test). Optional findings fixed: the chapters track had no `default`, so browsers left it disabled and never fetched it (it now loads, which the end-to-end test checks by its cues); with it on, the transcript under the player could follow it instead of a translated subtitle track, since some browsers mark it showing (the transcript now follows only tracks of words); the file, the share page's list, and the chapter-reach counting now leave out the same chapters, and the `404` is judged on the chapters kept; the Chapters file link shows only for a made video out of the trash, as the captions link does; the password test checks the chapters file's `401`, its unlock, and the owner's access; a test that looked up the player's track by tag alone now names the subtitles track; and the README and API wording.
  - Left as it is: the track's language is marked English whatever the script's, as the captions track is; and in an embed, a gated or password-protected video's chapters file needs the gate's cookie, which a browser blocking third-party cookies does not send, as for its captions.
- A share-page description drafted from the script (`f12a1f5`): the video page's Description card has Write it from the script for editors, which fills the field with a draft of one paragraph that the script provider writes from the video's title and script (pause markers left out; Claude when configured, asked for one to three plain sentences from the script's facts in its language, with the title and script given as content rather than instructions; the demo provider takes the script's opening sentences, as many as fit about 220 characters), cut at a word to fit the description's 500 characters as the field counts them. Nothing is saved until the editor saves it, the focus goes to the field, and a status line says it is a draft. `POST /api/videos/:id/describe` gives `{ "description" }` (editors only); a cinematic shot, a video HeyGen's Video Agent directed, or one of only pauses is a `400`, and a trashed video a `409`. `ScriptProvider` gains `describe`.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed before the commit: a draft landing after the editor had started typing replaced their words (the field is now read-only while the draft is written, and words typed and not saved are replaced only after a confirmation). Optional findings fixed: wrapping quotes are stripped only as one matching pair of double quotes with none inside, so `"Fresh" paint … "must-see"` and `'Tis … the Smiths'` keep theirs, and an empty answer is an error rather than an empty draft; the Anthropic call has the room its thinking needs and messages of its own for a refusal or a cut-off answer; the script reaches the model between delimiters, as content to describe; a trashed video is refused like the other actions on one; the button is not offered for a video of only pauses or a HeyGen template video; a failure is announced; the spinner replaces the button's icon while it works; and the API wording.
  - Left as it is: the draft's language follows the script as the model reads it, with no language to pick.
- CI: the e2e step has 24 minutes and the job 35 (`a5b309d`), since the suite, run one test at a time, now takes about 14 minutes and hit the step's old 14-minute limit on `0aad9f6` with every test that ran passing.
- Share links that start at a time (`7a03f86`): `/share/:id?t=…` starts the share page's player, an embed's too and behind the password or email form, at that time once it knows the clip's length: `90`, `12.4`, `1m30s`, `1h2m3s`, `1:30`, or `1:02:03` (`parseStartTime` in `share-time.ts`); a time past the end or within its last half second, one given twice, or one that is not a time plays from the beginning. The start applies once a page load, also when the page's video had its length before the page's script ran. Each chapter on the video page's Chapters card of a made video has a button copying a share link that starts there, a tenth of a second into it at most (to the millisecond when that would reach the next chapter), on the share origin the API reports, and the copy is announced each time.
  - An independent review found four blocking problems, fixed before the commit: the card's new state was declared after its early return, so a video going from one chapter to two, or the role loading, would have broken the page with a changed number of hooks; a chapter's link rounded down to the whole second started in the chapter before, which the list then marked as playing and the chapter-reach counting could count; opening a link counted watch-through quarters without anything played, since moving the playhead fires a time update while paused (quarters now count only while playing, so a seek while paused no longer counts either); and a repeated `t` crashed the share page (it is now no start). Optional findings fixed: a clip whose length is known only after its metadata still starts at the link's time; the copy button has a 24-pixel target and a darker icon; the link is joined onto a base that already has a query; the API wording; and tests for the clock forms refused, a repeated `t`, the chapter marked after a jump, and the watch-through left alone.
  - Left as it is: a chapter's link is offered while the share link is off or expired, as the page's other share buttons are.
- Titles suggested from the script (`64bb292`): the video page's Suggest titles button (editors, for a video with words of its own) has the script writer offer up to three titles from the video's title and script (pauses left out; Claude when configured, asked for three angles under 70 characters, else the script's first three sentences), shown under the header; picking one opens the title field with it, saved as a typed title is (Enter or leaving the field) or left with Escape, which brings the ideas back. `POST /api/videos/:id/titles` returns `{ titles }` and renames nothing; each is one line without list marks, wrapping quotes or bold, or a closing full stop, cut at a word to the title's 120 characters, without repeats, a preamble line, or the current title. The description draft shares the checks and the cut with it, unchanged.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Optional findings fixed before the commit: the button sat inside the page heading, which then read as the title plus "Suggest titles" (it is now with the page's actions); Dismiss dropped the focus to the page (it goes back to the button); the ideas arriving after a slow answer took the focus from someone typing elsewhere, where a key pressed on an idea would open the title field with it (the focus moves only from the button); the hint said only Enter saves, while leaving the field saves too; an earlier error stayed after a rename; ideas asked for again were not announced again; list marks inside bold, bold or quotes before a full stop, an abbreviation's stop, a preamble line, and a current title written with a stop were tidied wrongly; and the demo writer's cut could split a character. Tests cover each.
  - Left as it is: like the description draft, the route has no rate limit of its own beyond the editor role.
- oEmbed for share links (`ddd0d5b`): `GET /api/oembed?url=<share link>` answers oEmbed 1.0 with a `video` whose `html` is an iframe of the share page's embed player (the password or email form first where the owner set one), 640×360, 360×640, or 480×480 for the frame shape, scaled down to `maxwidth` and `maxheight`, starting at the link's `t`, so a share link pasted into an editor, chat app, or CMS that reads oEmbed becomes the player. The share page names the answer in its head (`<link rel="alternate" type="application/json+oembed">`, keeping `t`), and readers that stop at `</head>` (WordPress, Iframely, Embedly, Notion, and others) get the page's metadata in the head unstreamed, with Next's own list. The thumbnail is the cover at its own size, now read from the picture's header when it is set (`width` and `height` on the video's `cover`), or the renderer's frame at the video's size, left out behind a password, past the consumer's limits, or for a cover set before its size was kept. A link that is off, expired, in the trash, missing, or not a share page of this studio is a `404`, `format=xml` a `501`; answers are public, readable from any origin, and cached for five minutes at most, never past the link's expiry.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking; its optional findings were fixed before the commit: a custom cover's thumbnail was given the video's size (the cover's own size is now read and kept, and a test asserts it), a thumbnail larger than the consumer's limits was still given, a link starting at a time named an answer starting at the beginning, the path was public by prefix (now exactly), errors other than a missing video lacked the answer's headers, and the docs now say WordPress sandboxes an unknown provider's iframe, where the password and email forms cannot be sent. Its concern that the discovery tag streams after the head for WordPress did not reproduce, and it is now in the head by configuration as well. A second review of the fixes found nothing blocking; from it, sides past 65535 pixels are not taken at their word, an older cover gives no thumbnail rather than the frame it replaced, and the API's cover fields are documented.
  - Left as it is: without a custom cover, the thumbnail is the renderer's own link, as the link preview's picture is, which a consumer keeping the answer long may find expired; a JPEG's size is as stored, not turned for its EXIF orientation (the studio's own upload redraws the picture first).
- Views by place (`fba1f38`): a share view now says where it played, on the share page a viewer opened or in a frame on another page (the Embed snippet's, an oEmbed player's, or any page framing the link), told by the player itself rather than by its address; the video keeps `embedViews` beside `views` (kept through edits as the other counters are), the video page's Share views row reads "12 · 4 in embeds", and the analytics file gains a last `embed_views` column. The view beacon takes `{ "place": "page" | "embed" }`; a body that is not JSON, none included, is a share-page view and other fields are ignored, so an older page's views still count, while a place that is neither is a `400`. The per-viewer window is the same wherever a view plays, so a forged place can only move a view, not add one.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking; its optional findings were fixed before the commit: the place came from the `?embed=1` address, so a framed share page counted as a page view and an embed address opened in a tab as an embed (the player now asks whether it is framed); the beacon refused bodies it had accepted before, losing those views; the new column sat in the middle of the analytics file, moving the columns after it (it is now last); the separator was read out and the row could be cut short at phone width; and the README seemed to say library cards show the split.
  - Left as it is: views counted before this change are all share-page views, as the place was not known.
- Make in another language (`e44bde9`): the video page's In another language (editors, a video with spoken words, not in the trash) opens `/create?from=:id&language=…`, a copy in the editor with every scene's script translated as the editor's Translate does (pauses kept, brand terms left as they are, the kit's fixed translations used, regional ones for a tag such as `es-MX`), four scenes at a time, and a voice that speaks the language for each voice the video speaks in (the same voice when it already does or speaks many, else one of its gender, else any; when none does the original's is kept and the editor says so), titled with the language within the title's limit. Clips, silent scenes, on-screen text, and scene names stay as they are. `POST /api/videos/:id/language-copy { language }` answers `{ language, scripts, voices }` and saves nothing; the language is taken by its English name, code, or tag and given to the script writer as its English name, anything else is a `400`; twenty copies an hour per account, counted only for copies that pass their checks.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, fixed before the commit: words typed while the copy was being translated were replaced when it landed (the editor is now held, inert, with Generate off and nothing autosaved, until the copy is translated), and a failed translation left the untranslated copy saved where the translated one belongs, so asking again never translated (a failure now saves nothing and offers Try again, which loads the video again, or a plain copy). Optional findings fixed: leaving mid-translation left Generate off for good; twenty scenes one after another could outlast the request (now four at a time with a deadline, and no more scenes start once one fails); any two or three letters passed as a language, and a code skipped the kit's fixed translations (the language is now resolved to its English name); the title could pass its limit; the control showed for a trashed video and for one whose only words are clips; multilingual voices were swapped out; the popover now closes on Escape and its hint is linked to its field; the note's live region stays in place so each change is read out. A second review of the fixes found nothing blocking; from it, a regional tag now keeps the kit's rules for its country (`Spanish (Mexico)`), a failure to load the video holds the copy too, the allowance counts only copies that pass their checks, and two doc comments went back where they belong.
  - Left as it is: a scene started just before the deadline still runs to its end inside the request's five minutes; several speakers can be given the one voice that speaks the language; the copy's draft is kept by the language as written, so `es` and `Spanish` keep separate drafts.
- Copies in another language translate on-screen text and scene names (`fc86a49`): `POST /api/videos/:id/language-copy` now also answers `overlays` and `names`, each scene's on-screen text and name (its tab and chapter title) translated as short labels, as short as they were with figures, dates, addresses, and the names of people and companies kept as written (the Claude script writer gets a label prompt for them, not the spoken-script one), cut to their 120 and 40 characters as a reader counts them; the editor applies them, the text keeping its place, size, and colour on screen. A label with no letters (`$450,000`) is kept as it is without asking, and one the script writer will not translate keeps its own words rather than failing the copy. The editor's note and the video page's hint now say the whole scene is translated.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking; from it, labels get their own prompt (the spoken-script one could spell out a price or pad a name into a sentence), letterless labels are skipped, a failed label no longer fails a twenty-scene copy, and two route comments and the API reference were brought up to date.
  - Left as it is: a translated label longer than its place is cut at its limit, which can fall inside a word.
- Brand kit writing style (`08e2ef0`): the brand kit takes a `writingStyle`, up to 600 characters on how the brand sounds (words to use or avoid, how formal, who it speaks to), set on the Brand page with a count that says when the limit is reached (and tells a screen reader once, near and at the limit). Claude follows it when it writes a script (`POST /api/script`, from a topic or a web page), plans a video in the Video Agent, and shortens, expands, polishes, or changes the tone of one. It comes after the request, fenced, and decides the wording and how formal the script is, over the writer's voice rules (contractions, short sentences) but not its format, with the length asked for still holding; the tone the Write with AI form always sends sets only the mood, while a tone picked for a rewrite wins. Translations, labels, share-page descriptions, title ideas, interactive avatars, videos HeyGen's agent directs, and the demo writer do not use it. Blank is none; removing the kit clears it.
  - An independent review found one blocking issue, fixed: the form's default Friendly tone outranked the style, so a formal brand never got a formal script. From its optional findings: the style may now override contractions and sentence length; `expand` no longer asks to keep the old voice when there is a style; quotes in the style cannot close its fence; the count moved out of the hint into its own element with a quiet announcement; the docs say where it does not apply; and tests now cover writing from a web page and the prompts Claude gets.
  - Left as it is: no test drives `/api/script` itself with a workspace's style (the services test covers the owner it is given, and the route passes `requireEditor()`'s owner as every other script route does).
- Share-page descriptions and title ideas in the brand's writing style (`af6c761`): `POST /api/videos/:id/describe` and `/titles` now give Claude the video owner's brand kit `writingStyle`, fenced as for scripts and placed before the title and script, so the drafted description and the suggested titles use the brand's words and formality; every rule they already had still holds (one to three sentences under 300 characters, three titles under 70, the script's facts only, no hashtags or markdown). A video of an account or workspace without a style is drafted as before. The Brand page hint, the API reference, and the README say where the style applies.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking; from it, the style note goes before the title and script (so nothing follows the video's own words), names the script's language among the rules that still hold, says "the style" rather than "the description", collapses quotes in the style as the script note does, the titles reference no longer reads as if the style replaced the demo fallback, and the tests now check that another account's kit without a style sends none to either draft.
- A writing style drafted from a brand's website (`0a65683`): with Claude configured, `POST /api/brand/import` also answers `writingStyle`, a draft of how the site writes (tone, formality, audience, favoured words), read from the text a reader sees on the page (its `<main>` first, without scripts, navigation, headers, footers, forms, or hidden elements), with the page's name and text fenced as content. It is best effort: a page with under 200 characters of such text, a `NONE` answer, a draft carrying a web or email address or a phone number, a refusal, a failure, or a call still running twenty seconds on (or past fifty seconds into the import, with no retry) leaves it out and the rest of the import stands; the demo writer drafts none. The draft is unwrapped of quotes around the whole and cut to 600 characters after the last whole sentence that fits. The Brand page fills the writing-style field, asks on its own before replacing one already there (Cancel keeps it), and marks the field "Drafted by Claude from … 's text" (linked to it for screen readers) until it is edited or saved.
  - `extractReadableText`, which also feeds scripts and Video Agent plans written from a page, now runs in time linear in the page's size whatever its markup: a page of unclosed tags, comments, or one long tag name took seconds to minutes of CPU before; it also no longer drifts on text whose lower case is longer (Turkish "İ" leaked script and navigation text and broke titles), and a self-closed `<svg/>` keeps the text after it. Hidden elements are dropped only for the brand's style; scripts written from a page keep an FAQ's folded-away answers.
  - Two independent reviews. The first found three blocking issues, all fixed: the extraction was quadratic (a crafted page held an import for about 40 seconds), the Claude call had no timeout, so a slow one could fail the whole import, and the replace question read wrongly with four fields. The second found two more in the rewrite, both fixed: one long tag name was still quadratic, and full lower-casing shifted positions on Turkish pages. From their optional findings: hidden text and planted contact details are kept out of the draft, quotes naming a word survive the tidy, NONE is honoured however it is written, a sentence end just past the limit no longer hides earlier ones, the contact filter allows the brand's own domain name, years, and figures, and a hand-written style is asked about on its own.
  - Left as it is: a `<` inside an attribute value (`x-show="count < 5"`) now leaves part of that tag in the text rather than stripping it; a hero tagline inside `<header>` is not read for the style.
- Interactive avatars in the brand's writing style (`74cef9a`): a saved presenter takes `brandStyle` (off by default, so presenters saved before keep their replies as they were), and with it on its public replies at `/talk/:id` are worded in the brand kit's `writingStyle`, read from the kit at each turn so an edit on the Brand page reaches a link already shared. Claude gets the style fenced in the system prompt, after the reply rules, which still hold, with the agent's language kept and the owner's instructions winning where the two differ. `POST /api/chat` takes `brandStyle: true` for the Interactive page's own try-it chat. The page has a checkbox when saving (its hint, outside the label, says whether the kit has a style and that the demo writer does not follow one) and a "Brand style on/off" toggle on each saved presenter (`aria-pressed`, its name holding its visible words). A kit that cannot be read at that moment leaves the style out rather than failing the visitor's turn.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking; from it, a failed kit read no longer fails a turn, the chat note says the style is guidance and not a request, keeps the reply language, and lets the owner's instructions win, the checkbox's hint is read once and only once the kit is known, the toggle's name holds its visible text, and the tests now cover a presenter saved before the switch and a patch that turns it off. The docs say that an edit to the style reaches live public replies at once and that a visitor can get it repeated back.
  - Left as it is: each turn with the switch on reads the brand kit row, as the talk page's own load already does; a cache would save that read under heavy traffic.
- Editing a saved presenter in place (`5394c2e`): each saved presenter on the Interactive page has an Edit button that loads it into the setup form (presenter, voice, name, instructions, language, opening line, suggested questions, knowledge page, email capture, and brand style), headed "Editing …", and Save changes sends `PATCH /api/agents/:id` with only the fields changed in the form, so its `/talk/:id` link, embed code, transcripts, counts, link state, and a switch flipped on its row meanwhile all stay. A cleared knowledge page is sent as `null`, and a "Read the page again" box re-reads an unchanged one; a save that changes nothing sends no request. Cancel editing, or a save, puts back the new-presenter draft that was being written and returns the focus. The try-it chat starts afresh on entering or leaving an edit, without remounting the live video, and a reply still in flight from before is dropped. A presenter or voice the agent was saved with that is no longer in the library is named in a notice, and Save stays disabled until another is picked; a voice it was saved without stays without one. While a save runs, the rows' Edit, Delete, and switches wait, and the server reads the agent again after its page read and presenter lookup, so a link turned off or a delete that landed meanwhile holds.
  - Two independent reviews. The first found three blocking issues, all fixed: the guard against a missing presenter never fired, the saved voice did not survive a round trip through the form, and a slow save could race an Edit, a row switch, or a delete. The second found two more, both fixed: keying the chat panel on the edited agent remounted it and cut a live video session, and re-picking the presenter already highlighted was not saved. From their optional findings: the presenter and voice are looked up before the agent is read for the write, a voice is no longer swapped in when the presenter changes during an edit, the draft keeps the current presenter when it had none, the row switches sync into an untouched form, and the notices are tied to the heading and Save button for screen readers. The setup form now scrolls into view at once rather than smoothly, so a click made while it moved no longer lands on a different presenter.
  - Left as it is: a suggested question containing a line break does not round-trip through the one-per-line field, and leaving an edit with unsaved changes asks no confirmation.
- Duplicating a saved presenter (`9ca513a`): `POST /api/agents/:id/duplicate` (editors; viewers get `403`) saves a copy of a shared presenter under a new id, so a new `/talk/:id` link and embed, named "… (copy)" and then "(copy 2)" and on (a copy of a copy counts on from the original's name, within the 60-character name limit; `copyTitle` takes the limit). The copy keeps the setup (presenter, voice, instructions, opening line, questions, language, email capture, brand style) and the knowledge page as it was read, without reading the page again, starts with no conversations, transcripts, or counts, and has its link off until it is turned on, so nothing new is public by surprise. It counts toward the 20 per account, another owner's presenter is a `404`, and the workspace feed says "copied a shared presenter as …" (`agent.duplicated`). The Interactive page has a Duplicate button on each saved presenter (busy while it runs, disabled for viewers and while a save runs) and says the copy's name and that its link is off.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the copy is built from a list of setup fields, so a field added later for one presenter's own record is not carried over unnoticed; the feed no longer calls a copy with its link off "shared"; the note beside the list clears when a presenter is edited, switched, or deleted; the clicked button shows that it is busy, and its tooltip says it copies the saved setup; the docs name the viewer `403` and the race between two requests at the same moment; and the unit test checks that transcripts and the page's read time stay right.
  - Left as it is: the copy does not check that its presenter and voice are still in the library (the Edit form names one that is gone and holds Save until another is picked), and two copies requested at the same moment can between them pass the limit or take the same name, as two creates can.
- Searching a presenter's transcripts (`e74aaa2`): the Interactive page's transcripts panel has a search over each conversation's messages and the visitor's email and name (in one case and Unicode form, with "N of M" read out as it changes) and an "Only visits that left an email (N)" box, disabled while no visitor has left one. With the box on, the search counts only the visits it leaves, so a visit it hides never reads as a match or a miss. Both start cleared each time the panel opens. The panel now also answers only its latest opening: a slow load, or a Delete all still running, for one presenter no longer fills or empties another's panel, and closing the panel or deleting its presenter leaves a load still under way with nothing to fill and no error to show.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the search counts against what the box leaves (it had said "Nothing here matches" for a visit the box hid, and could show that beside a second empty message), Delete all and Close are covered by the same guard as opening, the search box keeps its usual width with the box on its own line below, the box is disabled rather than explaining an empty result, and the test now leaves a search typed before closing, so it shows the reopened panel clears it, and scopes its Close to the panel.
  - Left as it is: the CSV download is still every conversation, whatever is searched; and the search runs over the 50 newest visits the panel lists, not older ones.
- Reference notes for a presenter (`c54e284`): a shared presenter takes `notes`, up to 6000 characters in the owner's own words (prices, hours, policies; an empty string removes them), kept server-side like the instructions and never in the public view, the talk page, or an embed. The model gets them after the instructions and before any knowledge page, under a line that has it answer from them and say it is not sure rather than guess where they fall short; the page's text is now closed by a line saying the owner's notes come only before it and win where the two differ, so a line on a page worded like the owner's cannot pass as theirs, and a marker line the owner happens to type is kept from reading as a new section. `POST /api/chat` takes `notes` too, so the Interactive page's try-it chat answers from them as they are typed. The setup form has a Reference notes field with a count (red at the limit), loaded by Edit and sent only when changed; a duplicate copies them, the account export carries them, saved presenters with notes say so on their row, and the demo writer answers a question that shares a word of five letters or more with them, before it looks at the page.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the page's text is fenced at its end and the notes win where they and a page differ, Claude's system prompt names the notes between the instructions and the page, marker lines typed in the instructions or notes no longer split them, the field's hint says the presenter may repeat anything in the notes (it had said visitors never see them) and is read out with the field, the notes line asks for "not sure" rather than a remark about notes, and the tests now check the prompt Claude gets, the fence, the marker collisions, empty instructions, whitespace clearing, and the export.
  - Left as it is: the demo writer's word match is as loose as it was for pages, so a common long word in the notes can win over a better sentence on the page.
- A transcripts download that holds what the panel shows (`5d74060`): `GET /api/agents/:id/conversations?format=csv` takes `q` (up to 200 UTF-16 units; longer is a `400`) and `leads=1`, matched by one shared rule with the Interactive page's transcripts search (the visitor's email and name and every message, in one case and Unicode form) and its "Only visits that left an email" box, and the panel's download link carries both when they are set, reading "Download matches (CSV)". The panel now lists every visit kept (up to 100) rather than the newest 50, so its list and the download agree, and its search box stops at the 200 the download allows (`ListSearch` takes a `maxLength`).
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the panel and the CSV no longer disagree past 50 visits, the limit of visits kept lives with the shared rule so the panel's note and the server cannot drift apart, and the test now parses the CSV rather than counting lines, waits for the link to carry the search before reading it, checks that 200 units are allowed and 201 are not, and matches a decomposed accent from a precomposed search and the other way round.
  - Left as it is: the panel is a snapshot of when it was opened, while the download is read when clicked, so a visit that arrives in between is in the file but not yet on the list.
- Suggested opening questions for a presenter (`cffc630`): `POST /api/agents/starters` (editors) drafts up to three questions a visitor might open with, from the instructions and reference notes it is sent and, for a saved presenter, its knowledge page text, in the reply language and from what they say only; nothing is saved. Claude gets the presenter's text fenced as content (its name and language kept to one line each); each line is tidied as a suggested title is and cut at a word to a chip's 80 characters, questions first, with repeats, lead-ins, and lines with no letters left out. The demo writer asks what the presenter represents, about each "Label: value" note, and two questions that suit any presenter. The Interactive page's Suggest questions button fills the Suggested questions field with them, asking before it replaces questions already there.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: an answer that arrives after another presenter is opened is dropped, questions typed while it waits are kept, and its error clears on switching; a knowledge page cleared or changed in the form is no longer drawn on; the demo writer's questions now read as questions (it had made "What does Be warm and brief do?" and "Can you tell me about we sell lake homes?") and keeps "U.S." whole; a lead-in without a colon or a line of only "…" no longer takes a place, and lines ending in a question mark come first; the name and language cannot start text outside the prompt's fences; the docs say what the demo writer does and that fewer than three can come back; and the test now checks that the replace question is really asked.
  - Left as it is: like title and description suggestions, the endpoint has no rate limit of its own.
- Most asked first questions in a presenter's transcripts (`9210a84`): the Interactive page's transcripts panel lists, as chips with their counts, up to five questions that opened two visits or more, each in its newest wording and counted among the visits the "left an email" box leaves. A visit's opening is its first visitor message in one case and Unicode form, with runs of spaces as one, opening and closing marks such as "¿", "?!", or "…" set aside, and cut to its first 200 characters (`openingKey` in `src/lib/transcripts.ts`). Picking a chip lists exactly the visits it counts, which the search box then narrows, and the download link carries it as `first`, so `GET /api/agents/:id/conversations?format=csv&first=…` holds the same visits (up to 400 UTF-16 units; longer is a `400`).
  - An independent review found nothing blocking in the redesign; an earlier draft was redesigned after a first review found that a chip's count and the search it ran disagreed on spacing and Unicode marks, so a chip now filters by the same key on the page and the server. From the later optional findings: a long question that NFKC widens, or one of wide characters, no longer makes a link too long for the server's check; a lone surrogate reads as U+FFFD, as a URL carries it, so a picked question still matches after the round trip; a picked question that the email box leaves out of the top five stays on show at its count there (with "none here" when it is zero) so it can be unpicked; the status line stays mounted so a screen reader hears the change, "Show all" sits beside it and returns the focus to the list's heading, and a long question wraps on a phone; the search's "N of M" counts against what the box and the picked question leave; the page narrows by the same `conversationMatches` the download uses; and the docs and the download link's title name the picked question.
  - A third review found one blocking issue, now fixed: showing all, or unpicking a chip, when that chip was on show only because it was picked removed the whole list and dropped the focus to the page; the focus now goes to the list's heading, or to the email box when no question is left. From its optional findings: the closing marks are set aside in one pass rather than rescanned from each mark, so a visitor cannot make the panel or the download slow with a long run of them; the Arabic "؟", "‽", and the Greek question mark are set aside too; a screen reader hears "1 visit"; the search's count names both the picked question and the email box when both narrow it; the help text and docs say a picked question stays listed below two; and the test now downloads with both `leads=1` and `first`, and checks the focus after showing all with the box ticked.
  - Left as it is: the counts ignore the words in the search box, so a chip can read a larger number than the search then shows.
- Delete one visit's transcript (`80e6350`): `DELETE /api/agents/:id/conversations/:conversationId` (editors) deletes one visitor conversation of a shared presenter and answers `{ "deleted": boolean }`, `false` when the presenter has no such visit (already deleted, or rolled over at the 100 kept) and `404` when the presenter is not the caller's. In the Interactive page's transcripts panel an opened visit has "Delete this conversation", asked first, named with the visit's date, and showing "Deleting…" while it runs; the list, the search, the email box, and a picked "Most asked" question carry on from what is left, and the focus goes to the panel's heading. The presenter's count of visits made is kept, and a visitor who comes back with the deleted visit's id starts a new one. "Delete all" is now shown to editors only, as the server already required, and its errors show in the panel.
  - Visitor turns and emails now write an existing visit only while it is the row they read (the store's `putIf` on `updatedAt`, which each write moves forward with `nextUpdatedAt`, re-read and retried up to eight times), so a visit deleted while a turn or an email is on its way is not written back, and a turn and an email landing together are both kept where the later write used to drop the other. A visit that keeps changing is logged for a turn and answers `409` for an email, and the `lead.captured` webhook's last message is read from the row written.
  - An independent review found one blocking issue, now fixed: the page took any `404` as "already gone" and removed the visit from the list, including the `404` for a presenter no longer the caller's (after switching workspace in another tab), so a visit that was not deleted looked deleted; a missing visit now answers `{ "deleted": false }` and every error is shown. From its optional findings: the write-back race above; errors from "Delete all" show in the panel rather than above the setup form, and a later delete clears an old one; a second click while a delete runs cannot ask again; each button's name carries its visit's date; the docs say an email left on a deleted visit answers `404` until the visitor's next turn; and the tests now check that dismissing the question keeps the visit on the server, that a turn and an email together are both kept, and the `404` for another presenter.
  - A second review found nothing blocking. From its findings: each write now moves the visit's `updatedAt` past the one it read, so two writes in one millisecond cannot both pass the check and the newest-first order cannot step back; a write that cannot get through is told apart from a deleted visit; the webhook carries a turn that landed meanwhile; while a delete runs every visit's button waits, and focus moved elsewhere meanwhile stays there; and the unit test now slips an email, then a delete, between a turn's read and its write rather than relying on timing.
  - Left as it is: a presenter's turn and visit counters are still read and written back without a check, so turns landing together can lose a count.
- One visit's transcript as a text file (`ec0d2c2`): `GET /api/agents/:id/conversations/:conversationId` (viewers too) returns `{ "conversation" }`, and with `?format=txt` a plain-text file of it (`conversation-<presenter>-<date>.txt`, with the presenter's own name in `filename*`): the presenter, when the visit started and when its last message was, the visitor's name and email when left, a note when its oldest messages rolled off, then each message with its UTC time and `Visitor` or `Presenter`. The Interactive page's transcripts panel has "Download (.txt)" in each opened visit. A visitor's name left with an email is now one line of text (tabs read as spaces; a line break inside it is a `400`).
  - The file is built so that nothing a visitor or a reply wrote can start a line or pass for a heading or another speaker in a viewer at least 76 columns wide: every value is cut into lines of at most 76 columns, every line after its first indented four spaces; widths are counted so a line can only be narrower than counted (a character a viewer may show as an escape such as `<200d>` or `<U+10FFFD>` counts as wide as that escape, CJK and emoji two, a letter wider than a line is cut between its code points); and line breaks, control and direction characters, tags, padding, and towers of marks are taken out or thinned (see docs/API.md).
  - Seven independent reviews. The first found the file open to forged lines through a visitor's name and through line separators other than CR/LF, and "Last message" dated by an email left later; later rounds found forgeries through padding in viewers that wrap, presenter names longer than the lead, letters wider than counted (stacked spacing parts, re-joined graphemes, `〈`, `©️`, keycaps), and characters vim and less draw as escapes (joiners, direction marks, private-use code points, whose escapes in the high planes are nine or ten columns), a false "messages not kept" note at exactly 40, mark thinning that changed Burmese and Hebrew words, a `filename*` that broke on `'()` and on a lone surrogate, and a quadratic thinning loop. All are fixed with a test for each input.
  - Left as it is: ambiguous-width characters (Greek, Cyrillic, box drawing) count one column, as most terminals draw them, though CJK-locale terminals draw them two; subdivision flags show as a plain black flag, as their tag characters are removed.
- Visits per day for each shared presenter (`36e2585`): a presenter now keeps `visitsByDay` (UTC day → visits started that day, 90 days), counted on the day each new visit starts in the same write as its conversation count; a continued visit adds none, deleting transcripts keeps the counts, a duplicate starts without them, the visitor view never has them, and the API cannot set them. The Interactive page shows the last 14 days as a bar strip on each visited presenter's row, the same strip as a video's share views (now one `DaySparkline` component for both).
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a visit is counted on the day it started rather than when its counts were written, so one around UTC midnight lands on the right day; the unit test reads that day from the stored visit; presenters visited only before this change show no empty strip beside their conversation count; a screen reader hears the strip's total once; and the docs say where the field is returned and when old days are dropped.
  - Left as it is: the presenter's counters are read, then written back without a check, as they were before, so two first turns landing together can lose a count and an edit or delete landing in between can be written over (the video view counters share this).
- Mark a visit as followed up (`4d65892`): an owner or editor can tick "Followed up" on any visit in a presenter's transcripts (`PATCH /api/agents/:id/conversations/:conversationId` with `{followedUp}`), which stores the time it was first marked; unticking clears it. Each marked visit shows a "followed up" badge, "Only visits not followed up (N)" narrows the list to the visits still waiting, and the CSV download carries a `followed_up` column and honours the same filter (`pending=1`). Visits are now listed by the time of their last message rather than the time they were last written, so marking one does not move it.
  - An independent review found one blocking race: a mark written without a version check could be lost to a visitor's turn landing at the same moment. Marks now go through the same conditional write as turns and leads, and the unit tests cover a mark landing in the middle of a turn. From its optional findings: the editor check runs before the body is read; each visit has its own save state, so marking one does not lock the others; a visit marked while the filter is on stays in view until the filter changes, so focus is not lost; the checkbox reports busy rather than being disabled; a screen reader hears which visit each checkbox belongs to; and a visit deleted elsewhere drops from the list.
  - A second review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a click made while a save is under way is shown at once and sent when that save lands, so the last click wins (it was dropped before); a visit that has gone moves the focus to the panel's heading; the "not followed up" box is no longer disabled, so it keeps the focus when nothing is left to follow up; the "Most asked" counts include the visits kept in view, and a search's count calls them visits that "were not followed up when the box was ticked"; the CSV's last-message time uses the same rule as the list order; the docs give the `400`, `403`, and `404` answers; and the tests cover a bad body, a second click mid-save, a visit reopening closed, and a visit with no messages.
  - Left as it is: who marked a visit is not recorded, and marking adds no activity-feed entry; with the filter on, the CSV download leaves out visits marked in this sitting that the panel still shows until the filter changes; a viewer's `403` is checked by the shared editor guard rather than a test of its own (demo mode has one owner); and `aria-busy` on the checkbox is not read by every screen reader.
- A private note on a visit (`2c3dd68`): an owner or editor can keep a note of up to 1,000 characters on any visit in a presenter's transcripts (what was agreed, who called back), saved from a "Note" box in the opened visit (`PATCH /api/agents/:id/conversations/:conversationId` now takes `{ followedUp?, note? }`; an empty note removes it). A noted visit shows "note" in its row, viewers see the note without the box, the transcripts search and the CSV's `q` look in it, the CSV gains a `note` column, and the text download gains "Followed up:" and "Note:" lines. The visitor's page, the turn and lead answers, and the `lead.captured` webhook never carry it. The note is written in the same conditional write as the follow-up mark, so a visitor's turn or email landing at the same moment keeps it.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking (it checked who can read and write a note, every visitor-facing path, the text file's line rules, the CSV's formula guard, and the races). From its optional findings: a follow-up mark that fails or lands after a note was saved now writes back only the mark, so it cannot put an older note on the page; a visit whose new note the search no longer finds stays in view until the search changes, as an item renamed during a search does; a save is announced ("Note saved") and the focus returns to the note; the counter says when the limit is reached; and the tests cover a note landing mid-turn, a formula-like note in the CSV, a `null` note, and spaces trimmed before the limit is counted. The docs and comments now name the note wherever the search, the text file, and the race rules are described.
  - Left as it is: two editors saving a note on one visit at once leave the later one, without a conflict check (the docs say so); and a viewer's `403` is checked by the shared editor guard rather than a test of its own.
- "New visits" on a presenter's row (`abf7ba6`): a shared presenter now keeps `lastVisitAt`, when its newest visit started, written in the same update as its visit counts (not settable through the API, not copied to a duplicate, never in the visitor view, and not moved back when an earlier visit's write lands after a later one's). The Interactive page remembers in the browser's own storage what it has shown of each presenter's visits, and marks a row "New visits" once a visit starts after that presenter's transcripts were last opened there; opening them clears it. Times are the server's, so a computer whose clock differs cannot hide a visit. The logic is a small pure module (`src/lib/seen-visits.ts`) with its own unit tests.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem: the record was one list for the whole browser, pruned to the presenters on the page, while the page lists only the workspace in use, so switching workspaces both invented marks for the other workspace's presenters and dropped what had been seen in the first. The record now keeps a starting point per presenter owner (each workspace's presenters share one), seeded the first time that workspace's presenters are listed, and is never pruned; presenters made later are compared with it. From its optional findings: a presenter counts as seen only once its transcripts have loaded, and up to the newest visit among them, so a failed load clears nothing and a visit that arrived after the page loaded is not marked again; `lastVisitAt` only moves forward; the docs now say that with storage blocked no row is marked; and the tests cover two workspaces in one browser and a continued visit that adds no mark.
  - A second review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: each save is merged with what another tab may have saved meanwhile (an owner's first starting point stands, and a presenter keeps the later of its two seen times), so two tabs on different workspaces do not undo each other; and the docs now say that, as with the counts, two visits starting at the same moment can still lose one write.
  - Left as it is: the mark is per browser, not per person or shared with the team; a presenter's other counters are still read and written back without a version check, as before.
- Visitors rate the presenter's replies (`a738c78`): on the talk page and its website embed, each reply after the greeting has a thumbs up and down (the same thumb again takes the rating back; the studio's try-it chat has none). A turn's answer now names its reply by time (`at`, sent only when the turn made it into the transcript), and `POST /api/talk/:id/rate` with `{ conversationId, at, rating }` stores the rating on that reply, in the same conditional write as turns, leads, and the owner's marks, for a presenter still shared, a visit under it, and a reply still in the visit; it is limited to 60 per 10 minutes per client. The owner sees each rating on its reply in the transcripts with a count of replies rated each way, the CSV gains `helpful` and `not_helpful`, and the text download marks a rated reply `Presenter (rated helpful)` or `(rated not helpful)`.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem: the chat panel wrote back the transcript it held before a reply arrived, so a rating made while the next reply was on its way vanished from the page (and a failed turn did the same). The panel now adds the reply to the transcript as it stands, and takes out only the failed question. From its optional findings: a failed rating goes back to the last one the server confirmed, and only when it was the newest click on that reply; a reply that was not recorded comes with nothing to rate; the thumbs have a larger target and a focus ring; the rate limit has a key of its own; and the e2e test now rates a reply while the next one is held in flight.
  - A second review found one more blocking path: a question spoken into the microphone was sent by the panel as it was when listening started, so a rating made while listening was lost the same way. The panel now always sends with its newest state. From its optional findings: a reply whose transcript was written stays rateable even if a later counter write fails; "Start over" while a reply is on its way no longer brings the old visit back on the talk page; and the docs name the embed.
  - Left as it is: replies so early in a long visit that they have rolled off (a visit keeps its 40 newest messages) still show thumbs, and rating one says it is no longer in the conversation; two turns of one visit finishing in the same millisecond would share a time, so one rating would mark both.
- Website embed builder (`fed4f95`): a presenter's "Embed" on the Interactive page now opens a form on its row for the launcher's side, button colour (starting from the brand kit's primary colour), button text (up to 40 characters), and "Opens by itself" (never, or after 5, 10, 30, or 60 seconds), with the `<script>` snippet it makes, carrying only what differs from the defaults, and a Copy button. The launcher script gains `open=1…120`: the chat opens by itself that many seconds after the launcher appears, once per browser tab, and never after the visitor has opened or closed it there.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem: "never after the visitor opened or closed it" rested on the tab's storage alone, so on a site that blocks storage a visitor who opened and closed the chat before the timer had it opened over them again (and on every page). The launcher now also remembers that in the page itself. From its optional findings: a chat that opened by itself tells the talk page so (`auto=1`), which waits for the visitor's first question before starting a paid live-video session; it never opens by itself on a screen 640 pixels wide or less, where it would cover the page, and in a hidden tab it waits until the tab is in view; the builder's toggle points at its form only while the form is open and the auto-open hint is tied to its select; the docs say the timer starts when the launcher appears; and the tests cover storage blocked, a phone-sized screen, and `auto=1`.
  - Left as it is: nothing announces to a screen reader that the chat opened by itself (it takes no focus, so the visitor's place on the page is kept); the builder's choices are not saved with the presenter.
- A daily reply limit for each shared presenter (`8267770`): the presenter setup on the Interactive page gains "Daily reply limit (optional)", a whole number from 1 to 10,000 (`dailyReplyLimit` on `POST`/`PATCH /api/agents`, `null` to remove it, copied by a duplicate, never in the visitor view). Replies to visitors are counted per UTC day (`repliesByDay`, 90 days, in the same write as the other counts, not settable), and once today's count reaches the limit a visitor's turn is refused with `429` and "… can't answer any more questions for now. Please try again later." before the model is asked, so it costs nothing and is not recorded. The row shows "N of M replies today"; the studio's try-it chat is not counted.
  - An independent review found one blocking gap: live video ignored the limit, so a presenter at its limit still opened a paid LiveAvatar session for every visitor while the form said the limit capped costs. `POST /api/talk/:id/live` now answers `{ available: false }` for a presenter at its limit (a session already open runs to its own end, at most 15 minutes), and the check is one helper shared by both paths. From its optional findings: the docs and the field's hint say plainly that replies already on their way when the limit is reached still go through, so it can be passed by those; the field takes digits only (thousands separators allowed), so "1e3" or "0x10" no longer save as 1,000 or 16; its hint is read with it; the visitor's message no longer promises "tomorrow", which in UTC can be mid-afternoon; and the docs list the limit among what a duplicate keeps.
  - Left as it is: the count is read before a reply and written after it, without a version check, as the other counts are, so a burst of turns in flight can pass the limit; a live session already open can still be spoken through until it ends; there is no `Retry-After` on the refusal.
- Allowed websites for a shared presenter (`d9f23fa`): the presenter setup on the Interactive page gains "Allowed websites (optional)", up to ten sites, one a line (`example.com`, `https://www.example.com/contact`, or `*.example.com` for its subdomains), checked as the server checks them (`allowedSites` on `POST`/`PATCH /api/agents`, kept as origins, `[]` to remove the list, copied by a duplicate, not in the visitor view). With a list, `/talk/:id` is served with `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'self' <sites>`, so browsers show the presenter in a frame only on those sites and in the studio, and the website launcher adds nothing on any other page, logging a console warning there instead. The row's Embed form says which sites the launcher shows on. Without a list, any site can embed it, as before. The talk link itself still opens for anyone who has it.
  - The site check is one pure module (`src/lib/allowed-sites.ts`) used by the form, the API schema, and the header, and a unit test runs the generated launcher against the same origins as it, so the two cannot drift. The e2e test serves a customer's page from its own loopback server, the same server standing for an allowed site (127.0.0.1) and another (localhost), because Chrome refuses a routed page's loads from the studio's address.
  - An independent review found one blocking gap: the proxy matched the talk page's path before it was percent-decoded, while the router decodes it, so `/talk/agent%5F…` rendered the same presenter with no header and any site could frame it. The segment is now decoded as the router decodes it before the id is checked, and one that cannot be decoded is framed by the studio only; a unit test runs the proxy on the encoded forms. From its optional findings: an IPv6 address or a name ending in a dot is no longer taken, since browsers drop either as a frame-ancestors source; the launcher also checks the pages it is framed in where the browser lists them; a presenter that cannot be read now leaves the launcher out (`503`, not cached), as the talk page's header already refused other sites then; the presenter is looked up beside the sign-in check rather than after it; and the docs say the list can be read by anyone with the presenter's id and that a `*.` on a shared hosting domain lets every site under it in.
  - A second review of those fixes found nothing blocking (other spellings of the path are redirected or reach no presenter, and the decoding matches the router's). From it, a `/talk/` path that is not a presenter's id is now framed by the studio only too, so a server in front that decodes paths once more cannot bring the gap back.
  - Left as it is: a `*.` on a shared hosting domain is accepted, without a public-suffix list; and, as for every presenter field, a visit counted at the same moment as a save writes back the row it read, so it can put back the previous list.
- Visitors download their own conversation (`6d0a374`): once the presenter has replied, the talk page and its website embed show "Download this conversation" under the chat, which saves the visit as a text file laid out and named as the owner's `.txt` download, with the visitor's own ratings but without the owner's followed-up mark or private note (`POST /api/talk/:id/transcript` with `{ conversationId }`, the visit's id being the visitor's token as for a rating or a lead, sent in the body so it stays out of addresses and logs; 20 per 10 minutes per client; `404` for a private or deleted presenter or a visit not under it). "Start over" hides it until the next reply. The download's name is built by one helper (`transcriptFilename`), now shared by the owner's and the visitor's routes.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the service now builds the visitor's copy from the fields that are theirs (their messages with their ratings, and the details they left) rather than removing the owner's, so a field of the owner's added later cannot reach them, and it hands the route the presenter's name only; the saved file takes the presenter's own name (`filename*`), so "José" or "ダナ" is kept as the owner's download keeps it; the button shows only once a turn of the visit made it into the transcript, so a visit whose first save failed does not offer a download that answers "not found"; and a download still under way when the visitor starts over saves nothing and leaves no error or "Preparing…" on the new visit. The docs say a site that frames the talk page with `sandbox` and without `allow-downloads` blocks the download.
- Batch links for a mail merge (`5e279ea`): a personalized batch's videos download as a CSV from the library's batch view ("Download links (CSV)", `GET /api/videos/batch/:batchId`, `links-<batchId>.csv`), one line per recipient with the CSV row the video was made from, the recipient (the row's first cell), title, status, video id, created time, and the share link, which is filled in only once the video is ready and its link is on and not expired, so a blank cell means not to send it yet; beside it, when the link will lapse or asks for a password or an email first, `share_expires`, `password_required`, and `email_required` say so. Each new batch video keeps its row (`batchRow`), a retry keeps it too, and lines follow the CSV's order whatever order the renders started in; renders in flight are checked with the renderer first, as the library's list does; a video in the trash is left out.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its findings: one recipient could get two lines (a failed render retried twice, or a middle retry in the trash), so each row now keeps one video, a ready one if there is one, else the latest retry, a retry winning over the render it replaced even when both were stamped in the same millisecond (which a unit test now pins after it showed up as a one-in-five failure); renders still in flight are refreshed before the file is built, so a script polling it does not keep seeing "processing" until the library is opened; locked, email-gated, and expiring links say so in their own columns; the file is named after its batch; and the docs say that rows the monthly allowance kept from starting have no line and that a blank first cell reads `row N`.
- A share link that goes live at a set time (`409fc1e`): the video page's sharing settings gain "Schedule go-live" beside the expiry (`shareStartsAt` on `PATCH /api/videos/:id`, an ISO 8601 time with a UTC offset in the future, `null` to make the link live now). Until that time the share page, its embed, captions, cover, beacons, oEmbed, and email preview answer "not found" to the public, as for a link switched off, the batch links download leaves the link blank, and the email and post-the-link forms wait for it; the owner keeps the video page, which shows "Goes live …" with "Change go-live time" and "Go live now", and the library card shows "Scheduled". A go-live time at or after the link's expiry is refused, whichever of the two is moved.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking: every public surface goes through the same check, the not-found answers are not cached, and the start-before-expiry rule holds whichever time is moved. From its optional findings: the go-live badge says so when the link is also switched off (it then stays offline at that time until turned on); the page's badge, buttons, and email and post-the-link notes follow the time being saved, as the email preview already did, rather than mixing it with the stored one; emailing a link that is both off and scheduled says to turn it on; scheduling on an expired link says to set a later expiry first; the docs name the go-live time in the batch links, share email, oEmbed, and bulk switch descriptions and note that a cover, logo, or oEmbed answer fetched while a link was live may stay cached when a go-live is scheduled on it, as when a link is switched off; the unit test uses times relative to the clock; and the e2e test checks oEmbed answers again once the link is live.
- Schedule go-live for many videos at once (`a773338`): the library's multi-select bar gains "Schedule go-live", which opens a date and time field with "Schedule" and "Go live now", so a launch's videos (a personalized batch's, say) all go live at one moment (`POST /api/videos/bulk` with `{ "action": "schedule", "ids", "shareStartsAt": string | null }`, the time written and checked as for one video's `PATCH`). A video whose own expiry comes at or before that time is left as it was and reported in `refused`, and the page says how many, keeps them selected, and says to set a later expiry on them first; videos not there are reported in `missing`, as for the other bulk actions.
  - An independent review found one blocking gap: when some videos were refused and others were no longer there, the banner told only of the refused ones, so the missing ones read as scheduled. Both are now said. From its optional findings: the refused videos stay selected with the field open, so they can be found and their expiry moved; the field closes with the filter, the view, or its own close button, clears its error as a new time is typed, and offers no past time; a video deleted for good while the request runs is reported as missing instead of failing the rest; and the banner says "at or before", as the server checks.
- Set an expiry for many videos at once (`40063dd`): beside "Schedule go-live", the library's multi-select bar gains "Set expiry", which opens a date and time field with "Set expiry" and "No expiry", so a campaign's links (a personalized batch's, say) all stop working at one moment, or stay open again in one step (`POST /api/videos/bulk` with `{ "action": "expire", "ids", "shareExpiresAt": string | null }`, the time written and checked as for one video's `PATCH`). A video whose own go-live time comes at or after that expiry is left as it was and reported in `refused`, and the page says how many, keeps them selected, and says to set an earlier go-live time on them first; videos not there are reported in `missing`. The two time fields share one form and open one at a time, and the expiry's schema is the single-video one, now shared.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: opening either field no longer clears the banner, since after a refusal it says which time to move and that may be the field being opened; the bulk route's comment names the expiry; and the unit test also refuses a go-live strictly after the expiry, as well as one at the same time.
- Expiring links show on their cards (`48c4c97`): a live share link that stops working within seven days carries "Expires in N days" (rounded up) on its card in the library and on Home, and "Expires in N hours" once less than a day is left, with the exact time in its tooltip, beside "Link off", "Expired", "Scheduled", and "Password"; a link that is off, expired, not live yet, or in the trash shows no countdown. So an expiry set on one video or on a selection (the bulk "Set expiry") can be seen at a glance, and a campaign's links about to lapse can be found before they do.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a link minutes from lapsing reads in hours rather than as a day to spare; the badges wrap instead of running under the duration on a narrow card; the unit test also covers a link that went live already, the round-up at the top of the week, and an unreadable time; and the docs name the Home cards and the hours.
- Filter the library by share link (`2acd4cf`): a "Filter by share link" select beside the others (`GET /api/videos?share=`) shows the videos whose link is live (public now, password or not), expiring within a week (live and counting down on its card), going live later, off, or expired, in the same order of precedence as the cards' badges, so a link switched off is "off" whatever its times say; with the bulk go-live and expiry it finds a campaign's links that need fixing. A video in the trash is in none of these states, since its link answers "not found", and the trash view has no share select (leaving the trash starts from "Any share link"); the selection is cleared when the share or review filter changes, as for the others.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a trashed video could be listed as "live" while not as "expiring" (both now leave it out, and the select is hidden in the trash); the e2e test now waits for a card each filter drops before reading the badges, and picks "Link live" too; the unit test covers trashed videos and the filter combined with a search and a status; and the docs say off wins over a go-live time as well as an expiry, and that the states hold whatever the render's status.
- The selected videos' links as a CSV (`81f27ce`): the library's multi-select bar gains "Download links" (`POST /api/videos/links` with `{ "ids" }`, up to 200, viewers too), a file of the chosen videos in the order picked with each one's title, status, share link (filled in only where it can be sent now), and `share_state` saying why a blank one is blank (`failed`, `off`, `expired`, `scheduled`, or `not ready` while it renders), with the go-live time and expiry still ahead, whether a password or an email is asked, and the folder; so a campaign spread over several batches or folders can be checked and sent from one file, as a personalized batch already could. The selection stays, so its links can be fixed (go-live, expiry, links on) and the file fetched again; videos no longer in the library are left out, and the page says how many.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a failed render reads `failed` rather than "not ready" or "scheduled", since it will never be sendable; a go-live time or expiry still ahead is given whatever the state, as it applies once the link is on or the render done; the route counts the ids it left out (`X-Omitted`) and the page says so; the route checks who is asking before reading the body and caps each id's length; a note from an earlier go-live or expiry refusal stays when the file is downloaded, and a network failure reads as such; the unit test ranks the states against each other and checks the 200-id bound; and the docs say up to 10 renders in flight are checked at a time and how the "Link live" filter and `not ready` differ.
- One share password for many videos at once (`54b4b2b`): the library's multi-select bar gains "Password", which opens a password field (in the same place as the go-live and expiry fields, one of the three open at a time) with "Protect" and "Remove password", so a campaign's links can be locked together and opened with one password (`POST /api/videos/bulk` with `{ "action": "password", "ids", "sharePassword": string | null }`, 4–64 characters as for one video's `PATCH`). Each video keeps its own salted hash, so a visitor's unlock opens only the video it was entered on; the password is never stored, returned, or logged, and the page checks its length as the server does (the bounds now shared by both). Removing asks first.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking: no plain password anywhere, an earlier unlock stops working once a new password is set, and each video's lock and cookie are its own. From its optional findings: "Protect" now asks first when a selected video already has a password, since it replaces that one and its visitors must enter the new one; the e2e test finds the "Password" button by its exact name and checks the replaced password no longer opens the video; and the unit test shows protecting again rotates the lock (an old unlock cookie no longer opens it) and that the server holds the 4–64 bounds after trimming. A request cut off part-way leaves the videos written so far changed, as for the other bulk actions.
- Download and email gate for many videos at once (`8cbb69a`): the library's multi-select bar gains "Download & email", which sets "Viewers can download" and "Ask for an email before playing" each to yes, no, or leave as is for every selected video, with "Apply" (`POST /api/videos/bulk` with `{ "action": "settings", "ids", "shareDownload"?: boolean, "shareGate"?: boolean }`, at least one, else a `400`), in the same one-at-a-time place as the go-live, expiry, and password fields. It goes through the single-video path, so a setting left out is untouched, the link's other settings stay, and a review's approval holds, as for the video page's checkboxes.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: turning the email gate on asks first, since everyone already sent the links (or watching an embed) sees the form; the labels follow the video page's wording ("Viewers can download", "Ask for an email before playing") and the control is named for what it sets rather than "Link settings"; the "choose a setting" refusal points at the request as a whole; the email preview's cache note and the docs say a gate turned on reaches a cached preview or oEmbed answer within five minutes, as switching a link off does; and the unit test sets one on and one off in a request and checks the link's other settings are left alone.
- Retry many failed renders at once (`54ed722`): when the library's multi-select bar holds failed renders it offers "Retry failed (N)", which asks first and then starts each one again as its single retry would (same scenes, presenter, look, quality, folder, and batch row, carrying `retryOf`), the first 50, three at a time (`POST /api/videos/retry` with `{ "ids" }`, 1 to 50, editors only). The month's allowance is checked for all of them before any starts, as a batch's is, and the ones past it are not started; each start checks it again, so renders started elsewhere meanwhile count. Every id is accounted for: `started`, `failedAgain` (refused by the renderer straight away, or not created at all), `skipped` with a `code` and reason (not a failed render, in the trash, an edited cut, already started again, or past the allowance), and `missing`. The page words each reason, keeps what was not started selected (a copy refused straight away in place of its original), and clears a selection that all started.
  - An independent review found two blocking issues, both fixed: a failed render stays failed after its retry, so selecting the same cards again would have started (and charged) them twice; a failed render that already has a retry in the library is now left for its newest copy, on the server and in the button's count, and the page refetches after an error too, so renders that started before it are not started again. And every skip but the allowance read as "no longer a failed render", leaving an edited cut stuck with a false reason; skips now carry a `code`, each worded on its own, and edited cuts are not counted in the button. From its optional findings: each start checks the allowance again rather than relying on the one up-front check (a unit test makes another render start in between and is shown to fail without it); more than 50 failed renders keep the rest selected and say so, in the confirm and the banner; the failed-again note reads well for one or many; the cap is one constant shared by the page and the server; the endpoint has its own heading in the API docs with its `400`; and the unit test covers result order with more than three running at once.
- Starred videos (`8b35d77`): a video can be starred to find it again, with a Star toggle on its page (pressed when starred; viewers see a Starred label) and Star and Unstar in the library's multi-select bar (`PATCH /api/videos/:id` with `{ "starred": boolean }`, or `POST /api/videos/bulk` with `{ "action": "star", "ids", "starred" }`). Starred cards show a star beside the title, and a Starred filter beside Trash (`GET /api/videos?starred=1`) lists only those, combined with the search and the other filters, in the trash view too. A star is the workspace's, as a folder is: kept through the trash and by a retry of a failed render, never on the share page, and no change to what a review approved.
  - An independent review found one blocking issue, fixed: the pressed Starred filter (white on amber-500) failed WCAG AA contrast; it is now amber-800, and the e2e test runs axe with the filter pressed and a starred card shown, and on the video page with the toggle pressed. From its optional findings: an edit held up by a cover upload or a password hash no longer writes back the star as it read it (the stored star wins unless the edit sets it, and a unit test shown to fail without this races a password against a star both ways); the video page's toggle keeps one name ("Star") with its pressed state rather than renaming itself, is hidden on a trashed video, and viewers see a Starred label instead; a retried failed render keeps its star; the filter's tooltip no longer says "you" (stars are shared in a workspace); the empty state suggests starring only when Starred is the only filter, and otherwise says to change the filters or turn Starred off; and the bulk route's comment names the action.
- Filter the library by when videos were made (`024b401`): a select beside the share-link filter ("Made any time", or made in the last 7, 30, or 90 days; `GET /api/videos?created=7d|30d|90d`) shows the renders of this week, month, or quarter, whatever folder they went to, combined with the search, the other filters, and Starred, in the trash view too (still newest deletion first). The windows are rolling 24-hour days from when the render was asked for, so a retry counts from its own start; an unknown window is a `400`. A new window clears the selection, as the other filters do.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a video whose creation time cannot be read is in no window rather than in every one; the options say "Made …" so the select reads as a filter beside the sort; the trash view's empty state now says to change the filters (or turn off Starred) when they hide trashed videos the toggle still counts; the docs name the rolling windows and the `all` default; and the tests cover the unreadable time, the trash view's order with the filter, the exact selection count, and an axe pass with the select shown.
- The library as a list (`aa0b466`): a Grid/List toggle beside the sort shows the library as compact rows, one per video, with a thumbnail, the title, its star and review state, the shape, date made, length, folder, batch, and views, what its share link does now (link off, expired, scheduled, expiring soon, password; the exact times in its tooltip), the trash days left, the render's status, and "Waiting for your review" while HeyGen's Video Agent waits on a storyboard. Rows select as cards do, "Show more" pages them the same way, and the browser remembers the choice (read after the page loads, kept only where storage is allowed). The share line comes from one helper, `shareSummary`, in the badges' order of precedence.
