  - An independent review found one blocking issue, fixed: a row dropped "Waiting for your review", so an owner in the list layout could wait on a render that was itself waiting on them; it shows now, and the Video Agent e2e test checks it in the list layout. From its optional findings: the share line shows at phone width too (cut short rather than pushing the status off, with an e2e check at 375 px that nothing runs past the screen); the toggle's tooltips match its names (Grid, List); focus rings show whole on the toggle and inside each row; a returning list user sees row skeletons while loading; a selected row keeps its highlight under the pointer; the review badge keeps the card's colours, and the trash and share lines carry their dates in a tooltip; the README sentence reads cleanly again; and the unit test covers a scheduled or expired link with a password.
- Two more library sorts (`b0e114c`): "Latest viewer visit" (`GET /api/videos?sort=viewed`) puts the video a viewer opened most recently first, counted on the share page or in an embed (the studio's own visits do not count), so a campaign's live links can be watched as they are opened; "Longest first" (`sort=length`) orders by the render's length. A video never viewed, or not yet timed (rendering or failed), comes after the rest, newest first among ties; in the trash they sort the same way, as the other non-default sorts do; an unknown sort is a `400`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the option reads "Latest viewer visit" rather than "Recently viewed", which suggested the studio's own visits, and the docs say embed views count; the schema comment says each sort looks at its own field; the e2e test now gives the two videos different lengths and checks the longer is listed first (it could not tell before); and the unit test covers the trash view sorted by length.
- The analytics CSV of what the library shows (`57c0642`): `GET /api/videos/analytics` takes the library's query (search, status, folder, batch, review, share link, star, date made, and sort), so the file holds the videos the library shows, every match and in that order, with each one's views, watch-through, clicks, and share link; with no query it is the whole library, newest first, as before (the Insights panel's link is unchanged). The library's filter bar gains "Download analytics", built from the filters in use and shown once a video matches; the trash has none, and `trash` is a `400`, since a trashed video's link has no share analytics.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: `limit` is not read at all, so the library's page size never turns into a `400` on the file; the link is named for what it does ("Download analytics"), sits beside the sort, does not wrap inside its pill, and is hidden when nothing matches; the docs say statuses are as stored and that invalid values are refused, and the README sentence keeps the folder actions with the folders; and the e2e test checks the file in a non-default sort, with an oversized `limit`, and with a folder whose name needs encoding.
- Folder counts (`552cd2b`): the library's folder picker says how many videos each folder holds ("Launch (12)", "Unfiled (3)", "All folders (N)"), in the view shown (the library, or the trash with its own counts) and whatever the other filters, so picking a folder never comes as a surprise. `GET /api/videos` returns them as `folderCounts`, with `""` for the unfiled ones, beside the existing `folders` names.
  - An independent review found one blocking issue, fixed: a folder a user named "constructor", "toString", or "__proto__" read the object's own properties, so the API could send a function's text where a number belonged and the picker showed it; the counts are now taken in a Map on the server and read as the folder's own entry on the page, and a unit test covers those names surviving the trip as numbers. From its optional findings: "All folders" shows the library's `total` (or the trash's count) directly; and the e2e test checks that total, that a search leaves a folder's count alone, and finds the picker by its exact label.
- Empty trash (`e9e056a`): the library's Trash view gains "Empty trash" for editors, which deletes every video in the trash for good in one step, whatever the filters show, with their comments, the emails their share pages collected, short clips, and the copies made from them. The page reads the trash's count afresh and asks to confirm with it ("Delete all 3 videos in the trash for good?"), and sends that count as `POST /api/videos/empty-trash { "expected": 3 }`; a trash that holds another number is a `409` and nothing goes, so a teammate's deletions since the page was looked at are not destroyed unseen. Up to 50 go a call, the longest-trashed first, and no new deletes start after a minute, so the request ends in time; the response is `{ done, remaining }` and the page says when some are left. Each video is read again under its lock first, so a restore in flight keeps it; a delete that fails is logged and left; one `video.purged` activity counts them.
  - An independent review found two blocking issues, both fixed. First, 200 sequential deletes (each a dozen store calls) could outrun the request's time on Supabase and leave no activity line for what went; the cap is now 50, with a one-minute deadline, and a failed recount no longer turns finished deletes into an error. Second, the confirm used the count from page load, so videos a teammate trashed since would go unseen; the count is now fetched before the confirm and checked by the server. From its optional findings: videos past retention that no listing had deleted yet go too (not counted in `done`); a unit test proves the lock by queuing a slow restore ahead of the purge (it fails without the lock); the confirm and docs name the comments and collected emails; viewers are not shown the button; Select and the Trash toggle are disabled while emptying, and the focus moves to the Trash toggle afterwards; and the docs say the restore race is closed within one server instance. A second review found nothing blocking; from it, the confirm names the music copies too, the focus also lands on the Trash toggle when the trash was emptied elsewhere, a cancelled confirm refreshes a stale count, the deadline test moves its clock on the second delete rather than by counting clock reads, and the docs say the count check compares counts, not which videos.
- Restore all (`87822ee`): the library's Trash view gains "Restore all" for editors, beside "Empty trash", which brings every video in the trash back to the library in one step, whatever the filters show, each as a single restore does (folder, star, share settings, and counters kept). The page reads the trash's count afresh and asks to confirm with it, saying the share links and embeds work again; `POST /api/videos/restore-all` restores up to 200 a call, the longest-trashed first (the ones nearest their purge), none started after a minute, and answers `{ done, remaining }`, with a note on the page when some are left. Each is written from the record as stored at that moment under its lock; a video past retention stays gone, one deleted for good meanwhile is skipped, and one whose write fails is logged and left; one `video.restored` activity counts them.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the button now confirms, since every restored share link comes back online; the longest-trashed come back first, so a capped run leaves the newest rather than the ones about to be purged; single restores and this share one helper for the restored record; a unit test proves the write uses the stored record, not the listed one (it fails if the listed copy is written), and the failure test now checks that the video after the failed one still comes back; and the README says the two caps plainly.
- Select all matching (`e0a77ec`): when more videos match the library's filters than are loaded (48 a page), the multi-select bar offers "Select all N matching" (or "Select the first 200 matching", as one bulk action takes up to 200). It loads every match onto the page and then selects them, so the selection stays within the cards shown and the bar's own counts (failed renders, locked links) stay true; the bar's count is a live status, and the focus stays in the bar when the button goes. A unit test pins the page size, every bulk action, and the links CSV to that same 200.
  - An independent review found two blocking issues and a test that could not fail, all fixed. The select could wait forever, showing "Selecting…", when more videos matched by the time the page loaded (or the load failed); it now loads up to the new count, gives up on a failed load, and waits for a response newer than the click rather than reading the old page. Typing a search while the matches loaded could still select all of them for a list no longer shown; typing now cancels it at the first keystroke (and the select also checks that the search typed is the one applied), and a sort change drops the selection as a filter change does. The e2e test's "0 selected" check matched "50 selected"; counts are now matched exactly, and a new step holds the page's response while a search is typed (it fails with the cancel removed). From its optional findings: leaving select mode cancels a pending select; the button stays focusable while it loads; the test makes its 50 videos in one batch; and the README says the first 200 come in the current order.
- Move to folder picks a folder (`8bacee1`): the library's multi-select "Move to folder" opens a field in the bar in place of the browser's prompt. It suggests the library's folders as you type, says whether the name goes "Into the existing “Launch”" or makes "A new folder", and has Move (or Enter) and an "Unfiled" button that takes the videos out of their folders; only one of the bar's fields is open at a time. A name that matches an existing folder exactly goes there; one that differs only in letter case or spacing ("launch ", "LAUNCH") goes into that folder rather than starting a near twin of it; anything else becomes a new folder, trimmed as the server stores it. `MAX_FOLDER_NAME` moves to the shared types so the page checks the length after trimming, as the server does.
  - An independent review found one blocking issue, fixed: with two folders that already differ only in case (the video page allows that), picking "launch" moved the videos into "Launch", the first match, and "launch" could not be reached from the bar; an exact name now wins, with a unit test that fails without it. From its optional findings: a new name keeps its inner spaces, as the video page keeps them, rather than being collapsed; the field has no `maxLength`, so spaces pasted around a full-length name do not cut it short, and the length is checked after trimming; the hint is a polite live region; matching lowercases the same in every locale; and the e2e test submits with Enter. Not done: folders used only by trashed videos are not suggested or matched (the library's folder list is the live videos').
- The library past 200 (`ddccd5d`): two fixes for big libraries, found by a new e2e test that pages 205 videos. "Show more" grows the page 48 cards at a time, but the server refused any `limit` over 200, so past 192 cards the next page (240) failed with "Too big: expected number to be <=200" and the library could not show more; `GET /api/videos` now takes a `limit` up to 480 (`LIBRARY_PAGE_MAX`, ten pages, as each card carries its scenes and the page is fetched again every few seconds while renders are under way), and at that size the page says to search or filter instead of offering more. And a selection could be bigger than the 200 videos one bulk action takes, which the server answered with a bare `400`: "Select all shown" now picks the first 200 and says so, the count turns amber past 200 ("201 selected, over the 200 one action takes"), and every bulk action and "Download links" refuse such a selection before sending, saying how many to unselect (`overSelection` in `lib/client`); the note goes once the selection is back within the limit. The server's bulk and links schemas now take their limit from the same `BULK_MAX`. Posters on the cards load lazily.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the page limit is 480 rather than the first draft's 1000, for the payload a page carries and refetches; posters load lazily; the over-limit note clears itself; the last "Show more" stays a (loading) button until its page is in, and the limit note is a status message; the schemas use `BULK_MAX`; the e2e test also checks that "Download links" refuses and sends nothing; and the docs give the bulk endpoint's 1 to 200 ids and the page limit. Not done: cards still carry whole videos, and the over-limit check runs when an action is pressed rather than when its field is opened.
- Shift+click selects a run of videos (`6966eb2`): while selecting in the library, a plain click still turns one video on or off, and Shift+click sets every video from the one clicked last to this one, in the order shown, as the one clicked last now is, so a run of cards is picked (or dropped) in two clicks, in the grid and the list alike. Shift+Space on a focused card or row does the same from the keyboard. The Select button's tooltip says how, and the Keyboard shortcuts dialog lists both under "In My videos, while selecting". Done, a new filter, "Select all shown", "Select all matching", and a bulk action that leaves some videos selected each forget the video clicked last, so the first Shift+click after them picks one video. The set logic is one pure helper (`clickSelect` in `src/lib/client.ts`) with a unit test; an e2e test picks, extends, and drops runs in both layouts and from the keyboard.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the video clicked last is now forgotten whenever the selection is replaced rather than clicked (a run could otherwise start from a card the user had not just chosen); Shift+Space is listed and tested, since a focused card's keyboard click carries the Shift key; the hint moved from the selection count (a live region, whose name it became) to the Select button; the e2e test waits for each selection rather than reading it once. A check that a Shift+click selects no page text was dropped after a mutation run showed it could not fail in Chromium, which never extends a selection over a button; the cards keep `select-none` for browsers that do.
  - Left as it is: a Shift+click run is not capped at the 200 one action takes (the page can hold 480), since the count turns amber and the bar then refuses the action with how many to unselect.
- Keys for selecting in the library (`6fd46b8`): while selecting, Ctrl+A (⌘A on a Mac) selects every video shown, up to the 200 one action takes, as "Select all shown" does, and Escape closes the field open in the bar (the folder, a go-live time or expiry, the password, or the link settings) or, with none open, stops selecting, the keyboard landing on Select. Neither takes a key from a text box: Ctrl+A in the search still selects its text and Escape there leaves the bar as it is (inside the bar's own field Escape closes it), and nothing is done over an open dialog or while an action runs. A field closed by Escape or its X button now hands the focus back to the button that opened it (or, with that button disabled because nothing is selected, to "Select all shown"), where it used to drop to the page. The Keyboard shortcuts dialog lists both keys, and the two buttons carry `aria-keyshortcuts`. The decision is one pure helper (`libraryKey` in `src/lib/shortcuts.ts`) with a unit test; an e2e test drives both keys, the focus return, and the dialog in front.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: an Escape that ends an input method's composition (choosing Japanese or Chinese characters while typing a folder name or password) closed the field and lost what was typed; such keys (`isComposing`, or key code 229) are now left alone, as every other Escape in the studio does. From its optional findings: an Escape on the link settings' selects is theirs (closing an open list) rather than the field's; a held Escape no longer runs on from closing the field to clearing the selection; Ctrl+A is found by the key's place on a Cyrillic or Greek layout, as Ctrl+/ is; the listener's ref is set as each render commits rather than while rendering; the bar is found by a ref rather than its test id; and the e2e test now shows Ctrl+A selecting the page's text outside select mode and the dialog keeping Escape to itself (a mutation run without the open-dialog check fails it).
  - Left as it is: in Firefox an Escape that closes the folder field's list of suggestions also closes the field (Chromium keeps it to the list), since a page cannot tell whether that list is open.
- One call-to-action button for many videos (`57988ca`): the library's multi-select bar gains "Call to action", which takes a button's text and link with "Set button" and "No button" (`{ "action": "cta", "ids", "shareCta": { "label", "url" } | null }` on `POST /api/videos/bulk`), so a campaign's share pages can all point at the same booking page in one step, or stop. The button is held to the rules a single video's is (one shared schema: text of 1 to 40 characters after trimming, an `http://` or `https://` link), checked in the bar before anything is sent; it asks first when a selected video's button would be replaced or removed, or an approved video would go back to not reviewed, since, as on one video, a changed button is a change to what the share page shows. The clicks already counted are kept. The field is one of the bar's five, so Escape and its X close it back onto its button. A unit test covers setting, replacing, removing, approvals, missing videos, and the rules matching `PATCH`; an e2e test sets and removes buttons from the bar and checks the share pages.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the remove prompt reads "Remove it?" for one video; the button's text is checked after trimming rather than cut at 40 characters by the box, so pasted text with spaces around it is not shortened; the link's 2,000-character limit is one constant, checked in the bar so a longer link gets a plain message rather than a `400`; the field's draft is cleared with the others when selecting stops or the filters change; and the unit test refuses a link with a space inside it.
  - Left as it is: the prompt counts the buttons and approvals of the selected videos as the page has them, so one a teammate set after the page loaded is not counted, as with the password.
- Copy links (`bf9402d`): beside "Download links", the library's multi-select bar gains "Copy links", which puts the share links of the selected videos that can be sent now on the clipboard, one a line in the order shown, ready to paste into an email or a chat, and says what it did ("Copied 2 links; left out: 1 has no link that can be sent yet"). `POST /api/videos/links?format=text` answers with those links as plain text, with `X-Links` and `X-Unsendable` counts beside `X-Omitted`; the CSV is unchanged. A link still rendering, failed, switched off, expired, or not live until later is never copied, and when none of the selection can be sent the bar says so and leaves the clipboard as it was. The links are fetched inside the clipboard write, so a browser that only lets a click copy at once (Safari) still allows it. The text is one pure helper (`selectionLinkText`) with a unit test; an e2e test reads the clipboard after a mixed selection and after one with nothing to send.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: a refusal ("None of the selected videos has a link that can be sent yet", or the browser's) stayed on the page after a later copy worked, so the bar said both; a copy's own message now goes when the next copy starts or the selection changes, and the e2e test runs the refusal before a copy that works. From its optional findings: the note sits at the end of the bar, so the buttons do not move when it appears; a copy that finishes after the selection changed reports nothing; another bulk action clears the note; the clipboard item's own promise is handled, so a refused write leaves no stray error in the console; the server counts the links (`X-Links`) rather than the page counting lines; and the e2e test checks a refused write's message too.
  - Left as it is: only Chromium runs the e2e tests, so the `writeText` fallback for a browser without `ClipboardItem` is not exercised there.
- A QR code for the share link (`dccf0c1`): beside "Post the link", a finished video's page shows a QR code of its share link, for a flyer, a slide, or a sign at an open house, with "Download SVG" (sharp at any size) and "Download PNG" (1,024 pixels, whole pixels a module so nothing blurs), both named after the video (`qr-<title>.svg`). It follows the share link's rules: none while the video is in the trash or its link is off or expired, a note when the link goes live later (printing ahead works once it does), and a reminder when viewers need a password. The code is made in the browser by `qrcode-generator` (MIT, no dependencies), wrapped in `src/lib/qr.ts` (error correction level M, the four-module quiet zone, the text as UTF-8, one path of run-length rectangles). `jsqr` (Apache-2.0) is a development dependency only: the unit test decodes codes made from a share link, a non-Latin domain, and a long link back to their text, and the e2e test decodes the downloaded PNG back to the share link.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a title holding a control character (possible through the API) no longer makes the SVG file fail to open, as characters no XML file may hold are left out of its title; a link with an expiry says when a printed code stops working; the error line is always in the page, so a screen reader announces the first one; the file's temporary link lasts a minute rather than a second, as the editor's downloads do, for browsers that ask where to save; the code's path is worked out once per link rather than on every redraw of the page; and the unit test now fails on any stray segment in the path.
  - Left as it is: an expired link's cards (this one, the email, and the networks) still say to turn the link on rather than to set a later expiry; the QR library loads with the video page rather than on demand.
- Expired links say so (`a27e88b`): on a video's page, the "Send by email", "Post the link", and "QR code" cards told someone whose link had expired, though it was still switched on, to "Turn the share link on first", which it already was; they now say "Link expired: set a later expiry first.", as the email preview's note does, and change while the page is open when the link expires. An e2e step lets a link expire with the page open and checks all three cards.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the e2e step gives the page ten seconds before the link expires, so a slow CI worker still sees the QR code first; and the three cards test the same condition the same way.
  - Left as it is: while a cleared or later expiry is being saved, the cards keep the saved expiry's message until the server answers, as they did before.
- Copy the QR code as a picture (`72bc4d0`): the video page's QR code card gains "Copy image", beside "Download SVG" and "Download PNG", which puts the same 1,024-pixel PNG on the clipboard to paste straight into a slide, a flyer, or a document, and says so ("Copied: paste it into a slide, a flyer, or a document."). The picture is made inside the clipboard write, so a browser that only lets a click copy at once (Safari) still allows it; a browser that cannot copy pictures, or refuses, says to use Download PNG instead. The PNG is drawn by one pure helper (`qrDraw` in `src/lib/qr.ts`) for both buttons, with a unit test that paints it into a bitmap, checks every module is a whole-pixel square in its place inside a white quiet zone, and scans it back to the link; the e2e test reads the copied picture from the clipboard and scans it to the share link, and checks a refused write's message.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: when the picture itself could not be made, the message says so ("This browser could not make the picture; download the SVG instead.") rather than blaming the clipboard; the unit test checks every pixel of every module at two sizes, the smaller leaving a border, and scans both; the e2e test also checks a browser without `ClipboardItem`; the empty message lines take no room under the buttons while staying in the page for screen readers; and a comment now says the PNG is encoded, not drawn, inside the clipboard write.
  - Left as it is: the note that a copy worked is hidden when the link changes, but every later e2e step reloads the page, so that is not exercised there.
- A QR code for a presenter's talk link (`4803ebe`): on the Interactive page, each shared presenter's row gains "QR", beside "Embed", which opens a QR code of its talk link, for a sign at an open house, a flyer, or a slide, so a visitor can scan it and start chatting. It is the video page's QR code, with the same "Download SVG", "Download PNG", and "Copy image", named after the presenter (`qr-<name>.svg`, titled "Scan to talk to …"); while the link is off, the panel says to turn it on instead, as the talk page is not found until then. The wording for the two kinds of link is one pure helper (`qrWording` in `src/lib/qr.ts`) with a unit test; the e2e test scans the downloaded PNG back to the talk link, opens it, and checks the panel once the link is turned off.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: the e2e test left its presenter behind, and as an account holds at most 20, repeated local runs against the same server would in time make every test that saves a presenter fail; it now deletes it however the test ends. From its optional findings: the off message says what a scanner then sees ("This presenter is not available"), and to a workspace viewer that an editor has to turn the link on; the panel is a named region and the off message a status, so a screen reader hears the code go when the link is turned off; the e2e test checks the presenter's public API answers while the link is on and not once it is off (the talk page itself loads for any id), and that the SVG draws what the page shows; and a unit test's name now says it is the SVG that keeps the title to what a file may hold.
  - Left as it is: Copy image is not exercised again for a presenter, as it is the same component the video page's test covers.
- QR codes for many videos (`ba21cf4`): beside "Download links" and "Copy links", the library's multi-select bar gains "QR codes", which saves a zip (`qr-codes-<date>.zip`) of a QR code of each selected video's share link that can be sent now, one SVG each (sharp at any size), named after the video (`qr-<title>.svg`, with " (2)" on a name another file already has, ignoring case, so none overwrites another once unpacked) and titled "Scan to watch …", ready for a batch of flyers or signs, and says what it saved ("Saved 2 QR codes; left out: 1 has no link that can be sent yet"). `POST /api/videos/links?format=qr` answers with that zip and the same `X-Links`, `X-Omitted`, and `X-Unsendable` counts as `?format=text`, or `409` when none of the selection can be sent, which the bar shows. The files come from two pure helpers (`qrFileNames` and `selectionQrFiles`) with unit tests; the e2e test unzips the download, checks one file a sendable video, and scans each code back to its share link.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a refusal ("None of the selected videos has a link that can be sent yet") goes when the selection changes or a later file is saved, as a refused copy's does, rather than staying beside the note of a file that worked; the note says what was saved only once the file is, and only for the selection it was made for; and a title in a script of many bytes a letter (Devanagari, Thai) is cut so its file name fits the 255 bytes macOS and Linux allow, at a whole character, with a unit case for it and e2e steps for the refusal clearing.
  - Left as it is: names are compared for duplicates in lower case, which differs from Windows' and macOS' own case rules for a few letters (a Greek final sigma); and the QR library now also loads with the server's other services.
- Copy link from a card (`fc98c1b`): each card in the library's grid, and each row in its list, gains a copy button for its video's share link, beside (not inside) the card's link, so the link is copied without opening the video, with a tick and a "Link for … copied" announcement. It shows only when a viewer could open the link now (rendered, out of the trash, switched on, live, not expired), and not while selecting, when a click picks the video. The home page's recent videos have it too. `GET /api/videos` now says which origin share links are on (`shareOrigin`), as the video page's own answer does. Whether a link can be sent is one pure helper (`sendableShareLink` in `src/lib/client.ts`), which the links CSV, the copied links, and the QR codes now use too, with a unit test; the e2e test copies from a card and a row and checks the switched-off and selecting cases.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the link is worked out again on the click, so one that expired while the page stood open is not copied, and the page says so ("The link for … can no longer be sent; open the video to see why.") and reads the list again; a browser without the clipboard (a plain-HTTP address) shows the link to copy by hand, as the video page does; what a copy did is said once for the whole page rather than by a live region on every card; a card keeps its full height in the grid and is not remounted when its link becomes sendable; a row's hover covers its button, and a row with nothing to copy keeps the button's room, so the status badges line up; and the e2e test checks that alignment, runs an accessibility check on the list, and lets a link expire with the page open.
  - Left as it is: a link that goes live later gets its button only once the list is read again (a reload or a render finishing).
- The library follows share link times (`416491d`): a card or row whose video's share link goes live, or expires, while the library stands open now changes then, with no reload: "Scheduled" gives way to its Copy link button and any "Expires in…" badge, and at expiry the button goes and "Expired" shows. The page works out when the soonest go-live or expiry among the videos shown is due (`nextShareChange` in `src/lib/client.ts`, and each whole hour before an expiry, when an "Expires in N hours" or "days" badge counts down, and never for a link switched off or a video in the trash) and draws itself again then, with nothing fetched; filtered by share link, it reads the list again instead, so a video whose link has gone live leaves "Going live later". A tab shown again after being hidden (when browsers hold timers back) looks again at once. A unit test covers the helper; an e2e test watches a card go from Scheduled to live to Expired with the page open, and another a filtered view drop a video at go-live; the Copy link test now sees the button go by itself at expiry.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: views filtered by share link are read again rather than redrawn, as their membership changes; the hours-left badge counts down on the hour rather than up to an hour late; a tab shown again redraws at once; the timer test answers every list read as it was first answered, so it fails if only a poll (another video still rendering) changed the cards; the Copy link test keeps a step that moves the page's clock past an expiry without its timers firing and checks the click is still refused; and a unit case pins an invalid date as no change.
  - Left as it is: a Copy link button that goes while it has the keyboard focus leaves the focus on the page; and the page's clock, not the server's, decides when a link has gone live or expired.
- Share links to check, on Home (`c3c0445`): Home now says when share links need a look, above the quick actions: "2 share links expire within a week." with "Show expiring links", and "1 share link has expired but is still on: visitors see that it has expired." with "Show expired links", each opening My videos filtered to those videos (`/videos?share=expiring` or `?share=expired`; the library now takes its share filter from its address, and ignores a value it does not know). A link switched off, not live yet, or in the trash is not counted. The counts and the library's share filter are one pure helper (`inShareFilter`, with `shareAttention` counting, in `src/lib/client.ts`), which the server's `filterVideos` now uses too, so the notice counts what the filtered library lists; a unit test covers both, and the e2e test checks the counts against the API, follows each line to its filtered view, and an unknown filter in the address.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the library reads `?share=` again when its address changes, so the sidebar's My videos from a filtered view shows every link again; the expired line says the link is still on and what visitors see; the unit test compares the counts with the server's `filterVideos` rather than with the helper they are built from; the e2e test switches off every link it made however it ends, checks the singular wording and that Home shows no notice when nothing needs a look; and the API's list of share filters is the one the library and Home use.
  - Left as it is: the library does not write a share filter picked from its select back into its address, as none of its other filters do; and Home counts by the browser's clock where the library filters by the server's, so a link within minutes of its expiry can sit on either side of it.
- Failed renders on Home (`14a34ae`): Home's notice, now "Videos to check", also says when renders made this week have failed and not been retried ("2 renders made in the last 7 days failed and have not been retried.") with "Show failed renders", which opens My videos at `?status=failed&created=7d`. The library now takes its status and "made" filters from its address as well as its share filter, reads all three again whenever the address changes (the sidebar's My videos clears them), and ignores values it does not know. A render asked for over a week ago is not counted, so an old failure does not stay on Home for good. The 7-day window is one pure helper (`madeWithin`, with `failedThisWeek` counting, in `src/lib/client.ts`), which the server's `filterVideos` now uses too; a unit test covers the count and what the server's filter lists, and the e2e test shows Home failed renders (the demo renderer never fails), follows the line to the filtered library, and checks the sidebar link and unknown values.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its findings: a failed render that has been retried is no longer counted, so one put right does not stay on Home for the week, and the line says the renders were made in the last 7 days (when they were asked for) rather than that they failed then; the e2e test waits for the library's own filtered request before checking what it lists, and removes the video it made however it ends; and the unit test checks the server's filter against the videos it should list rather than against the helper it shares.
  - Left as it is: the library's Failed view still lists failures that were retried, beside the ones Home counts; and, as for the share filter, a filter picked in the library is not written back into its address.
- Videos in review on Home (`f364880`): in a workspace, Home's "Videos to check" notice also says how many videos are waiting for a review decision, to an owner (who decides) "2 videos are waiting for your review." and to editors and viewers "… for an owner's review.", with "Show videos in review", which opens My videos at `?review=pending`, filtered by review to those videos. The library now takes its review filter from its address too, with its share, status, and "made" filters, and reads them again whenever the address changes. A video whose review has been decided (approved, or changes asked for) is not counted. The count is one pure helper (`awaitingReview` in `src/lib/client.ts`), with a unit test checking it against the server's review filter, and the e2e test asks for a review in a new workspace, follows the line to the filtered library, and checks Home says nothing once the review is approved.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: a review filter set from the address stayed on with no control to clear it outside a workspace (after following Home's link, then switching to the personal space), so the library looked empty for no visible reason; the review select now shows whenever a review filter is on, and the e2e test clears one in the personal space. From its optional findings: the line says whose review it waits on, as only owners decide; the e2e test waits for Home's list before checking the notice is gone, and creates its workspace inside the step that removes it, so a failed set-up is the error reported.
  - Left as it is: the wording for editors and viewers is not exercised in the e2e test, which signs in only as a workspace's owner.
- Render allowance on Home (`ccdda38`): with a monthly render allowance (`RENDER_LIMIT_PER_MONTH`, or Pro's `PLAN_PRO_RENDERS`), Home's notice, now "Things to check", says how many renders are left once a fifth of the allowance or less remains ("3 renders left this month (17 of 20 used).", always at the last one), and once none are left that they are used up and the date renders can start again ("…on December 1 (UTC)", the 1st of next month, as the server counts the month), with "See plan and usage", which opens Settings. With no allowance, or plenty left, nothing is said, and a workspace's viewers, who make no renders, are not told. When to speak and the reset date are one pure helper (`allowanceNotice` in `src/lib/client.ts`) with a unit test, and the e2e test shows Home an allowance through `/api/status` nearly used, used up, with plenty left, and none.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the e2e test finds the line by its own test id rather than by words another line also has, and writes the expected date as the page does, in the browser's language; the used-up line says renders (not only new videos) can start again, and that the date is in UTC; the count is shown as it is, so a plan moved down mid-month reads "25 of 20" as Settings does; viewers are not shown the line; and the notice is named "Things to check", as it no longer holds only videos.
  - Left as it is: the reset date is worked out from the browser's clock, not the server's; and a render still in flight that later fails gives its render back before that date.
- Captions for many videos (`1cad2ba`): beside "QR codes", the library's multi-select bar gains "Captions", which saves a zip (`captions-<date>.zip`) of the captions of each selected video that has rendered, one SRT file each named after the video (`<title>.srt`, " (2)" on a name another file already has, ignoring case), to upload with the videos on YouTube, LinkedIn, or a course platform, and says what it saved ("Saved captions for 3 videos; left out: 1 has none (still rendering, failed, or no script of its own)"). Each file is what the video's own caption download gives: an editor's corrected captions where saved, else lines timed from the script. `POST /api/videos/captions` answers with that zip (WebVTT, with the caption position, with `?format=vtt`), with `X-Files`, `X-Omitted`, and `X-Missing` counts, or `409` when none has captions. The files come from a pure helper (`selectionCaptionFiles`), and their names from `uniqueFileNames`, which the QR codes' names now use too, both with unit tests; the e2e test saves the zip from the bar and checks each file against the video's own download, in SRT and WebVTT.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a cinematic shot or a HeyGen-directed video, which has no script of ours, is left out by name rather than because its script is empty, and the note and the refusal no longer suggest captions will come later; the docs say only finished renders are included (the single download also estimates lines for one under way), that names keep the title's spaces where the single download writes underscores, and that a video with captions burned into the picture needs no file beside it; and the unit test adds a queued render, a cinematic shot, and a translated track the zip leaves to its own download.
  - Left as it is: translated tracks are not in the zip (each has its own download on the video page); the e2e test saves only a selection that is all ready, as the unit test covers what is left out.
- Scripts for many videos (`dd2a8db`): beside "Captions", the library's multi-select bar gains "Scripts", which saves a zip (`scripts-<date>.zip`) of each selected video's script, one text file each named after the video (`<title>.txt`, " (2)" on a name another file already has), as the editor's Download script writes it (scenes apart on `---` lines, pause markers kept), so the editor's Import script reads it back scene for scene: to proofread a campaign's scripts, hand them to a translator, or start new videos from them. The render's state does not matter, as the words are there before it is made; a cinematic shot, a HeyGen-directed video, or a render of a HeyGen studio template, with no script of its own, is left out with any video that has no words, and the bar says what it saved. `POST /api/videos/scripts` answers with that zip, with `X-Files`, `X-Omitted`, and `X-Missing` counts, or `409` when none has a script. The files come from a pure helper (`selectionScriptFiles`) built on the editor's own `scriptFile`, with a unit test that reads a file back through the import; the e2e test saves the zip from the bar, checks each file byte for byte, and imports one in the editor as two scenes.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: a render of a HeyGen studio template was saved with the template's filled words as one scene, or only the template's name when its words came from an audio clip, though the editor does not open such a video; it is now left out with cinematic shots and directed videos. From its optional findings: names are made as the editor's own download makes them, so a title already ending in ".txt" is not given it twice (the QR codes and captions follow the same rule); the refusal and the bar's note name every reason a video has no script to save; and the docs say what the editor's own import does to a scene with no words or a line that reads as a break.
  - Left as it is: the selection's renders in flight are still checked with the renderer first, as the other bar downloads do, though a script does not depend on the render.
- One cover for many videos (`a315d18`): beside "Call to action", the library's multi-select bar gains "Cover", which takes one picture (made a JPEG fitted in 1280 pixels in the browser, as the video page's own upload is, and shown beside its name) and puts it on every selected video with "Set cover", or takes theirs off with "No cover": a campaign's videos can share one branded poster on their cards, share pages, and link previews without opening each. Each video keeps its own copy, so a later change to one leaves the rest, and the bar asks first when a cover would be replaced or removed, or an approved video would go back to not reviewed, as on one video. `POST /api/videos/bulk` takes `{ "action": "cover", "ids", "cover": dataUrl | null }`, held to the same rules as `PATCH` (the cover schema is now one shared by both), and a picture the server cannot keep is a `400` before any video is written. The unit test checks each video's own copy, removal, approvals, the refusal before any write, and parity with `PATCH`; the e2e test sets a cover on two videos from the bar (one replacing a cover it had), checks the cards, the served pictures, and the share pages, then removes them.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed: "No cover" over a selection that mixed videos with and without a cover sent an approved video with none back to not reviewed, unasked, though nothing its share page shows changed (removing a cover a video does not have is no longer a change to review, with a unit test), and with one video selected and no cover to remove the bar said it had one. From its optional findings: a picture still being read disables "Set cover", and only the last one chosen, while the field is open, is kept, so a slow large photo can no longer replace a later pick; the picker's name holds its visible text ("Choose picture"), and the chosen file is announced; the picture is checked once before the loop, rather than relying on the first video's write to refuse it; the bar forgets the picture when selecting stops; and the bar and docs say a removed cover gives way to the renderer's own picture of the video, not its first frame.
  - Left as it is: each video's copy is decoded and stored on its own, up to 200 in one request, as one video's upload is.
- Continue your draft on Home (`3582459`): Home shows the editor's unsent draft for the space being worked in, as this browser keeps it: its title, how many scenes and words (pause markers are not words, as the editor counts them), its opening words, and when it was saved, with "Continue editing", which opens the editor on it, and "Discard draft", which asks first and says that an editor still open in another tab keeps its copy. Each space's draft is its own, so a workspace shows its draft and never Personal's, and a workspace's viewers, who make no videos, are not offered one. The card follows the editor in another tab, and a tab brought back from the browser's page cache reads it again. The draft's key and the check of what the editor can restore now live in one module (`src/lib/editor-draft.ts`) that the editor and Home share, with a pure `draftSummary` behind the card. The unit test covers the summary (words, opening words cut at a whole character, a flag or an emoji with a skin tone included) and the drafts it refuses; the e2e tests continue a draft from Home, decline and accept a discard, and show each space's own draft and none to a viewer.
  - An independent review found no blocking problems. From its optional findings: a stored draft whose scenes the editor could not restore (a scene that is not an object, or a script that is not text) is no longer offered, and the editor itself now leaves such a draft alone rather than failing on it; the opening is cut by what a reader sees as one character, and words are counted by the editor's own counter; Home reads the draft again when a frozen or cached tab comes back; after a discard the keyboard moves to the first quick action and the discard is announced; the discard confirmation says an open editor keeps its copy; and the e2e tests wait for the autosave rather than a fixed time, check that a declined discard leaves the draft stored, and cover a workspace's draft and a viewer.
  - Left as it is: the card shows the plain new-video draft only (a copy, template, clip, or picture keeps its own, opened from where it started), and a space switched in another tab is followed when Home is next opened.
- Hover preview on library cards (`d3a77cd`): in the library's grid and on Home, a finished video's card plays its clip, muted and looping, over the poster while a mouse rests on it or the keyboard focuses it, after 300 ms so a pointer passing over the grid loads nothing, and not when the system asks for less motion (followed as it changes). The clip keeps the poster until it plays (or if the browser will not play it), is framed as the poster is, sits under the card's badges and copy button, and stops downloading as soon as it goes. Which videos have one comes from a pure helper (`cardPreviewSrc`): a completed render out of the trash, at a web address or a path on this site. The unit test covers those rules and the addresses refused; the e2e test measures the wait with the page's clock, checks the badge is drawn on top, and covers the copy button, Tab, a selection ended with Escape, less motion, and a tap on a phone.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed: on a phone a tap counted as a hover and started a clip that played on, downloading, until the next tap elsewhere (a clip now starts only for a mouse resting on the card or focus the keyboard brought, by `:focus-visible`); and ending a selection with Escape left the focused card's clip looping, as the button it was on was replaced without a blur (the preview now starts afresh whenever the card's element changes). From its optional findings: Tab from a card's link to its copy button no longer restarts the clip; the mouse and the keyboard are followed apart, so one leaving does not stop the other; a clip taken off the page stops downloading; the clip no longer turns the card black while it loads, and is framed as the poster; the path check refuses `/\host` and other paths a browser reads as another host; and the e2e test now measures the wait rather than sleeping, and hit-tests the badge.
  - Left as it is: the list layout's rows (a small thumbnail, hidden on a phone) have no preview, and the clip is the render's own file, as no smaller version is kept.
- "/" to search the library (`2266d1f`): in My videos, "/" pressed outside a text box (on the page, a card, or a button) moves to the search box with what it holds selected, so typing replaces the search; in a text box it is typed as ever, and with a dialog or the small-screen menu open it is left alone. The key is the "/" character however the layout types it (Shift+7 on a German keyboard, AltGr+Q on a Brazilian one, the number pad), never with a Ctrl, ⌘, or Alt of its own. The Keyboard shortcuts list shows it in a new "In My videos" group, and the search box names it (`aria-keyshortcuts`) and is now a search box (`type="search"`), like the studio's others. The check is a pure helper (`isSearchKey`, with `typesText` for where a key goes into text) with unit tests; the e2e test presses "/" from the page and from a card, types it in the box, opens the shortcuts list, and turns single-key shortcuts off and on again across a reload.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: a shortcut of one key pressed on its own fails WCAG 2.1.4 (Character Key Shortcuts, level A) unless it can be turned off, and speech input can send "/" by chance; the Keyboard shortcuts list now has a "Single-key shortcuts" switch, kept by the browser, that turns it off. From its optional findings: "/" no longer reaches behind the open small-screen menu; AltGr layouts are taken; a held "/" no longer fills the box after the first press moved focus to it; a checkbox, a button, or another input that takes no text no longer counts as a text box; and the unit tests now check the number pad, Shift, AltGr, and the switch, rather than cases that could not fail.
  - Left as it is: the held key is checked in the e2e test with keys the page sends itself, which a browser never types into a box, so that step shows the focus moves once rather than that repeats are held back; and the tests run in Chromium only, so Firefox's own "/" quick find is covered by reading (the jump cancels the key, so it does not open), not by a run.
- Search on the Templates page (`799611d`): one box, labelled "Search my templates and script templates", looks through the saved templates (their names, descriptions, and each scene's name and words) and the script templates (names, descriptions, categories, and words), pause markers aside, compared in one case and Unicode form as the studio's other list searches are. A line says how many of each match ("1 of 4 saved templates, 2 of 8 script templates in Marketing"), each list says when none of it does, and the category buttons still narrow the script templates; a category that hides every match offers to show the matches in the others. A template copied, renamed, or open for editing while a search is typed stays in view (the copy or the renamed one with its Edit button focused), so nothing vanishes from under the person or their unsaved changes. Each template's words are folded once, not on every key. The text searched comes from two pure helpers (`scriptTemplateTexts`, `savedTemplateTexts`) with a unit test; the e2e test searches scene words, a scene's name, and a category, checks a pause marker is not a word, follows the way out of a category, and covers a copy whose name the search does not find, a rename, and an open form.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, fixed: the e2e test claimed a copy stays in view while searching, but its copy matched the search by name anyway, so nothing tested that pinning works; the test now copies a copy ("(copy 2)", which "(copy)" does not find) and checks it stays with its Edit button focused, and covers a rename while searching. From its optional findings: the count line no longer says "0 of 0 saved" while the list loads, after it fails to load, or with no saved templates, and counts only real matches (not a copy kept in view); a category hiding every match offers the way back; a card with its edit form open is kept in view rather than its unsaved changes lost; the search has a visible label saying which lists it covers, in a search landmark; each template's words are folded once per load rather than on every key; and the e2e test no longer relies on the store holding nothing else.
  - Left as it is: HeyGen's own studio templates, a separate list between the two, are not searched (the label names the two lists it covers); and the count line is announced as it changes, as the studio's other list searches' are.
- Resolve review comments (`89cc832`): a note on a video can be marked resolved once it is dealt with, and reopened. Anyone who can comment on the video may, workspace viewers included, since the person who acted on a note is often not the one who wrote it (`PATCH /api/videos/:id/comments/:commentId` with `{ "resolved": true | false }` and nothing else). A resolved note records when and by whom (`resolvedAt`, `resolvedById`, `resolvedByEmail`); resolving it again keeps the first, and reopening clears all three. On the video page each open note has a Resolve button; resolved notes fold under a "Show N resolved" toggle (`aria-expanded`), most recently resolved first, each with "Resolved by …" and a Reopen button, and the heading counts both ("Comments · 2 open, 1 resolved"). After resolving, the focus goes on to the next open note's Resolve (else the one before, else the toggle), so a list can be worked through from the keyboard; a reopened note's Resolve takes the focus back, and a screen reader hears "Comment resolved" or "Comment reopened". The listing order is a pure helper (`splitComments`) with the service in a unit test; the e2e test resolves three notes from the keyboard, follows the focus, folds and unfolds the resolved ones, reopens one, reloads, and deletes a resolved note.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: a note deleted while it was being resolved (by its author, or with its video) is no longer written back, so it stays deleted (`404`); a resolve that fails because the note is gone reloads the list, so the dead note loses its button; a failed reload shows its error and no longer moves the focus later; the folded list stays in the page, hidden, so the toggle's `aria-controls` always names it; the toggle starts folded again once nothing is resolved; the change is announced to a screen reader; and the unit tests now check the note's own owner (not only the video's) and the delete race.
  - Left as it is: of two resolves at the same moment the later is kept (documented); and a note resolved through an API key with no email shows "Resolved by you", as a note written that way shows "You", until the studio can tell the viewer apart from the key's owner.
- Edit your own comments (`ce788d4`): the author of a note on a video can change its text in place (`PATCH /api/videos/:id/comments/:commentId` with `{ "text" }`; `403` for anyone else's note, the same limits as posting). The note keeps its place, its pinned moment, and whether it is resolved, and shows "· edited" (`editedAt`). In a workspace its mentions are read again from the new text, and only members the note has never named before are emailed, so fixing a typo, or taking a name out and putting it back, mails no one twice (`notified` keeps who was). The list marks the caller's own notes (`mine`), and the video page shows Edit and Delete on those alone, as only the author may use them. Edit opens a box on the note, focused, with Save and Cancel; Escape cancels, and either way the focus returns to the note's Edit button (after a save only if the person has not moved on); "Comment saved" is said to a screen reader. `PATCH` takes `{ text }` or `{ resolved }`, never both. The service (`editComment`) and `markMine` have unit tests; the e2e test edits a note from the keyboard, cancels with Escape, saves, and reloads.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: every write to a note now carries a revision (`rev`), so an edit and a resolve at the same moment can no longer undo each other (the later one is a `409` and the note keeps the first change); a name taken out and put back is not mailed again; a save that finishes while another note's box is open leaves that box open, the box is read-only while saving, and a failed save shows the note as it now is (one deleted elsewhere closes its box); a note being edited has no Resolve or Delete until the box closes; Edit, Delete, Resolve, and Reopen name the note they act on; a repeated status message is spoken again; every response carrying a comment marks it `mine`; and the tests now cover the race, the second mention, a pinned and resolved note keeping both, and `mine` for two authors.
  - Also fixed: the hover-preview e2e test (`d3a77cd`) could fail on a busy machine, as the page clock it installs keeps running in real time, so a slow step could carry the 300 ms wait past its check; it now holds the clock still before hovering, and passes with an added real delay.
  - Left as it is: the comments list looks up the signed-in person once more to mark `mine`, a second session read in Supabase mode.
- Open comments in the library (`6ab7f2e`): each card and list row in My videos shows how many review notes on that video no one has resolved yet (a speech-bubble count, "2 open comments" to a screen reader; nothing when there are none), and an Open comments toggle beside Starred shows only the videos the team still has notes to act on. `GET /api/videos` takes `comments=open` (in the trash view too; any value but `all` or `open` is a `400`) and returns `openComments`, the count for each video of the page that has any; the library reads `?comments=open` from its own address, and the analytics CSV follows the filter. An empty filtered list says how to get back ("or turn off Open comments"), or that no video has an open comment. The counts come from one more read of the owner's comments, made alongside the read of the videos rather than after it. `openCommentCounts` and the filter have unit tests (counts per owner and per video, only the page's videos counted, the trash, and a filter asked for without counts failing rather than matching nothing); the e2e test checks the badge and its spoken name in both layouts, the toggle, the address, and a video dropping out once its last note is resolved.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the comment count runs alongside the video read, and the analytics CSV reads comments only when its filter asks; `filterVideos` throws if the open-comments filter comes without the counts instead of quietly matching nothing; the e2e test checks what a screen reader hears rather than the joined text; the API docs list `comments` for the analytics CSV and say its default and its `400`; the empty state reads "No video has an open comment." (true whether notes were resolved or never left); and the unit tests now cover a page smaller than its matches and the trash.
  - Left as it is: in Select mode a card is a button named "Select …", which its badges (this one, the star, and the review state) do not add to; and turning the toggle off leaves `?comments=open` in the address, as the library's other address filters do.
- Open comments on Home (`1e20c44`): Home's "Things to check" now says how many review comments no one has resolved yet, and on how many videos ("3 comments on 2 videos are not resolved yet."; "1 comment on 1 video is…"), with a link that opens My videos filtered to them (`?comments=open`). It reads the counts `GET /api/videos` already returns for the whole library, so trashed videos are left out and the count matches what the link opens; anyone in the space sees it, viewers too, since anyone who can comment may resolve a note. The sum is a pure helper (`openCommentSummary`) with a unit test; the e2e test, in a workspace of its own, checks the plural and singular lines, the link and the filtered library, and that the line goes once every note is resolved. The Home tests that answer `/api/videos` themselves now say there are no open comments, so notes other tests leave open in the personal space cannot add a line they do not expect.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the README no longer reads as if the line were for workspaces only.
  - Left as it is: the new e2e test's clean-up does not check that its workspace was deleted (a leftover one holds nothing other tests count); and no test yet loads Home as a viewer.
- Replies to review comments (`cc8a31f`): a note on a video can be answered, and the answers sit under it as a thread, one level deep (`POST /api/videos/:id/comments` with `parentId`; a reply to a reply, or a reply pinned to a moment, is a `400`). A thread is resolved, reopened, and folded away by the note that starts it, its replies with it (resolving a reply on its own is a `400`), and counts once as open in the library, on Home, and in `?comments=open`. Replying to a resolved thread is a `409` until it is reopened. Deleting a note that starts a thread deletes its replies too, whoever wrote them, after a confirm that says how many ("Delete this comment and its reply?"); the page sends how many it showed (`?replies=N`), and a thread answered since is refused (`409`) and shown again rather than deleted unseen. On the video page each open thread has a Reply button; the reply box is labelled with the note it answers, Escape cancels, and posting or cancelling returns the focus to Reply; "Reply posted", "Reply deleted", and "Comment and its N replies deleted" are said to a screen reader. Replies are edited and deleted by their authors like any note, and count toward the 200 a video holds. `repliesByThread` and the thread-aware `splitComments` have unit tests with the service; the e2e test replies from the keyboard, edits the reply, resolves and reopens the thread, and deletes it through both confirms.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed: the server took a reply to a resolved thread (now a `409`; a thread resolved while a reply is being written takes the reply back too); and a reply could be left without its note, unseen and undeletable, if a delete failed part-way or a reply landed while its note was deleted (replies now go before the note, a reply whose note is gone is taken back before anyone is mailed, a late reply goes with the note it answered, and one left by any other path is listed, counted, and resolved on its own). A second review found nothing blocking. From the optional findings: deleting a thread answered since the page loaded is refused rather than silent, and a bad `?replies` is a `400`; deletes are announced and a deleted reply returns the focus to its thread (or to the thread's own action, or the comment box, when it has no Reply button); a thread cannot be resolved or deleted while one of its replies is being edited; a reply whose note is gone has no Reply button; and the docs say replies count toward the 200, list `parentId`, and say only open threads take replies.
  - Left as it is: a reply's mention email does not name the moment the thread is pinned to; and starting a reply on one thread drops a reply being typed on another, as the edit box does.
- Reply emails and links to a thread (`32bc0b3`): a reply to a review comment in a workspace emails the others in its thread, which means whoever wrote the note and each earlier reply, once each. It goes to the address each has as a member now (anyone who has left is skipped), and leaves out the one replying and anyone the reply mentions, who get the mention email instead. The email quotes the reply and the note it answers, and its reply-to is the one who replied. Mention and reply emails share one allowance of 60 an hour per author and 600 per instance. Both emails now link to `/videos/:id?ws=…&comment=<thread>` (with `&t=` for a pinned thread). The video page opened that way scrolls to the thread, unfolds the resolved notes if it is resolved, focuses it, marks it for a few seconds, and takes the parameter off the address. A link to a thread no longer there says so. `notifyReply` has unit tests (recipients, mentions, members who left or changed address, personal spaces, pinned threads, the shared allowance, a failed send). The e2e test opens a reply's link, a resolved thread's, and a deleted one's.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. The thread focused from the link lost its tabindex when its mark faded, so focus fell to the page. The row now stays focusable until the focus leaves it, and Tab goes on to the thread's buttons, which the e2e test checks. Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A mentioned thread member whose mention email did not go (the allowance, or a failed send) now gets the reply email instead.
    - The mention and reply emails are sent independently, so one failing does not stop the other.
    - An author's allowance is given back when the instance-wide limit refuses (`releaseRateLimit`, with a unit test).
    - Reply emails, and mentions inside a reply, name a pinned thread's moment.
    - A link to a thread that is gone is announced through the card's status line.
    - The docs say when the parameter leaves the address, and that a thread larger than the author's remaining hourly allowance is not emailed.
  - Left as it is: emails to a thread go all together or not at all, as mentions do.
- Copy link to a comment thread (`633232b`): each thread on the video page, open or resolved, has a Copy link button. It puts the thread's link on the clipboard, says "Link to the comment copied" to a screen reader, and shows a tick for two seconds. Where the clipboard is unavailable, the link is offered to copy by hand. The link has the same form as the one the comment emails carry, on the host the page is open on: `/videos/:id?ws=<workspace>&comment=<id>`, plus `&t=` for a pinned thread. It opens the page in the thread's workspace, at the thread. The emails and the button now build it with one helper, `threadLink`, which has a unit test. The e2e tests copy a thread's link, read the clipboard, and open it, in a personal space and from outside a workspace. Replies have no button of their own, since their link would be their thread's.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. A personal thread's link named the personal space, which the page ignores, so it showed "not found" when opened from a workspace. Personal threads' links now name no space and open in the one the reader is in. The page still never moves a reader out of a workspace on a link's say-so. The e2e test now checks the workspace case. Fixed from the optional findings: one timer for the tick, so a second copy keeps it showing; a clearer prompt when the clipboard is unavailable; and the docs list `ownerId` and the host the link uses.
- Comments as a spreadsheet (`e8a9d03`): the video page's comments card has a Download CSV link, and `GET /api/videos/:id/comments?format=csv` returns every note and reply as `comments-<videoId>.csv`. Its columns are `thread`, `kind`, `at`, `author`, `written`, `edited`, `status`, `resolved_at`, `resolved_by`, and `text`. Threads are numbered from 1 in the order they were started, each note is followed by its replies, oldest first, and a reply carries its thread's status. A reply whose note is gone is a thread of its own, as on the page, and is still marked a reply. Cells that start like a formula are prefixed with `'`, and the file opens as UTF-8 in Excel. `commentRows` has unit tests (order, numbering, the columns, a file the CSV parser reads back), and the e2e test downloads a thread from the link and reads the file it saved.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings, a reply whose note is gone is exported as a `reply`, not a `note`. The link is named "Download comments as CSV", since the page has another "Download CSV". The e2e test parses the downloaded file rather than a second request. Left as they are: the shared formula guard in `toCsv`, unchanged by this feature, and a failed download saving the error body, as the app's other download links do.
- Comments that mention you (`ed53e30`): Home names the open review threads that mention the person reading, as in "2 open comments mention you, on 1 video". A thread counts once whether its note or any of its replies has the `@email`. The line opens My videos at `?comments=mentions`. In a workspace the library has a Mentions me toggle beside Open comments; only one of the two is on at a time. The toggle also shows outside a workspace while the address set it, so it can be cleared. When nothing matches, it says "No open comment mentions you." `GET /api/videos` takes `comments=mentions` and returns `mentions` (open threads naming the reader, by video, for the page's videos) beside `openComments`. The analytics CSV follows the same filter. Both counts come from one read of the comments (`commentCounts(owner, reader)`), and a resolved thread stops counting. Unit tests cover the counts (note or reply, once per thread, resolved and reopened, a reply whose note is gone, other readers) and the filter. The e2e test follows Home's line into the library and works the toggles, in a workspace and out of one.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the reader is looked up only in a workspace, where mentions happen, so a personal space's library reads the session once per request as before. The docs say a request with an API key and no session matches no mentions. Two stale comments are brought up to date. Left as it is: following Home's link again while the library is open on the same address keeps the filter chosen since, as the other address filters do.
- Cards that mention you (`1b69f70`): in the library, a card or row whose open comments include threads that mention the reader shows its open-comments badge filled, with an @ in place of the speech bubble. Its title and screen-reader text say how many, as in "3 open comments, 2 mention you"; the count is never more than are open. The counts are the `mentions` that `GET /api/videos` already returns for the page. `openCommentsBadge` builds the label and has a unit test. The e2e test marks a card through the page's data and reads its badge.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Its one fix, the new helper placed between another function's doc comment and that function, is made.
- Ctrl+Enter posts a comment (`073f499`): in a video's comments, Ctrl+Enter (⌘Enter on a Mac) posts the new comment or the reply being typed, or saves the edit, as the form's button does. Enter alone still starts a new line. The key does nothing with Shift or Alt as well, held down, or as the Enter that ends an input method's composition. The boxes name the shortcut to screen readers. The Keyboard shortcuts dialog lists it under "In a video's comments", with Escape to cancel a reply or an edit. `isSubmitKey` has a unit test. The e2e tests post a note, post a reply, and save an edit with the key, check that Shift with it posts nothing, and find the dialog's entry.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. The new-comment box keeps the focus after Ctrl+Enter, so a note typed while the last one was still posting would be cleared when the post returned. The box is now read-only while posting, as the reply and edit boxes already were. Fixed from the optional findings: the three boxes share one handler, with a comment saying the form's own submit handler turns away an empty box or a post in flight. The dialog's wording is shorter. The e2e test checks through the API that Ctrl+Shift+Enter posted nothing. Left as they are: Post reply is no longer clicked in the e2e tests, since the key goes through the same form submit; and `aria-keyshortcuts` names both Control+Enter and Meta+Enter, as the sidebar's shortcuts do.
- Reactions on review comments (`250e955`): any note or reply on the video page takes emoji reactions. Its React button opens a row of five (👍 Thumbs up, ✅ Done, ❤️ Love, 👀 Looking, 🎉 Celebrate); Escape closes it, back on the button. The ones given show under the note as pills with their count, titled with who gave them, the reader as "you". A pill of your own is pressed, and pressing it takes yours back. `PATCH /api/videos/:id/comments/:commentId { reaction, on }` gives or takes back the caller's reaction on a note or reply, open or resolved. `reactions` keeps who gave each, in the order they did. Reactions given at the same moment all count: a write that lost the race is made again on the note as it now is, up to five times, then a `409`. Every comment response now carries `reactionList`, the note's reactions as its reader sees them. Edits and resolves keep a note's reactions. Unit tests cover giving and taking back, replies and resolved notes, three at once, the retry limit, the schema, and the summary. The e2e test reacts from the picker and from the keyboard, then takes the reactions back.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its optional findings:
    - Two reactions at once on a note from before revisions both count now. Such a note is given a revision first, so the writes after it are checked against that revision. A unit test covers it.
    - Every reaction control is disabled while one is on its way, rather than a press on another note doing nothing.
    - A pill that stays after it is pressed keeps the focus.
    - The picker closes when the focus leaves it, on Escape at its button, and when the note is opened for editing.
    - A bare heart is read as ❤️.
    - The tests no longer depend on the order in which a store keeps the emoji.

    Left as they are:
    - A text save or a resolve that lands at the same moment as a reaction is still a `409` asking for a reload, as any two changes at once are.
    - Pills name the email a person had when they reacted, as a note names its author's.
- Resolve all comments (`6b584ba`): with two or more open threads, the video page's comments card has a Resolve all button. It asks first ("Resolve all 3 open comments? Each can be reopened."), then marks the open threads it showed as done, by the person who pressed it. It says how many were resolved and how many are still open, and the focus goes to the resolved notes' toggle, or to the comment box when there is none. `POST /api/videos/:id/comments/resolve-all { ids }` takes the threads the caller was shown, 1 to 200, and returns `{ resolved, failed, open }`. Each thread is resolved as resolving it alone would be: its replies go with its note, and a reply whose note is gone counts as its own thread. An id that is no longer an open thread start is left alone. Up to eight are written at a time. A thread changed in the same moment, for example by a reaction, is read again and tried up to five times, and one that still fails is counted without stopping the rest. Unit tests cover the count, replies, a reply whose note is gone, a thread started since, an earlier resolver kept, the retry and its limit, a reaction at the same moment, another owner, and the schema. The e2e test says no to the question, then yes while a teammate posts a note, which stays open, and checks the API.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. The request named no threads, so a note posted while the question was open would have been resolved, folded away unseen. The page now sends the threads it showed, and only those are resolved.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The announcement counts what is still open, from the server's answer.
    - When there is no resolved toggle to take the focus, it goes to the comment box.
    - Threads are written eight at a time, with a 60-second limit on the route.
    - A thread that cannot be resolved is counted and shown as an error without stopping the others.
    - The header wraps at phone width.
    - A unit test forces the retry, and its limit, with a store that loses the race.
- Comments by their moment (`10e90aa`): with two or more open threads, one of them pinned to a moment, the video page's comments card has an Order select. "As written" is the order the threads were started. "By time in the video" lists the pinned threads from the earliest moment, then the notes on the whole video. Threads at the same moment keep the order they were written. This browser remembers the choice for every video page. Resolving the next note, Resolve all, and the links that open a thread all follow the order shown. `orderThreads` and `commentOrderFrom` have unit tests, and the e2e test switches the order, resolves a note in that order, and opens a second video, which keeps the order chosen.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings, the e2e test now opens another video to check the order is kept, and checks that resolving a note in the video's order hands the focus to the next note shown.
- Comments that mention you, or that you wrote in (`a135839`): in a workspace, the video page's comments card has a Show select with three choices:
  - "All open".
  - "Mentioning me": threads whose note or a reply names you as `@email`.
  - "I wrote in": threads whose note or a reply you wrote.

  The choice lasts for this visit. The card's counts stay those of every open thread. When nothing is left to show, the card says so ("No open comment mentions you." or "You have not written in any open comment.") and offers "Show all N open". Resolving hands the focus to the next thread shown. Resolve all takes only the threads shown, and its question says "the N comments shown". A link to a thread shows every thread again, so the one it names is there. `filterThreads` has unit tests. The e2e test runs in a workspace, where the page's data plays two teammates, one of whom names you, and narrows the threads each way.
