  - An independent review found two blocking problems, now fixed:
    - Reopening a note that the filter hides made it vanish and lost the focus. Now every open thread is shown again, and the focus is on the reopened note's Resolve button.
    - A filter left on after every thread was resolved, with its select gone, hid the next note posted. The filter now goes back to all with the select. A note posted under "Mentioning me" also shows every thread, since a note never names its own writer.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A screen reader hears how many threads are shown each time the filter changes, as in "1 of 2 open shown".
    - "Show all" hands the focus to the select.
    - When the last thread shown is resolved, the focus goes to "Show all".
    - Resolve all's title names the comments shown.
  - Left as it is: a reply or edit form on a thread that the filter hides keeps its draft until the thread is shown again.
  - The e2e test covers each of these.
- Comments as text (`651c3c7`): the video page's comments card has a Copy as text button. It puts the open threads on the clipboard as plain text, ready to paste into a chat or an email. Only the threads shown are copied, in the order shown, after a workspace's Show filter. The text opens with a heading naming the video and how many threads there are. Each thread is numbered, with its moment first when it is pinned to one and who wrote it after it (by email, or "you" or "a teammate" when the account mode knows none), and its replies are indented under it. A note of several lines keeps them under its first. A tick and the screen-reader line "2 comments copied as text" confirm the copy. Where the clipboard is unavailable, the text is shown in a box, already selected, to copy by hand; Done or Escape closes it. `commentsAsText` has unit tests covering the layout, replies, a note of several lines, and ten threads or more. The e2e test reads the clipboard in both orders.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. Without a clipboard, for example on a page served over plain http, the text was offered in a prompt, which collapses its lines into one. It is now shown in a box with its lines kept, and a screen reader is told to copy it from there.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A teammate's note with no email is written as "a teammate", not "you".
    - The title is set in curly quotes, so quotes in it read cleanly.
    - Windows line breaks in a note come out as plain ones.
    - Blank lines inside a note stay blank.
    - The title is required.
  - The unit and e2e tests cover each of these.
- Comments along the video (`d5ec4bd`): when the open threads shown include some pinned to a moment, the video page's comments card shows a strip under its heading. It holds one mark per moment, placed along the video's length, and a line for how far the player has got. Pressing a mark moves the player to that moment. A mark is named by its moment and how many comments it holds, as in "0:02: 2 comments", and hovering shows their first words. Threads at the same moment, or too close to press apart, share a mark at the earliest of them. Marks are kept at least 24 pixels apart, measured on the strip itself, so they can be pressed at a phone's width. The strip follows the threads shown: a workspace's Show filter narrows it, a resolved thread leaves it, and it goes when no thread shown is pinned. It is one tab stop: the arrow keys, Home, and End move between the marks, and Enter moves the player. `commentMarkers` has unit tests. The e2e test checks the names, the spacing on a phone, the mouse and the keys, and resolving.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. Marks were grouped at 2% of the length, only 6 or 7 pixels on the card, so neighbouring 14-pixel dots overlapped and were hard to press. The gap is now 24 pixels of the strip as measured, and each mark can be pressed over 24 pixels around its dot.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The strip is one tab stop rather than one per mark.
    - A mark's hint lists the first words of up to three of its comments.
    - The e2e test waits for the player to know the video's length before checking that a note on the whole video has no mark.
- Comments new since your last visit (`9ae4f7f`): the video page's comments card marks the notes and replies a teammate wrote since you last opened that video in this browser with a New pill, and says how many there are in the threads shown ("2 new since you last opened this video"). Your own are never new. On a first visit nothing is new, since there is nothing to compare with. The marks hold for the whole visit, and the next visit starts from the newest comment shown. What has been read is kept per person and per video, so people who share a browser each keep their own. `GET /api/videos/:id/comments` now also returns `you`, the caller's user id, for this. `newSince`, `seenMark`, and `commentsSeenKey` have unit tests. The e2e test plays a teammate through the page's data and checks a first visit, a note and a reply written while away, your own note, resolving, the next visit, and a video seen with no comments while the browser's clock runs an hour ahead.
  - An independent review found three blocking problems, now fixed:
    - A video seen with no comments kept the browser's own time, so a browser clock running ahead could hide a teammate's first note. It now keeps a mark from before any comment, so the first one written later is new.
    - The mark was shared by everyone who signed in on the same browser, so one person's visit could hide comments from the next. It is now kept per person, by the user id the comments come with.
    - The e2e test could check for no New pills before the page had read its mark, so it passed without testing anything. The card now says when the mark has been read, and the test waits for that.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The count follows a workspace's Show filter, counting only the threads shown.
    - The pill is set apart from the date, so a screen reader does not run them together.
    - Your own notes no longer set the mark, so a teammate's note saved just after yours is still new next time.
    - The comments card starts over when the page moves to another video.
  - Left as it is:
    - The count is not announced to a screen reader, though it can be read in place.
    - A new reply in a resolved thread is marked in that thread but not counted.
- C to comment at the player's moment (`b6ce88b`): on a video's page, pressing C outside a text box or form pauses the player and pins the new comment to that moment, with "Pin to m:ss in the video" ticked. The cursor then goes to the comment box. A screen reader hears "Paused at 0:04; the note will be pinned there". Before the video is finished, C only moves to the comment box. It follows the rules of the other single-key shortcuts:
  - Caps Lock counts.
  - On other keyboard layouts, the key where C sits counts.
  - Shift, Ctrl, ⌘, Alt, a held key, or an input method does not trigger it.
  - It is off when single-key shortcuts are turned off in the Keyboard shortcuts dialog, which now lists it.

  `isCommentKey` has unit tests. The e2e test checks:
  - playing, pausing, and the pinned note;
  - typing a C in the box;
  - the shortcut turned off;
  - an open reply form;
  - Caps Lock.
  - An independent review found three blocking problems, now fixed:
    - C acted behind the open menu drawer on a phone, leaving the drawer open over the page. It now does nothing while the drawer is open.
    - C did nothing with Caps Lock on.
    - C pressed on a reply's or an edit's buttons, or on an open menu such as In another language, moved away and left that form or menu open behind. C is now left alone inside a form or an open menu.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A screen reader is told the player paused and where the note will be pinned.
    - The comment box lists C among its keys.
    - Before the player has loaded the video, C does not pin to 0:00.
    - The README says what C does before the video is finished.
    - The e2e test formats the moment as the page does.
- New comments in the library (`c97ca47`): in a workspace, a library card's or row's open-comments badge has a dot when a teammate has written in its open threads since you last opened the video in this browser. A screen reader hears it with the count, as in "2 open comments; new since you last opened it".
  - It uses the same mark as the video page's New pills: what each person has read, per video, kept in this browser.
  - A video never opened here is not marked, and nor is a note of your own.
  - Opening the video clears the dot.
  - `GET /api/videos` now also returns `latestComment`, when someone other than the reader last wrote a note or a reply in an open thread on each video, and `you`, the reader's user id. Both are sent only in a workspace, and only to a signed-in person.

  `commentCounts`' new `latest`, `hasNewComments`, and the badge's label have unit tests. The e2e test runs in a workspace with a teammate played by the page's data, and checks:
  - a video written in since it was opened;
  - one opened since;
  - one never opened;
  - clearing the dot;
  - the list layout.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. The dot's amber was too faint on the light badge to be seen. It is now darker there, and lighter on the dark badge of comments that mention you.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A request with an API key and no session stands for the workspace itself, so it gets neither field rather than every comment as new.
    - The dots are worked out with the page's data instead of a render later, so a card does not shift when they appear.
    - The e2e test lets go of requests the page drops, and removes its fakes when it ends.
    - A unit test checks that the reader's own reply does not move the time.
  - Left as it is: a comment written in the very millisecond of the mark is not new, on the video page or in the library.
- New comments on Home (`b580830`): in a workspace, Home's Things to check names the videos a teammate wrote in since you last opened them in this browser, newest first, as in "New comments since you last looked, on Launch teaser, Q3 recap and Onboarding." Each title links to its video. Past three, the rest are counted, as in "and 2 other videos", which links to the library's open comments. Each title tells a screen reader why it is listed ("(new comments)"). It uses the same marks as the video page's New pills and the library's dots, now worked out by one shared helper, `freshVideoIds`, which has unit tests. The e2e test extends the library one to Home: it checks which videos are named, follows a link, and checks that, once opened, the video is no longer named.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its optional findings:
    - Long titles wrap rather than running out of the box on a phone.
    - The count of other videos is a link.
    - Each title carries its reason for a screen reader's links list.
    - The e2e test's last check waits for Home's videos to arrive, and goes back with the browser's Back.
  - Left as it is: a video opened in another tab stays named on a Home page already open until it is loaded again.
- Mark new comments as read from Home (`b2b5810`): Home's line of videos with new comments has a Mark as read button. It marks every video on the line as read in this browser, past the three named too, as opening each would, then says so to a screen reader and moves the keyboard to the first quick action. The line also follows a video opened in another tab: Home reads the marks again when one changes, and when the tab comes back to the front. To do this, `GET /api/videos` also returns `latestAnyComment`: for the videos in `latestComment`, when someone other than the reader last wrote in any of their threads, resolved ones too. That is the mark opening the video keeps. `markCommentsSeen` has unit tests, and so has the new count in `commentCounts`. The e2e test does the following:
  - opens a video in a second tab and checks that Home drops it;
  - uses the button, and checks the focus and the announcement;
  - reloads, and checks that the line stays gone.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. The button marked each video with the newest note in its open threads, which opening the video does not. A reply written later in a resolved thread then came back as new on the video page, and the video came back on Home once that thread was reopened. It now keeps the same mark as opening the video.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A later mark already kept, from a video opened in another tab say, is not overwritten.
    - The keyboard moves on only when every video was marked. Otherwise it stays on the button.
    - The same announcement twice is spoken twice.
    - The button's name says what it marks.
    - The e2e test checks the focus, and removes its fakes on every tab when it ends.
  - Left as it is: Home does not load its videos again when the tab comes back, so a video written in since Home was loaded appears on the next load.
- Sort the library by recent comments (`80ea277`): the library's sort menu has Recent comments, `sort=commented` on `GET /api/videos` and on the analytics CSV. The video with the newest note or reply comes first, whoever wrote it and in any thread, resolved ones too. Videos without comments come after, newest made first, as do videos commented at the same moment. `commentCounts` now also returns `lastAt`, the newest comment time on each video, which only orders the list and is never sent. The analytics route reads the comment times when this sort is asked for, so the CSV keeps the library's order. Unit tests cover the order, ties, unreadable times, and replies and resolved threads. The e2e test comments on each video in turn, and checks the library and the CSV each time.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The e2e test now checks orders that differ from newest made: right after the older video's note, and after a reply on its thread. Both the library and the CSV are checked each time.
    - A comment dated at the epoch counts as a comment. Only a time that cannot be read counts as none, and a unit test covers both.
    - The query schema's comment describes the new sort.
- Filter the library by presenter (`dfc25ff`): the library has a "Filter by presenter" picker, `presenter=<avatar id>` on `GET /api/videos` and on the analytics CSV. It keeps the videos a presenter appears in: as the video's own presenter where a scene shows it, as a scene's own presenter, or in a cinematic shot. The list now returns `presenters`, each presenter in this view (the library or the trash) with how many videos it is in, whatever the other filters, named as its newest video named it. The picker shows them as "Priya Nair (4)" once the view has more than one presenter. The library reads `?presenter=` from its address, and the video page's Avatar row has an Other videos link that opens it. `videoPresenters` lives in `src/lib/presenters.ts`, so the filter, its counts, and the link all use the same rule. Unit tests cover scenes, hidden presenters, cinematic shots, template and directed videos, the counts and names, and the trash. The e2e test filters from the picker, the address, and the video page, and checks the CSV and the empty state.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. A HeyGen template video was counted as having a presenter. The template's name stood in the presenter's name and a placeholder id stood in the presenter's id, so the picker offered the template as a presenter, and a newer template video could rename a real presenter. Template videos now show none of the account's presenters, since the template decides who appears.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The video page's link uses the filter's own rule. A video whose scenes all have other presenters links to the first of them.
    - A presenter chosen from the address reads "Chosen presenter" while the list loads, and "This presenter (0)" only once the list shows no video with them.
    - The API docs say exactly which presenters a video shows.
    - The tests cover a trashed video, template and directed videos, and the link. The e2e test no longer relies on the default presenter.
  - Two existing e2e tests checked the empty state's wording, which now names the presenter filter, and were updated to match.
- Presenters' video counts on the Avatars page (`45f2163`): each presenter that appears in a library video says how many, under its tile, as "In 4 videos". The count links to the library filtered by that presenter (`/videos?presenter=<id>`). The counts are the library's own presenter counts from `GET /api/videos`, so they match what the link shows: trashed videos, HeyGen template videos, and scenes that hide the presenter are not counted. Deleting a presenter that is in videos says so first, and says that rendered videos keep it but that retrying or copying one needs another presenter. While the counts are still loading, or could not be read, the prompt says the presenter may be in videos. The e2e test covers:
  - the counts, singular and plural, with a trashed video and a hidden-presenter scene left out;
  - the link and its accessible name;
  - both delete prompts, with the counts known and still loading;
  - the library the link opens.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The delete prompt no longer calls a presenter unused before the counts arrive.
    - The link's accessible name starts with its visible text, and the link uses the page's brand colour.
    - The e2e test covers what is not counted, the singular form, and the prompt while counting. It no longer depends on the order of two videos made in the same second.
  - Left as it is: the page asks the library list route for its counts, which also refreshes in-flight renders, as the library itself does. A counts-only route can come later if the cost shows.
- Filter the library by voice, with voice counts on the Voices page (`36b398c`). This works like the presenter filter.
  - **API and library picker:** `GET /api/videos` and the analytics CSV take `voice=<voice id>`. The library has a "Filter by voice" picker, which also reads `?voice=` from its address. A video matches when it speaks in that voice: as its own voice where a scene speaks in it, or as a scene's own voice. Scenes lip-synced to a recording, scenes of nothing but pauses, and cinematic shots speak in none. A HeyGen template video speaks in the voices its voice slots were given.
  - **Counts:** the list returns `voices`, counted and named like `presenters`.
  - **Video page:** the Voice row links to the filtered library.
  - **Voices page:** each voice in use says "In N videos", linked to the filtered library.
  - **Delete warnings:** the list also returns `needs`. It counts, across the library and the trash together, how many videos a retry or copy would ask for each presenter and voice. Those are the video's own and every scene's, whether or not a scene shows or speaks in them, plus a cinematic shot's presenters and a template's slots. Deleting a voice, or a presenter on the Avatars page, now warns with these counts.
  - **Shared rules:** the rules live in `src/lib/presenters.ts` (`videoPresenters`, `videoVoices`, `videoNeeds`).
  - **Tests:** unit tests cover the rules, the counts, the trash, templates, and silent and recorded scenes. The e2e test covers:
    - the picker, the address, and the CSV;
    - the video page link;
    - the Voices page counts and link;
    - the delete prompt with a trashed video counted.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed:
    - **Delete prompts counted too few videos.** They counted only videos a voice was heard in, so a retry or copy of a video that still named the voice could fail with no warning. That covered a video whose every scene had its own voice, and videos in the trash. The same held for presenters on the Avatars page. Both prompts now count every video that needs the voice or presenter.
    - **Template videos were left out.** A HeyGen template video was never counted, though its slots can hold one of your voices or presenters. Their slots now count, for the filters as well as the warnings, and the docs say so.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A scene of nothing but pauses does not count as speaking.
    - The video page's link names the voice or presenter it opens when that is not the one its row shows.
    - The tests cover a trashed video, template slots, silent scenes, and the needs counts.
  - Two existing e2e tests check the empty state's wording, which now names the voice filter, and were updated to match.
  - Left as they are: the pickers' counts are of the whole view, whatever the other filters, as the folder counts are. The e2e test does not make a recorded scene, which needs an uploaded clip; the unit tests cover it.
- Clear filters in the library (`29a74a7`): one button puts the search and every filter back. It covers status, folder, presenter, voice, review, share link, date made, Starred, and Open comments or Mentions me. The button shows beside the filters while any is on, and in the empty state when the filters hide every video, but only where clearing them would show some. The sort, the layout, and the trash view are not filters, so they stay. The filters the address asked for leave the address too, so a reload or the back button does not bring them back. That covers Home's notices, the video page's presenter and voice links, and a batch's results. The address is rewritten in the history at once, which Next keeps the page's search params in step with, and anything in it that is not a filter is kept. The clear is announced ("Filters cleared"), and the keyboard goes to the search box. `withoutLibraryFilters` and `LIBRARY_FILTER_PARAMS` in `src/lib/client.ts` hold the address's filter parameters, which the page also re-reads them by, and have unit tests. The e2e test covers:
  - arriving with a batch, a presenter, a voice, and a status in the address;
  - adding a search, Starred, and a date;
  - clearing from the empty state, then from the bar after a search and a presenter;
  - the address, the focus, the announcement said again, the sort kept, a reload, and the trash view kept.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The empty state's button shows only where clearing shows videos, not when every video is in the trash.
    - The address changes at once, rather than after a router navigation that left a batch's filter, its banner, and the button in place for a moment, or could undo a filter picked meanwhile.
    - The page re-reads its address filters from the same list Clear filters takes off, so the two cannot drift apart.
    - The e2e test makes its own two presenters and voices, so the pickers show whatever else the library holds. It also covers a batch link, a second clear, and the trash view.
- "Use in videos" filter on the Avatars and Voices pages (`939ceb6`): a select lists any presenter or voice, only those some video needs, or only those none does. It reads the list's `needs` counts: the videos, in the library and the trash together, a retry or copy of which would ask for it. Only videos are counted, not interactive presenters, saved templates, the brand kit, or a presenter's default voice, and the select's title and the README say so. The select is disabled until the counts arrive, and its title says when they could not be read. `passesUse` in `src/lib/client.ts` holds the rule and has unit tests. It reads an id's own count only, and lists nothing as either before the counts are read. The e2e tests filter both pages both ways and check the Avatars page's empty state with a filter on.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. The code comments and the README called the presenters and voices no video needs safe to delete, or a list to tidy up. The counts are of videos only, so an interactive presenter, a template, or the brand kit may still name one. The wording now says only what is counted.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The select's title says when the counts could not be read, rather than "Counting…" for good, and the Voices page shows the select then too.
    - With the counts not read yet, the rule lists nothing as unused, rather than everything.
    - With a filter or search on and nothing matching, the Avatars page says "No avatars match" rather than inviting a first avatar.
    - The select's title says the counts cover the library and the trash, which the "In N videos" links (library only) do not.
  - Left as it is: "Not used by any video" lists library presenters and stock voices too, which cannot be deleted. The filter is about use, and those are worth seeing there as well.
- Delete warnings name what else uses a presenter or voice (`019ad2d`): before deleting a photo avatar or a cloned voice, the Avatars and Voices pages now name the interactive presenters, saved templates (including any scene's presenter or voice), and brand kit defaults that use it, and for a voice the photo avatars that speak with it by default, beside the count of videos a retry or copy would need. `GET /api/usage` returns these per id, and `collectOtherUses` in `src/lib/services/usage.ts` builds them, listing each use once per thing that names it. `describeOtherUses` in `src/lib/presenters.ts` writes the sentence ("a and b", or "a, b, and c"): one of a kind by name, more than one as a count ("2 templates"). Unit tests cover both, and the e2e test for the Avatars page checks the prompt names an interactive presenter.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed. The first version listed the owner's photo avatars with `listAvatars`, which also asks the video renderer for its library on every page load. The service now reads the owner's photo avatars from the store, which is all it needs.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - With no video using it, the prompt says "It is used by", not "It is also used by".
    - When the other uses could not be read, or have not arrived yet, the prompt says interactive presenters, templates, and the brand kit could not be checked, rather than implying none use it.
    - A photo avatar's default voice is named as such ("the photo avatar “Ana” (as its default voice)").
    - Viewers, who cannot delete, do not fetch the other uses.
    - The unit tests also cover the two-item join, the plural photo-avatar wording, and a voice named only in a template scene.
  - Left as it is: the other uses are read once when the page opens, so a presenter or template made in another tab shows up after a reload. The videos count works the same way.
- "Used by" under each presenter and voice (`86521f3`): the Avatars and Voices pages now show, under each tile's "In N videos" link, the interactive presenters, saved templates, brand kit defaults, and (for a voice) photo avatars that use it, from the same `GET /api/usage` the delete warnings read. One of a kind is named in the line ("Used by the interactive presenter “Front desk”"); when a kind is counted ("Used by 2 templates"), the line opens to list every name. `OtherUsesLine` in `src/components/OtherUsesLine.tsx` renders it, and `usesOf` and `nameOtherUses` in `src/lib/presenters.ts` read and name the uses, with unit tests. Workspace viewers now read the uses too: the interactive presenters, templates, brand kit, and photo avatars they name are already theirs to read. The e2e tests check a named line on the Avatars page, a counted line that opens to two template names on the Voices page, and the line as a viewer sees it.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - A counted line first carried the names in a `title`, out of reach by keyboard, touch, or screen reader. It now opens to list them.
    - A long name wraps inside the tile rather than widening the grid column.
    - The line keeps the same gap whether or not a video count sits above it.
    - The usage route's and service's comments say the pages show the line, not only a delete warning.
    - The e2e tests also cover the counted line, the viewer's view, and the Avatars page's single-use line.
  - Left as it is: the brand kit is named as "the brand kit", without saying whether as the default presenter or voice. The page already says which: a presenter on the Avatars page, a voice on the Voices page.
- Sort the Avatars and Voices pages (`df98655`): a Sort select beside the filters lists presenters and voices with favourites first (the order they had before, and still the default), the most used in the library first (by the same counts the "In N videos" links show; ties keep the listed order), or by name A–Z (ignoring case, and reading numbers as numbers, so "Ava 2" comes before "Ava 10"). The sort applies after the search and filters. `sortPresenters` in `src/lib/client.ts` holds the rule and has unit tests. "Most used" is offered once the counts arrive, and the select's title says when they could not be read. The e2e tests sort both pages each way.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The option says "Most used in the library", since it counts the library's videos only, not the trash the Use in videos filter also counts.
    - The select's title says what Most used counts, or why it is not offered.
    - The Avatars test adds a presenter in no video whose name sorts first, so "most used" and "name" give different orders and the test tells them apart.
    - Each page builds its counts once per answer from the library, rather than on every render.
  - Left as it is:
    - The other two sorts do not keep favourites first, since a sort the person picks should lead.
    - On the Voices page, the Sort select shows with the other filters once the library has answered.
- The Avatars and Voices pages remember their sort (`c92fc75`): the Sort select's choice is kept in this browser, as the library keeps its layout, and read back on the next visit. `usePresenterSort` in `src/hooks/usePresenterSort.ts` reads and writes it, and `parsePresenterSort` in `src/lib/client.ts` reads anything it does not know as the default order. Where storage is blocked, the choice holds for the visit. The e2e tests reload each page and find the sort kept.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking.
  - Fixed from the optional findings: a remembered "Most used" is shown only once the counts arrive. Until then, or for good if they cannot be read, the page lists and names favourites first, while the stored choice is kept. `shownPresenterSort` holds that rule and has a unit test, and the e2e test that holds back the counts checks it.
  - Left as it is: the choice is per browser, not per workspace, as the library's layout is.
- Replace a presenter or voice everywhere else it is used (`0087839`). Under the "Used by" line on the Avatars and Voices pages, editors get a Replace… control: pick another presenter or voice and confirm, and every interactive presenter, saved template (its own presenter or voice and each scene's), the brand kit's default, and, for a voice, every photo avatar's default voice switches to it. Videos already made are left as they are, and the library's counts still show them. A note says what changed, and the focus moves to it. `POST /api/usage/replace` (`{ kind, from, to }`, editors only) does the work through `replaceUses` in `src/lib/services/usage.ts`. It looks up the new presenter or voice once, before writing anything: 404 for an unknown presenter, 400 for an unknown voice or one replaced with itself. An interactive presenter takes the new presenter's name and picture, or the new voice's language. Each record is written only while it still names the old presenter or voice, conditionally on its `updatedAt` and read fresh just before the write. So an edit landing meanwhile is kept, and a record deleted meanwhile stays deleted. `replaceInTemplate` and `replaceInAgent` hold the rewrite rules and have unit tests, and a service test runs a presenter replace and a voice replace against the memory store. The e2e test replaces a photo avatar in an interactive presenter and a template, checks the confirmation, the focus, and the server's refusals, and checks that a viewer gets no Replace.
  - An independent review found three blocking problems, all now fixed:
    - One interactive presenter whose other voice or presenter had been deleted stopped the whole replace part-way, with an error naming neither. The replacement is now looked up once and each presenter is switched on its own fields, so that case is switched too, and a unit test covers it.
    - Records were written from the list read at the start with a plain write, which could overwrite an edit made meanwhile and bring back a deleted template or brand kit. Writes are now conditional and retried on a conflict, as the background-image delete's are.
    - The focus was lost when the control closed. It now moves to the choice on open, back to the button on Cancel, and to the note after a replace.
  - A second review of those fixes found nothing blocking. Fixed from the optional findings of both reviews:
    - The note is always on the page, so a screen reader hears it change.
    - Cancel clears the choice and any error.
    - The "Used by" lines are read again after a failed replace too.
    - Records are read fresh just before each write.
    - The focus goes back to the choice when a replace fails.
  - Left as it is:
    - No entry in the activity feed.
    - A visitor's turn that saves an interactive presenter's counters in the moment between that read and the write can still be written over, or can write the old presenter back. Those counter writes are not conditional anywhere yet.
    - A photo avatar renamed in that same moment can lose the rename, since avatars carry no `updatedAt` to check.
- A replace is recorded in the activity feed (`0a901cb`). One that changed something adds a `usage.replaced` event to the workspace's feed, for example: `Dana replaced "Ava" with "Ben" in 2 templates and the brand kit`. The event stores the old and new names (`subject`, `replacement`), the old id (`subjectId`), and what changed (`replaced: { kind, agents, templates, brand, avatars }`). The sentence is worded from those counts when the feed is shown. A name the server cannot find is left out, and the feed says "a deleted presenter" or "a deleted voice" in its place. A replace that changes nothing records nothing. `describeActivity` words it and has unit tests: the full sentence, an API-key caller, every kind at once, and a deleted old one. The e2e test checks the event after a replace, and that a second replace finding nothing left records no new event.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed: the first version stored what changed as text cut at 80 characters, so a voice replace touching every kind of thing ended mid-word in the feed. The counts are stored instead.
  - Fixed from the optional findings:
    - The old presenter's or voice's id is no longer shown in place of a missing name.
    - The kind is stored, so the feed can say which was deleted.
  - Left as it is: the route looks the names up again after the replace (one more lookup each, with no effect on what was changed).
- Fix: sizes given to a text field or select now take effect (`bdbec26`). `inputClass` was a string of Tailwind utilities beginning with `w-full py-2 text-sm`, and about 80 controls appended their own size (`w-auto`, `w-40`, `py-1`, `text-2xl`). Which of two conflicting utilities won came down to the order Tailwind wrote them out, so `w-full`, `py-2`, `text-sm`, and the grey border always won. As a result:
  - The filter selects on the Avatars, Voices, and library pages each stretched across their own row.
  - Fixed-width fields filled theirs.
  - The brand kit's red border for an invalid colour never showed.

  The shared look is now the `.field` class in `src/app/globals.css`, in Tailwind's components layer, so a utility given beside it always wins. Filter rows now sit in one line, compact controls are compact, and the invalid-colour border shows. The two places that worked around this by string-replacing `w-full` now append their width. The e2e test for the Avatars page checks that its filters share a row and are sized to their content. Found while taking screenshots of the Avatars page.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its optional findings:
    - The bulk go-live and expiry date field is a little wider, as the video page's own date fields are, so its time and calendar icon are not clipped.
    - The selects sized to their content are capped at their row (the library's folder select at 14rem, as its presenter and voice selects are), so a long name cannot push a phone-width page sideways.
    - The Avatars search box keeps its 220px.
    - The new test asserts each measure on its own.
- Filter the workspace activity feed by area (`fc6c99e`). The Activity card on the Workspace page has a Show select with these choices:
  - Everything
  - Videos and batches
  - Reviews
  - Members
  - API keys
  - Brand kit
  - Shared presenters
  - Presenter and voice replaces

  `GET /api/activity` takes `?area=` (`videos`, `reviews`, `members`, `keys`, `brand`, `presenters`, or `replaces`). It filters before applying the limit, so 30 events of one area are listed even when other activity is newer. Any other value is a `400`. The answer echoes the area, so the page shows a list only under the filter it was fetched for, and switching never flashes the previous list.
  - A filter with nothing in it says so ("Nothing under “API keys” yet. The feed keeps the last 200 events.").
  - Once a filter is picked, a status line tells screen readers how many events it shows, Everything included.
  - Each event kind maps to its area in `src/lib/activity-text.ts`, typed over every kind, so a new kind does not compile until it is given an area.
  - The card's description now lists everything the feed records: deletions for good, batches, reviews, and replaces were missing.
  - An independent review found one blocking bug. The members, invites, and roles actions refetch the feed when they finish. If the filter was changed while one of them ran, that refetch fetched the old filter, and the list was dropped as not matching, leaving the feed blank. Those actions now refetch whichever filter is shown when they finish. Fixed from the optional findings: the empty-filter wording no longer suggests 200 events exist, switching back to Everything is announced too, and a doc comment is corrected.
- Download the workspace activity feed as a CSV (`6f982c9`). The Activity card on the Workspace page has a Download CSV link beside its Show select. It downloads every kept event under the filter shown (up to 200), not just the 30 on the page. `GET /api/activity?format=csv` (with `&area=` when a filter is on) answers `activity-<area or all>-<date>.csv`, which has these columns:
  - `when`: the stored ISO time
  - `who`: the email, or "A member" / "A script with an API key"
  - `area`: as the Show select names it
  - `kind`
  - `what_happened`: the feed's sentence, worded for no one in particular so the file reads the same for whoever downloads it

  The rows come from `activityRows` in `src/lib/activity-text.ts`, beside the feed's own wording. A cell a spreadsheet would read as a formula is defused by `toCsv`, as for the other downloads. The link is left out while the filter shows nothing, and its name says which filter it downloads ("Download CSV of Videos and batches activity").
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its optional findings:
    - A CSV request no longer checks `limit`, which it never applies, so `?format=csv&limit=500` downloads instead of answering `400`.
    - The docs say the file holds every kept event under the filter, not only the 30 the card lists.
    - The docs also note that a personal account's events all name "A script with an API key" (its actor is the owner itself), as its JSON feed does.
- Filter the workspace activity feed by person (`ea5fbbe`). The Activity card has an "Activity by" select beside the area select (renamed "Activity area", so the two controls' names no longer overlap). It lists Anyone, each current member by email (by id when there is none, as in the member list; you as "You"), and Scripts with an API key.
  - **API:** `GET /api/activity` takes `?actor=`: a member's user id, or the workspace's own id for requests made with an API key and no session. It combines with `?area=`, both apply before the limit, and the answer echoes it as `actor`.
  - **CSV:** the download follows both filters. A person's file is named `-by-<member id>` or `-by-api-key`.
  - **Wording:** the empty note, the screen-reader count, and the download link's name all name both filters, from one helper (`activityFilterPhrase`). For example: "Nothing under “Videos and batches” by a script with an API key yet.", "2 events shown under Videos and batches by you".
  - **Former members:** someone who has left drops out of the list, and their events stay under Anyone.
  - An independent review found one blocking bug. A pick of someone who then left (or was removed) kept filtering while the select fell back to showing Anyone, so the list, the wording, and the download disagreed with the control. The person in effect is now derived from the current members, so all four go back to Anyone together. The e2e test drives that case through a refetch that drops the member. Also fixed from its optional findings: members without an email are told apart, the two selects have distinct names, the person shows in the file name, and the README wording says either filter works alone.
- Lines in the workspace activity feed link to what they are about (`1093d3f`). `GET /api/activity` now answers `links`, keyed by event id, for the events whose subject is still in the space. Each one leads here:
  - a video made, trashed, restored, sent for review, approved, or sent back: its page (`/videos/<id>`), including a video in the trash while it can still be restored, whose page has its restore banner
  - a shared presenter made or copied: Interactive
  - a key still in use: Settings
  - a kit still kept: Brand kit
  - a replace: Avatars or Voices

  A bulk action, a member, or a thing since deleted (another space's video included) has no link, so a line never leads to "not found". Each record is checked once per answer, whatever the number of lines about it (`activityLinks` in `src/lib/services/activity.ts`). The sentence itself is the link, so its name says where it goes.
  - An independent review found one blocking bug. A video whose time in the trash was up, but which no listing had deleted yet, still got a link, and its page answers "not found". Such a video now gets no link, and the unit test covers it. Also fixed from its optional findings:
    - The checks run ten lines at a time instead of all at once.
    - A failed store read is logged and leaves the line unlinked, and the test covers that too.
    - A brand kit counts only when it names this owner, as the Brand page reads it.
    - The link underline is darker, so a linked line stands out without hovering.
- New activity since your last visit (`0443d48`). The workspace Activity card puts a New badge on each line a teammate (or a script with an API key) did since this browser last showed the reader the whole feed, with "N new since your last visit" above the list.
  - **Your own lines:** never new.
  - **First visit:** nothing is new.
  - **Per person and workspace:** this browser keeps a mark for each reader in each workspace (`avatar-studio:activity-seen:<user>:<workspace>`). It is read once per visit, so the badges stay while the page is open.
  - **Moving the mark:** only the unfiltered feed moves it forward, because a filtered view leaves out events that are still unseen. It moves to the newest teammate event shown, by the server's clock, and never back, even when the feed is open in another tab.
  - **Storage:** a browser that refuses storage shows nothing new, and the feed still works.
  - **Code:** `newActivityIds` and `activitySeenMark` in `src/lib/activity-text.ts`, beside the feed's wording.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its optional findings:
    - When every one of the 30 lines loaded is new, the count says "30+".
    - A screen reader hears "New:" before the line rather than after its time.
    - The doc comment says that events made with an API key count as a teammate's.
    - The e2e test clears the mark it moved ahead.
- Home says when the workspace has new activity (`909bed3`). In a workspace, Home's Things to check reads the Activity feed's newest 30 lines and the reader's mark from 260. It counts what teammates (or scripts with an API key) did since the reader last saw the whole feed in this browser, for example "3 new in the workspace's activity since you last opened it", or "30+" when every line loaded is new. An Open activity link opens the Workspace page at its Activity card (`/workspace#activity`).
  - Home only reads the mark, so the lines are still marked New when the feed is opened. Opening the feed moves the mark, and the Home line goes.
  - Home reads the mark again when the feed is opened in another tab. When the tab comes back to the front, it reads the mark and asks for the feed again.
  - Home asks for `?links=0`, which leaves out the link checks it does not use.
  - A first visit, a personal space, or storage that cannot be read shows no line.
  - The count's wording comes from `newActivityCount` and `newActivityLine` in `src/lib/activity-text.ts`, shared with the feed's own count.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems.
    - The link landed at the top of the Workspace page rather than its feed, because the card is drawn only once the workspace has loaded. The page now brings the card into view and focuses its heading once it is there, and the e2e test checks both.
    - The test's "the line has gone" check could pass before Home had read the feed. Home now marks when it has, and the test waits for that.
  - Also from its optional findings: the lighter request, the refresh on returning to the tab, wording that says what was last opened, and a cleanup in the test that the feed could no longer undo.
- Show more in the workspace activity feed (`ed554fc`). The Activity card listed only the newest 30 events of the 200 kept. A Show more button under the list now asks for 30 more at a time (`?limit=60`, then 90, and so on to 200), under the same filters, with "Showing the newest N." beside it.
  - **While loading:** the list shown, the button (busy, and keeping the focus), and the "30+ new" count stay as they were. They go by the length the shown list was answered for, which `GET /api/activity` now echoes as `limit`.
  - **Screen readers:** the status line announces the new count.
  - **When it goes:** the button goes once an answer brings fewer than asked for, or all 200 are shown.
  - **Filters:** picking an area or person, even the one shown before, starts again at 30 in one request.
  - **Code:** `nextActivityLimit` and `canShowMoreActivity` in `src/lib/activity-text.ts`. The kept total is shared with the server (`ACTIVITY_KEPT` is `MAX_ACTIVITY`).
  - An independent review found one blocking bug. Clicking Show more removed the button until the longer list arrived, because it judged the length just asked for rather than the one on screen. Keyboard focus fell to the top of the page, and the busy state never showed. The server now echoes the length it answered, and the button, the count, and the next length go by it. The e2e test delays the longer answer and checks that the button stays, busy and focused. From its optional findings: returning to a filter starts over, the shorter "Showing the newest N." wording is tied to the button, and the test checks every request made.
- Workspace members show when they were last active (`afc1834`). Each member row on the Workspace page now reads "joined <date> · last active <date>". It says "no activity in the last 200 events" for someone with nothing kept.
  - **API:** `GET /api/activity?summary=1` answers `{ lastActive }`, keyed by actor id, from every kept event rather than the page shown (`lastActiveByActor` in `src/lib/services/activity.ts`). Requests made with an API key appear under the space's own id. The summary answers on its own; the other parameters, `format=csv` included, do not apply to it.
  - **Refresh:** the list asks again after a member action, alongside the feed.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the summary now takes precedence over the other parameters instead of being overridden by `format=csv` or refused for a large `limit`, and the wording takes the kept total from the shared constant.
- Resend or renew a pending workspace invite (`f7d34b7`). Each row in the Workspace page's Pending invites list now has a button beside Revoke: Resend for an invite made with an email address, Renew for a link-only one. Either gives the same link a fresh 7-day expiry, so someone who has not got to it yet has another week. Resend also sends the email again when email sending is configured.
  - **Result:** the row says "Sent again" or "Renewed". The invite box shows the link with Copy link, and a line saying what happened: emailed again, the email failed (share the link instead), email sending is not configured, or the link was renewed. Each line ends with the new expiry date. A status line announces it to screen readers, and the focus stays on the button.
  - **API:** `POST /api/workspaces/:id/invites/:token` answers `{ invite, url, emailed, emailError? }`, as creating an invite does. It is for owners only. An expired, revoked, or unknown invite, or another workspace's, answers `404`; an invite that has already lapsed needs a new one. Each resend can send an email, so a second one within 5 minutes answers `429`.
  - **Code:** `renewInvite` in `src/lib/workspaces.ts`. Creating and resending share `deliverInvite` in `src/lib/invite-mail.ts`, which builds the link and sends the email.
  - An independent review found one blocking bug. A renew that read the invite just before it was accepted or revoked wrote it back for another week, so a used or revoked link could join again. The write now goes through only while the invite read is still stored (`putIf`), and a unit test removes the invite between the read and the write. From its optional findings: the 5-minute resend cooldown, the button keeping the focus, an always-present status line instead of announcing the whole link box, the row's own confirmation, and button labels that name the expiry.
- Invite several people at once, and copy a pending invite's link (`179ad49`). The Workspace page's email box now takes up to 20 addresses, separated by commas or new lines. The button then reads "Invite N people", and each address gets its own link, all with the chosen role. One address, or none, works as before. A list copied from a mail app ("Ana Diaz <ana@x.com>, ...") gives just the addresses.
  - **Skipped, not refused:** addresses that are already members, already have a pending invite, or repeat an earlier one are reported and left out. Matching ignores case.
  - **Room and allowance:** the room left under the 50-member limit, and an allowance of 100 invites an hour per workspace (each can send an email), are checked for the whole batch first. A batch too big for either invites nobody and says why.
  - **Result:** one line says who was emailed, whose link to share by hand (each failed email with its own reason, or sending not configured), and who was skipped and why. A status line that is always on the page announces it to screen readers. Emails go out five at a time.
  - **Copy link:** each row in Pending invites has a Copy link button for that invite's link, the same one its email carries. It says Copied and announces it.
  - **API:** `POST /api/workspaces/:id/invites` with `{ emails, role }` answers `{ invites: [{ invite, url, emailed, emailError? }], skipped: [{ email, reason }] }`. A wrong address in the list is named in the error.
  - **Code:** `createInvites` and `bulkInviteSchema` in `src/lib/workspaces.ts`; `splitEmails` and `bulkInviteSummary` in `src/lib/invite-text.ts`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the always-present status line (it lived in the Pending invites card, which a first batch has to create), each failed email's own reason, sending five at a time, the hourly allowance, mail-app name lists, no autofill of the owner's own address (and the email keyboard on phones), the batch note clearing when an invite is resent, and plainer wording when a link-only invite's link is copied.
- Expired invites, to invite again or dismiss (`8115ca8`). An invite that lapsed used to drop off the Workspace page without a word. Owners now see an Expired invites card under Pending invites, listing invites that lapsed in the last 30 days (address or "Link invite", role, and when it expired), the most recent first.
  - **Invite again:** makes a new link for the same address and role, with a full 7 days, and emails it when the invite has an address and email sending is configured. The new invite joins Pending invites, marked "Sent again" or "New link", and the link box shows it. The lapsed link stays dead. It is refused when the address has joined since or has another pending invite, when the workspace is full, or past the workspace's hourly allowance.
  - **Dismiss:** takes an expired invite off the list and says so in the status line. The focus moves to the next expired invite, or to the invite button.
  - **API:** `POST /api/workspaces/:id/invites/:token/again`, answering as creating an invite does; `GET /api/workspaces` lists `expiredInvites` for owners; `DELETE` on an expired invite dismisses it.
  - **Code:** `reinviteExpired` in `src/lib/workspaces.ts`; `EXPIRED_INVITE_DAYS` in `src/lib/invite-text.ts`, so the page's wording follows it. The hourly allowance of 100 invites per workspace is now `inviteAllowance` in `src/lib/invite-mail.ts`, and single invites draw on it too, as batches and Invite again do.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: Invite again claims the lapsed invite before writing the new one, so two clicks (or a Dismiss at the same moment) make one invite, not two; the 30 days apply to Invite again as well as the list; single invites draw on the hourly allowance; the button's name starts with its visible words; the focus lands on the new link's Copy button, or on the next row after Dismiss; and the workspace's invites are read once, not twice, for the page.
- Seats on the Workspace page (`891fec2`). A workspace holds 50 seats, counting members and pending invites, but nothing on the page said how many were used until an invite was refused. The Invite someone card now reads, for example, "12 of 50 seats used: 10 members and 2 pending invites."
  - **Nearly full:** with 5 or fewer seats left, the line is highlighted and adds "Room for N more." Pasting more different addresses than fit (a repeat counts once) adds a note that addresses already members or invited do not count, since the server skips those.
  - **Full:** the line says to remove a member or revoke a pending invite, and the invite button (and Invite again on expired invites) does nothing. It stays focusable, marked unavailable, and is described by the line, so a keyboard user is not dropped and a screen reader hears why. The status line says when the workspace becomes full, and when a seat is free again.
  - **API:** `GET /api/workspaces` gives owners `seats: { members, pending, max }` for each workspace they own; others, who do not see invites, get none.
  - **Code:** `seatsLeft` and `seatLine` in `src/lib/invite-text.ts`.
  - An independent review found one blocking bug: the invite button was disabled outright once the workspace filled, so the owner who had just taken the last seat was dropped from it to the top of the page. It is now marked unavailable instead, as busy buttons already are, and the e2e test presses Enter on it at full and checks the focus stays and nothing is sent. From its optional findings: Invite again follows the full state too, the seat count goes to owners only, repeats are not counted against the room, and the full and free-again changes are announced.
- Change a pending invite's role before it is accepted (`cf041a6`). An owner who invited someone as an editor and meant viewer had to revoke the invite and send a new one, with a new link and a second email. Each row on Pending invites now has a role select in place of the role's name.
  - **Changing it:** picking editor, viewer, or owner changes the role the invite will grant. Its link, address, and expiry stay the same, nothing is emailed, and whoever joins with the link joins in the new role. The status line says, for example, "dan@example.com will join as a viewer." or "The link invite now joins people as an owner." While the page is busy the select is marked unavailable rather than disabled, so the focus stays on it.
  - **API:** `PATCH /api/workspaces/:id/invites/:token` with `{ "role" }`, owners only. An expired, revoked, accepted, or unknown invite, or another workspace's, reads as `404`; another role is `400`. The write goes only over the invite as read (compared on its expiry), so an invite accepted or revoked meanwhile is not brought back.
  - **Code:** `setInviteRole` in `src/lib/workspaces.ts`.
  - An independent review found one blocking bug: the select sat inside the row's truncated text, so a long address pushed it out of view, where it could still take the keyboard focus. Now only the address truncates, the row wraps on narrow screens, and the e2e test checks that the select is shown whole beside a long address at desktop and phone widths. From its optional findings: while a change saves, the select shows the new role rather than snapping back, and the code comment says which simultaneous changes (a resend read just before, an accept at the same moment) can still win.
- Workspace invites in the activity feed (`196fe0d`). Member changes were in the feed, but invites left no trail, so a team could not see who invited someone, changed what an invite grants, or revoked it. Each of those is now a line under the feed's Members area, renamed "Members and invites":
  - **Lines:** "You invited "ann@example.com" as a viewer", "You made an editor invite link", "You invited 3 people as viewers" (one line for a batch), "You renewed the invite to …" or "You renewed an owner invite link" (renewed, since the email goes again only when email is set up), "You changed the invite to … to join as an editor", and "You revoked the invite to …". Inviting an expired address again counts as an invite. Picking the role an invite already has, revoking one that is already gone, or dismissing an invite that had expired leaves no line.
  - **Owners only see addresses:** the Workspace page shows pending invites to owners only, so the feed does the same. For a member who is not an owner, and for a script with an API key, the feed, its CSV, and the account data export leave the address off and read "invited 1 person as a viewer" or "revoked an invite". A missing address never reads as a link invite. One rule, `seesInviteAddresses` in `src/lib/workspaces.ts`, decides for all three.
  - **API:** new kinds `invite.sent`, `invite.resent`, `invite.role`, and `invite.revoked` under `area=members`. `setInviteRole` now says whether anything changed, and `revokeInvite` answers with the invite it removed.
  - **Code:** `hideInviteAddresses` in `src/lib/activity-text.ts`, applied in `src/app/api/activity/route.ts`.
  - An independent review found one blocking leak: any member can download the workspace's data export, and it copied the activity rows as stored, invitees' addresses included. The export now follows the same owners-only rule, with a unit test for an owner, an editor, a viewer, a script, and someone who is not a member. From its optional findings: a resend reads "renewed" rather than claiming an email went out, the line is recorded before the email is sent, and the Activity card's description mentions invites.
- Find a member or invite on the Workspace page (`a569e01`). A workspace holds up to 50 people, and finding one meant scrolling three lists: members, pending invites, and expired invites. Once a workspace has six or more of them together, a "Find a member or invite" box sits above the lists.
  - **Finding:** typing filters all three lists at once by the text each row is listed by: an address, a member's id when there is no address, or "Link invite". Case and spaces around the query do not matter. A list with no match says so ("No pending invite matches “zed”.").
  - **Announced:** a status line says what matched, for example "0 of 1 member, 3 of 5 pending invites, and 1 of 1 expired invite match “example.com”.", or "No member or invite matches “zed”.".
  - **Clearing:** Escape clears the box and keeps the focus in it, and a Clear button does the same, putting the focus back in the box. Inviting someone clears the search, so the new invite shows on Pending invites. A member who is not an owner sees members only, and the box and status line say "member" rather than "member or invite".
  - **Code:** `personMatches` and `peopleMatchLine` in `src/lib/invite-text.ts`. No API change.
  - An independent review found two blocking bugs. Clear removed itself while it had the keyboard focus, which dropped the focus to the top of the page; it now puts the focus back in the box. And a search that took the workspace below six people (by revoking while searching) hid the box but kept what was typed, so the box could come back later already filtering and hide a new invite; the box now stays while anything is typed. The e2e test covers both. From its optional findings: inviting clears the search, owners' and others' wording differs, an empty list is hidden rather than read out as "list, 0 items", and lists of three take a serial comma as elsewhere in the file.
- Download the workspace's people as CSV (`42d2977`). Owners keeping records of who has access, or reconciling seats with billing, could only read the Workspace page. A Download CSV link above the member list now downloads `people-<workspace>-<date>.csv`:
  - **Columns:** `email`, `user_id`, `role`, `status` (member, pending invite, or expired invite), `joined`, `expires`, and `last_active` (from the activity the workspace keeps).
  - **Who gets what:** every member gets the members, whose addresses they already see on the page. Owners also get the pending invites and those that lapsed in the last 30 days, as on the page, with a link invite reading "Link invite". Invite tokens are never in the file, so it cannot be used to join.
  - **Safe to open:** a cell a spreadsheet would run as a formula starts with an apostrophe, as in the other downloads.
  - **API:** `GET /api/workspaces/:id/people`, session only: `403` with an API key, which is not a person who could be an owner, and `404` for a workspace you are not a member of. Pending invites come soonest to expire first, whatever order the store lists them in.
  - **Code:** `peopleRows` and `PEOPLE_CSV_COLUMNS` in `src/lib/invite-text.ts`.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem: the link's spoken name ("Download the members and invites as CSV") did not contain its visible text, so a voice-control user saying "click Download CSV" could not reach it. It is now "Download CSV of the members and invites". From its optional findings: a request with an API key is refused, invites keep a fixed order across stores, and a unit test checks that a viewer's list has no invites.
- Filter the library by shape (`9fe9c4c`). Teams that make the same message as a landscape video and as portrait and square cuts for social had no way to list one shape. A "Filter by shape" select in the library (Any shape, Landscape 16:9, Portrait 9:16, Square 1:1) now does.
  - **Works like the other filters:** it combines with the search, the other filters and the sort, and applies in the Trash view too. A selection does not survive a change of shape, Clear filters takes it back to Any shape and off the address, and Download analytics and Select all matching follow it. A video stored without an aspect ratio counts as the 16:9 default.
  - **In the address:** `/videos?shape=portrait` opens the library filtered.
  - **API:** `GET /api/videos` and `GET /api/videos/analytics` take `shape=landscape|portrait|square`. Anything else, an aspect ratio such as `9:16` included, is a `400`.
  - **Code:** `SHAPE_ASPECT`, `SHAPE_FILTERS`, and `inShape` in `src/lib/client.ts`.
  - An independent review found one blocking gap: when the shape filter hid every match, the empty state's advice ("Try a different search, status, folder, presenter, voice, date, or share link") named every filter but the one doing the hiding, in the library and the Trash view. It now names the shape too, and the e2e test checks it. From its optional findings, two code comments now list the shape filter as well.
- Sort the Workspace member list (`f6214b1`). The members were listed in the order they joined, which says little once a team grows. A "Sort members" select above the list, shown once there are two or more members, now offers four orders, and this browser remembers the choice:
  - **Owners first** (the default): owners, then editors, then viewers.
  - **Name A–Z.**
  - **Last active:** the most recent first, from the activity the workspace keeps. Members with nothing kept come last.
  - **Newest joined.**
  - **Ties:** each order breaks ties by name (the address, or the id when there is none, numbers read as numbers), then by who joined first, so the list does not shuffle between loads. It works together with the Find box.
  - **Steady while editing:** the list is sorted again when the order is chosen or someone joins or leaves, not when a member's details change. A member made a viewer under Owners first keeps their row, and the role select keeps the focus.
  - **Code:** `sortMembers`, `memberSortFrom`, and `MEMBER_SORTS` in `src/lib/invite-text.ts`. No API change.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems. First, under a remembered "Last active", the list was drawn A–Z until the activity times arrived, then jumped, and stayed A–Z with the select still saying "Last active" if they could not be read. It is now drawn owners first, and says so, until the times arrive, with the option unavailable and a note saying why. Second, the row an owner just changed jumped to its new place, and the role select dropped the focus while saving. Rows now keep their places until the list is sorted again, and the select is marked busy rather than disabled. The e2e test changes a role and checks the row and the focus stay. From its optional findings: the ids sort as numbers and the e2e locator skips invite rows.
- Filter the library by length (`37ab2e7`). The library could sort by length but not show only the short cuts for social or only the longer walkthroughs. A "Filter by length" select (Any length, Under 1 minute, 1 to 3 minutes, Over 3 minutes) now does.
  - **By a finished render's running time:** under 60 seconds, 60 to 180 seconds, or over 180, judged by the time the card shows, so a video reading "3m 00s" is in 1 to 3 minutes. A video still rendering, or one that failed, has no running time and is in no length.
  - **Says what it leaves out:** with a length chosen, a line under the filters names how many matching videos are still rendering and are left out until they finish.
  - **Works like the other filters:** it combines with the search, the other filters and the sort, and applies in the Trash view too. Clear filters takes it back to Any length and off the address, the empty state's advice names it, and Download analytics and Select all matching follow it.
  - **In the address:** `/videos?length=short` opens the library filtered.
  - **API:** `GET /api/videos` and `GET /api/videos/analytics` take `length=short|medium|long`. Anything else, a number of seconds included, is a `400`. `GET /api/videos` also returns `lengthPending`.
  - **Code:** `LENGTH_FILTERS`, `inLength`, and `shownSeconds` in `src/lib/client.ts`.
  - An independent review found one blocking gap, older than this change but made visible by it. When HeyGen or D-ID reported a finished render by webhook, the video was stored with its link but no length or poster, because those events carry neither and a finished video is never checked again. Every such video would have been missing from all three lengths. A completion webhook that leaves them out is now checked with the renderer once, within 8 seconds, before the one write that records it, so the stored video and the completion notifications carry them. A check that fails records the completion without them.
  - From its optional findings: the note on renders left out, failed renders counted in no length, and fractional running times shown to the second from a minute up (180.4 seconds read "3m 0.4000…s" on cards). Under a minute they keep their tenth, so a silent scene still holds for 1.5s. A follow-up review of the webhook change found nothing blocking. From it, the renderer is asked only for what the stored video lacks, and the e2e test checks the in-flight render while it is still in flight.
- Sort My templates (`ab057c2`). Saved templates were always listed newest first, which makes a long list hard to scan by name. A "Sort my templates" select above the list, shown once there are two or more, now offers Newest first (the default), Oldest first, and Name A–Z, and this browser remembers the choice.
  - **Same rules as the Assets page:** names compare as people read them (digits by value, so "Alpha 2" comes before "Alpha 10"; case and accents ignored), equal names go newest first, and times go by when the template was saved, so a rename never changes its place by date.
  - **Steady while editing:** the order is worked out when a sort is chosen and when the list first loads, then held. A rename keeps its card where it is, a copy appears first, and a delete leaves the rest in place. "Sort again" shows once the cards shown are out of order, and pressing it moves the focus to the sort select.
  - **Works with the search:** the search narrows the sorted list.
  - **Code:** `assetSortFrom` and `TEMPLATE_SORT_KEY` in `src/lib/asset-sort.ts`, reusing `rankAssets`, `inRankOrder`, and `inSortOrder`. No API change.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: Sort again no longer drops the focus to the page when it leaves, it only shows for the cards in view (not one a search hides), and the e2e test saves its templates a few milliseconds apart and cleans up even when a save fails.
- Search and sort the Shared presenters list (`c07923f`). The Interactive page listed shared presenters newest first with no way to find one. Once there are two or more, a search box and a "Sort shared presenters" select now sit above the list.
  - **Search:** looks through each presenter's name, the avatar it shows, its opening line, and the site its knowledge page is on, with "N of M shared presenters" read out. A presenter just saved, copied, or being edited stays in view whatever is typed, and the count includes only real matches.
  - **Sort:** Newest first (the default), Oldest first, Name A–Z (digits by value, case and accents ignored), Most conversations, or Latest visit (never visited last). This browser remembers the choice.
  - **Steady order:** the order is worked out when a sort is chosen and when the list first loads, then held. A visit counted, an edit, or a copy (which comes first) does not move a row. "Sort again" shows once the rows in view are out of order, and pressing it moves the focus to the select.
  - **Code:** `rankAgents`, `agentsInSortOrder`, `agentSearchTexts`, `AGENT_SORTS`, and `AGENT_SORT_KEY` in the new `src/lib/agent-sort.ts`. No API change.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem: the search box only showed with two or more presenters, so deleting down to one while a search was typed removed the box but kept the search, hiding the last presenter with no way to clear it. The box now stays while anything is typed, and only the sort waits for two. From its optional findings: the match count includes only real matches, the presenter being edited stays in view, and the README wording.
- The Assets page remembers its sort (`cd9db55`). Its sort (Newest first, Oldest first, Name A–Z) went back to newest first on every visit, while the Avatars, Voices, Workspace, My templates, and Shared presenters lists all keep theirs. This browser now remembers it too.
  - **Quiet restore:** the saved sort is read after the first render and applied before the lists load, so they are ranked by it from the start with no "Sorted" note and nothing to sort again.
  - **Focus:** pressing "Sort again" moves the focus to the sort select, since the button leaves once the lists are in order (as on My templates).
  - **Code:** `ASSETS_SORT_KEY` in `src/lib/asset-sort.ts`. No API change.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings, the e2e test checks that the restore is quiet and in order.
- Move a brand kit between workspaces as a file (`ee30303`). An agency running a workspace per client, or someone moving from Personal into a team space, had to type a brand kit in again. Logo, colours, pronunciations, glossary, translation rules, writing style, caption style, and the opening and closing scenes all had to be redone. The Brand page now has "Download brand kit" and "Load from file", under the renamed "Fill from a website or a file" heading.
  - **Download:** saves the kit as `brand-kit-<name>.json`, holding every setting the page saves but not where the kit is kept or when it was saved. It is fetched first, so a signed-out session or a kit removed in another tab is reported on the page rather than saved as a file.
  - **Load:** fills the form from such a file for review, and saves nothing until Save. The server checks the kit by the rules a save applies. A default presenter, voice, uploaded background picture, or generated background clip that this space does not have is left out and named ("without the default presenter (not available here)"), so the rest saves as it stands. Loading asks before replacing a saved kit or anything already in the form.
  - **Refused files:** a file that is not a brand kit says so, and so does one from a newer version.
  - **API:** `GET /api/brand?format=json` (404 while none is saved) and `POST /api/brand/file` (editors only) → `{ kit, leftOut }`.
  - **Code:** `brandKitFile`, `brandKitFileName`, and `readBrandKitFile` in `src/lib/services/brand.ts`. The page's form filling is one `fillForm` for the saved kit and a loaded file.
  - The unit tests caught a file name bug before review: "Café Noël" became `cafe-noe-l`, because the separated accent mark turned into a hyphen. Accents are now dropped first.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the download is fetched rather than linked, so an error is no longer saved as a file. Load from file keeps the focus while it reads. The replace prompt counts every field (captions, format, quality, watermark, caption style, on-screen text, fit). A malformed version says "not a brand kit file" rather than "newer". A long name no longer leaves a trailing hyphen. The section's heading names both ways in, and the API docs claim less.
- Search the Video translate list (`eaf93f4`). The list held every dub ever made, one card per language, so finding last week's German cut of a listing tour meant scrolling past every other one. A search box above the list now narrows it by title, by language as the card shows it ("(Japanese)"), or by the site the source video is on, with a count of how many of the translations match.
  - **Stays in view:** dubs started while a search is typed stay on screen whatever their title, and so do dubs that failed to start, so the person sees what just happened.
  - **Kept, not removed:** a card the search leaves out is hidden rather than taken off the page, so corrections being made in a proofread are not lost when the search changes.
  - **Code:** `translationTexts` in the Video translate page, using the shared `useListSearch` and `ListSearch`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: cards are hidden rather than unmounted so a proofread in progress survives a search, dubs that failed to start are kept in view, the test names every record it creates and finds titles by a test id, and the README places the search at the end of the row. The empty state no longer flashes before the list loads.
- Filter the Video translate list by where each dub is (`33e0ab5`). With proofreading, a person has to come back for the dubs waiting on them, and with several languages per video those were spread through a long list. A "Show translations" select beside the search now narrows the list to In progress, Ready to proofread, Finished, or Failed. Each option shows how many of what the search finds are in that state ("Ready to proofread (2)").
  - **Counts:** the match count follows the filter as well as the search ("1 of 40 translations") and is read out when either changes. A filter that empties the list says so ("None of these translations have failed.") with a "Show all" button that returns the focus to the select.
  - **Stays in view:** a dub started under a filter it does not meet stays on screen until the filter or search changes. So does a dub whose lines were just saved or approved, so approving under "Ready to proofread" no longer hides the card the person is working in.
  - **Code:** `translationState`, `translationCounts`, and `translationFilterFrom` in the new `src/lib/translation-filter.ts` (which now also holds `translationTexts`). `ListSearch` takes `found` and `filtered` for a list with a second filter, and `useListSearch` gains `isPinned` and `unpin`. The other lists using them are unchanged.
  - **Fix:** the browser test's accessibility check found the proofread editor's line times below the contrast minimum (2.6:1). They now use the page's secondary text colour.
  - An independent review flagged as close to blocking that approving lines under "Ready to proofread" hid the card while it had the focus. The card is now pinned on save and approve, and a test covers it. From its optional findings: the count now follows the filter, and the empty-filter note is announced through the always-present count rather than a live region that appears already holding its text.
- Delete a translation from the Video translate list (`f056b65`). The list only grew: a test dub, a wrong language, or a dub replaced by a better one stayed in the list for good, so the search and filter had more and more to look through. Each card now has a Delete button for editors, and the browser asks first, saying what goes with the dub: the dubbed video and its subtitle links, the translated lines and any corrections, or that a dub still being made is no longer followed.
