  - **Focus and announcements:** the focus moves to the next translation's Delete, or to the list's heading when none is left (also when the next one went meanwhile). "Deleted …" is read out, and a translation already deleted elsewhere is said to be so. The buttons wait while a delete is sent, so a second click does not ask again.
  - **Deleted stays deleted:** status checks, provider webhooks, and the answer to a proofread dub's start used to read a job and write it back whole. Each now writes only over the job as it read it, and stops when the job is gone, so none of them brings a deleted translation back. A delete is refused (`409`) while a proofread dub is being started, until it shows as processing or the start is given up after two minutes. A start answered after its job was deleted logs the provider's job id. A retried start recognises its own write by the provider's job id even after a poll has moved the dub on.
  - **API:** `DELETE /api/translate/:id` (editors only) → `{ ok: true }`. It answers `404` when the translation is not there or is another account's, and `400` for an id that is not a translation's.
  - **Code:** `deleteTranslation` and `translationIdSchema` in `src/lib/services/translations.ts`, and `writeClaimed` for a proofread start's outcome.
  - **Reviews:** the first independent review found, as blocking, that a status check, a webhook, or a dub start in flight during a delete wrote the translation back. All three are fixed, and unit tests hold a provider call open, delete meanwhile, and check that the job stays gone. One existing test that failed the store's writes now fails its conditional writes too. From that review's optional findings: the busy state, the focus fallback, the confirm text for proofread lines, and a browser test that checks nothing is sent when the question is cancelled. A second review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the retried start's recognition by job id, a log line when a later start replaces an earlier one's job, and all Delete buttons marked as waiting.
- Try again on a failed dub (`df08e99`). A dub the provider refused, or that failed while being made, could only be set up again by hand: the source pasted again and the language, speakers, quality, music, speech, and proofread options picked again. A failed card now has a Try again button for editors.
  - **New attempt:** the new dub keeps the same source, language, and options. It also keeps the brand glossary and fixed translations the failed one was made with, so a retry dubs what was asked for. It takes the failed card's place and stays in view under the search and the status filter. "Trying … again…" is read out at once, then the outcome: under way, or failed again with the provider's reason. The focus moves to the new dub's Delete, or to its Try again if it failed again.
  - **Proofread dubs:** a proofread dub that failed after its lines were approved is not translated afresh. The same lines, with their corrections, open again to approve and dub once more, so no correction is lost.
  - **One dub per try:** the failed job is claimed with a conditional write before the provider is asked, so two clicks or two tabs start one dub (the other answers `409`). The claim is freed after two minutes if a try never finished. A delete waits while a try is claimed, and on the page each card's Try again and Delete wait for each other.
  - **Clean-up:** the failed job is removed only while it is as claimed. One a late webhook moved on is kept, and a failed removal is logged rather than reported, since the new job stands. A dub the provider accepted but the store could not record now logs the provider's job id. The route gets the same 60-second limit as starting a dub.
  - **API:** `POST /api/translate/:id/retry` (editors only) → `201 { translation }`.
  - **Demo mode:** the demo provider refuses a source with `demo=refuse` every time and one with `demo=refuse-once` the first time only, so a failed dub and a new try can be seen without HeyGen.
  - **Code:** `retryTranslation` in `src/lib/services/translations.ts`.
  - **Review:** an independent review found, as blocking, that trying a failed proofread dub again translated its lines afresh and deleted the only copy of the corrections. Those lines now open again instead, and a unit test covers it. From its optional findings:
    - the conditional, best-effort removal of the failed job
    - delete refused during a try, and the two buttons waiting for each other
    - the logged job id
    - the route's time limit
    - the immediate announcement, and every Try again marked as waiting
    - the refetch after any error
    - `demo=refuse` for proofreads too
    - notes on the provider and shared glossary
    - unit tests that check every setting reaching the provider and wait on a signal rather than a timer
    - a browser test of the success path
- Dub into another language from a translation card (`e0008df`). Adding a language to a video already dubbed meant pasting its address again and setting the lip-sync, speakers, quality, music, speech, and proofread choices again from memory. Each card on the Video translate list now has a "Dub … into another language" button for editors.
  - **What it fills:** the form takes that dub's source video, its title without the " (<language>)" that dubbing several languages at once adds, and its options. The glossary choice is switched on when the dub kept brand terms or fixed translations. A title the page made up itself ("Translation to German") is left empty, so the new dub gets one naming its own language.
  - **Languages and focus:** the languages are cleared, since this dub's own one is already made. The focus moves to the first language to pick (not that one, and never a disabled one), and the change is read out.
  - **Busy state:** the button waits while the form is sending or a dub is being retried or deleted, so nothing finishing then undoes the filling or takes the focus away.
  - An independent review found, as blocking, that a dub titled by the page carried "Translation to German" into an Italian dub. That title is now left out. From its optional findings: the glossary choice, the busy state, the disabled-language guard, and a test that sets every copied option away from its default, checks where the focus lands and the page-titled case, and runs an accessibility check.
- Sort the Video translate list (`d28ca55`). With dubs started over weeks, the list only ever showed the newest first, so finding all of one video's languages, or an early dub, meant scrolling. A "Sort translations" select beside the search and status filter now orders the list newest first, oldest first, by title, or by language. Each browser remembers the choice.
  - **Comparison:** titles and languages compare as the reader's language reads them, with digits by value ("Part 2" before "Part 10") and case and accents ignored. A language's dubs go by title, and ties go newest first. Dubs started together, one request's languages, keep the order they came in, so the list never reshuffles between renders.
  - **What doesn't move it:** a status change. Only a dub started or tried again lands in its own sorted place.
  - **Announced:** the new order is read out ("Sorted: Language A–Z.").
  - **Code:** `sortTranslations`, `TRANSLATION_SORTS`, and `translationSortFrom` in `src/lib/translation-filter.ts`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the announcement; ties kept in the order they came instead of by random id; test data whose sorted orders differ from the default, so a sort that did nothing would fail; an accessibility check; and comments that now say what case, accents, and a retried dub do.
- Recent batches on the Batch page (`141da71`). A personalized batch could only be found again from the library's batch view, reached from one of its videos, so checking whether last week's mail merge had finished meant hunting for it. The Batch page now lists the 20 most recent batches, newest first, each with its title, how many videos are ready, being made, or failed, and when it started. Each one links to its videos in the library and to its links CSV.
  - **Counting:** one video per recipient, chosen as the links CSV chooses it, so a failed row that was retried counts once, as ready if any of its videos is. Videos in the trash are left out.
  - **Freshness:** renders still in flight in the listed batches are checked with the renderer first, as the library's list does. Older batches are not polled.
  - **API:** `GET /api/videos/batch` returns `{ batches }` (viewers too). `listBatches`, `batchSummaries`, and `keptBatchVideos` are in `src/lib/services/batches.ts`; the links CSV now uses the same `keptBatchVideos`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: link names that start with the visible title and carry the start date, so two batches with one title can be told apart; long titles truncated instead of wrapping; a title taken from a recipient whose video title still ends with its name, so one renamed video does not rename the batch; polling only the listed batches; grouping in one pass; a note under the heading on how videos are counted; and tests for the retry count, the limit, and an empty library.
- Retry failed on a recent batch (`11e4b94`). A batch with failed rows could only be retried by finding its videos in the library, selecting the failed ones, and retrying them there. Each batch in the Batch page's Recent batches list that has failed renders now has "Retry failed (N)" for editors. It starts them again with the same settings and folder, up to 50 at a time, and says how many started and why any did not.
  - **Which renders:** the failed ones the batch's counts show, one per recipient. A row whose retry is already being made is not offered again, so a second click does not pay for it twice. `failedIds` on `GET /api/videos/batch` lists them for `POST /api/videos/retry`.
  - **Wording:** "already started again or no longer failed" for renders a teammate restarted (said plainly, not as an error). The allowance, when it stopped them, names it. Renders past the 50 are left for the next retry, which is pointed out only when the allowance did not stop them anyway.
  - **Focus:** a batch left with nothing to retry loses its button, so focus moves to the note that says what happened.
  - **Counts move on their own:** the list is read again every 8 seconds while any listed batch has renders being made.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed: focus fell to the page when a successful retry removed the button. From its optional findings: the note's wording when the 50 limit and the allowance combine, a neutral note for renders already started again, a test that runs both a started retry and one the server finds already started (and checks focus), a real batch without failures in place of a check that could not fail, a unit test for a row whose retry is still being made, and docs on which render a row offers.
- Copy links on a recent batch (`d35ad60`). Sending a batch's videos by email or chat meant downloading its links CSV and copying the link column out of a spreadsheet. Each batch in the Batch page's Recent batches list that has ready videos now has Copy links. It puts the batch's share links that can be sent now on the clipboard, one a line in the CSV's order, and says how many recipients were left out.
  - **Which links:** one per recipient, as the CSV picks them, so a failed row with a ready retry gives the retry's link. A video still rendering, failed, or with its share link switched off, expired, or not live yet is left out and counted.
  - **API:** `GET /api/videos/batch/:batchId?format=text` (viewers too) answers in plain text with the `X-Links` and `X-Unsendable` headers. It is built with the same `selectionLinkText` as the library's Copy links.
  - **Nothing to copy:** the note says why in place of copying an empty list. A browser that refuses the clipboard is pointed to Download links.
  - **Layout:** the row's controls wrap at phone width. Copy links and Retry failed wait for each other, since they share the note under the batch.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed: the "nothing to copy" message pointed to the CSV for the reason, but the batch CSV has no reason column, so the message now gives the reasons itself. From its optional findings: Copy links and Retry failed no longer overwrite each other's note or take focus from each other, a test comment that claimed more than it checked, unit cases for failed, expired, and not-yet-live links and for a row with a ready retry, and the list's intro now mentions copying.
- QR codes on a recent batch (`115bbe2`). Printing a batch's links on flyers, cards, or letters meant finding its videos in the library, selecting them, and saving their QR codes there. Each batch in the Batch page's Recent batches list that has ready videos now has QR codes. It saves a zip of one QR code (SVG) for each of the batch's links that can be sent now, named after the videos as the library's QR codes are, and says how many recipients were left out.
  - **Which links:** the same ones Copy links gives (one per recipient, as the CSV picks them). When none can be sent, the note says why in place of saving an empty zip.
  - **API:** `GET /api/videos/batch/:batchId?format=qr` (viewers too) answers with `qr-codes-<batchId>.zip` and the `X-Links` and `X-Unsendable` headers, or a `409` with the reasons. It is built with the same `selectionQrFiles` as the library's QR codes, now taking a batch's rows too.
  - **One action at a time:** Retry failed, Copy links, and QR codes share one busy state, so none starts while another is running or overwrites its note.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the "nothing can be sent" sentence is now one constant (`BATCH_NOTHING_TO_SEND`) shared by the server and the page. Tests now check that each SVG encodes its video's own link, the zip's headers, a `404` for an unknown batch, and the error style when nothing can be saved. A comment that still named only Retry failed and Copy links was corrected.
- Move a recent batch to the trash (`77b660e`). Clearing out a test batch, or one sent to the wrong list, meant finding its videos in the library and trashing them a page at a time. Each batch in the Batch page's Recent batches list now has Move to trash for editors. After a confirm, it moves every video of the batch to the trash: each recipient's, and the failed renders a retry replaced. Each can be restored from the library's Trash for 30 days. The move is recorded in the activity feed as videos moved to the trash.
  - **API:** `DELETE /api/videos/batch/:batchId` (editors) returns `{ moved, failed }`. A video trashed by another request in the meantime is not counted twice. One the store would not take is counted in `failed` while the rest still move, so asking again moves it. A batch with nothing left to move, or another owner's, is a `404`.
  - **On the page:** the batch's row goes, so a note for the whole list says how many moved and where to restore them, and focus moves to the list's heading. If another tab or a teammate trashed the batch first, the same note says so. When some videos could not be moved, the row stays with a note under it. After the last batch, the list says none are left.
  - An independent review found one blocking problem, now fixed: when the batch had already been trashed elsewhere, the error went to the batch's own note, which disappeared with its row, and focus fell to the page. From its optional findings:
    - A store error partway through now reports what moved and what didn't (and records the moved ones in the activity feed) in place of failing the whole request.
    - Nothing moved is a `404` in place of "Moved 0 videos".
    - The list note clears when another batch action starts.
    - Focus no longer depends on React batching two updates.
    - Clearer confirm wording, a visible focus style on the heading, and the list's intro mentions Move to trash.
    - Tests cover a video already in the trash, a refused write, and a batch trashed in another tab.
- Rename a batch (`f137939`). A batch named in a hurry ("Test 3"), or one reused for a new campaign, could only be renamed one video at a time, and each recipient's name had to be kept by hand. Each batch in the Batch page's Recent batches list now has Rename for editors. It opens a title field on the row; Enter saves and Escape cancels. Every video still titled after the batch, retries too, becomes "<new title> – <recipient>". Videos renamed by hand keep their own titles.
  - **What counts as titled after the batch:** each video now keeps the title its batch was given or last renamed to (`batchTitle`, set when a batch is made, retried, or renamed). A title cut at 120 characters, by the batch or by a rename, is still recognized next time, as are videos made before it was kept, by their " – <recipient>" ending. The list names a batch by this kept title, so a batch whose titles were all cut is still listed under its own title, not a recipient's.
  - **API:** `PATCH /api/videos/batch/:batchId` (editors) with `{ title }` (1–100 characters) returns `{ renamed, kept, failed, unapproved }`. A write the store refuses is counted while the rest are renamed. As with renaming one video, an approved video goes back to not reviewed; the note says how many did.
  - **On the page:** the note under the batch says how many videos were renamed, kept their own titles, or went back to review, and focus returns to Rename once it is enabled again. A batch trashed elsewhere while the field was open is said for the whole list, with focus on its heading.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, now fixed:
    - Titles cut at 120 characters (a long batch title, or long recipients such as email addresses) were taken for titles renamed by hand. The rename could cut them itself, so later renames skipped them and the note blamed a hand rename.
    - A failed write partway through ended the request with an error after some videos were already renamed.
  - From its optional findings: approvals sent back to review are counted and said; an Escape while an input method is composing, or while the title is saving, no longer closes the field; clearer wording in the note and the tooltip; and its own message for a title over 100 characters. Tests now cover cut titles, a second rename after a cut, a refused write, an approval going back to review, and an unknown batch.
- See who watched their personalized video (`1c646a8`). After a batch went out, the only way to see which recipients had opened their video was to open each one. Each batch in the Batch page's Recent batches list now says how many of its ready videos have been viewed ("2 videos · 2 ready · 1 viewed"). The batch's links CSV also says, for each ready recipient, how their video was watched, so the ones who have not watched yet can be followed up.
  - **CSV columns:** `views` (share-page views), `last_viewed`, `watched_to_end` (viewers who played it to the end), and `cta_clicks` (call-to-action clicks) come last, so a mail merge set up on the earlier columns keeps working. The counts are `0` before any view (`last_viewed` is blank), and all four are blank while a video is not ready. A recipient's line still follows its kept video (a ready retry over the failed render it replaced), so views are counted for the right recipient.
  - **API:** `GET /api/videos/batch` adds `viewed` to each batch: the ready videos whose share page has been viewed at least once, by anyone with the link (a check of the link before sending it counts too).
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings: the docs say that a view by anyone with the link counts, the account's own check included, and the browser test waits for both videos at once so a slow render fails the wait, not the test's timeout.
- Filter the library by views (`887e380`). There was no way to list the videos nobody had watched yet, such as the recipients of a personalized batch to follow up with. The library now has a views filter beside length: Any views, Viewed, or Not viewed yet. "Viewed" means the share page has been viewed at least once. A video still rendering, or one that failed, is in neither. On the Batch page, a recent batch with unwatched videos now says "N not viewed yet" after its viewed count, as a link to those videos in the library. There they can be selected and their links copied for a follow-up.
  - **API:** `GET /api/videos` and `GET /api/videos/analytics` take `viewed` (`yes` or `no`; anything else is a `400`). With `no`, the renders in flight that match the rest of the query are counted in `lengthPending`, since they join the list when they finish. The library reads `?viewed=` from its own address, and Clear filters takes it off. The analytics file follows it too.
  - **On the page:** the filter-bar note that says renders in flight are left out also shows for Not viewed yet. The batch banner says when it shows only the videos not viewed yet. The "not viewed yet" link is underlined, since a link inside a line of text must stand out without relying on colour (the accessibility check in the Batch page tests caught this).
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - With a length filter and Not viewed yet together, renders in flight were not counted, so the library did not say they were left out. They are counted now.
    - The note now also shows for Not viewed yet on its own.
    - The batch banner now names the views filter.
- Webhook delivery log and Redeliver (`84fdb88`). Settings showed only the last webhook delivery, so a delivery the receiving system missed (it was down, or answered with an error) could not be found or sent again. The webhook card now lists the last 20 deliveries to its URL under Recent deliveries, newest first. Each shows whether it was delivered (with the HTTP status) or why it failed, its event, and its time, and editors can Redeliver it.
  - **Ids:** each delivery's body now starts with an event `id` (`whe_…`) inside the signed body, and it stays the same when the delivery is sent again, so a receiver can ignore a repeat. Each attempt also carries its own id in the `X-Studio-Delivery` header (`whd_…`). A redelivery sends the same body with a fresh timestamp and signature, names the first attempt in `X-Studio-Redelivery-Of`, and is logged as "sent again".
  - **API:** `GET /api/notifications` lists `deliveries` as `{ id, at, event, ok, status?, error?, redeliveryOf?, resendable }`. `POST /api/notifications/deliveries/:id/redeliver` is for editors only and answers `{ "delivery" }`. An id no longer in the log is a `404`, and a body too large to have been kept (over 16,000 characters) is a `400`.
  - **Privacy:** the bodies sent, lead emails among them, stay on the server. Neither the API nor the account export returns them, and viewers see the log without Redeliver. The log starts over when the URL changes.
  - **Writes:** deliveries are now recorded with a conditional write over the settings as they are at that moment. Two deliveries at once keep each other's entries. A delivery that finishes after the webhook was removed, made again, or pointed elsewhere is not recorded. Before this, such a delivery could bring a removed webhook back.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - the account export left the bodies in and now leaves them out;
    - the record is a conditional write rather than a read then a write;
    - the event id is inside the signed body, so a redelivery can be recognised;
    - focus moves to the newest row when redelivering the oldest entry of a full log pushes it out;
    - a refresh no longer wipes a URL being edited.
- API key expiry (`7071f18`). A personal API key worked until someone remembered to revoke it, so a key given to a contractor or a short-lived script stayed usable indefinitely. When you create a key in Settings, you can now give it a lifetime: Never expires (the default, as before), 30 days, 90 days, or 1 year.
  - **After it expires:** every request made with the key is refused with a `401` that says so ("This API key expired on <date and time> UTC; create a new one in Settings"), so the script's owner doesn't mistake it for a typo. The key's last use stops being updated. An expired key stays listed, shown in red as expired, until it is revoked, and it still counts toward the ten keys an account can hold.
  - **API:** `POST /api/keys` takes `expiresInDays` (`30`, `90`, or `365`; anything else is a `400`) and the key carries `expiresAt`. `GET /api/keys` adds `expired` to each key, judged by the server's clock.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. It confirmed that every route that accepts an API key checks it through the same code, so an expired key cannot get in another way. From its optional findings:
    - The list now marks expired keys using the server's clock, not the browser's.
    - The refusal message says its date is in UTC.
    - A code comment that described the key check wrongly was corrected.
    - A test now covers an expired key.
- Choose which webhook events are sent (`ed14cf8`). A webhook used to receive every event, so an endpoint that only cared about leads still had to receive and ignore every finished render, and the reverse. The webhook card in Settings now has a "Send when" choice under the URL: a render completes (`video.completed`), a render fails (`video.failed`), and a visitor leaves an email (`lead.captured`). All three are ticked by default, as before. The webhook can't be saved with none ticked; the card says so, and screen readers hear the reason on the Save button. A note says when the ticks differ from what's saved.
  - **Behaviour:** an event that isn't chosen is neither sent nor logged. "Send test" sends a test whatever is chosen, and a logged delivery can still be redelivered.
  - **API:** `PUT /api/notifications` takes `events` (at least one of the three; repeats are dropped; an empty list, `test`, or another name is a `400`). Left out, the choice made before is kept, so a script that only changes the URL doesn't reset it. The webhook's `events` lists the choice and is absent when every event is sent.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - A list with repeats is no longer refused once it reaches four entries.
    - The card's intro now says only the chosen events are sent.
    - The "choose at least one" note is now tied to the Save button for screen readers.
    - The chosen events are worked out once when saving.
- Read-only API keys (`1974076`). Every key could create, change, and delete, so a reporting script or a dashboard that only needed to read the library had to hold a key that could also delete it. Settings now offers "Full access" or "Read only" beside a new key's expiry, labels each read-only key in the list, and shows a read-only key's example as a plain fetch.
  - **Behaviour:** a read-only key can list and fetch, and download the exports sent as `POST` (share links, captions, scripts). Anything that creates, changes, or deletes is refused with `403` and a message saying the key is read-only, review comments and asking for a review included. A read that workspace viewers can't make (a video's leads, a directed video's scenes) is still open to it. Like a viewer, it is not shown the webhook's signing secret or a digital twin's consent link. Keys made earlier keep full access.
  - **API:** `POST /api/keys` takes `access` (`full`, the default, or `read`; anything else is a `400`), and every listed key carries `access`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - The four reads that only editors may make (a video's leads, a directed video's scenes, the music catalog, a portrait's progress) now work with a read-only key instead of being refused as writes.
    - The docs now name the reads no key can make, and say what a read-only key isn't shown.
    - Settings takes its access choices from the server's list.
    - The end-to-end test also covers asking for a review, a scripts export, and a leads read.
- Rotate the webhook signing secret (`6b0d2e4`). The secret was made once and kept for good, so one that leaked, or was pasted somewhere it shouldn't have been, could only be replaced by removing the webhook and losing its log. Settings now has a Rotate secret button under the signing secret, with "Keep the old secret working for 24 hours" ticked by default. While the old secret is still in use, the card says until when and offers Stop using it now.
  - **Behaviour:** after a rotation with the overlap, each delivery is signed with the new secret in `X-Studio-Signature`, and also with the old one in `X-Studio-Signature-Previous` for 24 hours. A receiver still holding the old secret keeps verifying while it's moved over. Without the overlap, the old secret stops at once. The old secret is never shown again, by the API or the account export. Saving the webhook, or changing its URL, keeps the overlap as it keeps the secret, and the delivery log is kept.
  - **API:** `POST /api/notifications/secret` (`{ "keepPrevious"? }`; a bare POST rotates with the overlap) and `DELETE /api/notifications/secret` to stop the previous secret early. Both are for editors only and return `404` without a webhook. While the overlap lasts, the webhook carries `previousSecretExpiresAt`. The docs' Node verifier now accepts either header.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - Saving the webhook is now a conditional write too, tried again when a rotation or a delivery wrote first. A save racing a rotation can no longer bring back the secret just replaced or stopped. A save also no longer brings back a removed webhook, and no longer drops a delivery logged meanwhile.
    - An overlap that has run out is no longer carried forward by a save.
    - Settings for webhooks saved before revision ids existed are now updated with a conditional write as well.
    - Rotating during an overlap now says the earlier secret stops at once, and the checkbox is disabled while a change is being sent.
    - The docs' verifier compares byte lengths, so an odd header can't make it throw, and it rejects a timestamp that isn't digits.
    - Tests cover a save racing a rotation, the export, and a read-only key, which sees neither secret and can't rotate.
- Send a sample of a webhook event (`b393d7e`). Send test could only send a `test` ping, so a receiver's handling of a finished render, a failed one, or a new lead couldn't be tried until a real one happened. A "Test to send" choice now sits beside Send test: Test ping (as before), or a sample of a render completing, a render failing, or a visitor leaving an email.
  - **Behaviour:** a sample carries the event's own name in `X-Studio-Event` and the body, and data shaped like the real event's but made up (ids start `sample_`; addresses and links are on example.com). It is marked twice, `"test": true` in the body and `X-Studio-Test: true` as a header, so a receiver can try its handling and still drop it. It is sent whatever events the webhook is chosen to send, as a test always is. The log marks it "sample", and sent again from the log it is still a sample.
  - **API:** `POST /api/notifications/test` takes `{ "event"? }` (`test`, the default, or one of the three events; anything else is a `400`). A bare POST still sends the ping. Logged deliveries carry `test: true` for a sample.
  - Also: recording a delivery on settings saved before revision ids existed is now a conditional write, like every other write to the webhook.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - A sample now leaves out fields with no value, as a real event does, instead of sending them as null.
    - The docs say the lead sample is the share-page kind.
    - Last delivery in Settings says "(sample)".
    - Sending a sample again is announced as a sample, and its button's label says so.
    - A visible hint under the choice, not a tooltip, warns that a receiver checking neither mark will act on a sample.
    - The unit test now checks each sample against the data a real event sends, field by field and type by type, and covers logging on settings without a revision id.
    - The end-to-end test finds the sample by what it is rather than by its place in the log.
- Rename an API key (`a37e813`). A key's name was fixed when it was made, so one named after a script that later moved, or named in a hurry, could only be fixed by revoking it and handing every script a new secret. Each key in Settings now has a Rename button next to Revoke. It opens a field with the current name; Enter saves, and Escape or Cancel leaves the name as it was. Either way focus goes back to Rename.
  - **Behaviour:** only the name changes. The secret, lifetime, and access stay as they were, so scripts using the key keep working. Like making or revoking a key, renaming takes a signed-in session (a key can't rename itself or another), and in a workspace it takes an editor. The activity feed records it under API keys: "renamed the API key "Zapier" to "Zapier (prod)"". It links to Settings while the key exists and is included in the activity CSV.
  - **API:** `PATCH /api/keys/:id` with `{ "name" }` (1 to 60 characters; blank is a `400`) returns the key. It is a `403` with a key and a `404` for a key that isn't the account's. Activity events of kind `apikey.renamed` carry the new name as `subject` and the old one as `previous`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - A rename is now written only over the key it read. A key revoked while its new name is being saved stays revoked (the rename answers `404`) instead of being written back.
    - The once-a-minute last-use stamp a script's request writes is now conditional as well. It can no longer undo a rename made in the meantime, or bring back a key revoked while the request was in flight (which could already happen before renaming existed).
    - Saving one key's new name no longer closes a rename form opened on another key meanwhile.
    - The new-key banner follows its key by id, not by name, so two keys with the same name can't be confused.
    - The card's help text mentions renaming.
    - Unit tests cover a rename racing a revoke and a last-use stamp racing a rename.
- Pause and resume the webhook (`48cd156`). The only way to stop deliveries, say while an endpoint was being moved or was down, was to remove the webhook, which lost its signing secret and delivery log and meant re-entering the URL and events. Settings now has Pause beside Send test, Resume while paused, and a Status line: "Sending", or "Paused since …: no events are sent or logged. Send test still works."
  - **Behaviour:** while paused, no event is sent or logged, and none are queued for later. The URL, secret, chosen events, and log are kept. A test or a redelivery asked for by hand still goes, so the endpoint can be checked before resuming. Saving the webhook, even with a new URL, leaves it paused. Pausing a paused webhook keeps when it was first paused.
  - **API:** `PATCH /api/notifications` with `{ "paused": true | false }` (editors only; `404` with no webhook, `400` for anything but a boolean). While paused, the webhook carries `pausedAt`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - The pause is now checked on the webhook as it is at sending time, not on the copy the caller read. A lead captured just before a pause, whose dispatch runs after the response, is no longer sent or logged after the pause.
    - Viewers' Status line no longer tells them Send test still works, since they can't send one.
    - The Pause/Resume button no longer shows its icon beside the spinner while working.
    - The unit test also covers a stale copy of the settings and a rotation keeping the pause.
- Sample of a presenter's lead (`99d103e`). Send test could send a sample `lead.captured`, but only shaped like a lead from a share page, so an endpoint that handles leads from interactive presenters (which carry the presenter, the conversation, and the visitor's last message) could not be checked without a real visitor. The Send test choices now have "Sample: visitor leaves an email (share page)" and "Sample: visitor leaves an email (presenter)".
  - **API:** `POST /api/notifications/test` takes `{ "event": "lead.captured", "source": "presenter" }`; `source` defaults to `share`, and `source` with any other event is a `400`. The presenter sample has the same keys as a real presenter lead (`source`, `agentId`, `agentName`, `conversationId`, `email`, `name`, `lastMessage`, `capturedAt`), with sample values, and is marked as a test like the other samples.
  - The unit test captures a real lead through a public presenter and checks the sample's shape against it.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - The end-to-end test checks the request Settings sends for the presenter choice, and that `source` with `event` left out is refused.
    - The API notes say plainly when `source` is a `400`, and the route's description names `source`.
  - Two end-to-end tests that failed once each in the full run are steadier:
    - The Home test for comments that mention you now waits for the reload's link prefetches before it clicks. A click while one for the same page was cancelled left the router fetching the library without moving to it.
    - The Batch page's accessibility check now waits for the page to settle. Generate is enabled only once the debounced preview answers, and a check during the button's fade from disabled caught it at low contrast. This was seen in 2 of 40 checks before the change and in none of 60 after.
- Webhook events for dubs (`c0a2828`). The webhook told a receiver when a render finished or failed, but a dub (Video translate) could only be followed by polling `GET /api/translate`. Two events now cover it: `translation.completed` when a dub is ready and `translation.failed` when it fails. HeyGen sends the same pair for its own translations.
  - **Payload:** `{ id, title, status, targetLanguage, sourceVideoUrl, videoUrl?, captionUrls?, error?, createdAt, updatedAt }`. There is no provider job id and no proofread link secret.
  - **When:** each dub sends its event once, from whichever write moved it there: a status check, a listing's poll, the provider's callback, or proofread lines that never became ready. Each of those writes is conditional on the row as it was read, so of two pollers, or a poll and a callback, only the one that moved the dub sends the event. A dub refused as it is asked for (by `POST /api/translate` or a retry) is not sent, because the refusal is already in that answer.
  - **Choosing:** Settings lists "A dub is ready" and "A dub fails" with the other events, and Send test has a sample of each. A webhook sent every event is now sent these too. One that was chosen to send only some events keeps that choice until they are ticked.
  - **Repeats:** a dub can reach an end more than once. A proofread dub tried again keeps its id, and a late provider callback can finish a dub that was marked failed. A receiver should use the delivery's `id` (`whe_…`) to ignore repeats. An API save whose `events` lists the three earlier events is now a choice that leaves dubs out.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - The docs no longer say "once each", and they explain the repeats above.
    - The unit test now drives a failure through the provider callback (sent once, not again on a repeated callback or a read). It checks two racing reads send one completion, and that a stored proofread link secret is not sent.
    - The remove confirmation and two code comments now name dubs and leads as well as renders.
  - The two end-to-end waits added with the presenter lead sample now wait for a known signal instead of a quiet network. On a busy run, one request never finished, so the network never went quiet and the Home test ran out of time. The Home test now clicks its link again until the address changes. The Batch page's accessibility check waits for the preview answer and two frames, then any fade: clean in 60 of 60 checks.
- Webhook events for digital twins (`54d89ea`). A digital twin takes a while: it trains from the footage, waits for the person's consent, and is then kept as a photo avatar. The only way to know it was ready was to keep checking. The webhook now sends `twin.completed` once the twin is a presenter of the account, and `twin.failed` when training fails, the renderer never confirms the start, or the renderer loses it.
  - **Payload:** `{ id, name, status, consent, avatarId?, error?, createdAt, updatedAt }`. `avatarId` is the presenter to make videos with. The footage link, consent link, picture, and the renderer's ids are not sent.
  - **When:** each event is sent once, from the check that moved the twin there. Each of those writes is conditional on the twin as it was read, so of two checks only the one that moved it sends. A refused consent leaves the twin waiting for a new consent link, so it sends nothing.
  - **Choosing:** Settings lists "A digital twin is ready" and "A digital twin fails" with the other events, and Send test has a sample of each. As with the dub events, a webhook sent every event gets these too, and one chosen to send some keeps its choice.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - The docs list every way a twin fails: training or its picture fails, or the renderer refuses, never confirms, or loses it. They also say a retried start can be what marks a twin failed, and list the values `consent` can take.
    - The unit test now also covers a twin whose trained picture cannot be fetched, the samples of both events, and a webhook chosen without twin events getting none.
- Webhook event when a proofread dub's lines are ready (`403a8b1`). A dub asked to be proofread first stops once its lines are translated and waits for someone to correct and approve them. Until now that wait was silent unless someone kept the Video translate page open. The webhook now sends `translation.proofread_ready` at that point, as HeyGen does for its own proofreads.
  - **Payload:** `{ id, title, status: "processing", targetLanguage, sourceVideoUrl, proofreadStage: "ready", lineCount, proofreadUrl, createdAt, updatedAt }`. `proofreadUrl` is the Video translate page. The lines themselves, the link secret, and the provider's ids are not sent.
  - **When:** once, from the check that stored the lines. HeyGen does not call back when proofread lines are ready (or when a digital twin is trained), so these are found by the app's own checks: while the Video translate or Avatars page is open, or when `GET /api/translate` or `GET /api/twins` is read. The docs now say so. Saving corrections sends nothing. Once approved, the dub sends `translation.completed` or `translation.failed` as before. A proofread dub that fails after approval and is tried again opens its lines without a second event, since the account asked for that.
  - **Choosing:** Settings lists "A dub's lines are ready to proofread" with the other events, and Send test has a sample.
  - An independent review flagged that the first draft promised the event when the lines became ready, not when the app next checked. The docs and README now say when it is actually sent. From its optional findings:
    - The unit test now checks that a refused dub start and a retry that reopens the lines send nothing.
    - The docs note that a webhook chosen to send only some events gets this one once it is ticked.
- Background check for work nobody is watching (`7f3e64f`). HeyGen calls back when a dub is made or fails, but not when proofread lines are ready or a digital twin is trained. Those, and any dub whose callback was lost, moved on only while someone had the Video translate or Avatars page open, or read the API. So their webhooks could arrive hours late. A scheduled check now moves them on.
  - **Endpoint:** `GET /api/cron/checks` with `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET`, the header Vercel Cron sends. It asks about up to 20 running dubs or proofreads being translated, and up to 20 digital twins being trained or consented to, across every account. It works the least recently asked first, at the same pace a listing would. Whatever moves sends its webhook as if a page had seen it. The answer counts how many were checked and how many moved.
  - **Access:** without `CRON_SECRET` the endpoint is off (`404`). Any other caller, a signed-in session included, gets `401`. It sits outside the sign-in wall, since it checks its own secret.
  - **Schedule:** `vercel.json` runs it daily, which every Vercel plan allows. A Pro plan can run it more often, as can any outside scheduler calling it with the bearer token. `.env.example` and the docs say how.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - Dubs take turns across runs by a `checkedAt` noted on each. A scheduled run rarely lands on the server instance that ran the last one, so the in-memory order alone would have picked the same oldest dubs every day, and a handful stuck at the provider could have held every place.
    - The route has room to run (`maxDuration = 60`), so the webhooks sent after its answer are not cut off.
    - Each kind is reported on its own. If one fails, the other's counts still come back, with `ok: false` and a `500`.
    - The twin count says how many were really asked about. The dub `moved` count excludes changes a callback made meanwhile.
    - The end-to-end test proves the check itself sent the event, from the webhook log with no listing read first. The password-server test no longer depends on a local `CRON_SECRET`.
    - The docs define `checked` and `moved`, and note that on Hobby the daily run lands somewhere within the scheduled hour.
- Webhook events for the studio's other jobs (`02cd016`). B-roll clips, photo shoots, clean-ups, lip syncs, music mixes, voice recordings, and instant voices all take a while to make, and the only way to know one was ready was to open its page or read its listing. Each now sends `job.completed` when it is ready and `job.failed` when it fails.
  - **Payload:** `{ type, id, title, status, error?, pageUrl, createdAt, updatedAt }`. `type` says which kind of job it is (`broll`, `photo_shoot`, `cleanup`, `lipsync`, `music`, `voice_change`, `instant_voice`). `pageUrl` is the studio page that lists it. The renderer's ids and its signed links, which expire, are not sent; the job's listing gives the current link.
  - **When:** from the write that moves a job off "being made", so each finish is sent once. A finished job found failed later (an old link that could no longer be renewed, or an instant voice the renderer no longer has) sends nothing.
  - **Background check:** `GET /api/cron/checks` also moves on up to 10 of each kind still being made, across every account, so their events are sent with no page open. Its answer gains `jobs`, with `{ checked, moved }` for each type.
  - **Settings:** two new checkboxes under Send when, and samples of both events in Send test. A webhook chosen to send only some events keeps that choice; one sent every event is sent these too.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - A finished job is never moved back to "being made" by an answer that was out of date when it arrived, for example from a check on another server. Before, such an answer could overwrite a made clip, which would then finish, and send its webhook, a second time. A unit test covers it and fails without the guard.
    - The end-to-end test now proves the background check itself moved the clip, from its `broll.moved` count, rather than any finished job in the shared demo store.
    - The docs no longer promise a fresh link for voice recordings, whose links are not renewed, or for instant voices, which have none.
- Completion emails for dubs and digital twins, with a choice of kinds (`1526a2d`). The address in Settings used to get one email per rendered video, but nothing when a dub or a digital twin finished. Those take longest, and the owner is least likely to be watching. They now email too.
  - **Dub email:** names the language and links to Video translate.
  - **Twin email:** links to Avatars, where a ready twin is listed as a presenter.
  - **Failures:** a failure says why. Every field in the HTML is escaped.
  - **Choice of kinds:** Settings has checkboxes for Videos, Dubs, and Digital twins under the address, all ticked by default. `PUT /api/prefs` takes `emailKinds`. Leaving it out keeps the earlier choice, so a change of address, or turning emails off and on again, keeps it. Choosing every kind is stored as none, so kinds added later are emailed too. An address saved before keeps getting video emails and now gets dubs and twins as well, until they are unticked.
  - **When:** each email goes out from the check or callback that finished the job, including the scheduled background check for dubs and twins.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - `emailKinds` sent without the address is a `400`. Before, the save would have quietly turned emails off, since leaving the address out turns them off.
    - The Settings form stays locked until the saved choice has loaded, so nothing typed or ticked first is replaced by it. This also removes a race from the end-to-end test.
    - Dub emails link to Video translate rather than the provider's download link, which expires long before an email may be read.
    - The email and the webhook go out side by side, so a slow mail provider cannot hold the webhook back. A unit test shows a failing provider leaves the webhook untouched.
    - The kinds group is labelled by its visible text, and "Tick at least one kind to save" is announced. The confirmation reads "Results for dubs and digital twins will be emailed to …".
    - More tests: escaping of the language and twin name, starring keeps the kinds, and Turn off keeps them in the browser.
    - The docs say a retried dub can email again, and that only dubs and twins come from the background check.
- 4:5 portrait feed videos (`475f7b9`). HeyGen's v3 video API renders `aspect_ratio: "4:5"`, the frame Instagram and LinkedIn feeds show tallest. The studio offered only 16:9, 9:16, and 1:1.
  - **Where to choose it:** 4:5 is a fourth shape everywhere a video's shape is chosen: the editor's Aspect ratio picker, the Batch page, a saved template, the brand kit's default, and Make other sizes. It renders at 1080×1350 (720×900 at 720p).
  - **Playback:** the editor stage, video page, share page, and embed player play it in its own frame. oEmbed, the Embed snippet, and the email snippet size it 4:5. The library's Portrait shape filter now includes it.
  - **Imports:** a HeyGen Studio template made at 4:5 is kept as 4:5. A PDF deck of slightly tall pages becomes a 4:5 video instead of a letterboxed 9:16: a carousel post, or a Letter or A4 page.
  - **Not offered for music:** HeyGen's HyperFrames renders a music mix in 16:9, 9:16, or 1:1 only. A 4:5 video is left off the Music page, and the API refuses it with what to do: make a 9:16 or 1:1 size first.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - HeyGen Studio templates at 4:5 had been recorded as 16:9; they keep their shape now.
    - A 4:5 PDF deck had imported as 9:16 with white bands; it becomes 4:5 now.
    - The Embed snippet and oEmbed now size a 4:5 video the same way, from one function.
    - The Music page leaves 4:5 videos out rather than refusing them only at Mix.
    - Make other sizes takes a request naming every shape, or one with repeats, as it did before.
    - The music mix throws, rather than narrowing a 4:5 frame quietly, should the earlier check ever move.
    - The end-to-end test checks the player's own frame on the video and share pages, and that Music leaves the video out.
    - The docs list the new embed, email, and oEmbed sizes and the music refusal.
- 4K videos on HeyGen's Avatar III engine (`9c2e560`). HeyGen's v3 video API renders `resolution: "4k"` on its Avatar III engine for video avatars (digital twins and studio avatars) whose look supports it. The studio stopped at 1080p.
  - **Where to choose it:** 4K is a third quality in the editor's Output quality picker and on the Batch page. Templates, copies, retries, Make other sizes, and language copies keep it. It renders at 3840×2160, 2160×3840, 2160×2160, or 2160×2700, depending on the shape.
  - **Which presenters:** every presenter a scene shows must be a video avatar whose HeyGen look lists Avatar III. The avatar list now marks those looks with `avatarIII`.
    - A digital twin trained in the studio qualifies too, as it is a HeyGen digital twin look.
    - Photo avatars and uploaded portraits stop at 1080p, as HeyGen's do.
    - The editor holds 4K back while the presenter cannot render it, and says which presenter stops at 1080p.
    - The API refuses such a request with a `400` that names the presenter. A batch is checked once, before any row starts.
  - **What HeyGen gets:** `resolution: "4k"`, with `engine: { "type": "avatar_iii" }` on each presenter scene.
    - Avatar III takes neither Avatar V nor motion settings, so a 4K request with `avatarEngine: "avatar_v"`, `expressiveness`, or a `motionPrompt` is refused. The editor does not send them and says the engine choice is not used.
    - When HeyGen refuses a 4K render, the error says to make a copy at 1080p.
  - **Where 4K is not offered:** D-ID renders up to 1080p and refuses 4K. Brand kit defaults and cinematic shots stay at 720p or 1080p; a cinematic copy of a 4K video starts at 1080p.
    - A background music mix of a 4K video is rendered at 1080p, the size HeyGen HyperFrames is sent.
  - An independent review found two blocking problems, both fixed:
    - A digital twin trained in the studio is stored as a custom presenter, so it had been refused 4K with a message saying 4K was for digital twins. It is offered 4K now.
    - A 4K batch started from a template on Avatar V or with gestures had sent those settings and been refused, while the page said they were not used. The batch page now leaves them out, as the editor does.
  - From its optional findings:
    - The editor's Avatar motion and More blinking hints say they are not used in 4K, and More blinking is disabled there.
    - The 4K warning is a live region from the start, so a change to it is announced.
    - The unit tests check the hidden-presenter rule directly and match the engine message exactly.
    - The docs say a 4K video's music mix is 1080p.
- Avatar III as an engine choice (`ac1b5bd`). HeyGen's v3 video API renders scenes on its re-engineered photo-to-video Avatar III engine (`engine: { "type": "avatar_iii" }`) at any quality, for photo avatars as well as video avatars. The studio used it only for 4K; at 720p and 1080p the engine choice offered Avatar IV and Avatar V.
  - **Where to choose it:** Avatar III is a third choice in the editor's Avatar engine select. Templates, copies, retries, and batch rows keep it, and the API takes `avatarEngine: "avatar_iii"`.
  - **Which presenters:** a scene renders on it when its presenter is a look HeyGen lists Avatar III for (photo avatars included, at up to 1080p) or a digital twin trained here. Other presenters and uploaded portraits stay on Avatar IV. The editor names the choice "not sent" when no presenter in the video renders on it.
  - **Motion:** Avatar III takes neither expressiveness nor gestures. A photo avatar given them stays on Avatar IV with them, and a scene on Avatar III is sent none.
  - **Elsewhere:** the video page shows the engine. A batch started from a template on Avatar III says so, and can go back to Avatar IV. When HeyGen refuses a render that asked for Avatar III, the error says to make a copy on Avatar IV. More blinking stays an Avatar V setting.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - A digital twin trained here is treated as the video avatar it is: it renders on Avatar III when that is chosen, even with motion settings, and is never sent photo avatar motion. Before, on Avatar IV it was sent a gesture prompt, which HeyGen refuses for a video avatar.
    - The editor's More blinking and engine hints name the engine chosen, Avatar III included.
    - A 4K batch from a template with an engine says the template's engine is not sent, rather than contradicting itself.
    - The video page's 4K engine row says when no scene shows the presenter.
    - A unit test's title now matches what it checks.
- Docs: brand pronunciations and captions HeyGen burns in (`c6df179`). The API reference said the captions of a video with brand kit pronunciations always keep the real spelling. Captions HeyGen burns into the video are made from the script it is sent, which has the spoken spelling ("Nye-er" for "Nair"). The reference now says so, and that a video with a caption style is captioned from the stored script by the share page's subtitle track instead.
  - HeyGen's brand glossary was tried for these renders, which keeps the spelling in its captions, and left out: the workspace has one glossary shared by every account of the studio, and HeyGen only adds to it, so one account's pronunciations, or ones it has since removed, would reach every other account's videos.
- 5:4 landscape feed videos (`d71fc4a`). HeyGen's v3 video API renders `aspect_ratio: "5:4"`, the wide feed-post frame. With 4:5 already offered, 5:4 was the one shape HeyGen renders that the studio did not.
  - **Where to choose it:** 5:4 is a fifth shape everywhere a video's shape is chosen: the editor's Aspect ratio picker, the Batch page, a saved template, the brand kit's default, and Make other sizes. It renders at 1350×1080 (900×720 at 720p, 2700×2160 in 4K).
  - **Playback:** the editor stage, the video page, the share page, and the embed player play it in its own frame. oEmbed and the Embed snippet size it 5:4, and the library's Landscape shape filter includes it.
  - **Imports:** a PDF deck of slightly wide pages (a 5:4 feed post) becomes a 5:4 video. 4:3 and 16:9 decks stay 16:9, the usual video frame.
  - **Not offered for music:** HeyGen's HyperFrames renders a music mix in 16:9, 9:16, or 1:1 only. A 5:4 video is left off the Music page, and the API refuses it with what to do: make a 16:9 or 1:1 size first.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. From its optional findings:
    - The API reference gives the 5:4 sizes in the quality, email preview, and oEmbed entries, and says the music mix refuses a 5:4 video too.
    - The end-to-end test checks that the Music page leaves a 5:4 video out, and the unit test checks the PDF cut-offs, US Letter landscape staying 16:9 among them.
- HeyGen brand kit for directed videos (`c8f67fe`). HeyGen's v3 API imports a brand kit from a public website (`POST /v3/brand-kits`): the site's colours, fonts, and logos. Its Video Agent then applies the kit (`brand_kit_id`) to everything it builds.
  - **Import:** the Brand page has a HeyGen brand kit card. Enter the company's website and HeyGen reads it. The card checks back every three seconds and shows the colours, fonts, and logo it read, with Import again and Remove. The kit is used from the moment it is made, and the Brand page's own kit is left alone.
  - **Directed videos:** every video HeyGen's Video Agent directs for the account is dressed in the kit. The Video Agent page says so and links to the Brand page, and the video page names the kit. It works with a curated style too: the style sets the layout, the kit makes it the brand's.
  - **One account's own:** every account shares the studio's one HeyGen workspace, so the kit's id is read from the account's own record each time a video starts, a retry included. It is never taken from a request. A kit made by another renderer (after a move from the demo renderer to HeyGen) is not used.
  - **Cleanup:** HeyGen breaks a session whose kit is deleted. So a replaced or removed kit is deleted at HeyGen only ten minutes later, and only once none of the account's videos names it. Deleting the account deletes every kit it had at HeyGen; if HeyGen will not delete one, the account is kept, as for instant voices.
  - An independent review found no way for one account's kit to reach another. Fixed from its findings:
    - A kit replaced while a directed video was starting could be deleted at HeyGen before that video was stored. It is now kept for ten minutes after it stops being used.
    - Kits record the renderer that made them, so a demo kit is never sent to HeyGen and a HeyGen kit is deleted at HeyGen.
    - Account deletion no longer drops a kit's id when HeyGen fails to delete it.
    - The import's idempotency key is stable for a call made again after a timeout, so no second kit is left behind at HeyGen.
    - A kit HeyGen never finishes reading is taken as stopped early after fifteen minutes, so the card stops checking.
    - After Remove, the card discards a check still in flight, so the removed kit cannot reappear.
- HeyGen Voice audio tags in text spoken in a voice (`620affa`). HeyGen Voice performs audio tags in the text it speaks: an English word or phrase in square brackets, such as `[excited]`, `[whispers]`, `[laughs]`, or `[like a news anchor]`, that sets the feeling, the delivery, a sound, or a role. Public and instant voices on all three models perform them; professional voices do not.
  - **Writing tags:** the Voice changer's Speak text form has an Audio tag picker, with HeyGen's common tags first in each category (emotion, delivery, sounds, scenario) and all of its roughly 200 after. The chosen tag goes at the cursor, after any words selected. A tag that would not fit the 2,000 characters is refused rather than cutting the text.
  - **Claude's draft:** Add tags with Claude adds tags following HeyGen's own guidance: its common tags, one at a spot, moods changing a step at a time. The draft is taken only if every word, number, and mark is unchanged, the text's own tags stay where they were, and no tag lands inside other brackets. The text is locked while Claude works, and Undo is offered while the text is still the draft. It is offered only with Claude, or in the all-demo studio.
  - **What HeyGen will not do, said up front:**
    - The form points out bracketed text that is not a tag (HeyGen usually leaves it unspoken) and tags side by side.
    - A professional voice with tags is refused, on the page and by the API.
    - HeyGen drops the expressiveness boost when a tag other than [pause] is present, so the slider turns off and says why, no boost is sent, and the API refuses one.
  - **Words kept without tags:** a text's title and the transcript of a clip kept from it leave the tags out, closing only the gap each tag left, so a video's voice will not read them out. A tag HeyGen times as a word is left out of the captions.
  - **Fixed while testing:** the editor's Saved clips menu grew to fit a long clip name and pushed the page sideways on a phone. It now stays within the panel.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its findings:
    - A draft no longer lands over edits made while Claude worked, and Undo no longer restores an old text over later edits.
    - Inserting a tag near the limit no longer cuts the end of the text, and a selection is kept.
    - Text without tags keeps its exact spacing in titles and transcripts.
    - A draft that replaces the text's own tags, or puts one inside other brackets, is refused.
    - The demo writer is no longer offered as Claude in a studio that speaks with HeyGen.
    - Tags timed as words by HeyGen are left out of captions.
    - Drafts have an instance-wide hourly cap beside the per-account one.
- Lip sync options (`a40bde2`). HeyGen's lip sync takes a few options beyond the footage, the audio, and the quality, and the Lip sync page and `POST /api/lipsyncs` now offer them.
  - **Only part of the video:** From and To, as seconds (12.5) or minutes and seconds (1:05), sync just that part, from the start or to the end when one is left blank. The part must last at least a second and stay within the first 4 hours. For a library video, a part that starts within a second of its end, or ends past it, is said under the fields before anything is sent. The API refuses it too, saying how long the video is.
  - **Four switches:** take out the footage's background music, clean up the new audio's speech, keep the video's own length whatever the new audio's (HeyGen otherwise stretches the video to the audio), and keep its resolution and bitrate. Only the options turned on are sent to HeyGen, so its defaults stand otherwise.
  - **Kept with the job:** each lip sync stores its part and options, and its card lists them ("0:05 to 0:12.5 · music taken out · length kept"). A start retried with the same `Idempotency-Key` must ask for the same options, or it is a `409`.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its findings:
    - The page rounds moments to a tenth, as the server does, so it no longer allows a start the server refuses.
    - The latest start a video allows is named in whole seconds rounded down, so the moment named is one that is taken.
    - The page refuses a part past the first 4 hours itself, and the API's messages name the part's start or end instead of a field name.
    - `null` is taken as an option left out, as it already was for the part's start and end.
- Lip sync captions and frame rate (`ce4217e`). HeyGen makes captions for every lip sync and lets a lip sync's frame timing be chosen; the Lip sync page and API now offer both.
  - **Captions:** each finished lip sync's card has Captions: SRT and WebVTT, and `GET /api/lipsyncs/:id/captions` serves them (WebVTT with `?format=vtt`), named after the lip sync. They are HeyGen's own, timed to the lip-synced video (the SRT at `caption_url` on `GET /v3/lipsyncs/:id`). The signed link is asked for and read on every download, and nothing is stored. A lip sync still syncing, one that failed, and one HeyGen has no captions for are each said plainly. A failed download is said on the page rather than saved as a file. The demo renderer gives three lines over its sample video.
  - **Frame rate:** a Frame rate select under Options: constant (what most editing software expects), variable, or the footage's own timing, sent to HeyGen as `fps_mode` `cfr`, `vfr`, or `passthrough`. Left as it is, HeyGen chooses. The choice is kept with the job, listed on its card, and part of what a repeated `Idempotency-Key` must match.
  - HeyGen's `enable_caption` is left out: HeyGen now ignores it, since captions are always made.
  - An independent review found nothing blocking. Fixed from its findings:
    - HeyGen not answering, a timeout, or a dropped connection is now a `502` to try again, and HeyGen's own message (which names its job id) stays on the server.
    - Caption downloads are counted only once HeyGen is about to be asked, the account's own allowance first, so refused requests never use up the allowance every account shares.
    - The docs now say that a blank value means "left out" only for the frame rate.
